### Phase 4b — `hana_rubric`: `CommandRegistry`, startup validation, cached invocation · status: done (`3c5db62b`)

#### Work Order

**Goal:** a validated id → registration table, built once at startup, that dispatches a command by name with no reflection lookup on the input path.

**Spec:**

`src/command/registry.rs` — `CommandRegistry` and `CommandEntry` (title, description, cached `ReflectEvent` handle, and `Invocation`). They stay in one file until a second split criterion joins line count; the anticipated split is `entry.rs` (entry, invocation) vs `registry.rs` (construction, validation, lookup).

**`Invocation` — the resolved counterpart of the declared `Capability`** (settled Phase 4a Decision E). `Capability` is a compile-time const on the trait; `Invocation` is what the registry produces after allocating runtime resources, and it lives on `CommandEntry`:

```rust
pub enum Invocation {
    OneShot,
    Held(CustomInput),
    Unremappable,
}
```

Turning `Capability` into `Invocation` is the one place a `CustomInput` is allocated, and it is the only conversion between the two types. A `Held` command with no input is unrepresentable — do **not** reintroduce it as `Option<CustomInput>` plus a documented invariant.

- Built and validated **once in `Plugin::finish`** by scanning the type registry for `ReflectKeymapCommand` type data. Wire the plugin call in Phase 12; expose the construction and validation as callable functions now so this phase is testable with a bare `App`.
- Each entry's `CommandId` is produced here by `CommandId::try_from(entry.id)` — Phase 4a's trait carries `&'static str` because `CommandId` cannot be built in a const context. There is no generic `T` at scan time: the registry walks `TypeRegistration::data::<ReflectKeymapCommand>()` and sees only a `ReflectKeymapCommand` value (`id`, `title`, `description`, `capability`).
- Validation rejects: duplicate declared ids (`DiagnosticKind::DuplicateCommandId`), an empty title (`MissingCommandTitle`), and an empty description (`MissingCommandDescription`). Each diagnostic's `message` names the offending id. The consts are `&'static str` and always present, so **emptiness is the only checkable failure** — "missing" is not a reachable state.
- **Two further validation cases, for hand-written `KeymapCommand` impls** (settled Phase 4a Decision F). `reflect_command.rs`'s doc comment invites downstream crates to implement the trait directly, which bypasses `command!`'s compile-time id assertion and its `#[reflect(Event)]`. So add `DiagnosticKind::InvalidCommandId` (when `CommandId::try_from(entry.id)` fails) and `DiagnosticKind::CommandEventNotReflected` (when `TypeRegistration::data::<ReflectEvent>()` returns `None` for a type carrying `ReflectKeymapCommand`). Each `message` names the offending type path. A diagnostic, not a panic — an outside crate can trip these, and Phase 13's editor tooling needs the two failures distinguishable.
- **Shipped defaults that do not resolve are Phase 12's check, not this phase's.** This phase has no document model (Phase 6), no merge (Phase 7), and no embedded defaults (Phase 12's `.with_defaults(include_str!(…))`), and Phase 12's `Plugin::finish` already owns that validation.
- Invocation caches the `ReflectEvent` handle at build time. **No reflection lookup on the input path** — resolve id → handle once, then trigger through the cached handle. The handle is cached for **every** capability including `Held` — see the two-phase event below.
- **Allocate one `CustomInput` per `Held` command during construction** and expose a `CommandId` → `CustomInput` lookup on `CommandRegistry` (settled Phase 4a Decision E). `bevy_enhanced_input`'s `CustomInput` is a runtime-allocated `usize` newtype handed out by `CustomInputs::register_input()` (`bevy_enhanced_input-0.26.0/src/context/input_reader/custom.rs:23,79`), so it cannot be a const on the trait. This phase is the only one that walks every command, so it allocates them all in one pass; `Plugin::finish` has `&mut World` and can take `ResMut<CustomInputs>`. `hana_rubric` already depends on `bevy_enhanced_input` (`Cargo.toml:19`) — no new dependency.
- **A `Held` command fires a two-phase semantic event** (settled Phase 4a Decision E). Held commands are driven by `Action<A>` state, but BRP, scripts, and tests cannot hold a key down, and Phase 21 promises id-addressed invocation over BRP. So `command!`'s held form generates `pub struct $Event { pub phase: HoldPhase }` rather than a unit struct, where `HoldPhase` is `Begin` / `End`. **Both input paths converge on the same `CustomInput` before anything downstream sees them**: the physical key path has Phase 11's matcher write the input's `ActionValue`, and the event path has a `Begin`/`End` handler write the *same* input's `ActionValue`. Phase 19's single owner of held state watches one thing either way. Writing `End` when already released is a no-op, not an error.
- **`HoldPhase` lives in `src/command/capability.rs`** next to `Capability`, with the same derives (`Clone, Copy, Debug, Default, Eq, PartialEq, Reflect`) and `#[default] Begin`. It must be reflected and registered so BRP can construct the payload.
- **`command!` gains a third public arm, marked by a leading `held` token** and carrying no `capability:` key — the marker *is* the capability:
  ```rust
  command! { held, action: FreezeTrackingAction, event: FreezeTracking,
             id: "tracking::freeze", title: "…", description: "…" }
  ```
  Do **not** infer the held form from `capability: Capability::Held`. `macro_rules!` matches tokens, so a caller writing `crate::Capability::Held` or a renamed import falls through to the unit-event arm and silently gets an event with no phase. The leading `held` ident cannot collide with an arm beginning `action:`, and it is matched literally rather than through a fragment specifier, so arm order does not matter. **This needs no `bevy_kana` change** — Phase 4a already shipped `event!`'s field arm with `reflect:`, so `event!($Event { phase: HoldPhase }, reflect: KeymapCommand)` compiles today, and Phase 15's pin is unaffected.
- **`Held` commands are absent from the command palette** (settled Phase 4a Decision E). The palette is a discrete do-it-now list; a held command has a duration and no key to release, so a palette row for one either does nothing or sticks on. It still needs a title and description — those are its label in the key-binding editor and its hover text in the Phase 8 schema. Expose the two filters as methods on `Capability` so the palette, the keymap validator, and the schema generator read one answer rather than each re-deriving it:

  | | `is_palette_invocable()` | `is_user_bindable()` |
  | --- | --- | --- |
  | `OneShot` | true | true — any sequence |
  | `Held` | **false** | true — single keystroke only |
  | `Unremappable` | true | false |
- **The `Begin`/`End` handler is this phase's, and it is an observer registered by the same construction path** that allocates the inputs — it has to be, because that path is the only place holding the `CommandId` → `CustomInput` map. It lives in `registry.rs`. Phase 11 owns the *matcher* side of the same input; this phase owns only the event side.
- `#[reflect(KeymapCommand)]` is discovered automatically: `reflect_auto_register` is already enabled on this workspace's `bevy`. Note that automatic registration **never covers generic instantiations** (M4), so any generic command type needs an explicit `register_type`.

**Visibility:** `pub` — the read-only `CommandRegistry` query/invoke façade. `pub(crate)` — `CommandEntry`, its reflection handle, and mutable registry construction.

**Files:**
- `crates/hana_rubric/src/command/registry.rs` — create; `CommandRegistry`, `CommandEntry`, `Invocation`, construction, validation, the `CommandId` → `CustomInput` lookup
- `crates/hana_rubric/src/command/capability.rs` — add `HoldPhase`, `Capability::is_palette_invocable()`, `Capability::is_user_bindable()`
- `crates/hana_rubric/src/command/reflect_command.rs` — add `command!`'s leading-`held` arm
- `crates/hana_rubric/src/command/mod.rs` — declare and re-export
- `crates/hana_rubric/src/lib.rs`, `src/prelude.rs` — re-export the public surface

**Constraints from prior phases:**
- Phase 4a shipped `KeymapCommand` (with `ID`/`TITLE`/`DESCRIPTION`/`CAPABILITY` associated consts), the hand-written `ReflectKeymapCommand` type data populated by a blanket `FromType`, `Capability`, and `command!`. This phase consumes only that public output and adds no `bevy_kana` surface.
- Phase 4a added `Diagnostic::message` and three registry-validation `DiagnosticKind` variants. This phase adds exactly two more — `InvalidCommandId` and `CommandEventNotReflected`, both for hand-written impls (Decision F). Add no others: Phase 7 owns the capability and reserved-chord kinds. Decision D's variant-adding schedule is now three passes (4a, 4b, 7), not two.
- Phase 4a's `event!` already has a **field arm** accepting `reflect:` — `event!($E { $f: $t }, reflect: KeymapCommand)` — so the held command's `{ phase: HoldPhase }` event needs no `bevy_kana` edit. The field arm derives `Event, Reflect` (no `Default`, unlike the unit arm).
- Phase 4a's `command!` has two public arms plus an internal `@declare`; the `held,` arm is a third public arm delegating to the same `@declare` with `Capability::Held`.
- Phase 3's `CommandId::try_from` is the runtime entry point; its error is the payload-free `CommandIdParseError`, so a rejected id contributes no position of its own and the diagnostic's span comes from the declaration site.
- Diagnostics must not embed registry handles or `CommandId` — plain data only.

**Acceptance gate:** `verify.sh check|test|lint hana_rubric` green. Tests prove: the registry rejects duplicate ids, empty titles, and empty descriptions with diagnostics naming the offender and carrying the matching `DiagnosticKind`; a hand-written impl with a malformed id yields `InvalidCommandId` and one whose event carries no `ReflectEvent` yields `CommandEventNotReflected`, each naming the offending type path; an id resolves and `ReflectEvent::trigger` fires the event, observed by an `On<Event>` observer. For the held path: a `command! { held, … }` declaration compiles, lands as `Invocation::Held(_)`, and appears in the `CommandId` → `CustomInput` lookup; triggering its event with `HoldPhase::Begin` and then `HoldPhase::End` moves the *same* input value the matcher would write, and a stray `End` is a no-op; `is_palette_invocable()` is false for `Held` and true for `OneShot` and `Unremappable`, and `is_user_bindable()` is the inverse for `Unremappable`. A test fails when any registered command lacks a non-empty title or description.

### Retrospective

**What worked:** `Invocation` held its line — `Held(CustomInput)` is the only place a `CustomInput` is allocated and the bad state stayed unrepresentable. The cached `ReflectEvent` handle resolves once at build time for every capability, so no reflection lookup sits on the input path.

**What deviated from the plan:**
- `KeymapCommand` gained an `Event` supertrait and a **required** `hold_phase(&self) -> Option<HoldPhase>` method, and `ReflectKeymapCommand` gained a `pub(crate) register_held_observer: fn(&mut World, CustomInput)` field. The Work Order said this phase "consumes only that public output"; a typed `On<Event>` observer cannot be built from `ReflectKeymapCommand` alone, so Phase 4a's shipped surface grew by two items. The method has no default body deliberately: a hand-written held command that forgets it fails to compile instead of registering as silently inert.
- `command!` gained a fourth arm — a literal `capability: Held` match emitting `compile_error!`, placed **before** the generic `capability: $capability:ident` arm. Without it that spelling compiled into a command that allocated an input and ignored every event.
- `CommandRegistry`'s façade grew past `title`/`description`/`custom_input` to `CommandInfo<'_>`, `iter()`, and `capability()`. The declared `Capability` was otherwise dropped during construction, leaving `is_palette_invocable()` / `is_user_bindable()` with no external caller.

**Surprises:**
- Arm order matters after all. The Work Order's claim that "it is matched literally rather than through a fragment specifier, so arm order does not matter" holds for the leading `held,` marker but not for the `capability: Held` guard, which must precede the generic arm.
- `ReflectEvent` is re-exported at `bevy::prelude`, not `bevy::reflect`; importing it from the latter is an unresolved-import error.
- `iter()` has to sort explicitly — the backing map is a `HashMap`, and a palette listing and a generated schema both need run-to-run stability.
- `hana_rubric` has no reverse dependency anywhere in the workspace, so no application can reach its runtime path until Phase 12/14. The smoke target is `bevy_lagrange`'s `programmatic_control`.

**Implications for remaining phases:**
- **Phase 21** — ~~`CommandRegistry::invoke` takes an arbitrary `&dyn PartialReflect` … BRP-fed payloads must be validated before `invoke` is called.~~ **Superseded by Phase 11b.** `invoke` is now `invoke(&self, command_id: &CommandId, world: &mut World) -> Option<()>` — it takes no payload, consults no `TypeRegistry`, and dispatches through a monomorphized `fn(&mut World)` that builds the event with `KeymapCommand::build()`. The `from_reflect_with_fallback` panic hazard is gone; there is nothing to validate. The new constraint is the opposite one: **a BRP caller cannot supply an event payload at all**, so invoking a `Held` command over BRP always dispatches `HoldPhase::Begin`. Phase 21 must either accept that (BRP triggers the begin edge only) or add a separate payload-carrying entry point; it cannot get one from `invoke`.
- **Phases 8, 13, 16, 17** — read command metadata through `CommandRegistry::iter()` / `capability()` rather than re-deriving it; the enumeration is sorted by id.
- **Phases 11 and 18** — the `CommandId` → `CustomInput` lookup and the `Begin`/`End` observer both exist; the matcher writes the same input the observer does.

### Phase 4b Review

- **Phase 5** — `ConditionRegistry`'s enumeration must be stable run-to-run, or Phase 9 rewrites two `0444` companion files every launch and Phase 8's schema tests go flaky.
- **Phase 6** — corrected the `DiagnosticKind` schedule: three passes (4a, 4b, 7), 11 variants shipped, 14 after Phase 7.
- **Phase 7** — the registry exposes no index-shaped handle, so this phase defines it as an index into `CompiledKeymap`'s resolved-command table built in `CommandRegistry::iter()` order. Also removed the false "fuzzy matching is already in the crate" claim (that was hana's palette): a bounded hand-rolled Levenshtein at diagnostic time only, no new dependency.
- **Phase 8** — reads `CommandRegistry::iter()` (`CommandEntry` is `pub(crate)` and unreachable) and emits only `is_user_bindable()` commands; gate reworded, since Phase 7 rejects any file naming an `Unremappable` command. Same wording applied to Phase 13's coverage assertion.
- **Phase 11** — three constraints added: the command handle is a table index, never a `CommandId` rehashed per keystroke; `route_input` must be `fn route_input(world: &mut World)` using `resource_scope`, since `CommandRegistry` is a `Resource` and `invoke` needs `&mut World`; and Phase 4b's observer is a **second held source** in the refcount, not a peer writer, so a scripted `End` cannot kill a physically-held key and an event-driven hold survives a reload. **New pending decision** deferred here: how `route_input` obtains the event value `invoke` requires, given that `event!` registers no `ReflectDefault`.
- **Phase 12** — `initialize` returns a `Result` and does not insert itself, so `finish` owns the insertion; builder method names (`.with_app_name`, `.with_defaults`, `.with_protected_keystroke`) fixed here rather than only in Phase 17. **New pending decision** deferred here: registry validation is all-or-nothing, so one malformed third-party declaration yields no registry at all.
- **Phases 13, 16, 19, 20** — the shared `command!` constraint block said "two `macro_rules!` arms"; corrected to four public arms, with `capability: Held` documented as a deliberate `compile_error!`, plus a new bullet requiring every `KeymapCommand` impl to satisfy `Event + Reflect` and implement `hold_phase()`.
- **Phase 16** — its pending decision's option 2 is ruled out by the `Event` supertrait, and option 1's rationale was inverted: the two held events are the only BRP, script, and test path into those holds, not dead metadata. Options and recommendation rewritten; the decision itself stays open.
- **Phase 19** — materially smaller than written: read `CommandRegistry::custom_input()`, allocate nothing, register no second observer. "One owner for held state" clarified to mean one *consumer*.
- **Phase 21** — the claimed id → `TypeRegistration` map does not exist; replaced with the real registry surface. Added the `invoke` panic constraint: bevy's `from_reflect_with_fallback` aborts on a payload it cannot rebuild when the type has neither `ReflectDefault` nor `ReflectFromWorld`, which is exactly the held events, so BRP payloads must be validated before `invoke`.
- Phases 9, 10, 15, and 19 reviewed and need no change.

### Phase 4c — relocate `bevy_kana`'s entire `input` module into `hana_rubric` · status: done (`f876b36e`)

#### Work Order

**Goal:** `hana_rubric` owns keymaps end to end — keystroke parsing, multistroke matching, the authoring macros, and the `bevy_enhanced_input` binding builder. `bevy_kana` is left as math and cascade, with no input surface, no `bevy_enhanced_input`, and no `input` feature.

**Why this phase exists, and why here.** Every `event!` and `action!` call site across hana and nateroids is one half of a keyboard-bound command; nothing uses them for anything else (verified against `hana/crates/hana/src/input/global_shortcuts.rs`, which holds 32 `action!` and zero `event!`, pairing 1:1 by name with the 28 `event!` declarations outside `input/`, and against nateroids' identical split). Keystroke parsing is the same story one level down: `Keystroke`, `KeystrokeSequence`, `SequenceMatcher`, and `Modifiers` exist to turn a config file's `"cmd-shift-p"` into a match against a command, which is `hana_rubric`'s entire job. Phases 1 and 2 built them in `bevy_kana` because the plan then assumed `bevy_kana` owned input; that assumption is what this phase corrects.

**Why the whole directory rather than just the macros.** Splitting the module — macros to `hana_rubric`, parsing left behind — creates a boundary that three separate plan constraints already exist to work around, and moving everything deletes all three:

1. `PlatformShortcutMode` (`input/platform_shortcut_mode.rs:4`) is `pub(super)` and read by both `keybindings.rs:6` and `keystroke/mod.rs:21`. A partial move splits those two consumers across crates and forces the type into `bevy_kana`'s public API. Moving everything keeps it crate-private.
2. **Phase 15's central risk disappears.** That phase exists largely because two `bevy_kana` instances in hana's graph would make `Keystroke` and `SequenceMatcher` distinct types across the boundary. When those types live in `hana_rubric`, hana pins `hana_rubric` and `bevy_kana`'s instance count stops mattering for keymaps.
3. **Phase 11's global-allocator workaround disappears.** Its constraints record that `hana_rubric` "must install its own `#[cfg(test)] #[global_allocator]` (it cannot reuse `bevy_kana`'s `pub(crate)` one)" for the zero-allocation routing assertion. Same crate after this move — `allocation_test_support.rs` comes along.

**Why before Phase 5.** It is a relocation with no design dependency on Phases 5–13, so running it first means those phases are authored against the final layout instead of rewritten when it lands. It also frees Phase 11 to change `event!` and `sequence_matcher.rs` as ordinary same-crate edits, retiring the "last `bevy_kana` window" pressure on its pending decision.

**Breaking change is acceptable — the user has confirmed this.** `bevy_kana 0.3.0` shipped to crates.io with `keystroke` in it, so `0.4.0` removing it is breaking. Verified: **zero consumers**. Neither hana nor nateroids references `Keystroke`, `SequenceMatcher`, or `MatchOutcome` anywhere, and the only user outside `bevy_kana` is `hana_rubric/src/diagnostic.rs`.

**Spec:**

Move **all of** `crates/bevy_kana/src/input/`, plus the test allocator it needs, into `crates/hana_rubric/src/`:

| from | to | contents |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `input/keystroke/mod.rs` | `keystroke/mod.rs` | `Keystroke`, `Modifiers`, `CANONICAL_KEY_MAPPINGS`, the parse errors |
| `input/keystroke/sequence.rs` | `keystroke/sequence.rs` | `KeystrokeSequence` |
| `input/keystroke/sequence_matcher.rs` | `keystroke/sequence_matcher.rs` | `SequenceMatcher<T>`, `MatchOutcome` |
| `input/platform_shortcut_mode.rs` | `platform_shortcut_mode.rs` | `PlatformShortcutMode` |
| `input/event.rs` | `command/event.rs` | `event!` |
| `input/action.rs` | `command/action.rs` | `action!` |
| `input/bind_action_system.rs` | `command/bind_action_system.rs` | `bind_action_system!` |
| `input/keybindings.rs` | `command/keybindings.rs` | `Keybindings` |
| `allocation_test_support.rs` | `allocation_test_support.rs` | the `#[cfg(test)]` counting global allocator |

- Do **not** recreate an `input/` directory inside `hana_rubric`. The keystroke tree goes to `src/keystroke/` and the four `bevy_enhanced_input`-facing modules join the existing `src/command/`, which already holds `command!`, `registry.rs`, `capability.rs`, and `reflect_command.rs`. Keep each moved file's internal contents byte-identical apart from the import fixes named below.
- **`PlatformShortcutMode` becomes `pub(crate)` and stays private.** It is `pub(super)` today (`bevy_kana/src/input/platform_shortcut_mode.rs:4,10`), which works only because both readers sit under `bevy_kana::input`. After the move its two readers — `keystroke/mod.rs:78` and `command/keybindings.rs:86,117,188` — no longer share a parent module, so `pub(super)` stops resolving. `pub(crate)` at `src/platform_shortcut_mode.rs` is the replacement: both reach it, and it adds nothing to `hana_rubric`'s public API. Do **not** make it `pub`.
- **The three macros contain no `$crate` references.** They emit bare paths (`Event`, `Reflect`, `InputAction`) resolved at the call site, so no macro body needs rewriting when the defining crate changes. This is what makes the move mechanical rather than a rewrite.
- **`command!`'s call sites survive untouched.** `hana_rubric/src/lib.rs:15,17` reads `pub use bevy_kana::action;` / `pub use bevy_kana::event;` today, and `reflect_command.rs:172,192,195` already reaches them as `$crate::action!` / `$crate::event!`. Replace the two re-export lines with `#[macro_export]` definitions in the new modules; the `$crate::` references then resolve to `hana_rubric`'s own macros with no edit to `reflect_command.rs`.
- **`hana_rubric` re-exports the moved public types under its own root and prelude** exactly as `bevy_kana` did: `Keystroke`, `Modifiers`, `KeystrokeSequence`, `SequenceMatcher`, `MatchOutcome`, `KeystrokeParseError`, `KeystrokeSequenceParseError`, `EmptyKeystrokeSequenceError`, `Keybindings`, plus the three macros. `src/diagnostic.rs` switches from `bevy_kana::…` to the crate-local path.
- **`bevy_kana` sheds the whole input surface**: delete `src/input/` and `src/allocation_test_support.rs`; drop `mod input;`, `#[cfg(test)] mod allocation_test_support;`, and every `pub use input::…` / `TEST_ALLOCATOR` line from `lib.rs` and `prelude.rs`. What remains is `cascade.rs` and `math/`.
- **`bevy_kana/Cargo.toml` loses more than the feature.** Remove `[features] input`, the optional `bevy_enhanced_input` dependency, **and `bitflags`** — the latter exists only for keystroke's `ModifierFlags`. Re-check `bevy`'s feature list (`features = ["bevy_log", "keyboard"]`): `keyboard` was added for `KeyCode` in keystroke and is likely droppable, but verify against `cascade.rs` and `math/` rather than assuming. Also drop `"input"` from `keywords` and rewrite `description`, which currently reads "type-safe math, input wiring, and more".
- **`hana_rubric/Cargo.toml` gains `bitflags.workspace = true`** and drops `features = ["input"]` from its `bevy_kana` entry. **Then check whether the `bevy_kana` dependency is needed at all** — once `Keystroke` is crate-local, `hana_rubric` may have no remaining use for `bevy_kana`, in which case remove the dependency entirely rather than leaving a dead entry.
- **No feature gate on the `hana_rubric` side.** It already depends on `bevy_enhanced_input` unconditionally (`hana_rubric/Cargo.toml:19`), so gating the relocated code would buy nothing. The moved code goes in ungated.
- **Three in-workspace consumers need a `hana_rubric` dependency added and their imports repointed** — `bind_action_system!` call sites: `fairy_dust` (6 files: `restart.rs`, `screen_space_lights.rs`, `camera_control_panel/free_settings.rs`, `camera_control_panel/preset_switch.rs`, `screen_panels/help_overlay.rs`, `camera_home.rs`), `bevy_lagrange/examples/showcase/input.rs` (13 sites), `hana_valence/examples/triangles.rs` (1). Repoint the `use` line only.
- **Do not migrate any consumer to `command!`.** This phase is a relocation and nothing else. Those three crates are the examples/dev harness and keep declaring plain actions; the semantic migration to `command!` belongs to Phase 16 and applies to hana, not to them.

**Visibility:** unchanged from what each item has today, with one exception — `PlatformShortcutMode` goes `pub(super)` → `pub(crate)` because its two readers no longer share a parent module. The macros stay `#[macro_export] #[doc(hidden)]`; `Keybindings` and the keystroke types stay `pub`; nothing new becomes public.

**Files:**
- `crates/bevy_kana/src/input/` — **delete the whole directory** (all 8 files move)
- `crates/bevy_kana/src/allocation_test_support.rs` — delete (moves)
- `crates/bevy_kana/src/lib.rs` — drop `mod input;`, `#[cfg(test)] mod allocation_test_support;`, the `TEST_ALLOCATOR` re-export, and all 9 `pub use input::…` lines; rewrite the crate doc comment, which describes input wiring and an `input` feature
- `crates/bevy_kana/src/prelude.rs` — drop the 8 input re-exports
- `crates/bevy_kana/Cargo.toml` — remove the `input` feature, `bevy_enhanced_input`, and `bitflags`; re-check `bevy`'s `keyboard` feature; drop the `"input"` keyword; rewrite `description`
- `crates/hana_rubric/src/keystroke/{mod,sequence,sequence_matcher}.rs` — create (moved, contents unchanged)
- `crates/hana_rubric/src/platform_shortcut_mode.rs` — create (moved; `pub(super)` → `pub(crate)`)
- `crates/hana_rubric/src/allocation_test_support.rs` — create (moved)
- `crates/hana_rubric/src/command/{event,action,bind_action_system,keybindings}.rs` — create (moved, contents unchanged apart from the `PlatformShortcutMode` import path)
- `crates/hana_rubric/src/command/mod.rs` — declare the four new modules
- `crates/hana_rubric/src/lib.rs` — declare `keystroke`, `platform_shortcut_mode`, `allocation_test_support`; replace the two `pub use bevy_kana::…` macro re-exports with local ones; add the 9 moved public re-exports
- `crates/hana_rubric/src/prelude.rs` — add the moved public types
- `crates/hana_rubric/src/diagnostic.rs` — repoint its `bevy_kana::` keystroke imports to crate-local paths
- `crates/hana_rubric/Cargo.toml` — add `bitflags`; drop `features = ["input"]`, and drop the `bevy_kana` dependency outright if nothing still uses it
- `crates/fairy_dust/Cargo.toml` + its 6 `bind_action_system!` files — add the dep, repoint imports
- `crates/bevy_lagrange/Cargo.toml` (`[dev-dependencies]`) + `examples/showcase/input.rs` — add the dep, repoint imports
- `crates/hana_valence/Cargo.toml` (`[dev-dependencies]`) + `examples/triangles.rs` — add the dep, repoint imports

**Constraints from prior phases:**
- Phase 4a shipped `event!`'s field arm carrying `reflect:`, and Phase 4b's `command!` reaches both macros through `$crate`. Neither arm's behavior may change here — a relocation that also alters expansion is two changes wearing one commit. The same holds for every keystroke file: move the bytes, fix the imports, change nothing else.
- **This phase retires the "Phases 4a and 11 are the last permitted to edit `bevy_kana`" rule entirely.** After it lands `bevy_kana` has no input surface at all, so Phase 11's option B (`sequence_matcher.rs`) and any later `event!` change are ordinary same-crate `hana_rubric` edits. Phase 15 no longer has to reason about when `bevy_kana` last changed.
- **Phase 11's `#[cfg(test)] #[global_allocator]` constraint is void.** It reads: "`hana_rubric` must install its own … (it cannot reuse `bevy_kana`'s `pub(crate)` one) whose counter is `thread_local!`". `allocation_test_support.rs` moves here, so the zero-allocation routing assertion uses the same `TEST_ALLOCATOR` the keystroke tests already use. The `thread_local!` counter caveat still applies — the assertion must call the routing function directly rather than through `app.update()`.
- **Phase 15's dependency-unification problem is largely dissolved** but not deleted: hana still pins `hana_rubric` by git rev, and still needs one `bevy_kana` instance if anything in its graph uses `cascade` or `math`. What goes away is the type-identity hazard — `Keystroke` and `SequenceMatcher` now cross the boundary as `hana_rubric` types, so duplicate `bevy_kana` instances can no longer make them distinct.
- **`bevy_kana`'s `[dev-dependencies]` name `fairy_dust` (`Cargo.toml:30`)**, which this phase gives a `hana_rubric` dependency. If `hana_rubric` keeps its `bevy_kana` dependency that is a dev-dependency cycle; cargo permits these, but confirm the workspace still resolves rather than assuming it. If the `bevy_kana` dependency is dropped, the cycle goes away on its own.
- Phase 1's acceptance gate asserted `cargo tree -p bevy_kana --no-default-features -e normal` shows `bevy_enhanced_input` absent "while `keystroke` still compiles". That gate is **moot** after this phase — neither the dependency nor the module is in `bevy_kana` any more.
- `bevy_kana` is at `0.4.0-dev` (unreleased). Published `0.3.0` carries `keystroke` and `input`, so `0.4.0` removing both is a **breaking change, and that is accepted** — zero consumers reference the moved types. **nateroids stays on published `0.3.0` until it migrates**, so do not publish `0.4.0` before then.
- `.claude/config/release.toml` carries `[[publish_path_pins]]` for `bevy_kana`. Adding `hana_rubric` as a dev-dependency of a published crate (`bevy_lagrange`) may need a matching pin entry — verify before Phase 15, not necessarily here.

**Acceptance gate:** `verify.sh check|test|lint bevy_kana` and `verify.sh check|test|lint hana_rubric` green, plus `verify.sh check|test|lint fairy_dust`, `verify.sh example bevy_lagrange showcase`, and `verify.sh example hana_valence triangles` — the move touches all five packages, and the three consumer crates are exactly where a missed import surfaces. Proofs required:

- **The Phase 1 and Phase 2 keystroke suites pass unchanged in their new home.** That is roughly 1300 lines (`keystroke/mod.rs` ~650, `sequence_matcher.rs` ~459, `sequence.rs` ~198) covering modifier-order aliases, `cmd`/`super`/`win` normalization, platform chord distinctness, `Display` round-tripping, typed parse errors with byte offsets, non-empty `KeystrokeSequence` construction, and multistroke matching. Not one assertion may be edited or deleted. An unchanged passing suite is the proof of a correct relocation.
- `cargo tree -p bevy_kana -e normal` shows **neither `bevy_enhanced_input` nor `bitflags`**. `-e normal` is required: `bevy_kana` dev-depends on `fairy_dust`, so the default edge set reports dev-only crates as present.
- `bevy_kana`'s remaining modules are exactly `cascade` and `math`.
- `hana_rubric`'s existing Phase 4a/4b `command!` tests pass unchanged, proving the `$crate::event!` / `$crate::action!` call sites still resolve, and a `command! { held, … }` still lands as `Invocation::Held(_)`.
- The zero-allocation keystroke assertions still hold using `hana_rubric`'s own `TEST_ALLOCATOR`.

No new test is required for the relocation itself.

### Retrospective

**What worked:**
- The relocation was genuinely mechanical. `allocation_test_support.rs`, `keystroke/sequence.rs`, and `keystroke/sequence_matcher.rs` moved with **zero** body changes; the rest changed only import lines (1–4 each). The Phase 1/2 keystroke suite — 28 tests — passed unchanged in its new home, which is the proof the move was clean.
- The `$crate` prediction held exactly. `reflect_command.rs`'s `$crate::action!` / `$crate::event!` call sites needed no edit: `#[macro_export]` hoists a macro to the crate root regardless of module nesting, so `command/mod.rs` declares `mod action;` privately and `$crate::action!` still resolves.
- Dropping the `bevy_kana` dependency from `hana_rubric` outright was the right call — nothing remained that used it. `hana_rubric` now has **zero** `bevy_kana` references in `src/`.

**What deviated from the plan:**
- **The Work Order's Files list missed two real consumers**: `crates/bevy_lagrange/examples/programmatic_control.rs` and `crates/fairy_dust/src/fold_controls.rs`. fairy_dust has **7** `bind_action_system!` files, not 6, and `bevy_lagrange` has two example call sites, not one. Both were found and repointed. Total: 28 `use hana_rubric::` lines across 10 files.
- **The Spec's claim that `src/diagnostic.rs` imports `bevy_kana::` keystroke types is wrong.** It does not, and never did. It uses the *word* "keystroke" in an enum variant (`DiagnosticKind::Keystroke`) and a `String` field (`original_keystroke`), but never the types. No edit was needed there.
- **The acceptance gate's `bitflags` proof was impossible as written.** It demanded `cargo tree -p bevy_kana -e normal` show no `bitflags`; `bitflags v2.13.0` arrives transitively through `bevy_ecs` and cannot be removed. Only the **direct** dependency edge can go, and it did. Read that gate line as "no direct `bitflags` edge".
- `bevy`'s `keyboard` feature was in fact droppable, as the Work Order suspected — `cascade.rs` and `math/` do not need it. `bevy_kana` is now `features = ["bevy_log"]` only.

**Surprises:**
- **rustc does not report dead code originating from an *external* crate's macro expansion.** While `action!` lived in `bevy_kana`, `hana_rubric`'s test-only action marker structs were external-macro output and went unreported. Moving the macro made the expansion in-crate, so the lint began firing — with nothing about the test code changed. This is the single most transferable fact from the phase.
- The first implementation silenced that new warning by putting `#[cfg_attr(test, allow(dead_code, …))]` **inside the `action!` macro body**. Both reviews caught it as a blocker, correctly: a `macro_rules!` body expands at the *call site* and `cfg(test)` evaluates in the *calling* crate, so the suppression would have disabled dead-code detection for every `action!` in every downstream consumer's test build. The fix moved the allowance to the two `#[cfg(test)]` modules in `hana_rubric` that actually trigger it (`command/reflect_command.rs`, `command/registry.rs`). Afterwards `fairy_dust` surfaced no dead `action!` markers — the suppression was masking nothing real.
- `#[doc(hidden)]` now sits on the local `action!` / `event!` definitions where the `bevy_kana` originals had none. **This is not drift.** The pre-phase `hana_rubric/src/lib.rs` carried `#[doc(hidden)] pub use bevy_kana::action;`, so `hana_rubric::action` was already hidden from rustdoc; moving the attribute onto the definition preserves exactly the prior visibility. Do not "fix" it.
- A workspace-wide `cargo-mend --all-targets` job (the user's `lint` script) held this repo's build lock for ~10 minutes mid-phase. Cargo's target-dir lock is per-repo and there is no shared `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` here, so any `--workspace --all-targets` job blocks every other cargo invocation in the repo. Delegate stalls of that length are worth checking against `ps` before assuming the agent hung.

**Implications for remaining phases:**
- **The dead-code lint recurs only for `command!` / `action!` call sites *inside* `crates/hana_rubric/src/` — which means Phases 11 and 12, not Phase 16.** The rule is that rustc exempts expansions of *external*-crate macros, and hana imports `action!` (external both before and after Phase 15), so hana's declarations stay exempt. The two existing suppressions are at `command/registry.rs` and `command/reflect_command.rs`, both `#[cfg(test)]` modules; any new in-crate test command will need the same. The fix is always at the declaration site, never in the macro body.
- Phase 11's `#[cfg(test)] #[global_allocator]` constraint is now void as predicted — `TEST_ALLOCATOR` is crate-local. The `thread_local!` counter caveat still stands.
- Phase 15 shrank to a git-rev pin, dropping `features = ["input"]`, repointing macro imports, and `reflect_auto_register`. `bevy_kana` no longer has an `input` feature at all.
- `hana_rubric` is now a dev-dependency of `bevy_lagrange` and `hana_valence` and a normal dependency of `fairy_dust`. `.claude/config/release.toml`'s `[[publish_path_pins]]` may need a `hana_rubric` entry before anything publishes.

### Phase 4c Review

No remaining phase became redundant and none needs re-ordering. Changes made:

- **Delegation Context** — the `crates/hana_rubric/src/` layout list gained the nine files 4c added; the `bevy_kana` dependency is recorded as removed rather than "may be removed"; the public keystroke surface is re-attributed to `hana_rubric`; `bevy_kana`'s manifest line corrected to `features = ["bevy_log"]` with no `bitflags`; the `bevy_kana/src/lib.rs` prelude cite corrected to line 25.
- **Phase 6** — `KeystrokeParseError::at_offset` is `pub(super)` off the **crate root** now, so it is reachable crate-wide and `keystroke/sequence.rs` already calls it. Restated as a convention the phase keeps by hand, not a compiler-enforced boundary. `KeystrokeSequence` re-attributed.
- **Phase 10** — repaired a corrupted constraint bullet: the `Diagnostic::message` entry had been spliced into the middle of the Phase 9 entry, splitting `companion_files.rs (\`pub(super)\`…)` across two bullets.
- **Phase 11** — the "still permitted to edit `bevy_kana`" constraint is retired; option B's `sequence_matcher.rs` edit is same-crate now, so `bevy_kana` left the acceptance gate. Gained the in-crate dead-code-lint constraint.
- **Phase 12** — dependency diagram corrected from `keymap → …, bevy_kana` to `keystroke`. Gained the same dead-code-lint constraint.
- **Phase 15** — "both deps must move to the same rev or `Keystroke` stays distinct" is void: `hana_rubric` has no `bevy_kana` dependency, so pinning `hana_rubric` alone settles type identity. Goal restated as one **`hana_rubric`** instance. Version facts corrected (`0.4.0-dev`, `0.3.0` already published). Gained an explicit finding that `release.toml` needs no change (below).
- **Phases 16, 17, 19** — `PlatformShortcutMode` re-cited at `hana_rubric/src/platform_shortcut_mode.rs` as `pub(crate)`; the three private modifier action types re-attributed to `hana_rubric/src/command/keybindings.rs`; "Phase 15 unified `bevy_kana`" corrected to `hana_rubric`.
- **Phases 5, 9, 11, 12, 13, 19, 20** — the quoted `hana_rubric::prelude` inventory (7 items) corrected to the current 19; the load-bearing claim that it covers neither `command!` nor the four bevy/enhanced-input names is unchanged.
- **Phases 5, 11, 12** — acceptance gates gained `verify.sh check fairy_dust`. These phases edit `hana_rubric`'s crate root and prelude, and 4c made `fairy_dust` the normal-dependency consumer where a broken re-export surfaces; without it a re-export rename would only fail in CI's workspace run.

Findings resolved against the review rather than applied as written:

- **The retrospective's own claim that the dead-code lint would recur in Phase 16 was wrong** and has been rewritten. By the rule it cites, hana imports `action!` as an *external* macro both before and after Phase 15, so hana's declarations stay exempt. The lint can only fire for call sites inside `crates/hana_rubric/src/` — Phases 11 and 12 — which is where the constraint now sits.
- **`fairy_dust`'s new normal dependency on unpublished `hana_rubric` does not block any release.** Reviewed as a possible `cargo publish` failure; it is not one. `fairy_dust` is workspace version `0.0.1`, has no `[[publish_path_pins]]` entry, and is itself only a path-only dev-dependency of `bevy_lagrange` — so it is not published, and cargo strips path-only dev-deps at publish time, which is why `bevy_lagrange` and `hana_valence` taking `hana_rubric` as dev-deps needs nothing either. Recorded in Phase 15 as a constraint. **Do not re-raise.**

---

### Phase 5 — `hana_rubric`: conditions, the `KeymapContext` contract, `ActiveCondition` · status: done (`d75ffe2e`)

#### Work Order

**Goal:** an application declares its context enum by deriving strum traits, and the library registers its variants, descriptions, and active value with no glue code in the app.

**Spec:**

`src/condition.rs` — a single file, not a directory: hana owns `InteractionContext`, so what remains here is one small cluster. It holds `ConditionName`, `ConditionRegistry`, the `KeymapContext` trait and its blanket impl, the `ActiveCondition` resource, and the generic sync system.

**The crate cannot name `InteractionContext`** — that enum is hana's, does not exist yet, and hana already depends on this crate; reading it here would be a crate cycle. The crate declares the contract; the application satisfies it by deriving (R7):

```rust
#[derive(Resource, Copy, Clone, Eq, PartialEq, EnumIter, AsRefStr, EnumMessage)]
#[strum(serialize_all = "snake_case")]
enum GameContext {
    #[strum(message = "While flying the ship")]
    Flying,
    #[strum(message = "While the pause menu is open")]
    Paused,
}

app.add_plugins(KeymapPlugin::new().for_context::<GameContext>());
```

`KeymapContext` is a **blanket impl** over `Resource + Copy + Eq + IntoEnumIterator + AsRefStr + EnumMessage + 'static`, so no downstream app writes an impl by hand. One bound instead of six at the call site, a comprehensible compiler error naming the missing derive, and a documented extension point. It takes the same extension-point exception to `dont-create-traits-for-single-implementations` that `KeymapCommand` takes — one implementation *per application*, not one in total.

| library needs | source |
| --- | --- |
| every variant at startup — registry construction, rejecting unknown condition names, the schema's enum of valid strings | `EnumIter` |
| each variant's name as a string | `AsRefStr` + `serialize_all = "snake_case"` |
| each variant's description — schema hover text, generated reference file | `EnumMessage` via `#[strum(message = "…")]` |
| which variant is active right now | `as_ref()` on the current value |

**Two registration entry points over one generic sync system (R8):**

```rust
KeymapPlugin::new().for_state_context::<C>()  // reads Res<State<C>>, C: States + KeymapContext
KeymapPlugin::new().for_context::<C>()        // reads Res<C>,        C: Resource + KeymapContext
```

Both register `C`'s variants and descriptions into `ConditionRegistry` at startup and add the generic change-detection system that writes `ActiveCondition`. Supporting both costs one wrapper function, since the sync system is already generic over `C`. Hana takes the state path; the resource path serves an app that computes its context ad hoc.

- `ConditionRegistry` — variants and descriptions from strum, **duplicate condition ids rejected at load**. A missing `#[strum(message)]` on a variant is caught by startup validation, not by the compiler — same treatment as a missing command title.
- `ActiveCondition` — a plain resource holding **only a registry-resolved condition handle**, updated by change detection. The input path reads this one resource; it never scans contexts, compares strings, or reflects.
- **Mutual exclusivity is structural** — the enum has exactly one active variant by construction. Do not add a runtime check for it; do enforce that duplicate *names* cannot register.
- **The design never needs `in_state::<T>`** — that gates *systems*, while the keymap needs data-driven selection inside one system.
- **The context enum needs no Bevy reflection.** `EnumInfo::variant_names()` is not used and `reflect_documentation` is not needed. Commands still use reflection for dispatch; conditions use none.
- Export a public `KeymapSystems` ordering handle (with at least a `Route` variant) so an app can guarantee its own context derivation runs before the keymap reads it.

**Files:**
- `crates/hana_rubric/src/condition.rs` — populate the existing stub (Phase 3 shipped it as a one-line doc stub and `lib.rs` already declares `mod condition;`)
- `crates/hana_rubric/src/lib.rs`, `src/prelude.rs` — re-export `ConditionName`, the registration entry points, `ActiveCondition` and its update API, `KeymapSystems`, `KeymapContext`
- `crates/hana_rubric/Cargo.toml` — **no edit needed.** Phase 3 shipped root `Cargo.toml` with `strum = { version = "0.28", features = ["derive"] }`, and `crates/hana_rubric/Cargo.toml` with `bevy = { workspace = true, features = ["bevy_log", "bevy_state", "keyboard", "std"] }` (`bevy_state` is a `bevy` facade feature, not a separate dependency). Both this phase needs are already in place.

**Constraints from prior phases:**
- Phase 3's `Diagnostic` stores plain data only — a missing variant message or duplicate condition name becomes a diagnostic carrying the name as a `String`, never a `ConditionName`.
- Phase 4b established that startup validation is the enforcement point for authored text (missing title/description). Condition descriptions follow the same rule.
- `ConditionRegistry` is `pub(crate)`; only `ConditionName`, the registration entry points, and `ActiveCondition` are public.
- **`ConditionRegistry`'s enumeration must be stable run-to-run.** Phase 4b's `CommandRegistry::iter()` had to sort explicitly because a `HashMap` backing gave arbitrary order. Iterate in `EnumIter` declaration order or sort by name, and expose that order through the accessor Phases 8 and 9 consume — Phase 9 skips a companion write only when the generated bytes match the file on disk, so an unstable order rewrites two `0444` files on every launch and makes Phase 8's schema tests flaky.

- **`Diagnostic::message` is required.** Phase 4a added `pub message: String` as a non-`Option` field on a struct with no `Default`, so every construction site in this phase must supply it. Use `String::new()` where no sentence applies.
**Resolved decisions** (taken during implementation; both followed the recommendation):

- **`KeymapContext` carries no `Resource` bound.** The trait is
  `AsRef<str> + Copy + EnumMessage + Eq + IntoEnumIterator + Send + Sync + 'static`, blanket-impl'd.
  `for_context::<C>()` adds `C: Resource` to its own bound; `for_state_context::<C>()` adds
  `C: States`. A `States` enum is not a `Resource` (`State<S>` is the resource), so putting
  `Resource` on the trait would have made Phase 14's `InteractionContext` unable to satisfy it.
  Test `derived_state_context_satisfies_keymap_context_without_a_resource_impl` locks this in.
- **`DiagnosticKind::Context` carries both condition failures.** No new variant. A missing
  `#[strum(message)]` and a duplicate condition name both produce `DiagnosticKind::Context` with the
  offending name in `Diagnostic::context` and the specifics in `Diagnostic::message`. Decision D's
  two-pass variant plan (Phase 4a, then Phase 7) is unaffected.


**Acceptance gate:** `verify.sh check|test|lint hana_rubric` plus `verify.sh check fairy_dust` green — this phase edits `hana_rubric`'s crate root and prelude, and since Phase 4c `fairy_dust` is the normal-dependency consumer where a broken re-export surfaces. Tests prove: a test enum deriving the four strum traits satisfies `KeymapContext` with no hand-written impl; variants register with snake_case names; `#[strum(message)]` text reaches the registry; a variant missing its message produces a startup diagnostic naming it; two enums registering the same condition name are rejected; `for_context::<C>()` updates `ActiveCondition` when `Res<C>` changes and `for_state_context::<C>()` when `Res<State<C>>` changes; `ActiveCondition` holds a handle, not a string.


### Retrospective

**What worked:** the blanket `KeymapContext` impl needed no hand-written impl at any call site — four strum derives satisfy it, and a `States`-only test enum compiles against it. `ConditionRegistry` backed by `Vec<ConditionEntry>` gave stable `EnumIter` declaration order for free, with no sort step (contrast Phase 4b's `CommandRegistry`, which had to sort around a `HashMap`).

**What deviated from the plan:**
- The Files list gave `lib.rs` a re-export-only role, but the first implementation put ~138 lines of plugin wiring there. Corrected: `KeymapPlugin` and `KeymapSystems` now live in a **new `src/keymap_plugin.rs`** — the file Phase 12 already names — and the two context plugins live in `condition.rs` with the systems and resources they register. `lib.rs`'s Phase 5 diff is 6 lines, all `mod` and `pub use`.
- Both `**Pending decision:**` blocks were resolved as recommended during implementation rather than before dispatch — the pre-dispatch check did not stop for them. See **Resolved decisions** above.

**Surprises:**
- `ConditionEntry::description` has **no non-test reader** until Phases 8 and 9 consume it. A dead `!description.is_empty()` clause in `resolve` was accidentally acting as that reader; deleting it surfaced `dead_code`. Resolved with `#[cfg_attr(not(test), expect(dead_code, reason = "…"))]` on `ConditionInfo::description`. The `not(test)` scoping is load-bearing — tests *do* read the field, so an unconditional `expect` is unfulfilled and fails `--tests -D warnings`. `expect` rather than `allow` means the attribute self-warns and must be deleted when Phase 8 adds a real reader.
- `implement.sh`'s sub-task argument accepts only `implementation | review | mechanical | escalation`. Passing `fix` exits 1 in seconds with an empty output file.

**Implications for remaining phases:**
- **`ActiveCondition` now exists unconditionally**, even when registration fails, because its reader takes `Res<ActiveCondition>` and a missing resource panics. On failure the registry is empty, no sync system is added, and `is_initialized()` stays `false`. **Phase 11** — `route_input`, the sole reader — must treat `is_initialized() == false` as "no keymap active", not as a bug. (Phase 10 is the disk worker and never touches this resource.)
- **`ActiveCondition::handle` has no accessor and `ConditionName` has no constructor** outside `condition.rs`. Both were deliberately omitted: an unused `pub(crate)` item trips `dead_code` under `-D warnings`. **Phase 6 must add the `ConditionName` constructor** it needs to build names from authored text; **Phase 11 must add the `pub(crate)` handle accessor**, since it is the only phase that reads the *active* value. Phase 7 keys its compiled map off `ConditionRegistry::resolve` and must not add the accessor — doing so would fail its own gate on `dead_code`.
- `ConditionHandle(usize)` is the index into `ConditionRegistry::entries`, `pub(crate)`, `Copy + Eq + Hash`. Phase 7's compiled map keys by this type directly.
- `KeymapSystems` ships with `UpdateActiveCondition` and `Route`, chained in that order — but **in `Update`, which is the wrong schedule**. Phase 11 puts `route_input` in `PreUpdate`, so Phase 11 must move the `configure_sets` call and both sync systems to `PreUpdate` or routing reads a one-frame-stale condition.
- **Both sync systems take `Res<…>` unconditionally and can panic.** `sync_state_condition`'s `Res<State<C>>` is absent whenever a computed state's `compute` returns `None` — reachable in Phase 14 — and `sync_resource_condition`'s `Res<C>` is absent if an app never inserts `C`. Phase 11 relaxes both to `Option<Res<…>>`.
- **Startup registration diagnostics are written as `DiagnosticKind::Context` at plugin-build time and are never regenerated.** Phase 9's partitioned-clearing decision classifies `Context` as a reload-transaction kind, so partition-by-kind alone would delete them on the first clean load. Phase 9's decision block now carries the third producer and the retained-marker sub-option; Phase 12 implements whichever way it resolves.
- **Phase 12 must restructure the builder before adding to it.** `for_context`/`for_state_context` take `self` by value and return a plugin carrying nothing from the receiver, and `KeymapPlugin` has no fields — so `.with_app_name(…).for_state_context::<C>()` as Phase 17 writes it would drop every configured value.
- **Phase 12 extends `src/keymap_plugin.rs` rather than creating it.** `KeymapPlugin` is a unit struct with `new()`, `for_context::<C>()`, and `for_state_context::<C>()` returning `impl Plugin`; it does not itself implement `Plugin`. Phase 12 adds `.with_app_name`, `.with_defaults`, and `.with_protected_keystroke` to that same type.
- The prelude now exports 24 items: the 19 from Phase 4c plus `ActiveCondition`, `ConditionName`, `KeymapContext`, `KeymapPlugin`, `KeymapSystems`.

### Phase 5 Review

Fifteen findings; all applied. No user decision was presented — two were deferred into existing `**Pending decision:**` blocks per auto mode.

**Corrections to this retrospective's own first draft** (it named the wrong phases, and following it would have cost a fix pass):
- The `ActiveCondition::handle` accessor moved from **Phase 7 to Phase 11**. Phase 7 keys its compiled map off `ConditionRegistry::resolve` and never reads the active value, so adding the accessor there is an unused `pub(crate)` item — the exact `dead_code` trap this phase already paid for once.
- The `is_initialized() == false` rule moved from **Phase 10 to Phase 11**. Phase 10 is the disk worker and touches no condition state; `route_input` is the sole reader.

**New constraints written into remaining Work Orders:**
- **Phase 6** — add the `ConditionName` constructor, and `condition.rs` to **Files**; the field is private with no `new`/`From`/`FromStr`, so `document.rs` cannot build a `ContextExpr::Name` without it.
- **Phase 7** — stated explicitly that it does *not* touch `ActiveCondition`, with the `dead_code` reason.
- **Phase 8** — reading `ConditionInfo::description` retires the `expect(dead_code)` attribute, and a fulfilled `expect` is a **build error** under `-D warnings`, not a warning. `condition.rs` added to **Files** for that one deletion.
- **Phase 9** — gate gains `verify.sh check fairy_dust`; it edits `hana_rubric`'s crate root, which reaches `fairy_dust`.
- **Phase 11** — four `condition.rs` edits now belong to this phase: the handle accessor; the uninitialized-means-no-keymap rule; **moving `KeymapSystems` and both sync systems from `Update` to `PreUpdate`** (as shipped, routing in `PreUpdate` would read a one-frame-stale condition and defeat the reason the set is exported at all); and relaxing both sync systems to `Option<Res<…>>` (a computed state's `State<S>` is removed from the world when `compute` returns `None`, so the shipped signature panics). `condition.rs` added to **Files**; three matching gate assertions added.
- **Phase 12** — `keymap_plugin.rs` is **extend, not create**; the builder must be restructured so the `impl Plugin` returned by the terminal `for_*_context` call carries the `with_*` configuration, which the shipped unit struct discards; calling both context-source methods must be rejected rather than silently double-registering; the "nothing anywhere inserts `KeymapLoadFailures`" claim corrected (Phase 5's failure path does, and `init_resource` is idempotent); four gate assertions added.
- **Phase 14** — the quoted `KeymapContext` bound list replaced with the shipped one (`AsRef<str> + Copy + EnumMessage + Eq + IntoEnumIterator + Send + Sync + 'static`, no `Resource`), plus a note that `InteractionContext::compute` need not be total once Phase 11's relaxation lands.
- **Phases 13, 16, 19, 20** — the quoted prelude inventory corrected from 19 items to 24.

**Deferred into existing pending decisions** (they surface at those phases' pre-dispatch checks):
- **Phase 9's diagnostics-clearing decision** gained a third producer. Phase 5 writes context-registration failures as `DiagnosticKind::Context` at plugin-**build** time, and that kind is classified as reload-sourced — so partition-by-kind alone would delete a startup failure on the first clean load, permanently, since the registry is built once. Two sub-options added; the recommendation is a retained marker rather than a new `DiagnosticKind` variant, which keeps Phase 5's resolved Decision B intact.
- **Phase 12's Decision B** (all-or-nothing registry validation) extended to cover conditions. `ConditionRegistry::register` is atomic the same way, so one context variant missing `#[strum(message)]` yields an empty registry, every `"context"` in the shipped default fails to resolve, and Phase 12's shipped-default rule would have turned that into an app that does not start. **Resolved 2026-07-31 in Phase 12's Decision B:** the shipped-default hard-fail is suppressed when the registry is empty *because validation itself failed*, so the process starts. It routes no input at all in that state — see Phase 12's resolved global-routing decision, which kept that behavior deliberately.

**Confirmed clean, do not re-raise:** no remaining phase assumes `KeymapPlugin` lives in `lib.rs`, assumes `ConditionRegistry` is public, or assumes a condition-specific `DiagnosticKind` variant. Phase 5 made no phase redundant. Phases 11 and 12 already carried `check fairy_dust`; Phases 6, 7, 8, and 13 correctly omit it. The `Vec`-backed registry satisfies Phase 8's ordering requirement and Phase 9's skip-if-identical companion write.

### Phase 6 — `hana_rubric`: JSONC document model with retained source spans · status: done (1cfad3fa)

#### Work Order

**Goal:** a keymap file parses into typed stages that remember where every binding came from, so diagnostics can point at the offending key.

**Spec:**

`src/keymap/mod.rs` (table of contents), `src/keymap/constants.rs` (the sequence timeout — keymap policy, passed into the matcher; the matcher itself owns no timeout), and `src/keymap/document.rs`.

`document.rs` owns source decoding and location indexing: the `KeymapDocument` anchor, its private serde wire structs, JSONC parsing with retained source spans, `BindingEdit`, and `ContextExpr`. Parser tests live beside the types they construct. **Do not split into `wire.rs` + `parse.rs`** — that is the prohibited definitions-versus-construction split; every parser change would cross the boundary, widen visibility, and separate tests from the types they build.

**File format — root object envelope (R4, supersedes the bare root array):**

```jsonc
{
  "$schema": "./keymap.schema.json",
  "bindings": [
    { "bindings": { "space": "transport::toggle_playback" } },
    { "context": "dimension_lock",
      "bindings": { "space": "dimension_lock::pause_tracking", "b": "dimension_lock::toggle_coupling" } }
  ]
}
```

- The document root is an **object**; the ordered blocks array nests under `"bindings"`. The envelope is required because an editor learns which schema applies from a `$schema` member, and an array has no members.
- Each block has an optional `"context"` and its `"bindings"` map. **Order matters** — later blocks override earlier ones for the same key. JSON objects guarantee neither key order nor duplicate-key rejection, so an object keyed by condition cannot express "these bindings, then those on top," and overriding is the entire point.
- Parse with `serde_json_lenient` — comments and trailing commas. The shipped defaults need explanatory comments, and rejecting a trailing comma in a user file is a bad first experience.
- A binding value is a command id **string**, or `null` (the tombstone, R1/D5b). The `[id, args]` array form is *specified* in the type but **rejected in v1** with a diagnostic saying arguments are not yet supported — this keeps A3 additive.

**Typed stages, private where possible:**
- `ContextExpr` — an enum holding **only** `Name(ConditionName)` in v1, so appendix A2 (boolean `&&`/`||`/`!` composition) is additive. A bare name is a valid expression in any boolean grammar, so `"context": "rotating"` parses identically under a future grammar. Document this in the type's doc comment so nobody flattens it to a `ConditionName`.
- `BindingEdit::{ Bind(CommandId), Unbind }` — `Unbind` is the tombstone.
- `KeystrokeSequence` (crate-local since Phase 4c — `crates/hana_rubric/src/keystroke/sequence.rs`), guaranteeing at least one stroke.

**Source spans are deliberate work, not free.** `serde_json_lenient` reports line and column for *deserialization* errors, but unknown commands, contexts, and keystrokes are detected **after** deserialization, and its public byte offset covers completed stream values rather than per-binding spans. Index source locations during parsing: for each binding record its byte range, 1-based line and column, block index, context, and original keystroke text, keyed so a later semantic failure can retrieve them.

**Diagnose within the document, before any semantic resolution:** duplicate condition names across blocks, and duplicate keys within one block.

**Visibility:** `pub(super)` for `BindingEdit`, `ContextExpr`, and the resolved/merged stages shared among `keymap/` siblings. Note that a `pub(super)` item cannot be re-exported as `pub(crate)` — if the plugin root needs one, the source item must already be `pub(crate)`.

**Files:**
- `crates/hana_rubric/src/keymap/mod.rs` — populate the existing stub (Phase 3 shipped it as a one-line doc stub and `lib.rs` already declares `mod keymap;`)
- `crates/hana_rubric/src/keymap/constants.rs` — create (sequence timeout)
- `crates/hana_rubric/src/keymap/document.rs` — create
- `crates/hana_rubric/src/condition.rs` — add the `ConditionName` constructor this phase needs (see the constraint below)

**Constraints from prior phases:**
- **This phase adds no `DiagnosticKind` variants.** Decision D's schedule is three passes, not two: Phase 4a shipped `Diagnostic::message` plus `DuplicateCommandId` / `MissingCommandTitle` / `MissingCommandDescription`, **Phase 4b added `InvalidCommandId` and `CommandEventNotReflected`** (11 variants shipped), and **Phase 7 adds the last three** — `HeldCommandInSequence`, `UnremappableCommand`, `ReservedKeystroke` — because all three require registry resolution, which does not exist until Phase 7. This phase reuses the existing variants. Every diagnostic this phase produces sets `message` — the hold-to-act restriction, the reserved-for-recovery explanation, the closest matching command ids, and the registered condition names all go there, not in `suggestions`.
- A binding string parses as a **sequence**, so the error type is `KeystrokeSequenceParseError` — `Empty` (the string held no keystrokes) or `Keystroke` (one chord failed). Match on that enum, not on `KeystrokeParseError` directly. Its `.token()` and `.offset()` are already relative to the whole sequence string, so building a `Diagnostic` means adding the binding's span start to `.offset()` — nothing further.
- **`KeystrokeParseError::at_offset` is `pub(super)` — but since Phase 4c that is no longer a compiler-enforced boundary.** Its parent module is now the `hana_rubric` crate root (`crates/hana_rubric/src/keystroke/mod.rs`), so `pub(super)` reads as crate-wide and `keystroke/sequence.rs` already calls it. Treat "this crate reads parse failures; it never constructs them" as a convention this phase must keep by hand: do not call `at_offset` outside `keystroke/`.
- **`Display` for `Keystroke` emits a fixed canonical order** — `platform-`, `ctrl-`, `alt-`, `shift-` — and canonicalization has already folded a platform modifier into control off macOS. So an authored `cmd-p` renders as `ctrl-p` on Linux and Windows — and on macOS it renders as **`platform-p`**, never `cmd-p`: the writer emits the literal string `"platform-"` (`crates/bevy_kana/src/input/keystroke/mod.rs:173`), and `cmd`/`super`/`win` are parse-side aliases only. **Neither rendering matches what the user typed**, which is why diagnostics quote the preserved original text. Never echo a re-rendered keystroke back to the user as "what they wrote": diagnostics quote the **original keystroke text** field Phase 3 reserved for exactly this. Any test that asserts on rendered keystroke text must be split by platform with `cfg!(target_os = "macos")`, the way Phase 1 split its own round-trip tests; CI runs Ubuntu only, so the macOS half never runs there.
- **Phase 3's `Diagnostic` has ten fields and every one of them is required** — none is `Option`. Later phases cite this list rather than re-deriving it:

  ```rust
  pub struct Diagnostic {
      pub source_path:        String,
      pub byte_range:         Range<usize>,   // indexes the file's contents
      pub line:               usize,          // 1-based
      pub column:             usize,          // 1-based
      pub block_index:        usize,
      pub context:            String,
      pub original_keystroke: String,
      pub command_id:         String,
      pub kind:               DiagnosticKind,
      pub suggestions:        Vec<String>,
  }
  ```

  **Phase 4a adds an eleventh field, `pub message: String`** — the human-readable sentence, required like the rest, empty string when absent — **plus three `DiagnosticKind` variants** (`DuplicateCommandId`, `MissingCommandTitle`, `MissingCommandDescription`). This phase writes `message` on every diagnostic it produces.

  The convention for an absent value is the **empty string**, not `None`: a syntax error with no keystroke sets `original_keystroke: String::new()`. As shipped, the field order is `…, kind, message, suggestions`. This phase populates all eleven except `suggestions`.
- **`CommandIdParseError` is a payload-free unit struct**, unlike `KeystrokeParseError`, which carries `token()`/`offset()`. When a command id is malformed the error itself contributes no position, so the only positional data available for the diagnostic is the binding's own span in the source document.
- Phase 3's `CommandId` runtime parser validates the id string form here; *resolution* against the registry is Phase 7.
- Phase 5's `ConditionName` is the payload of `ContextExpr::Name` — **and this phase must add its constructor.** As shipped, `ConditionName`'s single `String` field is private to `condition.rs` (`crates/hana_rubric/src/condition.rs:33`) with no `new`, no `From<String>`, and no `FromStr`, so nothing outside that file can build one. Phase 5 deliberately omitted the constructor: an unused `pub(crate)` item trips `dead_code` under this workspace's `-D warnings`, so the constructor must land in the same phase as its first caller. Add `pub(crate) fn new(name: impl Into<String>) -> Self` (or the `From` impl, matching however `CommandId` does it) to `condition.rs` as part of this phase's diff, and call it from `document.rs` when parsing `"context"`.

**Acceptance gate:** `verify.sh check|test|lint hana_rubric` green. Tests prove: the envelope parses and a bare root array is rejected with a clear message; comments and trailing commas parse; block order is preserved; `null` yields `BindingEdit::Unbind`; the `[id, args]` form is rejected with an arguments-not-supported diagnostic; duplicate condition names and duplicate keys within a block are diagnosed. **Test several invalid bindings across repeated context blocks and assert each diagnostic points at its own key** — same line, different column, correct block index.

### Retrospective

**What worked:**
- The two-parser design landed as specified: `serde_json_lenient` decodes into private wire structs while a hand-written `JsoncScanner` walks the same text recording per-key byte ranges and 1-based line/column. A custom `Deserialize` for `WireBindings` (repeated `next_entry()` into a `Vec<(String, Value)>`) preserves both duplicate keys and source order, which is what keeps the two passes in step.
- The single-file constraint held. `document.rs` was never split; tests sit beside the types they construct.

**What deviated from the plan:**
- **`KeymapDocument::parse`'s signature is `Result<(Self, Vec<Diagnostic>), Vec<Diagnostic>>`, not `Result<Self, Vec<Diagnostic>>`.** The first implementation returned `Err` whenever any binding failed, discarding every valid block — which forecloses Phase 7's rule that an invalid edit is skipped and leaves the prior valid entry in place, and Phase 12's D7 two-tier failure model. The shipped signature encodes the two tiers directly: `Err` only for whole-file failures (serde decode failure, source-index failure, or a block/binding count mismatch between the two passes); `Ok` carries every valid binding plus every per-binding diagnostic.
- Two fix passes were needed. The second corrected a regression the first introduced: duplicate binding keys within one block were made first-wins by an added `continue`, contradicting the last-wins merge rule at v1.md:1103. Both duplicates now survive in source order; the document stage reports the duplicate and leaves resolution to Phase 7.

**Surprises:**
- **The blind reviewer approved the duplicate-key regression.** Round 1 caught the discarded-valid-edits blocker; round 2 returned APPROVE with no findings on a diff that had reversed binding precedence. Orchestrator review caught it. Delegate review is not a substitute for reading the diff against the plan.
- **`serde_json_lenient` reports a syntax error's column in bytes, not characters.** The first implementation resolved it with `char_indices().nth(column - 1)`, so any multibyte UTF-8 earlier on the line pushed the reported position late or to EOF. `byte_offset` now walks bytes and clamps to a char boundary; `line_and_column`, which builds this crate's own diagnostics, remains character-based.
- The two passes are paired with `zip`, which truncates silently. Explicit length checks now sit in front of both pairings (`parse_blocks`, `parse_bindings`) and raise a whole-file diagnostic on mismatch rather than attaching every subsequent error to the wrong key.
- **`document.rs` shipped at ~1411 lines (~1050 non-test)**, tripping this workspace's own split rule (>500 non-test lines plus independently testable sections). The Work Order forbids the `wire.rs`/`parse.rs` split specifically; the ~300-line `JsoncScanner` is a different cut. Phase 7 adds to this module.

**Implications for remaining phases:**
- **Phase 7 must consume the two-tuple return.** `KeymapDocument::parse` hands back a document *and* a diagnostic list on the success path; per-binding failures are already skipped from `blocks`, and duplicates are retained in order so last-wins is applied at merge time, not at parse time.
- **A block whose `context` is explicitly `null` is diagnosed but its bindings still parse with `context: None`** — that is, they currently land as global bindings. Whether Phase 7's merge should admit or drop a block whose context failed to resolve is an open layering question.
- `ConditionName::new(impl Into<String>)` now exists and is `pub(crate)` (`crates/hana_rubric/src/condition.rs`), called from `document.rs` when parsing `"context"`.
- `SEQUENCE_TIMEOUT` (`crates/hana_rubric/src/keymap/constants.rs`) is `pub(super)` and carries `#[expect(dead_code, reason = …)]`. Phase 11 consumes it; the `expect` must be removed in the same change that adds the first caller, or `unfulfilled_lint_expectation` fails the build under `-D warnings`.
- `KeymapDocument` is re-exported from `keymap/mod.rs` as `pub(crate)` under `#[expect(unused_imports, reason = …)]`. Same self-removal rule applies at its first use.

### Phase 6 Review

Applied directly to the remaining Work Orders:

- **Phase 7** gained three constraints: `KeymapDocument::parse` returns `(document, diagnostics)` on success and the caller must carry those diagnostics forward or they are silently dropped; duplicate keys within one block are retained in source order, which is what makes rule 3's last-wins correct; and `keymap/mod.rs`'s `#[expect(unused_imports)]` on the `KeymapDocument` re-export must be deleted in the same change that first uses it.
- **Phase 8** — corrected the stale `ConditionInfo::description` reference from `condition.rs:88-95` to `condition.rs:93-99`; Phase 6's `ConditionName::new` shifted it.
- **Phase 11** — added `crates/hana_rubric/src/keymap/constants.rs` to **Files**: it consumes `SEQUENCE_TIMEOUT` and so must delete that constant's `#[expect(dead_code)]` in the same change, or `unfulfilled_lint_expectation` fails its own gate under `-D warnings`.
- **Phase 12** gained four constraints: the `Err`/`Ok` arms of `KeymapDocument::parse` **are** D7's two tiers and the tier must never be inferred from `DiagnosticKind`, since both tiers emit `Syntax`; the byte-snapshot → `&str` decode belongs to this phase and a UTF-8 failure is whole-file; Phase 6 emits advisory diagnostics on *successful* loads, which the `KeymapLoadFailures` doc contract does not currently admit and its clearing rule would erase; and `KeymapDocument::schema` is currently parsed but read by nobody.

Deferred to Phase 7 as pending decisions, then resolved in a follow-up review before Phase 7 was briefed:

- Merge rule 7's cross-source duplicate check would have fired on every legitimate override — **deleted**, and the rule now states why no such check exists.
- A block whose context is present but unresolvable became a global block, contradicting merge rule 3 — **it now contributes no edits at all**; only an absent `"context"` means global.
- A misspelled block member (`"contxt"`) was accepted in silence — **caught in both layers**: Phase 8's schema gains `"additionalProperties": false` so a schema-aware editor underlines it before the file is saved, and Phase 7 emits a load-time **advisory** diagnostic for everyone else. This introduced an advisory/failure severity on `Diagnostic`; advisories are retained across a successful load so a later keymap UI can surface a user's mistakes, which also settles how Phase 12 clears the diagnostic resource.
- `document.rs` at 1057 non-test lines exceeds the split rule — **deferred deliberately** to the new "Deferred cleanup" section at the end of this plan. Phase 7 must not split it on its own initiative.

Three further Phase 7 items were contradictions rather than choices and were corrected directly: the compiled keymap described as immutable while holding stateful matchers (restated as *replaced wholesale, never edited in place*; routing takes `ResMut`); the protected-chord assertions rejecting `ctrl-p` on macOS where it is an ordinary bindable chord (platform-split, matching Phase 20); and no route carrying the recovery chord from the application into the library (`.with_protected_keystroke()` on Phase 12's plugin builder). Ten stale `crates/bevy_kana/src/input/keystroke/` paths invalidated by Phase 4c were corrected across phases 7–22.

Phases 9, 10, 13–21 were reviewed and need no changes.
