### Phase 7 — `hana_rubric`: layering, tombstone resolution, compiled per-context matchers · status: done (a95983bb)

#### Work Order

**Goal:** defaults plus user edits resolve to one compiled keymap holding a matcher per context, with tombstones applied before compilation. **`CompiledKeymap` is replaced wholesale, never edited in place** — that is the reload invariant Phase 12 depends on, and it is what earlier drafts of this phase meant by "immutable". It is not read-only: it holds one `SequenceMatcher<T>` per context, each owning its own pending state, and all three matcher methods (`match_keystroke`, `resolve_timeout`, `cancel_pending`) take `&mut self` (`crates/hana_rubric/src/keystroke/sequence_matcher.rs`), so Phase 11's routing system takes `ResMut<CompiledKeymap>`, not `Res`. Phase 11 owns the *routing* of pending state, not its storage: on a context change it calls `cancel_pending()` on the **previously active** context, never on the newly active one.

**Spec:**

`src/keymap/merged.rs` — `MergedKeymap` and `ResolvedEdits`. `src/keymap/compiled.rs` — `CompiledKeymap` and `Generation`. Both are pure semantic resolution and matcher construction: **no `World` mutation, no I/O**. `merge.rs` would be operation-named where `MergedKeymap` and `ResolvedEdits` already supply the anchor.

**The merge algorithm, exactly (all four review lenses converged on this):**

1. Process embedded defaults first, then the user file; within each source, blocks in file order.
2. Canonical merge identity is `(condition-or-global, complete canonical keystroke sequence)`.
3. At each identity the last **valid** edit wins, as either `Bind(handle)` or `Tombstone`. **An invalid edit is skipped and leaves the prior valid entry in place — a bad user line can never erase a working default.**
4. For active context `C` and sequence `S`: use `(C, S)` when present, **including when it is a tombstone**. Only genuine absence falls through to `(global, S)`. A tombstone dispatches nothing and suppresses the global fallback.
5. Tombstones apply to complete sequences, never to prefix subtrees. A tombstone on `g` does not remove `g g`; a tombstone on `g g` lets `g` fire immediately when no other longer sequence survives.
6. **Compile one resolved matcher per context after applying tombstones and global fallback**, so only live sequences contribute prefixes. Cost is `O(contexts × bindings)` of setup memory and reload-time work; it removes context scans, string comparison, reflection, and allocation from the input path.
7. **No cross-source duplicate check.** Repeating a context across sources is the override mechanism, not an error — a user file that customizes one binding inside `dimension_lock` necessarily restates that context, and diagnosing it would fire on every legitimate customization. "Duplicate keys within one block" likewise has no cross-source meaning, since a block exists in exactly one source. Phase 6's per-document duplicate-context and duplicate-key diagnostics are the whole of it: carry them through into this phase's output, add none.

**Three distinct states at an identity, and the distinction is load-bearing:** absent means inherit the global, a command means replace it, `null` means the key is inert. `"space": null` inside the `dimension_lock` block wins at that identity and dispatches nothing while a lock is engaged; it does **not** expose the global `Space`. To get global behavior inside a context, bind that command there explicitly.

**One precedence rule, no specificity ranking:** a binding with a condition beats a binding without one for the same keystroke. Mutually-exclusive computed states mean at most one block is live, so there is nothing to rank — which is why full boolean expressions are deferred (A2).

**Prefix collisions are not a merge concern.** Defaults binding `g g` while the user binds `g` are not the same keystroke, so layering sees no conflict; the matcher resolves it.

**Semantic resolution happens here, producing diagnostics with suggestions:**
- Unknown command id → diagnostic naming **the closest matches**. **There is no fuzzy matcher in this crate** — the earlier "already in the crate" claim referred to hana's command palette. Phase 4b supplies the candidate list (`CommandRegistry::iter()`, sorted by id); this phase supplies the ranking. Hand-roll a bounded Levenshtein over the id strings at *diagnostic* time only, never on the input path, capped at the three nearest within a small edit distance. **Add no dependency** — this phase's **Files** has no `Cargo.toml` bullet. This covers typos, which are likelier than renames while the only keymap files are ours. (No alias machinery in v1 — A1.)
- Unknown condition name → diagnostic listing the registered condition names.
- **A block whose `"context"` is present but does not resolve contributes no edits at all** — not to its intended context, and not to global. Only an *absent* `"context"` key means global. This covers both the unknown-condition-name case above and Phase 6's explicitly-`null` context, which parses to `context: None` (`crates/hana_rubric/src/keymap/document.rs:180-190`) and is otherwise indistinguishable from an absent key. Without this rule those bindings merge at `(global, S)` and override working global defaults, contradicting rule 3. Test it: a user block with an unresolvable context leaves every global default intact.
- **An unrecognized block member is an advisory diagnostic, never a failure.** `"contxt": "dimension_lock"` currently yields a global block in silence: `WireBlock` has no `deny_unknown_fields`, `context` is `#[serde(default)]`, and `JsoncScanner::parse_block` skips unknown members by design (pinned by Phase 6's `source_index_finds_bindings_after_unknown_block_members`). Keep that tolerance — `deny_unknown_fields` would reject the whole block and cost its valid bindings, the wrong tier — and instead have the scanner record unrecognized member names with their spans so this phase emits one **advisory** diagnostic per occurrence, naming the member and the closest recognized name. Phase 8's schema catches this in the editor before the file is ever saved; this path covers everyone editing without a schema-aware editor.
- **Advisory diagnostics are a new severity, and this phase introduces it.** Phase 3's `Diagnostic` (`crates/hana_rubric/src/diagnostic.rs`) has no severity field, so today every diagnostic reads as a load failure. Add one — advisory versus failure — and classify accordingly: an unrecognized member, a duplicate context name, and a duplicate binding key are **advisory** (the load succeeded and the bindings work); an unknown command id, an unparseable keystroke, and a reserved-keystroke rejection are **failures** (that binding does not exist). Advisories are logged at warn level and **retained in `KeymapLoadFailures` across a successful load** rather than cleared by it, so a later keymap-editing UI can list the mistakes a user made and offer corrections. Phase 12 owns the clearing rule and consumes this distinction.

- A `Held`-capability command bound to a multi-key sequence → per-binding rejection naming the hold-to-act restriction (R3).
- An `Unremappable`-capability command named anywhere in a file → per-binding rejection saying it is reserved for recovery (G3).
- **Protected recovery chord:** reject any sequence whose **first canonical stroke** is the protected chord — `cmd-p x` can never complete because recovery consumes its first stroke. **Compare parsed, platform-normalized keystrokes, never raw strings**, so `super-p`, `win-p`, and left/right modifier spellings cannot slip through. **`ctrl-p` is platform-dependent** — it canonicalizes to the protected value off macOS and to a different value on macOS, where it stays an ordinary bindable chord; assert it split on `cfg!(target_os = "macos")` per the protected-chord section below. Report the stroke index and the reserved command. Define **one canonical parsed keystroke constant** shared by the always-on handler, protected-entry validation, the generated default comment, and the tests (M1). **Keep the protected list to exactly one entry** — the bar for a second is that the app is unrecoverable without it.
- Command and condition names resolve to **stable handles** here. No name survives into the compiled form.

`CompiledKeymap` carries a `Generation` and is **replaced wholesale on reload, never edited in place** (see the Goal — it is not read-only). It holds one `SequenceMatcher<T>` per context, where `T` is an opaque index into a table of resolved command handles.

**Files:**
- `crates/hana_rubric/src/keymap/merged.rs` — create
- `crates/hana_rubric/src/keymap/compiled.rs` — create
- `crates/hana_rubric/src/diagnostic.rs` — add the advisory/failure severity distinction to `Diagnostic` (see the Spec); `KeymapLoadFailures`'s doc contract changes from "unsuccessful load" to "problems reported by the most recent load"
- `crates/hana_rubric/src/keymap/document.rs` — have the scanner record unrecognized block-member names and spans so this phase can diagnose them
- `crates/hana_rubric/src/keymap/mod.rs` — declare and re-export

**Constraints from prior phases:**
- Phase 2's `SequenceMatcher<T>` is constructed from `(KeystrokeSequence, T)` pairs only. **The timeout is not a construction parameter** — it is passed on every `match_keystroke`/`resolve_timeout` call, so `keymap/constants.rs`'s value is consumed by Phase 11's runtime, not here.
- **`SequenceMatcher<T: Copy>`** — the bound is on the struct itself (`crates/hana_rubric/src/keystroke/sequence_matcher.rs:14`), so even naming the type requires `Copy`. The opaque command-handle index this phase defines must derive `Clone, Copy, Debug, Eq, PartialEq`; `MatchOutcome<T>` derives `Eq`/`PartialEq` and tests assert on it with `assert_eq!`.
- **Phase 4b's `CommandRegistry` exposes no index-shaped handle**, so this phase defines it. Every registry lookup — `title`, `description`, `capability`, `custom_input`, `invoke` — is keyed by `&CommandId`, a `String` newtype, and `CommandEntry` / `Invocation` are `pub(crate)` with private fields. Build a `Vec` of resolved command entries on `CompiledKeymap` in `CommandRegistry::iter()` order (sorted by id, so the table is reproducible) and let `SequenceMatcher`'s `T` be its index. Phase 11 then dispatches through that table instead of hashing a heap string per keystroke.
- **`SequenceMatcher::new` silently last-wins on a duplicate sequence** (`sequence_matcher.rs:103-111`) and reports no collision. Resolve every `(context, sequence)` identity to a single edit **before** constructing the matcher — a merge bug would otherwise be invisible.
- `KeystrokeSequence::first(&self) -> Keystroke` is **infallible and returns by value** (`crates/hana_rubric/src/keystroke/sequence.rs:41`); `len()` is `const fn`; `iter()` yields `&Keystroke`, so `.copied()` is needed to feed `Keystroke` values, and `IntoIterator for &KeystrokeSequence` exists.
- **Only render a `Keystroke` that came from the parser or from `CANONICAL_KEY_MAPPINGS`.** `Display` returns `fmt::Error` for any other `KeyCode` (`crates/hana_rubric/src/keystroke/mod.rs:185`), and `to_string()`/`format!` panic on a `Display` that errors. Phase 20's `KeyCode::KeyP` is in the table.
- Phase 4b's registry resolves id → cached `ReflectEvent` handle and carries the capability (`OneShot` / `Held` / `Unremappable`) on each entry. Capability checks happen here, at merge time, not at dispatch.
- Phase 5's `ConditionRegistry` supplies the valid condition names and their handles; `ActiveCondition` holds a handle, so the compiled per-context map must be keyed by the same handle type. Concretely: `ConditionRegistry` and `ConditionHandle` are `pub(crate)` in `crates/hana_rubric/src/condition.rs`, this file is a crate sibling and can see both, and `ConditionRegistry::resolve(&self, &str) -> Option<ConditionHandle>` (`condition.rs:163-167`) is how a parsed `ContextExpr::Name` becomes the map key. **This phase does not touch `ActiveCondition`** — it never reads the active value, only the registry — so do not add an accessor for `ActiveCondition::handle` here. Phase 11 is the reader and adds it. An unused `pub(crate)` accessor trips `dead_code` under `-D warnings` and fails this phase's own gate.
- Phase 6's document stages (`BindingEdit`, `ContextExpr::Name`, source spans) are `pub(super)` — this file is a `keymap/` sibling and can see them.
- **`KeymapDocument::parse` returns a two-tuple on success:** `parse(source_path: &str, source: &str) -> Result<(Self, Vec<Diagnostic>), Vec<Diagnostic>>` (`crates/hana_rubric/src/keymap/document.rs:34`). The `Ok` arm carries the document **and** every per-binding diagnostic the parse produced. Those diagnostics must be carried into this phase's own output list — dropping the tuple's second element silently discards every syntax error the user needs to see.
- **Per-binding failures are already skipped from `blocks`, but duplicate keys within one block are retained in source order.** `parse_bindings` diagnoses a repeated key and deliberately does *not* `continue` (`crates/hana_rubric/src/keymap/document.rs:245-254`), so both entries reach this phase. That retention is exactly what makes merge rule 3's last-wins correct — the document stage reports the duplicate, this phase resolves it.
- **`keymap/mod.rs` re-exports `KeymapDocument` under `#[expect(unused_imports, reason = …)]`** (`crates/hana_rubric/src/keymap/mod.rs:6-10`). This phase is its first user, so **delete that attribute in the same change**: a fulfilled `expect` emits `unfulfilled_lint_expectation`, which `-D warnings` turns into an error and fails this phase's own gate.
- Phase 3's `Diagnostic` carries `suggested replacements` — this is the phase that fills that field.
- `Display` for `Keystroke` renders a fixed canonical order (`platform-`/`ctrl-`/`alt-`/`shift-`) over already-canonicalized modifiers, so a suggestion rendered from a parsed keystroke comes back as `ctrl-p` off macOS even when the file said `cmd-p`. Suggestions are fine to render this way — they are corrections, not echoes — but any test asserting exact suggestion text must be platform-split (`cfg!(target_os = "macos")`). CI is Ubuntu-only, so the macOS half never runs there.

- **`Diagnostic::message` is required.** Phase 4a added `pub message: String` as a non-`Option` field on a struct with no `Default`, so every construction site in this phase must supply it. Use `String::new()` where no sentence applies.
- **This phase adds the last three `DiagnosticKind` variants** (settled Phase 4a Decision D): `HeldCommandInSequence`, `UnremappableCommand`, `ReservedKeystroke`. All three require registry resolution, which is why they land here and not in Phase 6. Phase 4a already shipped `Diagnostic::message` and the three registry-validation variants — do not re-add those.
**Protected recovery chord — how it arrives, and how it is asserted:**

The chord's value belongs to the application, its enforcement belongs to this crate, and there is exactly one route between them. `hana_rubric` may not depend on hana — that inversion is what the whole crate split prevents — and the library deliberately never learns what an input context is, so it cannot infer the chord. Configuration injection is therefore the only workable route: **Phase 12 adds `.with_protected_keystroke(Keystroke)` to `KeymapPlugin`'s builder**, accepting zero or more and storing them on the plugin; **this phase** reads that list and rejects any binding whose sequence *starts with* a protected keystroke — bare chord or sequence prefix — with a diagnostic naming it as reserved for recovery; **Phase 20** passes hana's `RECOVERY_CHORD` constant through that builder call. Note that Phase 4a's `Unremappable` capability does not cover this: it protects a *command* from being rebound, so a user could still bind some other command to the recovery chord and shadow it.

**The rejection assertions must be platform-split, because `ctrl-p` is a real binding on macOS.** Phase 1's canonicalization sets `PLATFORM` on macOS and folds to `CONTROL` elsewhere (`Modifiers::with_platform()`), so `ctrl-p == platform-p` on Linux and Windows but **`ctrl-p != platform-p` on macOS**, where `ctrl-p` is an ordinary, perfectly bindable chord the protected-chord check must *not* reject. Assert it the way Phase 1 split its own tests, on `cfg!(target_os = "macos")`:

- **Everywhere:** `cmd-p`, `super-p`, `win-p`, and modifier-order permutations of them are rejected, as bare chords and as sequence prefixes.
- **Off macOS only:** `ctrl-p` is also rejected, because it canonicalizes to the same value.
- **On macOS:** `ctrl-p` is **accepted** as an ordinary binding, and a test asserts that — this is the case that proves canonicalization does real work rather than collapsing everything to Ctrl.

CI runs Ubuntu-only, so the macOS half of the split never runs there. **Phase 20's gate carries the same wrong assertion and takes the identical split.**

**`document.rs` is knowingly over the module-split limit — do not split it in this phase.** It shipped at 1057 non-test lines (1419 total) against this workspace's 500-non-test-line rule, and the change this phase makes to it (recording unrecognized block-member names) grows it slightly further. That is accepted deliberately: the split is deferred to the end-of-plan cleanup, not forgotten. **Do not reorganize the module on your own initiative** — the legal cut is recorded in the cleanup entry at the end of this plan, and taking it now would collide with this phase's own edits to the same file.

**Acceptance gate:** `verify.sh check|test|lint hana_rubric` green. Tests prove each numbered rule above: a user block overrides a default at the same identity; an **invalid** user line leaves the prior valid default intact; a tombstone in a context suppresses the global fallback rather than exposing it; a tombstone on `g` leaves `g g` live; a tombstone on `g g` lets `g` fire immediately; with `g` and `g g g` both live, `g g` followed by a timeout still fires `g` — the intermediate `g g` node carries no binding of its own, and Phase 2 shipped and had to fix exactly this dropped-carry bug; a conditioned binding beats an unconditioned one; an unknown id names close matches; a `Held` command bound to `g f` is rejected; an `Unremappable` command is rejected wherever it appears; a block whose context is present but unresolvable contributes no edits and leaves every global default intact; the protected chord is rejected as a bare chord, as a sequence prefix, and through the `super-p` / `win-p` / modifier-order aliases — with the `ctrl-p` assertion **platform-split** per the protected-chord section above (rejected off macOS, **accepted** on macOS, both asserted).

### Retrospective

**What worked:**
- The merge algorithm's seven numbered rules mapped 1:1 onto tests — every acceptance-gate item got a named test with no interpretation gap, including the dropped-carry timeout case and the `cfg!(target_os = "macos")` split.
- Pre-merging the global set into each per-condition matcher made merge rule 4 free at dispatch time, exactly as specified.

**What deviated from the plan:**
- `CommandRegistry` needed two additions, not one: `iter_entries()` (id-sorted, so the compiled table is reproducible) plus `CommandEntry::invocation()` and `Clone`/`Copy` derives, re-exported `pub(crate)` from `command/mod.rs`. No public API changed.
- `document.rs` grew a second source span per binding (`BindingSource` now holds `key` and `value` `SourceLocation`s). The Spec asked only for unrecognized-member spans; the command-value span turned out to be required because unknown-command suggestions are machine-applicable replacement text and were pointing at the keystroke.
- `parse_context` now returns the context's source location for an explicit `"context": null`. Without it, `resolve_context`'s `(None, Some(_)) => Invalid` arm was unreachable and a null context would have merged as global — the opposite of the Spec's rule.

**Surprises:**
- **`MergedKeymap::global()` is not reachable from a per-condition matcher.** The first implementation compiled only `merged_keymap.conditions`, so an application registering zero conditions — and every frame before context synchronization first runs, since `ActiveCondition::handle` is `Option<ConditionHandle>` — had no matcher at all. `CompiledKeymap` needs its own `global: SequenceMatcher<CommandHandle>` field alongside the per-condition map. Phase 11 must consult it when no condition is active.
- **The compiled table must hold `CommandEntry`, not `CommandId`.** Storing ids kept a heap string on the dispatch path via `CommandRegistry`'s `&CommandId`-keyed hash, violating "no name survives into the compiled form".
- `from_sources` silently dropped the defaults document's diagnostics whenever the user document failed as a whole — the `?` returned only the user's error vector. Both sources' diagnostics now flow into the `Err` arm.
- **This plan's line 709 claim that `hana_rubric` has no reverse dependency in the workspace is stale.** `bevy_lagrange`, `hana_valence`, and `fairy_dust` all depend on it since Phase 4c moved the input directory in. The smoke target (`bevy_lagrange`'s `programmatic_control`) therefore does link this crate; what it cannot exercise is Phase 7's merge/compile code, which has no plugin wiring until Phase 11/12.

**Implications for remaining phases:**
- Phase 11 takes `ResMut<CompiledKeymap>` and must select `compiled.global` when `ActiveCondition` holds `None`, and `compiled.matchers[&handle]` otherwise. Per-condition matchers already contain the global bindings, so there is no fallback chain to walk.
- Phase 11 dispatches through `CompiledKeymap::invocation(CommandHandle) -> Option<Invocation>`, never through `CommandRegistry` by id.
- Phase 12 clears failure diagnostics on a successful load but retains advisories; `Diagnostic::severity` is the discriminator and `KeymapLoadFailures` now documents that contract.
- Phase 8's schema work can assume the scanner records unrecognized block members with spans.

### Phase 7 Review

An architect pass over Phases 8–22 returned eighteen findings. All were applied; none needed a scope or ordering decision.

- **Phase 12 re-scoped — most of the reload pipeline already shipped.** `MergedKeymap::from_sources` + `compile` already parse both sources, layer them, carry both sources' diagnostics on the `Err` path, and build the matchers. D7's two tiers were restated as `from_sources`'s `Result` arms (`Err` = whole-file, `Ok` = per-binding, both emitting `DiagnosticKind::Syntax`), and `reload.rs` narrowed to UTF-8 decode → `from_sources` → `compile` → swap. Without this a delegate would have reimplemented layering.
- **Phase 12 Decision A narrowed.** `from_sources`/`from_documents` already take `protected_keystrokes` and reject prefixes with tests; only the `KeymapPlugin` builder field and its threading remain. The decision's "add a Phase 7 test" clause is marked satisfied.
- **Phase 11 gained the `CompiledKeymap::global` contract**: route through `global` when `ActiveCondition::handle()` is `None`; no fallback chain (a lookup would resurrect tombstoned bindings); `global` has its own pending state so cancel-on-previous-context treats it as a context; dispatch via `CompiledKeymap::invocation`, never `CommandRegistry`.
- **Phase 11 gained a missing-resource rule.** Nothing inserts `CompiledKeymap` before Phase 12 and it derives no `Default`, so an unconditional `world.resource::<CompiledKeymap>()` would abort under this workspace's `clippy::panic` denial. Absent resource now means return without routing and without a diagnostic, with a test — the same rule the plan already wrote for `ActiveCondition`.
- **Four module-scope lint `expect`s added to Phases 11/12 Files.** `compiled.rs:3-6`, `merged.rs:3-6`, `document.rs:3-6`, and `keymap/mod.rs:12-16`'s `ResolvedEdits` re-export. A fulfilled `expect` errors under `-D warnings`, and a module-scope one stays fulfilled while any single item in the file is still dead — so narrowing is often right rather than deletion. The `ResolvedEdits` re-export is marked for deletion: its accessors are private to `merged.rs` and reload needs none of them.
- **`DiagnosticSeverity` is not Phase 9's retained marker.** Phase 7's severity answers "did this binding load?"; Phase 9's decision needs "can any later load reproduce this?". `condition.rs:229` writes `Failure`, so retain-advisories/clear-failures alone still deletes Phase 5's startup registration failure. Phase 12's Spec now states they are two separate fields.
- **`Diagnostic::severity` documented as required at every construction site** in Phases 9, 10, and 12, alongside the existing `Diagnostic::message` rule; `Companion` and `Disk` diagnostics are `Failure`.
- **The "immutable `CompiledKeymap`" wording was factually inverted** and is replaced everywhere with "replaced wholesale on reload; `ResMut`, never `Res`". `runtime.rs`'s reason to exist was rewritten accordingly: held-source refcounts, inhibition, generation tracking, and the `Instant` clock have no home in a resource swapped wholesale.
- **Phase 8's claim that `CommandEntry` is unreachable was false** — Phase 7 re-exported it `pub(crate)`. The instruction (read metadata through `CommandRegistry::iter()` → `CommandInfo`) stands; the justification now says the entry exposes only `invocation()`.
- **Phase 8 gained a single-source constraint for the legal block-member list.** `RECOGNIZED_BLOCK_MEMBERS` at `merged.rs:76` is the load-time authority; the schema's `additionalProperties` enumeration must either read it or be covered by an equality test.
- **Phase 12 gained the missing advisory for an explicit `"context": null`.** That arm marked the block invalid and pushed no diagnostic, so its bindings vanished silently while every other unresolvable-context path reported.
- **Phase 12 owns the `Generation` counter.** `Generation` ships with no successor and no `Default`; `Generation(previous.0 + 1)` is the intended construction and the first committed number is Phase 12's choice.
- **"Off the main thread" overstated Phase 7** and is restated as "pure with respect to `World` mutation" — both entry points take the registries by shared reference and `from_merged` clones every `CommandEntry`.
- Mechanical corrections: the prelude is 25 items, not 24 (Phases 13, 16, 19, 20); drifted line refs in `condition.rs` and `document.rs`; the deferred-cleanup entry for splitting `document.rs` updated to 1526 total lines / ~430-line cut, noting `SourceLocation` must be imported back for `ContextSource` and `BindingSource`; and the Delegation Context layout bullet gained `command/capability.rs` and marks `merged.rs`/`compiled.rs` shipped.

---

### Phase 8 — `hana_rubric`: JSON Schema generation · status: done (2b663825)

#### Work Order

**Goal:** a generated schema drives editor autocomplete, hover text, and squiggles for the keymap file with no editor-specific setup.

**Spec:**

`src/keymap/schema.rs` — inside `keymap/`, not at the crate root, because the schema describes the keymap envelope, binding representations, and `ContextExpr` — it depends on the document model, not only on the two registries. Its builders stay **private behind one crate-private entry point** for the plugin.

Generate a JSON Schema (draft the editors' JSON language servers accept) from the command registry, the condition registry, and the document model. **Construct the document as a `serde_json_lenient` value and serialize it — not reflect-derived, and not `schemars`.** The content is a runtime enumeration of whichever commands and conditions the application registered, not the shape of a Rust type, so there is nothing for reflection to derive; `serde_json_lenient` is already a dependency, so this needs no manifest change. (Bevy 0.19 keeps its reflect-driven generator in `bevy_remote`, which this crate does not enable and should not pull in for a serializer.)

- The command-name field is an `anyOf` of `{"type": "string", "const": "<id>"}`, each carrying the authored **description** as `description` (→ hover text). This is Zed's technique; the values come from `CommandEntry`, not from doc comments.
- The `"context"` field's value is an enum of the registered condition names, each with its `#[strum(message)]` description.
- The binding value accepts a command-id string or `null`, and describes what `null` means (the key is inert at this identity, not inherited).
- A root `"description"` marks the file generated.
- **`"additionalProperties": false` on the root object and on each block object**, so a schema-aware editor underlines a misspelled member (`"contxt"`) as it is typed, before the file is ever saved. This is the editor-side half of the unrecognized-member rule; Phase 7 carries the load-time half as an advisory diagnostic for anyone editing without schema support. The generated schema must therefore enumerate every legal member name, and gains one when the document model does.
- **Emit command and condition alternatives as constants with descriptions**, and include a **condition catalog** in the generated default reference file (Phase 9 writes it).

**The schema cannot be "messed with" in any way that matters: the app never reads it.** It is written at startup, overwritten at the next, and consumed only by the editor's JSON language server. Tampering yields wrong autocomplete until relaunch and cannot change a single binding.

**hana needs neither Zed's custom LSP request nor its URI scheme.** Zed associates the keymap path with `zed://schemas/keymap` over `workspace/configuration` and answers the language server's `vscode/content` request in-process — it can do that because it is both the editor and the app. Nothing knows that about `~/.config/hana/keymap.jsonc`. We generate the schema at startup, write it next to the keymap, and reference it with a **relative `"$schema": "./keymap.schema.json"`**; any editor's JSON LS then gives identical behavior.

**Files:**
- `crates/hana_rubric/src/keymap/schema.rs` — create
- `crates/hana_rubric/src/keymap/mod.rs` — declare; expose one `pub(crate)` entry point returning the schema bytes
- `crates/hana_rubric/src/condition.rs` — delete one lint attribute (see the constraint below); no other change

**Constraints from prior phases:**
- **Read command metadata through `CommandRegistry::iter()`**, which yields `CommandInfo { id, title, description, capability }` sorted by id (`crates/hana_rubric/src/command/registry.rs`). `CommandEntry` is crate-visible — Phase 7 re-exported it `pub(crate)` from `command/mod.rs` — but it exposes only `invocation()`; every field the schema needs is on `CommandInfo`, so read metadata through `iter()` and never through the entry. Title and description are guaranteed non-empty by Phase 4b's startup validation, so the schema never emits an empty `description`.
- **Emit only commands where `capability.is_user_bindable()` is true.** Phase 7 rejects an `Unremappable` command wherever it appears in a file, so offering one as a completion would autocomplete a guaranteed error. `Held` commands **are** bindable — single keystroke only, never a sequence — so they must appear; state that restriction in their emitted `description` text.
- Phase 5's `ConditionRegistry` carries variant names and `#[strum(message)]` descriptions; a missing message is already a startup diagnostic. Read them through `ConditionRegistry::iter()`, which yields `ConditionInfo { name, description }`; both the registry and `ConditionInfo` are `pub(crate)` in `crates/hana_rubric/src/condition.rs` and this file is a crate sibling, so both are visible.
- **Reading `ConditionInfo::description` retires a lint attribute, and leaving it in place is a build error, not a warning.** Phase 5 shipped that field with `#[cfg_attr(not(test), expect(dead_code, reason = "read by schema and companion-file generation in later phases"))]` (`crates/hana_rubric/src/condition.rs:93-99`) because nothing outside tests read it. This phase is that reader. A fulfilled `expect` emits `unfulfilled_lint_expectation`, which `-D warnings` turns into an error — so **delete the whole `#[cfg_attr(...)]` attribute from the field** as part of this phase's diff. That is the intended mechanism: `expect` was chosen over `allow` precisely so it would announce itself here rather than sit silently forever.
- **The legal block-member list already has a load-time authority — do not hand-write a second one.** Phase 7 shipped `RECOGNIZED_BLOCK_MEMBERS = ["bindings", "context"]` (`crates/hana_rubric/src/keymap/merged.rs:76`), consumed at `merged.rs:194` to emit the unrecognized-member advisory. The schema's `"additionalProperties": false` enumeration must agree with it exactly, or the editor and the loader disagree about what is legal. Take one of two routes and say which in the diff: promote the const to `pub(super)` and read it from `schema.rs`, or keep the schema's own list and add a test asserting the two are equal.
- Phase 6's document model defines the envelope (`$schema` + `bindings` array of blocks), the `null` tombstone, and the rejected-in-v1 `[id, args]` form — the schema describes v1's accepted forms only.
- The schema is generated **in memory** and returned as bytes. It does not touch the filesystem; Phase 9 publishes it.
- Any keystroke text the schema emits as an example or pattern is rendered by `Display` for `Keystroke`, which uses a fixed canonical order (`platform-`/`ctrl-`/`alt-`/`shift-`) over canonicalized modifiers — a `cmd-` example becomes `ctrl-` off macOS and **`platform-`** on macOS — the writer emits the literal string `"platform-"` (`crates/hana_rubric/src/keystroke/mod.rs:173`), so a schema example never reads back as the `cmd-` spelling a user would type. The generated schema is therefore platform-dependent wherever it names a concrete chord, and no rendering matches authored input. Either avoid concrete chord examples in the schema or platform-split the tests that assert them; CI is Ubuntu-only.

**Acceptance gate:** `verify.sh check|test|lint hana_rubric` green. Tests prove: the generated schema is valid JSON and parses as a schema document; every **user-bindable** registered command appears as a `const` with its authored description and **no `Unremappable` command appears**; every condition appears with its message; `null` is an accepted binding value and is described; **the root and block objects both carry `"additionalProperties": false`, and a test asserts a document with a misspelled block member fails schema validation**; **a test fails when any user-bindable public command or any condition is missing from generated completion or lacks a description.** Manual acceptance deferred to Phase 13b.

### Retrospective

**What worked:**
- The `serde_json_lenient`-value construction the Spec settled on cost no manifest change and produced the whole document in one file; no reflection or `schemars` machinery was needed for what is a runtime enumeration.
- Phase 5's `#[cfg_attr(not(test), expect(dead_code, …))]` on `ConditionInfo::description` announced itself exactly as designed — deleting it was a mechanical, unambiguous part of this diff rather than something the implementer had to discover.

**What deviated from the plan:**
- The block-member constraint allowed two routes; the implementation landed on **route 2** (schema keeps its own member list, a test asserts the two agree) rather than route 1 (production code reads the shared const). `RECOGNIZED_BLOCK_MEMBERS` was still promoted from an associated const on `MergedKeymap` to a module-level `pub(super)` in `merged.rs`, but after the fix pass it is referenced from `schema.rs` only by the test module.
- Two `#[cfg_attr(not(test), expect(…))]` attributes were added — one on `schema.rs`'s module, one on `keymap/mod.rs`'s re-export — because the entry point has no non-test caller until the plugin's `finish` step exists. They must be removed when that caller lands, by the same fulfilled-`expect` mechanism as `ConditionInfo::description`.

**Surprises:**
- The drift test that guards route 2 shipped **vacuous**: `block_schema` seeded its `properties` map from `RECOGNIZED_BLOCK_MEMBERS` with every member mapped to `true`, then overwrote both real entries. The test therefore compared the schema's keys against the constant those keys were derived from, and a third member would have shipped as a bare `true` with the test still green. Fix pass 1 removed the seed and added an assertion that every block property is a JSON object, which is what makes route 2 sound.
- The blind reviewer's single finding — the tests use a hand-written partial validator rather than a real JSON Schema validator — was downgraded from blocker: no schema-validation crate is in the dependency set and the phase forbids a manifest change, so "parses as a schema document" cannot mean handing it to a real validator here. Its salvageable half was real and was fixed: the misspelled-member test asserted only `is_err()` and now asserts the error names the offending member.

**Implications for remaining phases:**
- Phase 12 owns calling the schema entry point from `Plugin::finish`; a constraint saying so was added to its Work Order during this phase. Phase 9 publishes the bytes and must not generate them.
- Whichever phase adds a legal block member must update both `RECOGNIZED_BLOCK_MEMBERS` and `schema.rs`'s own list — the drift test will fail until both move.

### Phase 8 Review

- **Phase 10** — its "empty user stub" was unbuildable: the shipped schema requires `bindings` and `WireDocument.bindings` has no serde default, so an empty stub fails its own parse on every launch. Spec now names the exact stub contents (`$schema` + empty `bindings`), which also makes Phase 13's no-workspace-settings gate reachable.
- **Phase 12** — added `schema_bytes`'s real signature (fallible, needs both registries at once) and the note that `ConditionRegistry` is present from `build` while `CommandRegistry` is inserted by `finish` itself.
- **Phase 12** — added the constraint that there is no single terminal plugin type: `for_context` / `for_state_context` return two distinct plugin types, so the `finish` assembly is one shared crate-private routine both call, not a duplicated body and not a third plugin.
- **Phase 12** — added deletion of the two `#[cfg_attr(not(test), expect(…))]` attributes Phase 8 shipped; they become fulfilled the moment `finish` calls the entry point, and a fulfilled `expect` is a build error under `-D warnings`.
- **Phase 12** — added a root-level unrecognized-member advisory with a shared constant, mirroring Phase 7's block-level rule. The schema forbids unknown root members while the loader ignores them.
- **Phase 12** — dependency diagram corrected: `keymap/schema` imports `Capability`, `CommandRegistry`, and `ConditionRegistry` only; it never touches the document model, so the `keymap/document` edge was removed.
- **Phases 9 and 12** — every `condition.rs` line reference shifted by 7 (Phase 8 deleted a 7-line lint attribute) and every `merged.rs` / `keymap/mod.rs` reference by 1–8 (the block-member constant moved to module scope, `mod schema;` was added). All re-pinned against the current source.
- **Phase 13** — added a real JSON Schema validator as a dev-dependency plus a gate line. Phase 8 could not do this (no validator in the dependency set, manifest change forbidden); Phase 13's Files already own the manifest, so this closes the gap the blind reviewer raised.
- **Phase 18** — gate now requires hana's shipped default keymap to validate against the schema generated by the same run; `finish` resolves it against the registries, which does not catch a member the schema forbids.
- **Deferred to Phase 9** — the condition catalog promised for `keymap.default.jsonc` has no owner: Phase 8 shipped no catalog generator, Phase 9 may not call one, Phase 18 hand-authors the file. Recorded as a pending decision (add a second generator vs. drop the catalog).
- **Deferred to Phase 13** — its description-coverage clause is unimplementable as written (`schema_bytes` is crate-private; examples see the public API only) and partly duplicates a test Phase 8 already ships. Recorded as a pending decision (read the published schema back off disk vs. move the example's declarations into `src/`).

### Phase 9 — `hana_rubric`: config paths and non-fatal companion publication · status: done (ca2d1a94)

#### Work Order

**Goal:** the config directory resolves per-platform, and the two generated read-only companion files publish atomically without ever being able to break startup.

**Spec:**

`src/disk/mod.rs` (exports worker startup and message types only), `src/disk/constants.rs`, `src/disk/paths.rs`, `src/disk/companion_files.rs`.

**Layout (D6, R4):**

```
~/.config/hana/
  keymap.jsonc          ← user's, hot-reloaded; stub created on first launch only if absent
  keymap.default.jsonc  ← generated, 0444, reference to copy from
  keymap.schema.json    ← generated, 0444, drives editor autocomplete
```

- Honor `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME` when set; use `dirs::config_dir()` on Windows. Matches nvim, helix, starship, and Zed — the path you would guess without looking it up.
- **Follow Zed's deliberate split** (`crates/paths/src/paths.rs:122`): hand-edited config goes where humans can reach it; app-managed data goes where the platform wants it. `dirs::config_dir()` on macOS yields `~/Library/Application Support/hana/keymap.jsonc` — hidden by Finder, typed by nobody — so macOS falls through to a hardcoded `~/.config/<app>`.
- The app name is a plugin parameter, not a hardcoded `hana` — this crate has a second consumer.
- `.jsonc` for the user file and reference; `.json` for the schema, which carries no comments. VS Code treats `.json` as strict JSON and reports every comment as a syntax error; both VS Code and Zed handle `.jsonc` as its own language mode.
- `constants.rs` holds filenames, `0444` permissions, and debounce/retry/poll intervals. **There is no crate-root `constants.rs`** — filenames, permissions, and I/O timing belong to `disk/`; the sequence timeout is keymap policy and lives in `keymap/constants.rs`.
- `paths.rs` holds `KeymapPaths`, built from the filenames.

**Companion publication is non-fatal, and the ordering matters:**

1. **Load embedded defaults before touching the config directory.** Defaults are embedded via `include_str!` and **always load from the embedded copy, never from disk** — they must load with no config directory at all.
2. Generate both byte buffers in memory and **skip the write when an existing regular file already matches**.
3. Otherwise write a **same-directory temporary file, set read-only permissions, and replace the entry without following destination symlinks**. A direct truncate-and-write fails against the existing `0444` file — this is the specific failure to design for.
4. **Any failure** of directory creation, write, permission change, or replacement continues with embedded defaults and a readable user keymap, recording a **durable companion-publication warning separate from keymap parse errors**.
5. **Companion-write failure must not prevent the watcher from starting.**

`keymap.default.jsonc` is written purely as a readable reference — hana is not a text editor, so writing it is the equivalent of Zed's `zed: open default keymap`, which opens the bundled asset in a read-only buffer. It carries a generated JSONC comment header — the unremappable recovery chord (a use for choosing JSONC) and the condition catalog — produced by Phase 12's generator and handed to this phase as finished bytes.

`companion_files.rs` is **callable only by `DiskWorker`** (Phase 10) — `pub(super)` at most.

**Files:**
- `crates/hana_rubric/src/disk/mod.rs` — populate the existing stub (Phase 3 shipped it as a one-line doc stub and `lib.rs` already declares `mod disk;`)
- `crates/hana_rubric/src/disk/constants.rs` — create
- `crates/hana_rubric/src/disk/paths.rs` — create
- `crates/hana_rubric/src/disk/companion_files.rs` — create
- `crates/hana_rubric/src/lib.rs` — re-export the keymap-path accessors (`mod disk;` is already declared)

**Constraints from prior phases:**
- Phase 8 returns schema bytes in memory through one `pub(crate)` entry point, and Phase 12 adds a second generator beside it for the reference file's comment header. **This phase receives two finished buffers and calls neither generator** — `disk/companion_files.rs` never reads a registry, which would require a `disk → condition` edge the Phase 12 dependency diagram forbids.
- Phase 3's `Diagnostic` is the durable surface — companion-publication warnings are a distinct `DiagnosticKind` from keymap parse errors, so the UI can separate "your keymap has an error" from "we could not refresh your reference file."
- **`keymap` does not depend on `disk`.** The plugin root hands generated companion buffers to the worker, receives byte snapshots back, calls the pure keymap compiler, and then invokes the runtime transaction. Declaring `keymap → disk` would let parse or reload code start a read and put filesystem latency back on the frame schedule.
- **This phase's companion warnings are guaranteed to survive an unrelated keymap-load success — Phase 12 implements that guarantee, not this phase.** Resolved 2026-07-30: `KeymapLoadFailures` gains a second, separately-owned collection for diagnostics that no later load can regenerate, and the reload transaction's clearing partitions by producer instead of emptying the resource. Write `Companion`/`Disk` failures into `diagnostics` as normal; implement none of the clearing rule here.

- **`Diagnostic::message` is required.** Phase 4a added `pub message: String` as a non-`Option` field on a struct with no `Default`, so every construction site in this phase must supply it. Use `String::new()` where no sentence applies. **`Diagnostic::severity` is likewise required** — Phase 7 added `pub severity: DiagnosticSeverity` as a non-`Option` field on the same `Default`-less struct, so every construction site must supply it too; this phase's `Companion` and `Disk` diagnostics are `DiagnosticSeverity::Failure`.
**Resolved decisions:**
- **Three producers contend for one diagnostics collection — resolved 2026-07-30.** `KeymapLoadFailures` gains a second, separately-owned collection for diagnostics no later load can regenerate (today: Phase 5's build-time context-registration failures), and the reload transaction's clearing partitions by producer instead of emptying the resource. **Phase 12 implements the rule**; this phase only relies on the guarantee. No field is added to `Diagnostic` and no `DiagnosticKind` variant is added.
- **The condition catalog's owner — resolved 2026-07-30.** The catalog is **generated**, not hand-written. **Phase 12 owns a second `keymap/`-side generator** alongside `schema_bytes` and calls both in `finish`; this phase receives **two** buffers and generates neither.

**Acceptance gate:** `verify.sh check|test|lint hana_rubric` green, **plus `verify.sh check fairy_dust`** — this phase edits `hana_rubric`'s crate root to re-export the keymap-path accessors, and `fairy_dust` depends on `hana_rubric` (`crates/fairy_dust/Cargo.toml:41`), so a crate-root change reaches it. Phases 11 and 12 carry the same line for the same reason.

**Every test in this phase must set both the plugin's app-name parameter and `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME` to a temp directory** — no test may fall through to a real user config directory on any platform. CI runs this suite on Ubuntu through `--workspace --all-features`, where an unset `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME` resolves to the runner's `~/.config`; on a developer's macOS machine the same hole writes into their actual `~/.config/<app>`. Assert the resolved path is inside the temp directory before any test writes.

Tests prove (against a temp directory, with `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME` overridden): the path resolves under `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME` when set and under `~/.config/<app>` on macOS otherwise; publication over an existing `0444` file succeeds via the temp-and-replace path where a direct write fails; matching content is not rewritten; a read-only *directory* produces a companion-publication warning and returns success to the caller; a symlink at the destination is replaced, not followed.

### Retrospective

**What worked:**
- The atomic-publication flow shipped as specified: skip-when-matching, same-directory temp file, `0444`, replace without following symlinks, and every failure returning a `Companion` diagnostic instead of an error. All five "tests prove" clauses are covered; 100 tests pass.
- `fs::symlink_metadata` (not `metadata`) is what makes the symlink clause hold — the destination is never followed, so a symlinked companion is replaced rather than written through.

**What deviated from the plan:**
- The first implementation hand-rolled a `#[cfg(windows)]` `MoveFileExW` FFI call for the replace step. It was deleted in favor of `std::fs::rename`, which issues the same call **plus** an `ERROR_ACCESS_DENIED` fallback (`SetFileInformationByHandle` + `FILE_RENAME_INFO`). Since companions are published `0444` — `FILE_ATTRIBUTE_READONLY` on Windows — the bare call would have failed on every refresh after the first. The workspace has no MSRV pin, so the fallback is always available.

**Surprises:**
- **The isolation rule above and the macOS clause in "Tests prove" cannot both be satisfied through the shipping resolver.** Proving `~/.config/<app>` is chosen when `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME` is absent requires either unsetting the variable (violating the isolation rule) or reading the real home directory (violating it in the other direction). Resolution: the macOS branch is proven against the **pure** resolver `configuration_root(None, None, Some(injected_temp_home))` while the process environment stays pinned to a temp directory for the whole test. The environment-reading wrapper `configuration_directory()` stays covered end-to-end by the `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME`-set test. The one line not covered by any test is the wiring of `dirs::home_dir()` into the resolver's third argument, which is untestable under this constraint.
- A review round proposed the opposite fix — run the real resolver with the variable unset — and had to be reversed against the isolation rule's literal text. Recorded so it is not re-derived.

**Implications for remaining phases:**
- Phase 10 must delete four lint attributes this phase shipped (see its Work Order) or it cannot compile.
- Phases 10 and 11 carry the same isolation requirement and will hit the same conflict if they need to prove a platform-fallback branch. The pattern above is the answer: pin the environment to a temp directory, test branch selection against the pure helper with injected arguments.

### Phase 9 Review

- **Phase 10** — added the required deletion of the four `expect(dead_code)` attributes Phase 9 shipped; a fulfilled `expect` is a hard error under `-D warnings`.
- **Phase 10 / Phase 11** — the isolation rule is clarified in place: it governs the process environment and filesystem writes, and branch-selection tests use the pure resolver with injected arguments rather than unsetting the variable.
- Remaining phases otherwise reviewed with no scope, ordering, or assumption changes.

### Phase 10 — `hana_rubric`: the disk worker — watch, debounce, retry, poll · status: done (a182c2a9)

#### Work Order

**Goal:** one I/O worker owns every filesystem touch, so editor save patterns, network filesystems, and watcher failures never reach the frame schedule.

**Spec:**

`src/disk/worker.rs` — `DiskWorker`. It owns initial read, first-launch stub creation, the parent-directory watch, debounce, retry, the polling audit, confirmed deletion, and companion publication. It communicates with the app through a **bounded coalescing slot**.

**Give a dedicated I/O worker ownership** rather than running this on the schedule: `notify` documents that **NFS may emit no events at all without reporting watcher failure**, so an error-triggered fallback is not sufficient. Run a **slow polling audit alongside native events** rather than waiting for an error. This keeps network-filesystem latency off the frame schedule.

**Watching:**
- **Watch the parent directory non-recursively** and filter the canonical filename **including rename destinations**. Watching the file directly loses the watch when an editor replaces the directory entry.
- **The gotcha to design for, not discover:** editors save by writing a temp file and renaming over the target. The watcher sees several events per save and may briefly observe the file as *missing*. **Debounce, and treat transient absence as "wait", never as "the user deleted their keymap, revert to defaults".**
- Coalesce notifications into **one bounded dirty flag with trailing-edge debounce**.
- **Retry transient `NotFound` for a bounded grace interval**, then apply a documented **confirmed-deletion policy: deleting the user file removes its overrides and restores embedded defaults.**
- Treat **watcher errors, rescan signals, and channel disconnection as durable health failures: reread completely and recreate the watch.**

**First launch (R4):** write the user stub **only if absent**, and never overwrite it afterward. **The stub is not empty and must not be** — `schema.rs`'s root object emits `"required": ["bindings"]` and `WireDocument.bindings` has no `#[serde(default)]`, so an empty file or a bare `{}` is a whole-file parse failure that the app would then report against its own stub on every launch. Write exactly:

```jsonc
{
  "$schema": "./keymap.schema.json",
  "bindings": []
}
```

The `$schema` key is also what makes Phase 13's "completions work with **no workspace settings**" reachable: the editor's JSON language server resolves the relative path with no per-project configuration. Editor assistance therefore begins after the app has run once — state that as the installation contract in the crate docs.

**Startup order:** embedded defaults load first (they cannot fail), then the config directory is touched. Companion publication (Phase 9) runs from this worker; its failure does not prevent the watch from starting.

**The worker never parses.** It produces byte snapshots plus a source path and hands them across the slot; parsing, merging, and compiling are pure and happen on the app side.

**Files:**
- `crates/hana_rubric/src/disk/worker.rs` — create
- `crates/hana_rubric/src/disk/mod.rs` — export worker startup and the message types only
- `crates/hana_rubric/Cargo.toml` — **no edit needed.** Phase 3 shipped `notify = "8"` in root `[workspace.dependencies]` and `notify.workspace = true` here, at default features.

**Constraints from prior phases:**
- Phase 9 built `KeymapPaths`, `disk/constants.rs` (filenames, `0444`, debounce/retry/poll intervals), and `companion_files.rs` (`pub(super)`, callable only from here). The atomic temp-and-replace path is already written — call it, do not reimplement it.
- **`Diagnostic::message` is required.** Phase 4a added `pub message: String` as a non-`Option` field on a struct with no `Default`, so every construction site in this phase must supply it. Use `String::new()` where no sentence applies. **`Diagnostic::severity` is likewise required** — Phase 7 added `pub severity: DiagnosticSeverity` as a non-`Option` field on the same `Default`-less struct, so every construction site must supply it too; this phase's `Companion` and `Disk` diagnostics are `DiagnosticSeverity::Failure`.
- Phase 9 established that companion-publication failures are a **distinct** `DiagnosticKind` from keymap parse errors, and that failure must not prevent the watcher from starting.
- Phase 3's diagnostic resource is durable — watcher health failures are recorded there, not only logged.
- Same guarantee as Phase 9: the worker's `Companion`/`Disk` failures are not erased by a successful keymap load. Phase 12 owns the partitioned-clearing rule; this phase writes into `diagnostics` normally.
- The worker's channels are `pub(crate)`.
- **Delete the four lint attributes Phase 9 shipped — this phase cannot compile otherwise.** Phase 9's items are unreachable until `DiskWorker` calls them, so they carry `expect(dead_code)`: `#[cfg_attr(not(test), expect(dead_code, …))]` on `publish_companion_files` (`crates/hana_rubric/src/disk/companion_files.rs:21-27`) and `#[expect(dead_code, …)]` on `DEBOUNCE_INTERVAL`, `POLL_INTERVAL`, and `RETRY_INTERVAL` (`crates/hana_rubric/src/disk/constants.rs`). Once this phase uses them the `expect` is fulfilled, and a fulfilled `expect` is itself a warning — a hard error under `-D warnings`. Remove each attribute as you wire up the item it guards.

**Acceptance gate:** `verify.sh check|test|lint hana_rubric` green.

**Same isolation requirement as Phase 9:** every test sets the plugin's app-name parameter and `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME` to a temp directory, and asserts the resolved path lies inside it before writing. A file-watching test that escapes into a real config directory would watch — and create a stub in — the developer's own keymap directory. **The rule governs the process environment and every filesystem write: never unset `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME` and never consult the real home directory.** Phase 9 hit the conflict this creates when a test must prove a platform-fallback branch that only fires with the variable absent; the resolution is to keep the environment pinned to a temp directory and test branch selection against the pure resolver with injected arguments (`configuration_root(None, None, Some(injected_temp_home))`), never against the environment-reading wrapper.

Tests prove (temp directory): a write-temp-then-rename save produces exactly one debounced snapshot, not several; transient absence during a rename does **not** trigger the deletion policy; absence persisting past the grace interval **does**, restoring embedded defaults; a deleted-and-recreated parent directory re-establishes the watch; the polling audit detects a change when native events are suppressed (simulate by not emitting); the stub is created when absent and left untouched on a second run; the coalescing slot never grows unbounded under a burst of events.

### Retrospective

**What worked:** the Work Order's two sharpest phrases — "bounded coalescing slot" and "transient absence is never deletion" — gave the blind review testable criteria, and it used them to find three real logic bugs plus three tests that passed for the wrong reason.

**What deviated from the plan:**
- The phase shipped as `src/disk/worker/` (`mod.rs`, `channels.rs`, `runtime.rs`, `watch.rs`), not the single `worker.rs` the **Files** list named — the implementation crossed the workspace split threshold at ~608 non-test lines. No submodule now exceeds 378 non-test lines.
- `WorkerTimings.poll` is `Option<Duration>`, not `Duration`, so a test can disable the polling audit and prove a watcher-driven path in isolation.
- `disk/constants.rs` gained `MAX_RETAINED_DIAGNOSTICS: usize = 64`, which the Work Order did not anticipate.

**Surprises:**
- "Bounded" was violated in a second place the spec never named: the diagnostics `Vec` *inside* the capacity-one message. Each unread publication prepended the previous message's diagnostics, so an app that stopped draining grew one vector without limit. Bounding a mailbox means bounding its payload, not just its slot count.
- Three timing tests were vacuous because the test poll interval equalled the test debounce interval and the polling audit reads unconditionally — the poll alone satisfied every assertion whether or not the debounce or the native watch worked. Any future timing test must disable polling to prove a watcher-driven path.
- A retry firing inside a pending debounce window silently cancelled the debounce, so an ordinary editor save could be published as a confirmed deletion. Two deadlines that can both be due in the same iteration need an explicit precedence rule, not a shared `if`.
- A `#[cfg(test)]` counter on the already-shared `WorkerStatus` was the cheapest way to convert "eventually the bytes match" assertions into "exactly one read happened" assertions, with no public API surface and no release cost.

**Implications for remaining phases:**
- **Phase 12** consumes `DiskWorkerChannels`, `DiskWorkerMessage`, and `DiskSnapshot` from `disk::worker`. Draining with `CoalescingSlot::take()` may now yield **one extra synthesized diagnostic** reading "N older disk diagnostics were discarded before delivery." — the partitioned diagnostic-clearing rule must tolerate a diagnostic that has no source span and no originating binding.
- **Phase 12 must delete the item-scoped `#[expect(dead_code, …)]` in `crates/hana_rubric/src/disk/worker/runtime.rs`** as it wires up the worker's entry point. Once Phase 12 calls it the expectation is fulfilled, and a fulfilled `expect` is a hard error under `-D warnings` — the same trap Phase 10 had to clear for Phase 9's four attributes.
- The first-launch stub shipped exactly as specified (`{"$schema": "./keymap.schema.json", "bindings": []}`), so **Phase 13**'s "completions work with no workspace settings" acceptance criterion is still reachable.

### Phase 10 Review

- **Delegation Context** — the layout bullet now names `disk/worker/{mod.rs, channels.rs, runtime.rs, watch.rs}` and `MAX_RETAINED_DIAGNOSTICS`, and declares that every `disk/worker.rs` path cited below reads as `disk/worker/`.
- **Phase 12** gained the worker's shipped API verbatim, because it is the worker's only consumer and its Work Order previously described it in one sentence. Most consequential: the message is **tri-state**, and the third state — `snapshot: Some(contents: None)` — is the confirmed-deletion policy crossing the worker/app boundary, which no plan text named. Also added: `take_message` may append a synthesized truncation diagnostic with no source span; `KeymapLoadFailures` must be capped or replaced rather than appended, since the partitioned-clearing rule never clears `Disk` entries; `commit_reload` takes `(Option<DiskSnapshot>, Vec<Diagnostic>)` rather than a `DiskWorkerMessage`, because the message's `pub(super)` field makes it unconstructible outside `disk::worker` and every acceptance case needs synthetic input; `DiskWorkerChannels` is wrapped in a newtype resource declared in `keymap_plugin.rs`, because `disk/` imports no bevy and must not start; and its `Drop` is the shutdown, so no second shutdown system.
- **Phase 12** — three defects in its own text, fixed rather than deferred. `commit_reload` **must** be an exclusive system: `CompiledKeymap` has no `Default` and nothing inserts it, so a `ResMut` system would never run and the first commit would never happen. The schema-generation `Err` arm as written would publish a zero-byte `keymap.schema.json` at `0444` and break Phase 13's `$schema` acceptance; it now takes `Option<Vec<u8>>` and skips publication. And nothing committed a keymap until the first worker message arrived, leaving input inert from `finish` until disk I/O completed and permanently if paths failed to resolve — so a defaults-only generation is now committed synchronously in `finish`, before the worker starts.
- **Phase 12** — `KeymapPaths` was being resolved twice on two threads (once inside the spawned worker, once on the app thread for `from_sources`), which can disagree and send the worker into the developer's real `~/.config`; `start_disk_worker` now takes `&KeymapPaths`, resolved once. Its **Files** gained the three `disk/` files this requires, having previously named none.
- **Phase 12** — the dead-code constraint was rewritten as a sweep with a search command. Wiring the worker fulfils roughly ten expectations across `disk/` and `keymap/` in one diff, not the one the retrospective first named, and each fulfilled `expect` is a hard error.
- **Phase 12** — gained the test-isolation paragraph. It is the first phase to start a worker from a Bevy `App`, so any test calling `app.finish()` writes a stub into the developer's real config directory; Phase 10's isolation helpers are `pub(super)` to `crate::disk` and are widened to `pub(crate)` here.
- **Phase 11** — its `keymap/runtime/` (`mod.rs`, `dispatch.rs`, `held.rs`, `key_edge.rs`) entry now pre-authorizes the `runtime/` directory form with named seams, since that module owns at least as much as the worker did and `self_named_module_files` is denied. Also carries a **Pending decision** on splitting the phase into 11a/11b/11c; deferred rather than applied because the open authorization window covers "phase 11" and scope is the user's call.
- **Phase 13** — dropped "a repeat launch does not overwrite the user stub" from the manual editor pass, now covered by an automated test, and gained the fact that its read-back test is race-free by construction (publication precedes the watch loop, `Drop` joins the thread) so no sleep is added and no assertion is weakened as flaky.
- **Gate G1 de-gated throughout.** The Gates table already marked it superseded, but the banner above Phase 14, the "Phases 1–13…" line, and seven `**Blocked by:**` stamps still blocked every hana-side phase on a merge that is not happening. As written, no hana phase was dispatchable. They now point at `/Users/natemccoy/rust/hana_tool_graph` on `init/hana_catalyst` and keep only the reconcile-before-dispatch requirement.

### Phase 11a — `hana_rubric`: the modifier model · status: done (4f4134a3)

#### Work Order

**Goal:** physical Control and physical Super are represented independently on every platform, and a `secondary` spelling names the platform's usual shortcut modifier.

**Spec:**

**Adopt Zed's two-bit representation (user decision, 2026-07-30).** Today `PLATFORM` means "the platform shortcut modifier" and is physical Super on macOS but physical **Control** everywhere else, which leaves no bit for physical Super off macOS, so `from_pressed` discards it (`crates/hana_rubric/src/keystroke/mod.rs:118-127`). The consequence: Super+`g` on Linux produces a bare `g` and fires the `g` binding. Replace the collapse with gpui's model, verified in `zed-industries/zed@main`:

> **Correction (2026-07-31, during 11a).** This paragraph originally claimed a second consequence — that "a binding spelled `ctrl-g` can never match on Linux or Windows." **That was wrong.** Off macOS the old `from_pressed` took the Control branch and called `with_platform()`, and the old `with_platform()` resolved `PlatformShortcutMode::Control` by delegating to `with_control()` — so the `CONTROL` bit *was* set and `ctrl-g` *did* match. The discarded-Super defect above was the only real one. The delegate wrote a test against the false claim and it passed identically before and after the change; the blind review caught it and fix pass 1 replaced it with bit-level assertions. **Do not reintroduce the `ctrl-g`-never-matches framing anywhere in this document.**

- `PLATFORM` means the **physical** Cmd/Win/Super key on every platform, never discarded, never merged. (gpui `Modifiers::platform`, `crates/gpui/src/platform/keystroke.rs:466-471`.)
- `CONTROL` means the **physical** Control key on every platform.
- `cmd` / `super` / `win` / `platform` keep spelling `PLATFORM` on every platform (gpui `keystroke.rs:152-158`); `ctrl` / `control` keep spelling `CONTROL`.
- Add a **`secondary`** modifier token meaning "the platform's usual shortcut modifier" — `PLATFORM` on macOS, `CONTROL` elsewhere (gpui `keystroke.rs:143-150`). This is the cross-platform spelling and the only token whose meaning is platform-dependent.
- Matching stays exact equality over the whole canonical modifier set (gpui `keystroke.rs:112`). **No blocking mechanism and no platform branch in the routing hot path:** Super+`g` canonicalizes to `PLATFORM`+`g`, which is simply not equal to a bare `g`, so nothing fires. The suppression `spawn_key::<A>()`'s `BlockBy` used to provide falls out of equality.

`Display` gains `secondary` nowhere — it keeps emitting the canonical physical order `platform-`, `ctrl-`, `alt-`, `shift-`. `secondary` is a parse-side spelling only, exactly like `cmd`/`super`/`win`.

**Migration is free at this commit:** every `cmd-`/`ctrl-` spelling in the tree is in tests or plan docs; no shipped default binding depends on the collapse, and the protected recovery chord is not implemented yet (Phase 20 owns it). Two done-phase test expectations invert and must be updated in this phase: Phase 2's "`ctrl-p` and the platform chord are **equal** on Linux/Windows" becomes distinct on every platform, and any `merged.rs` protected-chord test that relies on `cmd-p` and `ctrl-p` colliding off macOS (`crates/hana_rubric/src/keymap/merged.rs:1080`, `:1155`) must respell its protected keystroke `secondary-p`. Phase 4c's "not one assertion may be edited" rule covered a pure relocation and does not bind here; this is a deliberate model change, and the inverted assertions are named above so the change is auditable rather than silent.

**Files:**
- `crates/hana_rubric/src/keystroke/mod.rs` — `from_pressed` sets `CONTROL` from physical Control and `PLATFORM` from physical Super on **every** platform (delete the `cfg!(target_os = "macos")` split at `:118-127`); `parse_modifier` gains a `"secondary"` arm resolving to `Modifier::Platform` on macOS and `Modifier::Control` elsewhere (`:272-284`); update the doc comment on `from_pressed`, which currently states the discarded-Super behavior
- `crates/hana_rubric/src/keymap/merged.rs` — respell the protected keystroke in the tests at `:1080` and `:1155` to `secondary-p` so they keep asserting the collision they were written for
- `crates/hana_rubric/src/keymap/schema.rs` — add `secondary` wherever the generated schema enumerates modifier spellings for editor completion (if it enumerates them; the Phase 8 generator may describe the keystroke field as a free string, in which case no edit)

**Constraints from prior phases:**
- **Only render a `Keystroke` that came from the parser or from `CANONICAL_KEY_MAPPINGS`.** `Display` returns `fmt::Error` for any other `KeyCode` (`crates/hana_rubric/src/keystroke/mod.rs:185`), and `to_string()`/`format!` panic on a `Display` that errors.
- **This phase has no reason to touch `bevy_kana` at all.** Phase 4c moved the whole `keystroke` tree into `hana_rubric`, so every edit here is a same-crate `hana_rubric` edit.
- **Phase 2's keystroke tests are the audit surface.** Search `crates/hana_rubric/src/keystroke/` and `crates/hana_rubric/src/keymap/` for every `cfg!(target_os = "macos")` and every `cmd-`/`ctrl-`/`platform-` spelling in a test before editing; the two `merged.rs` sites named above are the ones known today, not necessarily all of them. CI is Ubuntu-only, so an assertion that silently changes meaning off macOS will not fail locally on the developer's Mac.
- **`Modifier` is a bitflags type and `Modifiers` is its set** (`keystroke/mod.rs`). Adding `secondary` adds **no new bit** — it resolves at parse time to one of the two existing bits. Do not widen the flags type.

**Acceptance gate:** `verify.sh check|test|lint hana_rubric` plus `verify.sh check fairy_dust` green. Tests prove: `ctrl-g` matches a physical Control + `g` press on **every** platform; `secondary-g` matches physical Super + `g` on macOS and physical Control + `g` elsewhere — split on `cfg!(target_os = "macos")`, since CI is Ubuntu-only; `cmd-g`, `super-g`, and `win-g` all match physical Super + `g` on every platform and are **not** equal to `ctrl-g` anywhere; physical Super + `g` does not produce a bare `g` keystroke on any platform; `Display` round-trips a `secondary`-parsed keystroke to its canonical physical spelling (`platform-g` on macOS, `ctrl-g` elsewhere) and never emits `secondary-`. The two `merged.rs` protected-chord tests still assert the collision they were written for, now via `secondary-p`.

### Retrospective

**What worked:** The model change itself was small and landed clean — `from_pressed` lost its `cfg!` split, `with_platform` lost its `PlatformShortcutMode` indirection, `parse_modifier` gained one arm, and no bit was added to the flags type. `schema.rs` needed no edit at all: the generator declares the keystroke field as a free string, so no modifier enumeration exists to extend. `fairy_dust` compiled unchanged.

**What deviated from the plan:** The plan named two existing test sites to invert; the audit found **four**, all off-macOS `cfg` arms asserting the collapse (`ctrl == platform`, platform construction equalling `ctrl`, physical Control matching `cmd`, and physical Super producing no modifier). Instructing the delegate to audit rather than to edit the two named sites is what surfaced them — CI is Ubuntu-only and the developer runs macOS, so none of the four would have failed locally either way. The `merged.rs` alias fixture also had to become platform-split: its macOS branch exercises three distinct alias families (`cmd`/`super`/`win`) that all canonicalize to `PLATFORM`, and off macOS only the `ctrl`/`control` family maps to `CONTROL`, so the same fixture text cannot serve both.

**Surprises:**
- **The plan's own bug claim was half wrong.** It asserted `ctrl-g` could never match off macOS. It could: the old `from_pressed` took the Control branch and called `with_platform()`, which resolved `PlatformShortcutMode::Control` by delegating to `with_control()`, setting the `CONTROL` bit. The discarded-Super defect was the only real one. The delegate wrote a test against the false claim; it passed identically before and after, and the blind review caught it.
- **Whole-keystroke equality cannot detect two bits collapsing into one.** Both of the review's findings were the same shape: the physical fixture and the parsed comparand collapsed to `CONTROL` together under the old model, so the assertion held straight through the failure it was named for. The fix was bit-level assertions (`has_control()` / `has_platform()`) plus inequality against the adjacent chord. **Any future test of a canonicalization change needs the same treatment** — assert the components, not just that two canonicalized values agree.
- **`Keybindings` still routes through `PlatformShortcutMode` and now means something different from `Modifiers::with_platform`.** `spawn_platform_key` binds physical Super on macOS and physical Control elsewhere — `secondary` semantics — while the keystroke spellings `platform-`/`cmd-`/`super-`/`win-` now mean physical Super everywhere.

**Implications for remaining phases:**
- **Phase 19** gained a migration constraint and a deletion: every `spawn_platform_key` call site becomes `secondary-`, never `cmd-`/`platform-`, or the shortcut silently moves to the Super key on Linux and Windows; and `platform_shortcut_mode.rs` is deleted there as its last consumer disappears.
- **Phase 11c** must not reintroduce a `cfg!(target_os = …)` on the routing path — Super+`g` failing to match a bare `g` now falls out of set equality, with no platform branch and no `BlockBy`.
- **Phase 20**'s recovery chord should be authored `secondary-p`, matching what the two `merged.rs` protected-chord tests now use.

### Phase 11a Review

- **The plan's `ctrl-g`-never-matches claim was corrected in place**, with an explicit instruction not to reintroduce the framing. It was the premise for one of the two vacuous tests.
- **Phase 19** gained the `secondary-` migration rule and the `platform_shortcut_mode.rs` deletion (above). Without it, every migrated `spawn_platform_key` shortcut would keep working on macOS and move to the Super key everywhere else — a failure invisible to Ubuntu-only CI and to a macOS developer alike.
- **This phase's acceptance gate** dropped its "(it cannot today off macOS)" parenthetical, which restated the incorrect claim.
