- **The root object's member list has an editor-side authority and no load-time counterpart — add one.** Phase 8 enumerates the root's legal members (`$schema`, `bindings`) hand-written in `schema.rs` under `"additionalProperties": false`, while `WireDocument` carries no `deny_unknown_fields`, so an editor underlines an unknown root member that the loader silently ignores. Mirror the block-level treatment Phase 7 shipped: a shared constant of legal root members with an advisory `DiagnosticKind::Syntax` diagnostic when a user file carries one that is not in it, and the same schema-vs-loader agreement test `block_schema_members_match_the_loader_authority` already gives the block level. This is the root-level half of the user's standing rule that a mistake must be caught at load time for anyone editing without schema support, not only in the editor.
- Phase 9's companion publication is non-fatal and runs from the worker; its warnings are a distinct `DiagnosticKind`.
- **Phase 9's diagnostics-contention decision resolved 2026-07-30; this phase writes the rule.** `KeymapLoadFailures` gains a **second, separately-owned collection** holding diagnostics that no later load can regenerate — today that is exactly Phase 5's build-time context-registration failures, written once from `register_context` (`crates/hana_rubric/src/condition.rs:287-293`). The clearing performed on a successful keymap load **never touches that collection**, and within `diagnostics` it clears only *failure*-severity entries whose `kind` the keymap-load producer owns (`Syntax`, `Keystroke`, `Command`, `Context`), leaving advisories and the worker's `Companion`/`Disk` entries in place. With build-time failures moved out, `DiagnosticKind::Context` inside `diagnostics` is unambiguously reload-produced, which is what makes partitioning by `kind` sound. The change to already-shipped code is at the **push site**, not the construction site: `register_context` pushes into the retained collection instead of `diagnostics`. No field is added to `Diagnostic`, no `DiagnosticKind` variant is added, and Phase 5's resolved "reuse `DiagnosticKind::Context`" decision stands. Any consumer wanting every current problem reads both collections; expose that as one accessor on the resource rather than making callers remember.
- Phase 8's schema generator is a single `pub(crate)` entry point returning bytes; the plugin calls it and hands the bytes to the worker.
- **Phase 7's compile is pure with respect to `World` mutation, which is not the same as thread-free.** Both `from_sources` and `compile` take `&CommandRegistry` and `&ConditionRegistry`, and both are `Resource`s (`crates/hana_rubric/src/command/registry.rs:27`, `crates/hana_rubric/src/condition.rs:98`); `CompiledKeymap::from_merged` additionally clones every `CommandEntry` out of the registry (`crates/hana_rubric/src/keymap/compiled.rs:44-47`). So it runs in an ordinary `Res<…>` system and the swap is cheap — but nothing here licenses a task-pool build without first extracting both registries.
- Phase 4b's registry validation runs in `Plugin::finish`; the functions already exist and are called here for the first time in a real plugin. **`CommandRegistry::initialize` returns `Result<Self, Vec<Diagnostic>>` and does not insert itself into the world** — this phase owns that insertion. Reaching it needs `&mut World` (`app.world_mut()`), the same exclusive-access pattern Phase 11's `route_input` uses.

- **`Diagnostic::message` is required.** Phase 4a added `pub message: String` as a non-`Option` field on a struct with no `Default`, so every construction site in this phase must supply it. Use `String::new()` where no sentence applies.
- **This phase fulfils roughly ten dead-code expectations at once, not one — treat it as a sweep, not a checklist.** The whole worker surface is dead transitively today and goes live in a single diff, and every fulfilled `expect` is `unfulfilled_lint_expectation`, a hard error under `-D warnings`. **After wiring, run `rg -n 'expect\(dead_code|expect\(unused_imports' crates/hana_rubric/src/disk crates/hana_rubric/src/keymap` and narrow or delete every attribute this diff fulfils.** Known at the time of writing: the item-scoped one in `disk/worker/runtime.rs`; four `#[expect(unused_imports, …)]` on the re-exports in `disk/mod.rs`; five `#[cfg_attr(not(test), expect(dead_code, …))]` in `disk/worker/channels.rs` covering `DiskSnapshot`, `DiskWorkerChannels`, `take_message`, the shutdown path, and `WorkerControl::Stop`; plus the two already itemized above in `keymap/mod.rs` and `keymap/merged.rs`, and check `keymap/document.rs`. **Narrowing is often right rather than deletion** — a module-scope `expect` stays fulfilled while any single item in the file is still dead, so removing it wholesale can trade one lint error for another.
- **Test isolation is mandatory here and is not inherited — this phase is the first to start a worker from a Bevy `App`.** `Plugin::finish` starts the worker, so **any test that calls `app.finish()` — including this phase's own gate cases for duplicate-id rejection, an unresolvable shipped default, and `with_app_name` — writes a first-launch stub and companion files into the developer's real config directory.** Same rule as Phases 9 and 10: every test sets both the plugin's app-name parameter and `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME` to a temp directory and asserts the resolved path lies inside it before anything runs; **never unset `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME`, never consult the real home directory.** Phase 10's helpers are not reachable from here — `ENVIRONMENT_LOCK`, `TestDirectory`, and `XdgConfigHome` are `#[cfg(test)] pub(super)` in `crates/hana_rubric/src/disk/paths.rs`, visible only inside `crate::disk`. Widen all three to `pub(crate)`; they are already `#[cfg(test)]`, so this costs nothing in a release build. **Holding the shared `ENVIRONMENT_LOCK` is mandatory, not advisory:** unit tests share one binary and run in parallel, and Phase 9's and Phase 10's tests mutate the same process-wide variable — an unlocked test here corrupts theirs and theirs corrupts this one.

**Resolved decision (2026-07-31) — Decision A: add `.with_protected_keystroke(Keystroke)` to the builder. Approved as recommended, with one amendment: the call must be chainable so an application declaring more than one protected keystroke writes `.with_protected_keystroke(a).with_protected_keystroke(b)`.** Signature is `#[must_use] pub fn with_protected_keystroke(mut self, keystroke: Keystroke) -> Self`, taking `self` by value and pushing onto a `Vec<Keystroke>` field, matching how the existing builder methods chain by value. Repeated calls accumulate; they do not replace. Duplicate keystrokes are not an error — de-duplicate on read or ignore the duplication, and do not emit a diagnostic for it. The `Vec` threads through to Phase 7's already-shipped `from_sources(protected_keystrokes: &[Keystroke])` parameter unchanged. Acceptance: a builder that registers two distinct protected keystrokes rejects a user binding on **each** of them, proving accumulation rather than last-write-wins. The original decision text follows as the archive record.

**Decision A: add `.with_protected_keystroke(Keystroke)` to the builder**

Actual problem:
Phase 7 must reject any binding whose sequence starts with the protected recovery chord, and Phase 20 needs one canonical parsed constant for it. The value lives in hana's `global_shortcuts.rs`; `hana_rubric` may not depend on hana; and this plugin's builder — the only surface an application configures the keymap through — has no parameter for it. There is currently no route from the application into the library's validation.

What exists now:
- Phase 4a's `Unremappable` capability protects a *command*, not a keystroke: a user can still bind a different command to the recovery chord and shadow it.
- **The library half is already shipped.** Phase 7's `MergedKeymap::from_sources` and `from_documents` both take `protected_keystrokes: &[Keystroke]` and reject any sequence starting with one — bare chord or prefix — with a diagnostic saying it is reserved for the application's recovery command. Tests `merged.rs:1080`, `:1126`, `:1155` cover it. The "add a Phase 7 test" clause of this decision is satisfied.
- This phase's builder surface has no protected-keystroke parameter — that is the only remaining gap.
- The library deliberately never learns what an input context is, so it cannot infer the chord.

What should change:
- Add `.with_protected_keystroke(Keystroke)` to `KeymapPlugin`'s builder, accepting zero or more into a `Vec<Keystroke>` field on the plugin.
- Thread that field through to the existing `from_sources` parameter. No new validation logic — the enforcement already exists.
- Phase 20 supplies hana's `RECOVERY_CHORD` through this call.

Recommendation:
Take it. The application owns the value, the library already owns the enforcement, and the dependency direction stays intact. The remaining work is a builder field plus threading, and a test here that a protected keystroke registered through the builder reaches `from_sources`.

**Resolved decision (2026-07-31) — Decision B: registry and condition validation stay all-or-nothing. Route 1 approved as recommended, for both registries.** A malformed *declaration* is code, not user data, so it fails whole rather than partially. `Plugin::finish` records the diagnostics, logs at error level, inserts an **empty** `CommandRegistry`, and the app starts; every keymap binding then fails to resolve and is reported individually. `ConditionRegistry::register`'s existing atomic behavior is left as-is — no partial-success return is added to either, and neither Phase 4b's `CommandRegistry::initialize` nor Phase 5's `ConditionRegistry::register` changes signature.

**Shipped-default softening (required by this route):** the rule that `finish` hard-fails on a **shipped default** that does not resolve is **suppressed when the registry is empty because validation itself failed** — otherwise one bad third-party declaration is fatal, and `clippy::panic` is denied so there is no survivable abort. Report and start. The rule still fires normally when the registry built successfully but a shipped default names a command or context that does not exist.

**Precedent (Zed, read 2026-07-31, `zed-industries/zed@main`).** Zed draws the same line between code declarations and user files, and is stricter than this route on the code side. Its action registry panics: `ActionRegistry::load_actions` (`crates/gpui/src/action.rs:286`) calls `panic!` on a duplicate action name (`:296`, and `:311` for an alias), and the doc comment at `:53` states registering two actions with the same name "will result in a panic during `App` creation" — no degradation, no partial table, the process dies before a window opens. Route 1 is the closest reachable equivalent under the denied-`panic` lint; route 2 is the option Zed explicitly rejected. Zed's *keymap file* handling is the two-tier model this phase already commits to in D7: `KeymapFileLoadResult` (`crates/settings/src/keymap_file.rs:158`) is `Success` / `SomeFailedToLoad { key_bindings, error_message }` / `JsonParseFailure`, and `load_keymap` carries the comment "Accumulate errors in order to support partial load of user keymap in the presence of errors in context and binding parsing." Two further details taken from it: a bad **context predicate** skips only its own section and leaves every other section loaded (`:287-297`, the `continue`) — precedent for the global-routing decision below; and shipped defaults are held stricter than user files (`load_asset` at `:180` turns a partial failure into a hard error, while `load_asset_allow_partial_failure` at `:204` tolerates one unless **zero** bindings survived — a "never end up with nothing" floor).

**Acceptance:** a context enum missing one `#[strum(message)]` yields a diagnostic and a **running** app rather than a startup failure; a command declaration with an empty description does the same, with the shipped defaults reported as unresolved rather than fatal. The original decision text follows as the archive record.

**Decision B: whether registry *and condition* validation stay all-or-nothing**

Actual problem:
Phase 4b's `validate_declarations` returns `Err` if **any** registered command fails, and `initialize` returns `Result<Self, Vec<Diagnostic>>` — so a single third-party crate shipping a command with an empty description leaves the application with *no* command registry at all, and therefore no keybindings whatsoever. This phase is where that `Err` is first handled in a real plugin, so this is where the policy is set.

**Phase 5 shipped the same all-or-nothing shape for conditions, which sharpens the question.** `ConditionRegistry::register` returns `Err` if any variant is bad and leaves the registry **entirely unchanged** (`crates/hana_rubric/src/condition.rs:150`); both context plugins then skip adding their sync system (`condition.rs:239-244`, `:265-270`). One hana context variant missing its `#[strum(message)]` therefore yields an *empty* condition registry — so every `"context"` in the shipped default keymap fails to resolve, and this phase's rule that `finish` "separately rejects any **shipped default** that does not resolve" turns a one-word omission into an app that does not start. Whatever policy this decision picks must cover both registries, or they will diverge.

What exists now:
- `crates/hana_rubric/src/command/registry.rs` — `validate_declarations` collects every diagnostic and returns `Err(Vec<Diagnostic>)` if the vector is non-empty; there is no partial-success path.
- **After Phase 11b, the `ReflectEvent` requirement is validation-only and has no functional consumer.** `CommandDeclaration.reflect_event` (`command/registry.rs:226`) is read once by `validate_declarations` and dropped; dispatch goes through the monomorphized `fn(&mut World)` and never touches it. Record in this phase's diff **why** the requirement stays — stock BRP `world.trigger_event` and general reflection both need it — or a later reader deletes it as vestigial. `command!` always emits `#[reflect(Event, KeymapCommand)]`, so only a hand-written impl can trip this check.
- `crates/hana_rubric/src/condition.rs` — `register` is atomic by construction: it builds a local `declarations` vector and only extends the registry after the diagnostics-empty early return.
- `clippy::panic` is denied workspace-wide, so the `Err` arm cannot abort.
- This phase already commits to two-tier failure for *keymap files* (D7 above): whole-file failure keeps the last good state, per-binding failure applies the good ones. Registry validation currently has no equivalent tier.

What should change — two routes:
1. **Keep all-or-nothing, degrade loudly.** `finish` records the diagnostics in `KeymapLoadFailures`, logs at error level, and inserts an **empty** `CommandRegistry`. Every keymap binding then fails to resolve and is individually reported, and the app starts. Simple, and a malformed declaration is a compile-time-adjacent programming error rather than user data. **Requires softening the shipped-default rule**: a shipped default that fails to resolve must not hard-fail startup when the registry is empty *because* validation already failed — otherwise a single bad declaration is fatal. Report it and start.
2. **Skip the bad command, keep the rest.** `initialize` gains a partial-success return — the valid entries plus the diagnostics — mirroring D7's per-binding tier. One bad third-party command costs only that command. Requires a Phase 4b signature change made here, and the matching change to `ConditionRegistry::register`.

Recommendation:
Route 1 for both registries, plus the shipped-default softening named above, plus a test that a context enum missing one `#[strum(message)]` yields a diagnostic and a **running** app rather than a startup failure. Command and condition declarations are code, not user-editable data, so the failure is a developer's to fix immediately; D7's per-item tier exists because keymap *files* are edited by users mid-session, which does not apply here. Route 2 also lets a partly-broken registry look healthy.

Approve this direction, or modify it?

**Resolved decision (2026-07-31) — does a failed context registration also silence *global* bindings? YES. Today's behavior is kept deliberately: a failed context registration leaves input routing disabled entirely, global bindings included.** The recommendation to fall back to global-only routing was **rejected**. Rationale, recorded so a later pass does not relitigate it: the context enum is the application's own — it enters through `for_state_context::<C>()` / `for_resource_context::<C>()`, one per app, never contributed by a third-party plugin — so the failure is a developer's own authoring mistake, surfaced on the first launch after the enum changes. Being maximally guarded is acceptable at that cost. A partly-live keymap invites shipping the defect.

**Concrete consequences to implement and pin:**

- **No code change on the failure path.** `register_context` keeps calling `await_context()` unconditionally (`crates/hana_rubric/src/condition.rs:299-303`); nothing restores `is_initialized` on the `Err` path; `route_input` keeps returning at `crates/hana_rubric/src/keymap/runtime/dispatch.rs:41-43`. Do **not** add an `enable_global()` call there.
- **This overrides the wording in Decision B above.** "A diagnostic and a **running** app" means the process is alive and does not abort — it does **not** mean input-responsive. For the *context*-registration failure specifically, the app runs and routes nothing.
- **The recovery chord is unavailable in this state.** Accepted, on the same first-launch grounds.
- **The error-level log is the only signal, so it must name the offending variant.** Both failure causes already produce a `Diagnostic` carrying the variant name (`condition.rs:126-131` for a duplicate serialized name, `:146-152` for a missing or empty `#[strum(message)]`); route them to the retained collection per Phase 9's resolved contention rule and log at error level.
- **Acceptance (this pins the behavior rather than leaving it accidental):** a context enum with one variant lacking `#[strum(message = "…")]` yields a diagnostic, a process that starts, and **no dispatch at all** — the test asserts that a *global* binding does **not** fire. A second case covers the duplicate-serialized-name cause. Both compile cleanly, so the tests must construct the bad enum rather than expect a compile failure.

The original decision text follows as the archive record.

**Superseded recommendation — does a failed context registration also silence *global* bindings?**

Actual problem:
Phase 11c made this concrete and it is worse than Decision B's text assumes. `register_context` calls `ActiveCondition::await_context()` unconditionally before registering (`crates/hana_rubric/src/condition.rs:299-303`), which sets `is_initialized = false`. If `ConditionRegistry::register::<C>()` returns `Err`, neither context plugin adds its sync system (`condition.rs:264`, `:290`), so nothing ever sets it back — and `route_input` returns immediately at `keymap/runtime/dispatch.rs:41-43`. One context enum variant missing its `#[strum(message)]` therefore yields an application with **no keyboard input at all**, including bindings that have nothing to do with contexts.

What exists now:
- Decision B route 1 promises "a diagnostic and a running app". The app runs, but it is deaf.
- `ActiveCondition` has exactly one initialized bit and no way to express "context resolution failed, but global routing is still valid".

What should change:
- On a context-registration failure, call `enable_global()` rather than leaving `await_context()`'s cleared state in place, so `route_input` proceeds with `active_condition == None`. Context-scoped bindings do not resolve and are reported; global bindings keep working.
- The alternative is to keep today's behavior and say so explicitly in the gate, on the grounds that a half-working keymap is more confusing than none.

Recommendation:
Degrade to global-only routing. The failure is a developer-side authoring mistake in the context enum, and the recovery chord itself is a global binding — losing all input is the one outcome that removes the user's way out. Write it into this phase's spec next to the `KeymapLoadFailures` text, and extend the existing acceptance case ("a context enum missing one `#[strum(message)]` yields a diagnostic and a running app") to assert that a global binding still dispatches.

Approve this direction, or modify it?

**Acceptance gate:** `verify.sh check|test|lint hana_rubric` plus `verify.sh check fairy_dust` green — this phase edits `hana_rubric`'s crate root and prelude, and `fairy_dust` is the normal-dependency consumer where a broken re-export surfaces. **The five named acceptance cases each get a test:** reload while a key is held; remove or rebind that held action; **generation** change during a pending sequence; parse failure during a pending sequence; multiple aliases held for the same action. (Corrected after Phase 11c: the case was written as a *context* change, which 11c already covers in `keymap/runtime/dispatch.rs`. Retarget it at the transaction this phase owns.) Plus: `Plugin::finish` rejects a duplicate id and an unresolvable shipped default; a whole-file parse failure leaves the generation, pending sequence, and held actions untouched; a partial failure commits defaults plus every valid edit and reports the rest; the durable diagnostic resource is populated and readable after each. Plus, from the constraints above: **a clean keymap load leaves a startup context-registration diagnostic in place** (it is never regenerated, so clearing it loses it permanently); a value set through `with_app_name` survives the terminal `for_*_context` call and is observable after `build`; calling both context-source methods is rejected rather than silently double-registering; and a context enum missing one `#[strum(message)]` yields a diagnostic and a process that starts, not a startup failure — and, per the resolved decision above, routes **no** input at all: the test asserts a **global** binding does *not* dispatch, pinning that behavior rather than leaving it accidental. A second case covers the duplicate-serialized-name cause of the same failure. **Plus protected-keystroke accumulation:** two distinct keystrokes registered through chained `.with_protected_keystroke(a).with_protected_keystroke(b)` calls are **each** rejected when a user binding names them, proving the calls accumulate rather than overwrite. **Plus the generated reference header:** every registered condition appears in the comment block with its message text and the protected chord is named; the embedded default text that follows the header is byte-identical to the asset; and the whole file still parses as JSONC through `KeymapDocument::parse`.

### Retrospective

**What worked:**
- The transaction is an exclusive system taking `&mut World`. `CompiledKeymap` has no `Default`, so a `ResMut` system would never have run before the first commit and the app would have routed nothing forever.
- Both failure tiers key off `from_sources`'s `Result` arms, not `DiagnosticKind` — both tiers emit `Syntax`, so a `kind`-based split would have been silently wrong.
- `install_runtime`'s guard survived the restructure returning *before* `enable_global()`, which is what stops a later bare `add_plugins(KeymapPlugin)` re-enabling global routing over a context plugin's `await_context()`.

**What deviated from the plan:**
- **The Work Order's Files list was incomplete.** The Spec requires the root-member advisory work in `keymap/document.rs`, the null-context advisory in `keymap/merged.rs`, and the lint sweep across `keymap/runtime/`; none were listed. A review finding read the resulting diff as out-of-scope. The Files list is corrected below; the Spec governed.
- **Startup order had to change to satisfy an older constraint.** As first implemented, `finish_assembly` returned when `KeymapPaths::new` yielded `None`, before `commit_defaults` — leaving an app with no config directory routing nothing, contradicting the standing rule that embedded defaults "must load with no config directory at all". Defaults now commit before app name, paths, schema, and worker are touched, with `EMBEDDED_DEFAULTS_SOURCE` as the source label.
- **`allow_default_failures` needed a dedicated marker.** Deriving it from "any retained diagnostic exists" let a schema-generation or disk failure disable strict shipped-default validation. A `RegistryValidationFailed` resource, inserted only by `CommandRegistry::initialize`'s `Err` arm and `register_context`'s failure path, now carries it.
- **`CommandRegistry` did not gain `Default`.** The empty-registry fallback is `pub(crate) fn empty()`; a public `Default` would have handed downstream applications a constructor that skips declaration validation, held-input allocation, and observer registration.

**Surprises:**
- Assertion integrity failed again, in six places, despite the Work Order naming the defect class and listing seven must-fail-if-deleted checks. The two "app goes deaf on a bad context" tests asserted only that dispatch stayed zero — a result an app whose `finish` bailed out entirely produces identically. The add-order test never asserted the one property its guard exists for. The whole-file-failure test created no held state, so it could not observe held actions being cleared.
- `RECOGNIZED_ROOT_MEMBERS` was declared and then not used by the loader, which hard-coded the same two names. The schema-vs-loader agreement test the Spec asked for would have stayed green through any drift.
- An unrestricted `#[strum(message)]` containing a newline emits uncommented text into the published defaults and makes the file unparsable; only the first line was being comment-prefixed.

**Implications for remaining phases:**
- `hana_rubric` is now assembled end to end: `Plugin::finish` builds the registries, publishes companion files, starts the disk worker, and commits the first generation; `commit_reload` runs before routing every frame. Phase 13's example wires an app on top of a working system, not a partial one.
- `KeymapLoadFailures` carries two collections. `diagnostics` is regenerated per load and partially cleared; `retained_diagnostics` holds build-time failures no later load can reproduce and is never cleared. Any consumer wanting the current problem set reads both.
- `pub fn cancel_pending_sequences(&mut World)` is public at the crate root and in the prelude — the recovery entry point later phases call.
- `EMBEDDED_DEFAULTS_SOURCE` is the source label stamped on diagnostics produced before path resolution; a UI listing problems by file will see it.

### Phase 12 Review

- **Phase 13** — the "state which install shape" paragraph carried over from Phase 11c described an install that does not exist. Replaced with the corrected minimum wiring: a bare `add_plugins(KeymapPlugin)` builds nothing (`install` inserts `KeymapPluginConfiguration` only when `has_configuration()` is true, and `finish_assembly` returns when it is absent or `defaults` is `None`, `keymap_plugin.rs:136-152`), so `with_app_name` is mandatory alongside `with_defaults` and `for_state_context::<C>()`.
- **Phase 13 Files** — added the embedded keymap asset (`crates/hana_rubric/examples/<name>.jsonc`, which has no home yet and must be named) and `crates/hana_rubric/tests/`.
- **Phase 13 Constraints** — recorded that `ENVIRONMENT_LOCK`, `TestDirectory`, and `XdgConfigHome` are `#[cfg(test)] pub(crate)` in `disk/paths.rs:105-165` and therefore invisible to a `tests/` binary; and that `cancel_pending_sequences` ships unexercised, so the example must bind it and one crate-level test must cover it.
- **Phase 14** — dropped the stale "if this phase runs before that relaxation lands" clause; the relaxation shipped (`sync_state_condition` takes `Option<Res<State<C>>>` at `condition.rs:206-212`). Also corrected "leaves `ActiveCondition` at its last value": before the first resolve there is no last value, so nothing routes — which is what Decision C already required.
- **Phases 13/16/19/20** — the prelude inventory said 25 types; it is 26 entries and no longer types-only, since it carries the free function `cancel_pending_sequences`.
- **Phase 16** — its held-prefix acceptance bullet was propagated into Phase 19's gate so the two cannot drift.
- **Phase 18 gate** — added two qualifications: the shipped-default rejection is suppressed while `RegistryValidationFailed` is present; and a failing shipped default returns at `keymap_plugin.rs:185-187` *before* paths, schema, and worker, so that run writes no `keymap.jsonc`, no `keymap.default.jsonc`, and no `keymap.schema.json`.
- **Phase 18 Files** — must enumerate the 23 `bind_action_system!` call sites per file, naming the known homes, rather than leaving the count as a total.
- **Phase 19** — replaced the `platform_shortcut_mode.rs` deletion instruction with **do not delete**: `keybindings.rs` stays, and `PlatformShortcutMode` has two live consumers at `command/keybindings.rs:86` and `:117`. Also added the empty-registry rule — when command declaration validation fails, `CommandRegistry::custom_input()` returns `None` for both held commands, so skip the `Binding::Custom` spawn; do not synthesize an id and do not panic.
- **Phase 20** — added the exclusive-system constraint (`fn(&mut World)` reading `world.resource::<ButtonInput<KeyCode>>()`, ordered `.before(KeymapSystems::Route)`) and the fact that `with_protected_keystroke` is inert unless `with_defaults` is also called.
- **Phase 21** — the error surface must read both diagnostic collections through `all_diagnostics()` (`diagnostic.rs:99-100`), since context-registration failures live only in the retained half; `source_path` is not always a file path, so the `EMBEDDED_DEFAULTS_SOURCE` sentinel maps to `KeymapPaths::default_keymap()` for display and open/reveal; and the command invocation signature is `pub fn invoke(&self, command_id: &CommandId, world: &mut World) -> Option<()>` (`registry.rs:173`), which forces a `world.resource_scope::<CommandRegistry, _>`.
- **Phase 22** — `crates/hana_rubric/Cargo.toml` already carries every metadata field the step asks for, and `README.md` exists. Two real blockers remain: `version.workspace = true` must become a literal version (as `crates/bevy_kana/Cargo.toml:12` does), and `homepage` points at the wrong repository.
- **User decision — Phase 13 is split into 13a and 13b** (approved 2026-07-31). It owned four independently verifiable deliverables against a **Files** list naming two paths. **13a** builds the example app, its embedded `.jsonc` asset, the `Cargo.toml` example target, the `cancel_pending_sequences` binding, and the `tests/` read-back harness. **13b** adds the JSON-Schema validator dev-dependency and its draft-07 + misspelled-member tests, then the manual Zed/VS Code pass. 13b depends on 13a and cannot run first. The split isolates the one deliverable that cannot be automated — the editor pass — from everything that can, so a delegate never blocks on it.
- **User decision — the description-coverage route resolved to route 1** (read the published file back), carried by the approved 13a description. The pending-decision block is removed and 13a's Spec now says: run the example under a temp `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME`, read back `keymap.schema.json`, and assert a described completion for every command and condition it registers. Route 2 (moving the example's declarations into `src/`) was not taken — it re-tests the generator Phase 8 already covers and puts example-only code in the library.
- **Rejected — the implementation did not exceed its file boundary.** A blind-review finding claimed the Phase 12 patch touched files outside the Work Order's **Files** list (`command/registry.rs`, `keymap/{document,merged,compiled}.rs`, `keymap/runtime/*`). Phase 12's **Spec** explicitly requires the root-member advisory work in `document.rs`, the null-context advisory in `merged.rs`, and the lint sweep across `keymap/runtime/`. The Files list was incomplete and the Spec governs; none of that work was reverted. Do not relitigate.

### Phase 13a — `hana_rubric`: the example app and its published companions · status: done (869bb7ef)

#### Work Order

**Constraint from Phase 12 — the minimum wiring, corrected.** A bare `add_plugins(KeymapPlugin)` builds **nothing**: `install` inserts `KeymapPluginConfiguration` only when `has_configuration()` is true, and `finish_assembly` returns immediately when that resource is absent or when `defaults` is `None` (`crates/hana_rubric/src/keymap_plugin.rs:136-152`). No registry, no compiled keymap, no worker. The earlier "global-only vs context" choice is void — the global-only shape does not exist as a working install.

**`with_app_name` is also mandatory here, not optional.** Without it `finish_assembly` records a `DiagnosticKind::Disk` diagnostic and returns *before* `KeymapPaths::new`, `schema_bytes`, and `start_disk_worker` (`keymap_plugin.rs:189-199`), so `keymap.schema.json` and `keymap.default.jsonc` are never written and all three manual editor bullets are unreachable. The example's minimum wiring is:

```rust
KeymapPlugin::new()
    .with_app_name("…")
    .with_defaults(include_str!("…"))
    .for_state_context::<C>()
```

The context path is the one to demonstrate — it is the only shape that exercises the context half of the system. Say so in the example's own doc comment.

**Goal:** a real downstream app exists — it wires nothing but the public builder surface, publishes its companion files, and a test reads those files back.

**Spec:**

**No benchmarks.** The input-path budget — no heap allocation, string parsing, reflection lookup, or full-keymap scan; inspect only current prefix candidates on a key edge; check the timeout only while a sequence is pending — is a *structural* property, and Phases 11 and 12 assert it directly with allocation counts. A wall-clock number nobody acts on is not worth a bench target, a `criterion` dev-dependency, or the `bench_support` feature that would be needed to reach the crate-private routing types. Phase 3 therefore adds no `[features]` section.

**The manual editor pass is Phase 13b's, not this phase's.** This phase produces the artifacts that pass points an editor at; it does not perform it.

Build a tiny example app in `crates/hana_rubric/examples/` that registers a handful of commands and one context enum, runs once to publish the companion files, and gives the manual check something real to point an editor at. The example doubles as the crate's documentation.

**Description coverage — assert it against the published file, not the generator.** `schema_bytes` is `pub(crate)` and `crates/hana_rubric/examples/` compiles against the public API only, so no example-side test can reach the generator; Phase 8 already covers the generator with `every_user_bindable_command_and_condition_has_a_described_completion`. What is unproven is that the bytes survive publication and land where an editor will find them.

**The test cannot execute the example binary.** Cargo sets `CARGO_BIN_EXE_<name>` for `[[bin]]` targets only; there is no such variable for an example, and an integration test cannot link one either. Do not shell out to cargo from a test to work around this.

Instead, the integration test in `crates/hana_rubric/tests/` builds an app through the **same public builder the example uses**, declaring its own commands and context enum and embedding the **same** keymap asset via `include_str!("../examples/<name>.jsonc")`. It points `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME` at a temp directory, calls `finish()`, drops the app so the worker publishes and joins, then reads back the `keymap.schema.json` it wrote and asserts **every command it registered appears with a non-empty description, and every condition appears with its message text**. That proves the public path end to end — generate, publish, land on disk readable — which is what is unproven. The example itself is covered by `verify.sh example hana_rubric <name>` and by Phase 13b's hand-run editor pass. `Unremappable` commands are excluded by design — Phase 8 does not emit them, because Phase 7 rejects a file that names one. The test must fail if publication is skipped or truncated, not merely if the generator regresses.

**Files:**
- `crates/hana_rubric/examples/` — create the demonstration app
- `crates/hana_rubric/examples/<name>.jsonc` (or an `assets/` sibling) — **the embedded default keymap the example passes to `include_str!`. It has no home yet; name one.** Without it `with_defaults` has nothing to take and the phase cannot start.
- `crates/hana_rubric/tests/` — the companion read-back harness
- `crates/hana_rubric/Cargo.toml` — example target and dev-dependencies

**Constraints from prior phases:**
- Phase 12 delivered the full pipeline behind `KeymapPlugin`; the example wires exactly the public builder surface a downstream app would.
- **Phase 12's test-isolation helpers do not reach an integration test.** `ENVIRONMENT_LOCK`, `TestDirectory`, and `XdgConfigHome` are `#[cfg(test)] pub(crate)` in `crates/hana_rubric/src/disk/paths.rs:105-165`; `pub(crate)` does not cross into a `tests/` binary and `#[cfg(test)]` is off for the lib when integration tests build. A read-back test in `tests/` must re-implement its own `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME` guard, or move into `src/` and reuse the existing helpers. Note separately that the **example itself**, run for the manual editor pass, deliberately writes into the developer's real config directory — that is the point of the pass, not a leak.
- **`cancel_pending_sequences` ships unexercised.** Phase 12 made `pub fn cancel_pending_sequences(world: &mut World)` public at the crate root and in the prelude for Phase 20, which lands in a different repository behind gate `G2`. No `hana_rubric` gate calls it. Bind it in the example and add one crate-level test so the public entry point is covered inside this workspace.
- Phase 9's companion publication writes `keymap.default.jsonc` and `keymap.schema.json` at `0444` under `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME` (or `~/.config/<app>`); the app name is a plugin parameter, so the example uses its own.
- Phase 10's first-launch stub is created only if absent and never overwritten, and Phase 10 already tests it — this phase does not re-test it.
- **A read-back test needs no sleep and is not flaky.** Phase 10's worker publishes the companion files *before* entering its watch loop, and `Drop for DiskWorkerChannels` sends `Stop` and joins the worker thread (`crates/hana_rubric/src/disk/worker/channels.rs`). So by the time the example app has run and dropped its plugin resources, `keymap.default.jsonc` and `keymap.schema.json` are on disk and complete. Do **not** add a sleep, a retry loop, or a polling wait, and do not weaken the assertion on suspicion of flakiness — if a read-back fails, the publication genuinely failed.
- **Gate commands: `verify.sh example hana_rubric <name>` belongs to this phase only**, because this is the phase whose Files own the example.

- **A `command!` call site needs five names imported unqualified**, not four: `InputAction`, `Event`, `Reflect`, `ReflectEvent`, and `ReflectKeymapCommand`. The expansion writes `#[reflect(Event, KeymapCommand)]`, so bevy's derive forms *both* `ReflectEvent` and `ReflectKeymapCommand` by concatenation in the caller's scope. **`hana_rubric::prelude::*` does not cover this** — 26 entries as of Phase 12, which also made the prelude no longer types-only: it now carries the free function `cancel_pending_sequences` alongside `ActiveCondition`, `Capability`, `CommandId`, `CommandInfo`, `CommandRegistry`, `ConditionName`, `Diagnostic`, `DiagnosticKind`, `DiagnosticSeverity`, `HoldPhase`, `Keybindings`, `KeymapCommand`, `KeymapContext`, `KeymapLoadFailures`, `KeymapPlugin`, `KeymapSystems`, `Keystroke`, `KeystrokeSequence`, `MatchOutcome`, `Modifiers`, `ReflectKeymapCommand`, `SequenceMatcher`, and the three keystroke error types — but **not** the `command!` macro and **not** `Event`/`Reflect`/`ReflectEvent`/`InputAction`. Write the five imports explicitly.
- **`command!`'s keys are positional and fixed**: `action`, `event`, `id`, `title`, `description`, and optionally `capability` — in that order, `capability` last. It is `macro_rules!` arm matching, not a keyword parser; a key out of order produces an opaque "no rules expected this token" error. Omitting `capability` yields `Capability::OneShot`. **`capability: Held` is a deliberate `compile_error!`** — a held command is declared with the leading `held,` marker and no `capability:` key, which is what gives its event the `phase: HoldPhase` field. `capability: Capability::Held` matches no arm at all (the generic arm takes a bare ident).
- **Every `KeymapCommand` impl must satisfy `Event + Reflect` and implement **both** `fn hold_phase(&self) -> Option<HoldPhase>` **and Phase 11b's `fn build() -> Self`**, and `#[reflect(KeymapCommand)]` additionally requires `for<'a> <Self as Event>::Trigger<'a>: Default` (`command/reflect_command.rs:53-57`) — a hard compile error, not a silent skip** (`crates/hana_rubric/src/command/reflect_command.rs:15`). `command!` generates the method — non-held forms return `None`, the `held,` form returns `Some(self.phase)`. A hand-written impl that omits it fails to compile; that is deliberate, so a held command cannot register as silently inert.

**Acceptance gate:** `verify.sh check|test|lint hana_rubric` green plus `verify.sh example hana_rubric <name>` **and `verify.sh test hana_rubric <integration_test>`**. That last line is not optional: `verify.sh test <package>` pins `--lib`/`--bins` targets, so an integration test in `tests/` compiles under the lint gate but **never runs** unless it is named explicitly. Without it, deleting the worker's publication call passes the phase gate. The read-back test builds an app through the public builder under a temp `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME`, finds both `keymap.default.jsonc` and `keymap.schema.json`, and asserts a described completion for every command and every condition it registers. One crate-level test in `src/` exercises `cancel_pending_sequences` through the public `crate::cancel_pending_sequences` path.

### Retrospective

**What worked:**
- The example needs **no `[dev-dependencies]` at all**. Examples, tests, and benches link the package's `[dependencies]`, so the crate's feature-pinned `bevy` (`bevy_log`, `bevy_state`, `keyboard`, `std`) already covers both the example and the integration test. `MinimalPlugins.set(ScheduleRunnerPlugin::run_once())` + `StatesPlugin` runs one pass and exits — `run()` calls `finish()`, and `Drop for DiskWorkerChannels` joins the worker, so a single pass publishes both companion files.
- The read-back test asserts against the enums rather than literals: it iterates `DemoContext::iter()` reading each `get_message()`, and matches each command's published description against `<Event as KeymapCommand>::DESCRIPTION`. Adding a command or context cannot silently escape coverage.

**What deviated from the plan:**
- The first implementation used `DefaultPlugins` and added `bevy = { features = ["default"] }` to dev-dependencies — the only `features = ["default"]` in a workspace whose root sets `default-features = false` and whose nine crates each pin explicit lists. It also never exited. Both were corrected in the fix pass.

**Surprises:**
- **`verify.sh test <package>` pins `--lib`/`--bins`, so an integration test in `tests/` compiles under the lint gate and never runs.** The phase's entire verification deliverable was unexecuted until the explicit target line was added. Any future phase adding a `tests/` target must name it in its gate.
- A temp directory built from `std::process::id()` and created with `create_dir_all` is not fresh — PIDs are reused, so stale companion files from an aborted run could satisfy every assertion with publication deleted. The test now removes any existing directory, uses `create_dir`, and asserts both companion paths are absent before the app is built.

**Implications for remaining phases:**
- `crates/hana_rubric/examples/keymap_demo.rs` + `keymap_demo.jsonc` are the reference wiring any later phase or downstream migration copies.
- `crates/hana_rubric/tests/keymap_companion_files.rs` owns its own `ENVIRONMENT_LOCK`, `TemporaryDirectory`, and `XdgConfigHome` — the `src/` versions remain unreachable from `tests/`.
- The crate still has **no `[dev-dependencies]` section**; Phase 13b creates it for the schema validator.

### Phase 13b — `hana_rubric`: real schema validation and the editor pass · status: done (7a7b9f77) · editor pass Zed only, VS Code declined

#### Work Order

**Goal:** the generated schema is validated by an actual JSON Schema implementation rather than a hand-written partial checker, and the whole thing is confirmed working in two real editors.

**Spec:**

**Real schema validation belongs here, and nowhere earlier.** Phase 8 could not validate its output against a real JSON Schema validator — no such crate was in the dependency set and that phase forbade a manifest change — so its tests use a hand-written partial validator and the gap was recorded rather than closed. Phase 13a's **Files** opened `crates/hana_rubric/Cargo.toml`'s dev-dependencies; this phase adds a draft-07-capable validator there (`jsonschema` is the obvious candidate — confirm draft-07 support before committing to it) and two tests:

1. the schema `schema_bytes` generates is itself a **valid draft-07 document** under that validator;
2. a keymap document with a **misspelled root member and one with a misspelled block member** each fail validation under it, while the shipped defaults pass.

Point (2) is the one that matters: Phase 12 made `RECOGNIZED_ROOT_MEMBERS` the single loader authority and `block_schema_members_match_the_loader_authority` proves schema and loader agree, but nothing yet proves the schema a *third-party validator* reads actually rejects the misspellings. A test that only exercises the crate's own loader re-proves Phase 12, not this.

**The editor pass is human work — the delegate does not perform it and must not claim to.** The delegate's deliverable for this phase is the validator dev-dependency and the two tests above, nothing else. The manual pass is run by the user afterwards and recorded in this phase's retrospective by the orchestrator.

**Editor acceptance (manual, recorded in this phase's retrospective).** Fresh profile in **both Zed and VS Code**, pointed at the config directory the Phase 13a example writes when run normally:
- the relative `"$schema": "./keymap.schema.json"` resolves,
- completions work with **no workspace settings**,
- comments produce **no diagnostics** in the `.jsonc` file.

The manual pass does **not** cover "a repeat launch does not overwrite the user stub" — Phase 10 shipped that as an automated test (`stub_is_created_once_and_never_overwritten`, `disk/worker/runtime.rs`, built on `OpenOptions::create_new(true)`, covering the zero-byte-existing-file case too). Only the three editor-dependent bullets above need a human.

**Files:**
- `crates/hana_rubric/Cargo.toml` — the validator dev-dependency
- `crates/hana_rubric/src/keymap/schema.rs` — the two validation tests, alongside the existing generator tests (they need `pub(crate) fn schema_bytes`, so they live in `src/`, not `tests/`)

**Constraints from prior phases:**
- **This phase depends on 13a and cannot run before it.** The manual pass points an editor at the files 13a's example publishes, and the dev-dependency section 13a creates is the one this phase extends.
- **`schema_bytes` is `pub(crate)`.** Both tests must live inside `src/` — an integration test in `tests/` cannot reach the generator. This is the mirror of 13a's constraint, where the read-back test must live in `tests/` because it runs the example binary.
- **Phase 12's test-isolation rules still apply** to anything that calls `app.finish()`: set both the app name and `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME` to a temp directory, assert the resolved path is inside it, and hold `ENVIRONMENT_LOCK` (`crates/hana_rubric/src/disk/paths.rs:105-165`). The two validation tests should not need `finish()` at all — they call `schema_bytes` directly.
- **`verify.sh example hana_rubric <name>` belongs to 13a**, not here; this phase's Files do not own the example.

**Acceptance gate:** `verify.sh check|test|lint hana_rubric` green. The generated schema validates as a draft-07 document under a real validator; a document with a misspelled root member and one with a misspelled block member each fail under it; the shipped defaults pass. The manual editor check is performed in both Zed and VS Code on a fresh profile and its result recorded in this phase's retrospective.

#### Retrospective

**Editor acceptance — Zed passed, VS Code not tested (user declined; VS Code is not installed).** Run against `~/.config/keymap_demo`, the directory `cargo run --example keymap_demo` publishes, opened as the workspace root with no workspace settings of any kind:

- **The relative `"$schema": "./keymap.schema.json"` resolves.** Proven by diagnostics that could only come from the generated schema — an invalid `"context"` value reports `Value is not accepted. Valid values: "browsing", "editing"`, which is the condition registry's contents.
- **Completions work with no workspace settings.** All four `demo::` command ids offered at a binding value.
- **Comments produce no diagnostics.** A `//` line in the `.jsonc` file is clean. This is the bullet Phase 18 depends on, since hana's shipped default is authored with explanatory comments.
- **A misspelled block member is flagged** — `"contxt"` squiggles in the editor, which is the human-visible half of what `misspelled_block_member_fails_draft_seven_validation` proves at the schema level.
- Not a defect: `"context": ""` also squiggles, because the empty string is not one of the two registered contexts.

The VS Code half of the original gate is **not** satisfied and is not planned. Anything downstream that assumed two-editor coverage — Phase 18 in particular — rests on Zed alone.

**Open item, no decision yet — the shipped defaults are still not validated by the real validator.** This phase's gate claimed "the shipped defaults pass", but `correct_document_passes_draft_seven_validation` (`crates/hana_rubric/src/keymap/schema.rs`) uses a five-line hand-written document, not `examples/keymap_demo.jsonc`, and the one test that reads a generated schema off disk (`crates/hana_rubric/tests/keymap_companion_files.rs`) checks descriptions structurally and never validates. The close is small: validate `include_str!("../examples/keymap_demo.jsonc")` against the published schema inside that integration test, where `jsonschema` is already available as a dev-dependency. Offered to the user at the Phase 14 gate and not answered there. Note that `verify.sh test hana_rubric` pins `--lib`/`--bins`, so the only gate that would run it is `verify.sh final` in Phase 24 — name the target explicitly wherever it lands.

**What worked:**
- `jsonschema` 0.49 with `default-features = false` is the right shape for this: it drops HTTP/file `$ref` resolution and the TLS stack, which a local reference-free generated schema never needs, and keeps `jsonschema::draft7::new`.
- The three new documents are genuinely falsifiable, verified against the generator rather than assumed: root (`schema.rs:87`) and block (`schema.rs:171`) each mark **only** `bindings` required and `context` is optional, so `bindngs` and `contxt` are each rejected by nothing but `additionalProperties: false`. Removing either closure makes the corresponding test fail — which is exactly what the deleted structural assertions used to check, now proven behaviorally.

**What deviated from the plan:**
- The Work Order said two tests; four shipped — draft-07 compilation, misspelled root, misspelled block, and a correct document that passes. The fourth is what keeps the two rejection tests from passing merely because the validator rejects everything.
- The hand-written `validate_against_schema` checker was deleted rather than left alongside the real validator. That was the point of the phase, but it also silently dropped the one caller that mattered — see below.

**Surprises:**
- **`jsonschema` operates on `serde_json::Value`; this crate parses with `serde_json_lenient::Value`.** They are distinct types, so the first implementation did not compile — four `E0308`s at `schema.rs:347/466/489/513`. The resolution is two conversions in the test module only: test documents deserialize through the comment-tolerant reader straight into the strict type (`serde_json_lenient::from_str::<serde_json::Value>`, the same shape the production loader uses at `document.rs:42`), and the generated schema converts once inside the validator helper via `serde_json::to_value`. `serde_json` is therefore a **required** dev-dependency here, unlike in 13a where it was redundant.
- **`cargo check` does not build `#[cfg(test)]` code**, so a `check`-green tree can have a test module that does not compile at all. The delegate reported all five gates passing on a tree whose `test` gate exited 101. Verifying the gate independently rather than trusting the report is what caught it.
- Deleting a hand-rolled checker deletes its call sites too. `null_is_a_described_binding_tombstone` lost the document it used to validate and was left asserting only that the schema text contains "inert" — a structural fact with no behavioral proof that a null-valued binding is accepted. Restored through the real validator.

**Implications for remaining phases:**
- `crates/hana_rubric/Cargo.toml` now has a `[dev-dependencies]` section carrying `jsonschema` and `serde_json`, both `.workspace = true`; `jsonschema` is newly declared in the root `[workspace.dependencies]` at `Cargo.toml:82`.
- The crate's library test count is **160**, plus one integration test target (`keymap_companion_files`). Any later phase touching `hana_rubric` treats a drop below that as a regression.
- Any future test that hands a crate-built `Value` to a third-party JSON tool needs the same conversion; the two helpers to copy are `draft_seven_validator` and `jsonc_document` in `schema.rs`'s test module.

### Phase 13b Review

- **Phase 14's code sketch could not compile.** `bevy_state 0.19.0` has a blanket `impl<S: ComputedStates> States for S`, so deriving `States` on a type that also implements `ComputedStates` is a coherence error. The derive list lost `States`, the sketch gained the `ComputedStates` impl and `add_computed_state`. Fixed rather than raised — an impossible design is a mistake, not a decision.
- **Phase 14's claim that the uninitialized window is "empty in practice" was factually wrong** and is now corrected in place: `add_computed_state` never inserts `State<S>`, so the boot window during which hana routes nothing is real. Phases 17–20's input tests inherit the frame-advance requirement.
- **Phase 14 could not satisfy its own gate** — it demanded a compile-time assertion against `KeymapContext` while hana has no `hana_rubric` dependency until Phase 15 adds it. The assertion moved to Phase 15's gate; the phases were not reordered.
- **Gate hole closed across every hana phase (14–17):** hana is a bin-only package, so `verify.sh test hana` pins `--lib`/`--bins` and its two existing integration targets (`bei_chord_overlap`, `cursor_integration_tests`) compile under nothing and run under nothing — during the very phases that rewrite chord handling. Both are now named explicitly in each gate, and each phase carries the constraint that its own new tests live in `src/`, not `tests/`.
- **Phase 15's test proved neither mechanism the phase is named for.** Constructing a `Keystroke` passes identically whether the graph holds one `bevy_kana` instance or three and whether auto-registration is on or off. Replaced with two falsifiable tests, plus the observation that `reflect_auto_register` is already on `bevy_app`/`bevy_ecs` but absent from the `bevy` facade's feature list, so "turn it on" may be a no-op the old gate could never have detected.
- **Phase 16's gate passed on a completely broken registry** — `finish_assembly` inserts an empty `CommandRegistry` and lets the app start when validation fails, and the failure type is `pub(crate)`. The gate now asserts the declaration count and an empty diagnostic list, with visibility widening in scope. The "32" in the phase title is stale; `init/hana_catalyst` has 33.
- **Phase 18's schema clause had no implementable route** and now names one: `schema_bytes` is `pub(crate)`, so hana reads the published schema off disk, and hana's manifest needs both the validator and the comment-tolerant parser as dev-dependencies plus Phase 13b's two conversion helpers. Its **Files** gained hana's manifests. Its gate also absorbed the failure-case correction its own Constraints had called for — "startup succeeds" is true even when every default rejection was suppressed.
- **Phase 18 now depends on the outstanding two-editor pass**, since its shipped default is authored around comments; a negative result changes that file.
- **Phases 19, 19, and 21 had structural-only or check-only assertions** re-pointed at behavior: an `rg` for source text became a behavioral assertion; counting the protected list became binding a second keystroke and confirming it is accepted; `cargo build` in both consumers became `cargo check --all-targets` plus `cargo test`, because `cargo build` does not compile test code and Phase 22 performs an import swap across tested modules.
- **Phase 22's manifest inventory** records the new `[dev-dependencies]` section and the `jsonschema` workspace entry.
- **Deferred to Phase 14 — whether hana boots deaf.** A computed state whose `compute` returns `None` leaves hana with no keyboard input at all, globals included, and the plan currently permits a partial `compute`. Recommendation is a resting variant making it total; recorded as a `**Pending decision:**` block in Phase 14's Work Order.

### Phase 14 — hana: `InteractionContext` · status: done (`205f40a`, in `hana_tool_graph` on `init/hana_catalyst`)

**Blocked by:** nothing — `G1` is superseded. Work on `init/hana_catalyst` in `/Users/natemccoy/rust/hana_tool_graph`. **Reconciled against that branch 2026-07-31** — every file path, line reference, and type name below was verified there; the `ComputedStates` → `States` correction and the scope decision both came out of that pass.

#### Work Order

**Goal:** the context enum the whole condition system depends on exists in code for the first time.

**Spec:**

**It does not exist anywhere today.** `hana/docs/hana/computed_state.md` is committed architecture, but the enum lives **only in that document** — no code in the tree references it. D3's condition names, load-time validation, schema generation, and per-context compiled matchers all depend on it.

Create `hana/crates/hana/src/input/interaction_context.rs` holding **`InteractionContext` and the one system that derives it.**

**It is an ordinary `States` enum, not a `ComputedStates` — reconciled against `init/hana_catalyst` 2026-07-31.** `ComputedStates::SourceStates` accepts only other **states**, and hana has exactly one state family: `HanaState` plus its `ReadyPhase` sub-state (`crates/hana/src/hana_state.rs:24,35`), both app-lifecycle. Selection, dimension lock, and the rotation gizmo are **ECS components** (`DimensionLock`, `RotateAffordance`, `Expanded`) with no state mirror anywhere in the tree, so there is nothing for a `compute` to read. The only way to keep `ComputedStates` would be to invent one intermediate `States` enum per component fact and drive each with its own system — three types and two systems where one of each does the job, and precisely the speculative framework `computed_state.md` says not to build. A plain `States` satisfies `for_state_context::<C>()`'s `C: States` bound identically.

```rust
#[derive(Copy, Clone, Eq, PartialEq, Hash, Debug, Default, States, EnumIter, AsRefStr, EnumMessage)]
#[strum(serialize_all = "snake_case")]
pub enum InteractionContext {
    /// Nothing engaged — the ordinary state.
    #[default]
    #[strum(message = "…")]
    Resting,
    /// A dimension lock is engaged and the rotation gizmo is expanded.
    #[strum(message = "…")]
    DimensionLockRotation,
}
```

Register with `app.init_state::<InteractionContext>()`, which inserts `State<InteractionContext>` immediately — there is no uninitialized window and no `None` to survive. Totality is structural: a plain state always holds a value.

- **Scope is the one context `computed_state.md` seeds, and nothing more (user decision, 2026-07-31).** That document is headed *"Status: ARCHITECTURE DIRECTION — not yet built"*, states it "deliberately does not commit to a framework up front", and closes with *"Do not build the framework speculatively. Seed the pattern with the first feature that needs it."* It names exactly one concrete context — **dimension-lock rotation: a lock is engaged and the rotation gizmo is shown** — gating `B` (coupling toggle) and `P` (freeze). It names **no other variants**, so this phase invents none. Its listed prerequisite — coupling stored as a field on `DimensionLock` rather than derived — **already shipped**: `Coupling::{Pinned, Carried}` at `crates/hana/src/selection/dimension_lock/lock_state.rs:91-110`, the field at `:136`, toggled by `lock_state::toggle_coupling_command` (`:261`). Later phases add variants as they migrate the shortcuts that need them.
- **The derivation system** runs in `PreUpdate` and writes `NextState<InteractionContext>` only when the derived value differs from `State<InteractionContext>`. It sets `DimensionLockRotation` when a `DimensionLock` component exists **and** a `RotateAffordance` carries `Expanded`, otherwise `Resting`. `B` and `P` stay bound as they are today (`input/global_shortcuts.rs:97,99`); rebinding them to the context is Phase 17's work, not this phase's.
- **Both source types need a crate-visible re-export.** `dimension_lock` and `rotate` are private modules of `selection` and `crates/hana/src/selection/mod.rs` re-exports neither type (it re-exports `Rotating` at `:36`, not `Expanded`). Add `pub(crate) use dimension_lock::DimensionLock;` and `pub(crate) use rotate::Expanded;` there; `rotate/mod.rs:9` already re-exports `Expanded` from its `affordance` module.
- **Every variant needs `#[strum(message = "…")]`.** A missing one is caught by startup validation, not the compiler.
- The enum shape carries D3's mutual-exclusivity guarantee **structurally** — one variant is active by construction, not by assumption.
- **It needs no Bevy reflection.** `EnumInfo::variant_names()` is not used and `reflect_documentation` is not needed for condition descriptions.
- Hana takes the **state** path (R8) — `for_state_context::<C>()`, not `for_context::<C>()`. The enum is a Bevy state; it is simply a directly-driven one rather than a computed one, for the reason given above.

**This is the one hana-side phase with no collision against `init/hana_catalyst`** — it is an entirely new file. If catalyst stalls, it may be pulled forward on its own branch; nothing else here can be.

**Files:**
- `crates/hana/src/input/interaction_context.rs` — create
- `crates/hana/src/input/mod.rs` — declare the module (its `mod` list is `constants`, `escape`, `global_shortcuts`, `shift_diagnostics` at `:1-4`), re-export `InteractionContext`, and register both the state and the derivation system in `EnhancedInputAppPlugin::build` (`:48-64`)
- `crates/hana/src/selection/mod.rs` — the two `pub(crate) use` re-exports named above
- `Cargo.toml` (workspace root) — `strum = { version = "0.28.0", features = ["derive"] }` is already there at `:114`; confirm only, no edit expected

**Constraints from prior phases:**
- Phase 5 defined `KeymapContext` as a **blanket impl** — hana writes no impl, only derives. Decision A resolved as recommended, so the shipped bound list is `AsRef<str> + Copy + EnumMessage + Eq + IntoEnumIterator + Send + Sync + 'static` (`crates/hana_rubric/src/condition.rs:51-59`), with **no `Resource` bound**; `for_state_context::<C>()` adds `C: States` itself and `for_context::<C>()` adds `C: Resource`. Two things to read carefully: the bound is `AsRef<str>` (the std trait) while the *derive* that satisfies it is strum's `AsRefStr` — different names for the two halves — and `Send + Sync` are new relative to the earlier draft. Phase 5's test `derived_state_context_satisfies_keymap_context_without_a_resource_impl` proves a `States`-only enum satisfies the trait, which is exactly hana's case here.
- **`sync_state_condition` tolerates an absent `State<C>`** — it takes `Option<Res<State<C>>>` and returns early when `None` (`crates/hana_rubric/src/condition.rs:206-212`). Do not re-implement that. It is not exercised here: `init_state` inserts `State<InteractionContext>` at registration, so the resource is never absent.

**`route_input` returns while its context is unresolved** (`keymap/runtime/dispatch.rs:34-37`), routing nothing at all — globals included. Phase 12's resolved decision C chose that over falling back to globals, so it is intended behavior. Choosing `init_state` over `add_computed_state` closes the boot window it would otherwise open: `add_computed_state` only registers systems and a message (`bevy_state-0.19.0/src/app.rs:157-184`) and never inserts `State<S>`, which would first appear only after a `StateTransition` run, whereas `init_state` inserts it at registration.

Still true for tests: a `NextState` write lands on the following `StateTransition`, so **a test that changes the source components must advance a frame before reading `State<InteractionContext>`.** Same for Phases 17, 18, and 19, whose input tests all run against this state.
- Phase 5's `for_state_context::<C>()` reads `Res<State<C>>`; wiring the call is Phase 17, not this phase.
- Phase 5 rejects duplicate condition names at load and diagnoses a missing `#[strum(message)]` at startup.

**Resolved decisions (2026-07-31).**

1. **The context must always have a value** — approved. "Nothing selected" is the normal state, not an ambiguous one, so it gets a name (`Resting`) rather than leaving hana with a dead keyboard. Choosing an ordinary `States` enum over a computed one satisfies this structurally: a plain state always holds a value, so there is nothing left to assert about totality.
2. **Scope is the seeded context only** — the user picked this over designing a fuller selection/lock/modal variant set now. See the scope bullet above.

**Also update `/Users/natemccoy/rust/hana_tool_graph/docs/hana/computed_state.md`** as part of this phase: record that the pattern is now seeded in code, that the enum is a directly-driven `States` (with the reason — no source states exist to compute from), and name the two shipped variants. Leave its "do not build the framework speculatively" guidance intact.

**Acceptance gate:** `verify.sh check|test|lint hana` green, plus `verify.sh test hana bei_chord_overlap` and `verify.sh test hana cursor_integration_tests` (see the constraint below — hana is bin-only, so its `tests/` targets run under no other line).

Tests must drive the world and read `State<InteractionContext>` after a frame advance — never inspect the enum's shape, and never assert something that would pass with the derivation system deleted:

1. **Every variant carries a non-empty `#[strum(message)]`**, by iterating `InteractionContext::iter()` and calling `get_message()`. Fails if a message is missing or empty.
2. **A world with no lock and no expanded gizmo resolves to `Resting`.**
3. **A `DimensionLock` plus an `Expanded` `RotateAffordance` resolves to `DimensionLockRotation`** after one frame advance.
4. **Each half alone stays `Resting`** — lock without the expanded gizmo, and expanded gizmo without a lock. This is the test that fails if the derivation drops either conjunct, and the reason a single positive case is not enough.
5. **Removing either component returns the state to `Resting`** on the next frame — the derivation is re-evaluated every frame, not latched on first entry.

**The compile-time `KeymapContext` assertion moves to Phase 15.** It cannot be written here: hana has no `hana_rubric` dependency at all until Phase 15 adds the local path dependency (`/Users/natemccoy/rust/hana_rubric/Cargo.toml:76` carries only `bevy_kana`), so nothing in this phase can name the trait. Phase 15's gate asserts it once the crate is reachable.

**Any test this phase adds must live in `src/` under `#[cfg(test)]`, not in `tests/`.** hana is a bin-only package (`crates/hana/src/main.rs`, no `lib.rs`), and `verify.sh test` pins `--lib`/`--bins` targets from cargo metadata — an integration test in `tests/` compiles under the lint gate and never executes unless named explicitly. This is the mirror of Phase 13b's constraint, where the tests had to live in `src/` because `schema_bytes` is `pub(crate)`.

### Retrospective

**What worked:**
- The whole phase is one new file plus three small edits — `crates/hana/src/input/interaction_context.rs` (177 lines including tests), the module declaration and registration in `input/mod.rs`, two `pub(crate) use` re-exports in `selection/mod.rs`, and a `#[cfg(test)] DimensionLock::for_tests()` fixture in `selection/dimension_lock/lock_state.rs`. 333 tests pass, lint clean.
- **All seven tests are falsifiable** — each negative case seeds `State::new(InteractionContext::DimensionLockRotation)` before the frame advance, so the derivation must actively change it. This is the first phase in the plan where every test fails if its subject is deleted; 11a, 11b, 11c, 12, 13a and 13b each shipped at least one test that passed identically before and after.

**What deviated from the plan:**
- The `ComputedStates` design was **impossible** and was replaced with a plain `States` enum plus one `PreUpdate` derivation system before dispatch. `ComputedStates::SourceStates` accepts only other `States` types, and hana's only states are `HanaState`/`ReadyPhase` (`crates/hana/src/hana_state.rs:24,35`) — both app-lifecycle. The lock and gizmo are ECS components, so there was nothing for a `compute` to read.
- Scope was cut to the one context `computed_state.md` seeds (`Resting` + `DimensionLockRotation`), by user decision, rather than a fuller selection/lock/modal variant set.
- One extra file beyond the plan's **Files** list: `lock_state.rs` gained the test-only `DimensionLock::for_tests()` constructor, because `DimensionLock::new` requires an axis, position and rotation the tests do not care about.

**Surprises:**
- `bevy_state 0.19.0` carries a blanket `impl<S: ComputedStates> States for S` (`computed_states.rs:95`), so deriving both is `E0119` — worth knowing if any later phase reaches for a computed state.
- `init_state` inserts `State<S>` at registration; `add_computed_state` never inserts it at all (`bevy_state-0.19.0/src/app.rs:157-184`). Choosing `init_state` structurally closed the deaf-boot window that `route_input`'s unresolved-context early return would otherwise open.
- **`State<InteractionContext>` is not observable over BRP** — the enum derives no `Reflect` (deliberately; the plan says reflection is not needed), and neither do `RotateAffordance`/`Expanded` (`crates/hana/src/selection/rotate/affordance.rs:52,63`). The live smoke test can therefore prove the system runs without panicking against a real `DimensionLock`, but not read the resulting value. Any later phase that wants runtime state inspection over BRP has to add reflection first.

**Implications for remaining phases:**
- Phase 18 binds `B`/`P` to the context via `for_state_context::<InteractionContext>()`. The type is now real and importable as `hana::input::InteractionContext`; the derivation already runs every frame in `PreUpdate`.
- Later phases that need a **new** context variant add it to this enum and extend the same derivation system — there is no per-context type to create.
- Input tests in Phases 17–20 must advance a frame after mutating source components, since `NextState` lands on the following `StateTransition`.
