### Phase 14b — `hana_rubric`: `DerivedContext<C>` — declaring a context from world facts · status: done (`581c7d87`)

**Blocked by:** nothing. **This is library work in `/Users/natemccoy/rust/bevy_hana_rubric` on `feature/rubric`, not hana.** It cannot touch hana: hana has no `hana_rubric` dependency until Phase 15 adds the local path dependency (`hana_rubric/Cargo.toml:76` carries only `bevy_kana` before that phase). Rewriting `InteractionContext` onto this API is **Phase 17's** work — and Phase 17's Work Order still names `for_state_context`, application-owned `init_state`, and edits to the hand-written derivation system. **Reconcile those passages to `for_derived_context` before Phase 17 is dispatched**, or Phase 17 will restore the exact bespoke system this phase replaces.

#### Work Order

**Goal:** turn Phase 14's hand-written derivation system into a declared table, so adding a context variant is two lines of authoring instead of a bespoke system.

**Spec:**

Phase 14 shipped the pattern once, by hand: an enum deriving `States + EnumIter + AsRefStr + EnumMessage`, plus one `PreUpdate` system that queries components and writes `NextState<C>` only on change (`hana_tool_graph/crates/hana/src/input/interaction_context.rs:31-46`). Every later context variant would repeat the `if`-chain by hand, in an order nothing enforces. This phase moves the derivation into `hana_rubric` as an ordered table of Bevy run conditions.

**A context variant is a run condition.** Bevy already ships the vocabulary — `any_with_component::<T>()`, `resource_exists::<R>()`, `.and_then()`, `.and_eager()`, `.or_else()`, `.or_eager()`, and the free function `not(…)` — and already boxes conditions as `pub type BoxedCondition<In = ()> = Box<dyn ReadOnlySystem<In = In, Out = bool>>` (`bevy_ecs-0.19.0/src/schedule/condition.rs:11`). Nothing new is invented here; the table stores what Bevy stores. **`.and()` and `.or()` are deprecated in 0.19** (`condition.rs:203,531`) — using them fails a lint gate that denies warnings. The short-circuiting `.and_then()`/`.or_else()` are correct for the read-only marker queries this API is for.

Authoring shape (this is what hana writes in Phase 17, shown for the API's sake — do not write it in this phase):

```rust
app.add_plugins(
    KeymapPlugin::new().for_derived_context(
        DerivedContext::new(InteractionContext::Resting)
            .when(
                InteractionContext::DimensionLockRotation,
                any_with_component::<DimensionLock>()
                    .and_then(any_with_component::<Expanded>()),
            )
            .when(
                InteractionContext::DimensionLock,
                any_with_component::<DimensionLock>(),
            ),
    ),
);
```

Three pieces, all in a new `crates/hana_rubric/src/derived_context.rs`:

1. **`DerivedContext<C>`** — an explicit fallback plus an ordered `Vec<(C, BoxedCondition)>`.

   ```rust
   pub struct DerivedContext<C> {
       fallback: C,
       rules:    Vec<(C, BoxedCondition)>,
   }

   impl<C: KeymapContext + FreelyMutableState> DerivedContext<C> {
       #[must_use] pub fn new(fallback: C) -> Self;
       #[must_use] pub fn when<M>(self, context: C, condition: impl SystemCondition<M>) -> Self;
   }
   ```

   **The fallback is a constructor argument, not `#[derive(Default)]`.** Tying it to `Default` would make an unrelated change to the enum's default silently change keymap routing, and it would drag a `FromWorld` bound in for no reason. Passing the variant costs one argument and removes the `Default` bound entirely.

   **The bound is `FreelyMutableState`, not `States`.** `NextState<C>` is defined only for `C: FreelyMutableState` (`bevy_state-0.19.0/src/state/resources.rs:181`) and `insert_state` requires it (`bevy_state-0.19.0/src/app.rs:122`); `C: States` does not prove either inside generic code, so the generic implementation will not compile with `States` alone. `#[derive(States)]` supplies `FreelyMutableState` already, so this changes nothing at the call site. Import it from `bevy::state::state::FreelyMutableState`.

   `when` calls `IntoSystem::into_system` and boxes the result. The `M` marker generic matches Bevy's own helper shape (`bevy_ecs-0.19.0/src/schedule/config.rs:15`), so combinator chains infer at the call site. **Declaration order is priority; first match wins.** No scoring, no specificity rules — the author reads the table top to bottom and sees the precedence.

   **A rule must be a predicate over current world data.** Do not use `Changed<T>`, `Added<T>`, `RemovedComponents<T>`, `MessageReader<T>`, or `Local<T>` history in a rule. Evaluation stops at the first match, so a lower-priority rule's change window and local state do not advance while a higher-priority rule is matching; when the higher rule stops matching, the lower one fires on accumulated history rather than the current world. Bevy documents this hazard for short-circuiting conditions (`bevy_ecs-0.19.0/src/schedule/condition.rs:79`). State this contract in `when`'s doc comment.

2. **`KeymapPlugin::for_derived_context(self, derived: DerivedContext<C>) -> impl Plugin`**, beside the two existing entry points at `crates/hana_rubric/src/keymap_plugin.rs:77,86`.

   **`DerivedContextPlugin<C>` stores the table in `Mutex<Option<DerivedContext<C>>>`.** `Plugin::build` receives `&self` (`bevy_app-0.19.0/src/plugin.rs:57`) and `BoxedCondition` is not `Clone`, so there is no other way to move the table into the world. The mutex is touched exactly once, during plugin build; evaluation reads a plain `Vec` out of a resource and takes no lock.

   `build` does, in this order:

   - `self.keymap_plugin.install(app)`
   - add `StatesPlugin` if it is not already installed — `insert_state` panics without it, because `StateTransition` is absent (`bevy_state-0.19.0/src/app.rs:96`)
   - `register_context::<C>(app, ContextSource::Derived)`
   - **only inside the `is_ok()` branch:** take the table out of the mutex; run the three startup validations below and strip any fallback-targeting rules; `insert_state(fallback)`; call `System::initialize(app.world_mut())` once on every remaining boxed condition; insert the rule table as a resource; register an `On<CheckChangeTicks>` observer that forwards `check_change_tick` to every stored condition; add `evaluate_derived_context::<C>` in `PreUpdate` `.before(KeymapSystems::UpdateActiveCondition)` and `sync_state_condition::<C>` in `KeymapSystems::UpdateActiveCondition`.

   A rejected registration performs **none** of the `C`-specific actions — no state, no table resource, no observer, no systems. This copies the `if register_context::<C>(…).is_ok()` shape from `StateContextPlugin::build` (`condition.rs:292-304`) and extends it to the state itself, so a rejected second context source cannot leave `State<C>` behind.

   `finish` calls `KeymapPlugin::finish_assembly` like the other two.

   **All three context plugins must return `false` from `Plugin::is_unique`.** `Plugin::is_unique` defaults to `true` (`bevy_app-0.19.0/src/plugin.rs:87`), so adding the same context plugin type twice makes `add_plugins` **panic** before the second `build` ever runs (`plugin.rs:144`) — the retained single-source diagnostic never fires for the most likely form of the mistake. Returning `false` lets every repeated registration reach `register_context` and be rejected there, with a diagnostic instead of a panic. Apply this to `ResourceContextPlugin<C>` and `StateContextPlugin<C>` as well as `DerivedContextPlugin<C>`.

   Calling `insert_state` here is deliberate and is the difference from `for_state_context`, which registers no state at all (`condition.rs:292-304`). One call registers the state, the derivation, and the keymap side, so the application cannot register two of the three and forget the third.

3. **`fn evaluate_derived_context<C>(world: &mut World)`** — an exclusive system. Take the rule table out with `world.resource_scope`, walk it in declaration order, and call `run_readonly((), &*world)` on each condition:

   - `Ok(true)` selects that rule's variant and stops.
   - `Ok(false)` and `Err(RunSystemError::Skipped(_))` continue to the next rule.
   - `Err(RunSystemError::Failed(error))` is sent to the world's fallback error handler with `ErrorContext::RunCondition` and counts as `false`, matching Bevy's own executor (`bevy_ecs-0.19.0/src/schedule/executor/single_threaded.rs:224-241`).
   - No rule matches → the declared fallback.

   Write `NextState<C>` only when the result differs from `State<C>`, matching Phase 14's behavior exactly.

   **`System::validate_param` does not exist in Bevy 0.19.** Parameter validation happens inside `run_readonly`, which returns `Result` (`bevy_ecs-0.19.0/src/system/system.rs:206,211`); a missing required `Res<R>` produces `Failed` and an unmatched `Single<T>` produces `Skipped` (`system_param.rs:704,360`). **Boxed conditions must be initialized before their first run** or they panic on absent parameter state (`function_system.rs:634,674`) — that is what the `initialize` call in `build` is for. Query archetype refresh needs no separate call; `QueryState` updates archetypes itself (`query/state.rs:438,559`). `check_change_tick` **does** need the observer: the scheduler only supplies it to systems stored in schedules (`system.rs:164`, `schedule.rs:652`), and without it change detection degrades over a long run. `resource_scope` is safe here — it explicitly supports touching the rest of the world while the resource is out, and reinserts it via a drop guard during unwinding (`world/mod.rs:2816,2884`).

**Per-frame cost, stated rather than assumed.** The evaluator performs one boxed dispatch and one parameter acquisition per rule up to the first match. Query conditions do a constant-time archetype-generation comparison on an unchanged world and process only newly relevant archetypes after a structural change. `resource_scope` removes and reinserts the table resource each frame. Because the evaluator takes `&mut World` it is an **exclusive** system, which the multi-threaded executor runs alone (`multi_threaded.rs:441,543`) — a second scheduler barrier in `PreUpdate`, ahead of the already-exclusive routing system.

An ordinary-system implementation exists and is **not** taken: it needs a private candidate resource, reset and finalize systems, one ordered candidate-setting system per rule, and each rule's boxed condition attached through `run_if_dyn` guarded by `candidate_is_unset::<C>.and_then(…)` (`bevy_ecs-0.19.0/src/schedule/config.rs:231`) — `N + 2` schedule nodes and a guard dispatch per rule to remove one barrier. A single composite ordinary system instead requires a hand-written `System` impl that merges child access sets and contains unsafe execution code. Phase 14b keeps the exclusive evaluator as the smaller established mechanism.

**Totality is structural.** The declared fallback is unconditional, so the context always holds a value and the `Resting`-always-exists guarantee from Phase 14 needs no separate assertion. There is no "keep the previous variant" mode: a context that latched would make routing depend on history the author cannot see in the table.

**Names still come from strum, unchanged.** `#[strum(serialize_all = "snake_case")]` on the enum makes each variant's snake_case name an authorable `"condition"` value in the keymap file, and `#[strum(message = "…")]` remains its description in the generated schema. **Adding a variant is adding an authorable context — there is no second registration step**, which is the whole point of the request.

**`ContextSource` gains a `Derived` variant** with description `"derived"`, so the single-context-source rejection message names which kind is already installed. The existing hard rejection (`condition.rs:331-341`) is unchanged and still applies: `for_context`, `for_state_context`, and `for_derived_context` remain mutually exclusive, one per app.

**Anything the combinators cannot express is an ordinary system returning `bool`** — that is already a `SystemCondition`, so it needs no escape hatch in the API. This is how Phase 17 writes the lock/gizmo correlation:

```rust
fn lock_and_gizmo_share_a_selection(
    locks: Query<(), With<DimensionLock>>,
    gizmos: Query<&RotateAffordance, With<Expanded>>,
) -> bool {
    gizmos.iter().any(|gizmo| locks.contains(gizmo.selection))
}
```

**Startup validation** — three checks, all run synchronously inside `build` after successful registration, while the extracted table is still local. All emit `DiagnosticSeverity::Advisory` through the existing context-diagnostic retention path; the app still runs, and none of them insert `RegistryValidationFailed`:

- **A non-fallback variant with no rule** is unreachable: authorable in the keymap file, never activatable. Detected by iterating `C::iter()` against the rule table. One advisory per such variant.
- **A rule targeting the fallback variant** is emitted as one advisory **and the rule is removed from the table before insertion.** An advisory alone is not enough: because the walk stops at the first match, an early fallback-targeting rule would silently mask every later rule, which contradicts both "dead code" and "the fallback is unconditional by construction." Stripping at startup costs nothing per frame.
- **The same non-fallback variant targeted by more than one rule** occupies two priority positions, which usually means the author meant `.or_else()`. One advisory naming the variant, recommending the combinator. The rules are left in place — the behavior is deterministic, just easy to misread.

**Rejected alternative, recorded so it is not relitigated:** backing the derived context with a `Resource` instead of a state would let `sync_resource_condition` see the new value in the same frame. It is not taken; the state path keeps `in_state()`, `OnEnter`/`OnExit`, and state-scoped entities available to application code, which matters more to hana.

**What the state path costs, precisely.** `StateTransition` runs after `PreUpdate` (`bevy_app-0.19.0/src/main_schedule.rs:29`, `bevy_state-0.19.0/src/app.rs:332`), and `sync_state_condition` runs earlier in that same `PreUpdate` (`keymap_plugin.rs:114`). So: a world fact visible **before** the evaluator produces **one** stale routing pass; a fact that changes **after** that frame's evaluator produces **two**. A key pressed during either pass is routed through the previous context and dispatched immediately (`keymap/runtime/dispatch.rs:105`) — the later transition cannot undo it. This is accepted, and it is tested rather than assumed (test 15).

**Files:**
- `crates/hana_rubric/src/derived_context.rs` — create: `DerivedContext<C>`, the rule-table resource, `DerivedContextPlugin<C>`, `evaluate_derived_context<C>`, the `CheckChangeTicks` observer, the three validations, and the tests
- `crates/hana_rubric/src/keymap_plugin.rs` — add `for_derived_context` beside `for_context` (`:77`) and `for_state_context` (`:86`)
- `crates/hana_rubric/src/condition.rs` — add `ContextSource::Derived` and its `description()` arm; **make `ContextSource`, `register_context`, `condition_diagnostic`, and `retain_context_diagnostics` `pub(crate)`** — all four are currently private to this module (`:230`, `:312`, `:327`, `:364`), so this is a real change, not a visibility check; add a `DiagnosticSeverity` parameter to `condition_diagnostic`, which today hardcodes `Failure` (`:241`), passing `Failure` from the existing registration call sites and `Advisory` from derived-table validation; make retention log `Failure` with `error!` and `Advisory` with `warn!`, since it currently logs everything at error level (`:364`); add `is_unique() -> false` to `ResourceContextPlugin` and `StateContextPlugin`. Keep `ContextSourceInstalled` private. `sync_state_condition` is already `pub(crate)`.
- `crates/hana_rubric/src/lib.rs` — declare the module and re-export `DerivedContext`

**Constraints from prior phases:**
- Phase 5's `KeymapContext` is a **blanket impl** with bound list `AsRef<str> + Copy + EnumMessage + Eq + IntoEnumIterator + Send + Sync + 'static` and **no `Resource` bound** (`condition.rs:51-59`). `DerivedContext<C>` adds `FreelyMutableState` itself and adds no `Default`.
- **`register_context` hard-rejects a second context source** with a retained diagnostic (`condition.rs:331-341`). `DerivedContextPlugin` must go through it, not around it.
- **`sync_state_condition` tolerates an absent `State<C>`** — `Option<Res<State<C>>>`, early return on `None` (`condition.rs:206-212`). Do not re-implement it; reuse it.
- **`KeymapSystems` is already public and documented as the ordering point for application context derivation systems** (`keymap_plugin.rs:363-370`). `UpdateActiveCondition` and `Route` are already chained in `PreUpdate` (`keymap_plugin.rs:114-127`).
- **Test timing is exact, not approximate.** Mutate world facts, then `app.update()`: that one update evaluates the rules and applies `NextState<C>` to `State<C>` in the `StateTransition` that follows `PreUpdate` — so **every `State<C>` assertion uses exactly one update.** `ActiveCondition` does not see the new state until the next `PreUpdate`, so **every `ActiveCondition` assertion uses exactly two updates.** A fact produced after `PreUpdate` needs one additional update before it is even evaluated.

**Acceptance gate:** `verify.sh check|test|lint hana_rubric` green. Every change is inside `hana_rubric`; no other package is affected.

Tests live in `crates/hana_rubric/src/derived_context.rs` under `#[cfg(test)]`, add `StatesPlugin` before the derived plugin, drive a real `App`, and respect the timing rule above. **Every negative test seeds the opposite state first** (`world.insert_resource(State::new(<the non-fallback target>))`) so it fails if the evaluator is deleted — this is Phase 14's falsifiability discipline and it is not optional. Each test below is written so that at least one of {evaluator deleted, rule walk reversed, validation made a no-op} makes it fail:

1. **An empty world resolves to the declared fallback**, seeded from a non-fallback state.
2. **One true rule targeting a named non-fallback variant** moves `State<C>` to that variant after one update, starting from the fallback.
3. **Declaration order decides overlap.** A table with two always-true rules targeting two distinct non-fallback variants: the first declared wins. A second app with the declarations reversed produces the other winner. Both tables emit zero derived-context advisories.
4. **`.and_then()` needs both conjuncts.** Three worlds for one rule: left fact alone (seeded from the target) falls back; right fact alone (seeded from the target) falls back; both facts together activate the target from the fallback.
5. **A custom `fn(…) -> bool` system works as a rule** — the correlation escape hatch. The condition reads a test component holding an `Entity` and checks a marker on that referenced entity. Correlated entities activate the target; facts split across two different entities (seeded from the target) fall back.
6. **The evaluator re-evaluates every frame** — removing the component that satisfied a rule returns the state to the fallback on the next frame, proving nothing latches on first entry.
7. **`ActiveCondition` reflects the derived variant.** A true non-fallback rule changes `State<C>` after update one and `ActiveCondition` after update two; assert `ActiveCondition::handle()` equals `ConditionRegistry::resolve()` for that variant's exact snake_case name.
8. **A second context source is rejected, in both orders, using two apps.** Control app: derived plugin first, then a resource context — exactly one retained diagnostic naming `"derived"`, and the derived rule still drives `State<C>`. Rejection app: resource context first, then the derived plugin — the retained diagnostic names `"resource-backed"`, and a rule that would otherwise be true leaves the derived state absent; assert neither `State<C>` nor the rule-table resource was installed.
9. **An unreachable non-fallback variant produces exactly one `Advisory`** naming that variant.
10. **A fallback-targeting rule is stripped, not merely flagged.** Declare an always-true fallback-targeting rule *before* an always-true non-fallback rule: exactly one `Advisory` whose context names the fallback variant, **and the non-fallback rule still wins.**
11. **The same non-fallback variant targeted twice produces exactly one `Advisory`** naming that variant, and the table still evaluates.
12. **`NextState` is written only on change.** Start at the fallback, add a matching fact, run `PreUpdate` and assert `NextState::Pending(target)`; run `StateTransition`; run `PreUpdate` again with the world unchanged and assert `NextState::Unchanged`.
13. **A `Skipped` condition falls through without panicking.** A rule using `Single<&Marker>` with no matching entity (seeded from the target) falls back; spawning one matching entity activates the target.
14. **A `Failed` condition is handled, not fatal.** A rule requiring a `Res<R>` that does not exist returns `Failed`; the evaluator continues to the next rule, which wins. Proves the error branch counts as `false` rather than aborting the walk.
15. **The stale routing pass is real and bounded.** Bind the same keystroke to different commands in two contexts. Change the rule's world fact and press the key before a single `app.update()`; assert the **previous** context's command fired. Advance one more update; assert `ActiveCondition` now names the derived variant.
16. **Validation edges.** An empty table on a multi-variant enum emits one unreachable advisory per non-fallback variant. A single-variant enum with an empty table emits none.

**What this subsumes.** Both decisions the Phase 14 review deferred to Phase 17 stop being design questions and become authoring lines: the lock-only `DimensionLock` context is one more `.when()` declared after the rotation rule, and the selection-correlation fix is swapping `any_with_component::<DimensionLock>().and_then(…)` for the `lock_and_gizmo_share_a_selection` system above. Phase 17 still decides *whether* to do each; it no longer has to decide *how*.

### Retrospective

**What worked:** The four-expert design review before dispatch caught five compile-blockers in the draft Work Order; the implementation then landed in one pass with the gate green and no deviations. Specifying the evaluator's three-way `run_readonly` match verbatim in the Work Order meant the delegate had no room to improvise the error semantics.

**What deviated from the plan:** Nothing in scope or approach. One fix pass, on two blind-review findings: the failed-condition test asserted only the resulting state, so deleting the evaluator's error-handler call left it green — it now counts exactly one `ErrorContext::RunCondition`; and `DerivedContextPlugin` had to move directly below the imports per `nate_style/rust/module-structure.md:23`.

**Surprises:**
- Seeding count is a poor proxy for test falsifiability here. `insert_state(fallback)` makes the fallback the starting state and `NextState` starts `Unchanged`, so any test asserting a *non-fallback* value cannot pass vacuously. Only tests asserting the fallback need explicit seeding — seven do, and those are exactly the seven that seed.
- The strongest test in the file is the fallback-stripping one: because rule 1 targets the fallback with an always-true condition, an implementation that merely *flagged* rather than *removed* it would return `Resting`, and the test asserts `Active`.
- `condition.rs` violates the same plugin-ordering rule with three `impl Plugin` blocks low in the file. Pre-existing, left alone.

**Implications for remaining phases:** Phase 17 no longer designs a derivation mechanism — it deletes `derive_interaction_context` and authors `.when()` rules. Its two deferred questions are now one-line authoring choices rather than design work.

### Phase 14b Review

Four expert reviewers (correctness, type-system/ergonomics, risk/cost, acceptance-gate) reviewed the first draft of this Work Order. No premise challenge was raised; the gate's package scope was confirmed correct. All findings were applied. The five that would have stopped a fresh implementer cold, each found independently by two or more reviewers:

- **`System::validate_param` does not exist in Bevy 0.19.** The draft's evaluator would not have compiled. Validation happens inside `run_readonly`, which returns `Result`; the `Skipped`/`Failed` split is now specified.
- **Boxed conditions must be `initialize`d before their first run** or they panic. The draft never initialized them.
- **`Plugin::build` receives `&self` and `BoxedCondition` is not `Clone`**, so the draft gave no way to move the rule table into the world. Resolved with `Mutex<Option<…>>`, taken once at build.
- **`C: States` is the wrong bound** — `NextState<C>` and `insert_state` need `FreelyMutableState`. Generic code with `States` alone does not compile.
- **`ContextSource`, `register_context`, `condition_diagnostic`, and `retain_context_diagnostics` are private to `condition.rs`**, not crate-visible as the draft claimed, and `condition_diagnostic` hardcodes `Failure` — so the promised advisories were unreachable through the stated path.

Also applied: `.and()`/`.or()` are deprecated in favor of `.and_then()`/`.or_else()`; the fallback became an explicit `DerivedContext::new` argument instead of `#[derive(Default)]`; registration now precedes every `C`-specific side effect so a rejected plugin leaves no state behind; all three context plugins return `is_unique() -> false` so a repeat call is diagnosed rather than panicking in `add_plugins`; fallback-targeting rules are stripped at startup rather than merely flagged, since first-match-wins would let them mask later rules; a duplicate-target advisory was added; rules are contractually restricted to predicates over current world data because short-circuiting starves lower rules' change windows; the exclusive-system cost and the rejected ordinary-system alternative are both stated with their node counts; the routing-latency claim was sharpened from "1–2 frames" to the exact one-or-two stale routing passes with the user-visible consequence named; and the acceptance list grew from ten tests to sixteen after a falsifiability audit found six of the original ten would pass against a reversed walk, an always-false conjunction, a constant-true custom condition, or a no-op validation.

---

### Phase 14b Post-Implementation Review

An architect pass over Phases 15–21 (as numbered before this review) returned eleven findings. One needed a scope decision, ten were applied directly. Nothing 14b shipped made a later phase redundant.

**Approved by the user: Phase 17 was split, and every unfinished phase renumbered sequentially.** The old Phase 17 carried two independent bodies of work with different blockers — migrating hana's interaction context to the declared table (needs only Phase 15's dependency) and authoring the whole default keymap file (needs Phase 16's ids). They separate cleanly because `KeymapPlugin::finish_assembly` returns early when `defaults` is `None` (`keymap_plugin.rs:163-165`), so the context registers and drives `State<InteractionContext>` with no keymap file, app name, or disk worker present. New **Phase 17** wires `for_derived_context` alone; new **Phase 18** adds `.with_app_name`/`.with_defaults` and the file. Completed phases keep their original numbers and letters — they carry commit hashes — while unfinished phases lost their letter suffixes: old 18→19, 19→20, 20→21, 21→22. All cross-references were retargeted, including the fifteen whose "Phase 17" meant the keymap-file half.

**Approved by the user: the active keymap context becomes readable over BRP** (Phase 21). `ActiveCondition` derives no `Reflect` and `DerivedContextRules<C>` none either, so once Phase 18 gates bindings on context, nothing outside the process can answer "which context is active" — the first question a dead key raises. Phase 21 gained the derive-and-register work plus a gate bullet requiring the read to change across a world-fact change, since a startup-only assertion passes against a hardcoded value.

Applied without a decision:

- **Phase 15** — the "phases that shape `hana_rubric`'s public surface" range corrected from `4a–13b` to `4a–14b`, and the gate gained a compile-time `use hana_rubric::{DerivedContext, prelude::KeymapPlugin};` reachability check against the selected dependency. Without it Phase 15 could ship green against the wrong tree and the failure would surface in Phase 17, one repo away from its cause.
- **Phase 17** — its two pending decisions were still written as edits to `derive_interaction_context`, the system 14b deletes. Both are now `.when()` declarations: the lock-only variant is one more rule declared *after* the rotation rule (first match wins, `derived_context.rs:126`), and the selection correlation is the custom `lock_and_gizmo_share_a_selection` system 14b's Work Order already specified.
- **Phase 17** — the `.when()` example silently dropped the `With<RotateAffordance>` conjunct that `derive_interaction_context` actually requires (`interaction_context.rs:33`), which would have fired the context on any expanded entity in the world. Corrected to `any_match_filter::<(With<RotateAffordance>, With<Expanded>)>()`. The phase is billed behavior-preserving; that conjunct is the difference.
- **Phase 17** — the instruction to "rebuild the seven tests on the plugin" now names the recipe: 14b's `app_with_derived_context` fixture (`derived_context.rs:434-442`), which omits `with_app_name`/`with_defaults` so `finish_assembly` returns before path resolution. The naive full-production-plugin shape reaches `KeymapPaths::new` and writes into the developer's real `~/.config/hana/`. The seven assertions themselves carry over unchanged.
- **Phase 17** — gate strengthened twice. "No error-level diagnostic" no longer suffices, because 14b's three startup validations emit `Advisory` and `retain_context_diagnostics` logs those at `warn!`; the gate now requires zero `DiagnosticKind::Context` entries of any severity. And `is_unique() -> false` turned duplicate registration from a loud `add_plugins` panic into a silently dead keyboard, so the gate now asserts `ActiveCondition::handle()` resolves to `resting` after two updates.
- **Phase 21** — the error surface must split on `Diagnostic.severity`. That constraint was written when every diagnostic was a failure; `all_diagnostics()` now mixes 14b's authoring advisories with real breakage, and a flat rendering gives "variant `dimension_lock` is unreachable" the same weight as a parse error that broke the user's keymap.
- **Deferred cleanup** — gained the `condition.rs` plugin-ordering bullet. Its two context plugins sit well below the imports, the same rule 14b's fix pass applied to `DerivedContextPlugin`; pre-existing, so left out of this phase's diff.

Also fixed in code, inside 14b's own commit: `DerivedContext` was exported from `lib.rs` but absent from `prelude.rs`, so `use hana_rubric::prelude::*` let a caller invoke `for_derived_context` without being able to name its argument. Phase 17's Work Order was updated to import the prelude alone.

### Phase 15 — hana: dependency unification and `reflect_auto_register` · status: done (`07208b2`)

**Blocked by:** nothing — `G1` is superseded. Work on `feature/rubric` in `/Users/natemccoy/rust/hana_rubric`, a clean git worktree created from committed `init/hana_catalyst` tip `b41a606` on 2026-08-01. Do not edit `/Users/natemccoy/rust/hana_tool_graph` or `/Users/natemccoy/rust/hana`. Reconcile **Files** and **Spec** against this branch before dispatch. Clears `G2` on completion.

#### Work Order

**Goal:** one **`hana_rubric`** instance in hana's dependency graph, `hana_rubric` reachable, and reflect auto-registration on. (`hana_rubric` is the load-bearing one — it now owns every type that crosses the boundary. A duplicate `bevy_kana` is hygiene, not correctness.)

**Spec:**

**The problem this fixes — substantially smaller after Phase 4c.** As originally written, this phase's core hazard was type identity: hana consumed `bevy_kana` from crates.io while `hana_rubric` consumed the workspace copy, and hana's `Cargo.lock` on `main` already resolved **two** `bevy_kana` instances — one direct from crates.io, one git-rev pulled transitively via `bevy_diegetic`, `hana_conduit`, and `hana_prosody`. With `Keystroke` and `SequenceMatcher` living in `bevy_kana`, two instances meant two distinct types across the boundary and nothing type-checked.

**Phase 4c moved those types into `hana_rubric`, so that hazard is gone.** hana takes `hana_rubric` as a path dependency and gets one definition of `Keystroke`, `KeystrokeSequence`, `SequenceMatcher`, and `MatchOutcome` regardless of how many `bevy_kana` instances the graph contains. What remains for this phase is ordinary: add the `hana_rubric` path entry, drop hana's now-invalid `features = ["input"]` on `bevy_kana`, repoint the macro imports (below), and turn on `reflect_auto_register`.

**`bevy_kana` definitely survives as a hana dependency — verified on the catalyst base 2026-07-31, stop hedging about it.** `ToF32`/`ToU32`/`ToUsize`/`ToF64`/`Position` and the `prelude` are used in 42 files across **five** workspace members: `crates/hana`, `crates/hana_effects`, `crates/hana_sdf`, `crates/hana_tools`, `crates/hana_video` — each declares `bevy_kana.workspace = true`. The version and features live in the workspace root (`/Users/natemccoy/rust/hana_rubric/Cargo.toml:76`, `bevy_kana = { version = "0.3.0", features = ["input"] }`, a **crates.io** dependency, not a git one), so **dropping `features = ["input"]` is a single root-manifest edit that changes all five members at once** while `verify.sh check|test|lint hana` compiles only one of them. See the acceptance gate — it now covers the other four. **Do not trust any version number quoted in this plan** — Phase 1 published `0.2.0`, `main` has since released `0.3.0`, and the workspace member is `0.4.0-dev`. Re-read hana's `Cargo.toml` and `Cargo.lock` at dispatch time.

**Decision: path dependency, not a git rev and not a publish** (G2 resolved 2026-08-01 — see the resolved decision below).

- Add `hana_rubric = { path = "../bevy_hana_rubric/crates/hana_rubric" }` to `[workspace.dependencies]`. Both checkouts sit under `~/rust/`, so the relative path resolves from the workspace root. `hana` is an application and is never published, and this workspace already declares ten local crates this way (`Cargo.toml:39-48`) — this is the house convention, not an exception.
- **Drop `features = ["input"]` from `bevy_kana`** (`Cargo.toml:76`), because Phase 4c deleted that feature. Leave the entry itself in place — five members use its math traits. It stays a crates.io dependency; do not convert it.
- **A path dependency needs no push and no rev.** Cargo reads the working tree, so `hana_rubric` changes are visible to hana the moment they are saved. ~~Phase 24 converts this to a version dependency when both crates publish.~~ **Void as of 2026-08-03 — publishing was removed from the plan; the path dependency is the permanent end state for this plan's duration.**

**Pinning `hana_rubric` alone IS sufficient for type identity.** `hana_rubric` has no `bevy_kana` dependency at all after Phase 4c (`crates/hana_rubric/Cargo.toml`), so `Keystroke` identity does not depend on `bevy_kana`'s version. hana's `bevy_kana` entry is unrelated to the boundary and needs no coordination.

- **Remote facts, corrected 2026-08-01.** `bevy_hana_rubric`'s origin is `https://github.com/natepiano/hana.git` — the *same* remote as the four crates pinned at `Cargo.toml:85-90` (`hana_conduit`, `hana_diegetic`, `hana_lading`, `hana_prosody`, rev `df97b2b`). The earlier claim that those point at "a different repository" from `hana_rubric` was wrong. hana itself is `https://github.com/hanallc/hana.git`; `/Users/natemccoy/rust/hana_tool_graph` and `/Users/natemccoy/rust/hana_rubric` are git worktrees of `/Users/natemccoy/rust/hana`, on `init/hana_catalyst` and `feature/rubric` respectively. None of this affects a path dependency; it is recorded because Phase 24 needs it right. Do not touch the four `natepiano/hana` entries.
- After the change, `cargo tree -p hana -i hana_rubric -e normal` must show **exactly one** instance. `-e normal` is mandatory: Phase 1 hit a dev-dependency phantom where the default edge set reported a crate present through a dev-only cycle, and the same trap applies here.
- The ambiguous-package-spec workaround Phase 1 established was verified against **this** workspace only. hana is a different workspace with different members — re-verify that `-p hana` and `-p bevy_kana` resolve unambiguously there once the dependency lands, before relying on either line as a gate.

**`reflect_auto_register` is already on — this is a confirmation, not an edit (verified 2026-07-31).** `/Users/natemccoy/rust/hana_rubric/Cargo.toml:69,74` already declares `bevy_app` and `bevy_ecs` with `features = ["reflect_auto_register"]`, and `crates/hana_catalyst/Cargo.toml:16,18` takes both via `.workspace = true` while `crates/hana/Cargo.toml:32` depends on `hana_catalyst` — so cargo feature unification has the flag on for the hana binary already. **Do not add the feature to the `bevy` facade's own list** (`Cargo.toml:55-67`); the underlying crates are what carry it, and adding it to the facade changes nothing. The phase's real work is the `hana_rubric` dependency plus the import repoint below; the gate test named "auto-registration is actually on" is what proves the flag is genuinely reaching the binary. **Note M4: automatic registration never covers generic instantiations**, so any `Action<A>`-shaped or otherwise generic command type still needs an explicit `register_type`.

**`reflect_documentation` is not needed and must not be added.** R6 authors command text through `command!`; R7 puts condition descriptions on the enum variants. Adding it would cost binary size for embedded doc strings and buy nothing.

- **Repoint the macro imports in the same commit as the dependency — but there are two of them, not ~60 (verified 2026-07-31).** hana reaches `event!`, `action!`, and `bind_action_system!` through **`#[macro_use] extern crate bevy_kana;` at `crates/hana/src/main.rs:52-53`**, not per-file `use` statements, so the whole macro surface moves with that one line becoming `#[macro_use] extern crate hana_rubric;`. The only other input-side import in the entire workspace is `use bevy_kana::Keybindings;` at `crates/hana/src/input/global_shortcuts.rs:3`. Every remaining `use bevy_kana::` hit is a math trait (`ToF32`/`ToU32`/`ToUsize`/`ToF64`/`Position`, 42 files across five members) and **must stay pointed at `bevy_kana`**. This is a mechanical swap with **no semantic change** — do not migrate any declaration to `command!` here, that is Phase 16's job. Splitting the swap out of this commit leaves hana uncompilable for the whole gap between Phases 15 and 16.
- `bevy_kana`'s dependency entry also loses `features = ["input"]`, because Phase 4c deleted the feature.

**Files:**
- `Cargo.toml` (workspace root) — add `hana_rubric = { path = "../bevy_hana_rubric/crates/hana_rubric" }`; drop `features = ["input"]` from `bevy_kana` at `:76`. **Leave `bevy_app`/`bevy_ecs` at `:69,74` alone** (`reflect_auto_register` is already there) and **leave the four `natepiano/hana` git entries at `:85-90` alone**.
- `crates/hana/Cargo.toml` — add `hana_rubric.workspace = true`
- `crates/hana/src/main.rs:52-53` — `#[macro_use] extern crate bevy_kana;` → `#[macro_use] extern crate hana_rubric;`
- `crates/hana/src/input/global_shortcuts.rs:3` — `use bevy_kana::Keybindings;` → `use hana_rubric::Keybindings;`
- `Cargo.lock` — regenerated; commit it

That is the entire import surface. There is no per-file macro-import sweep: the earlier "~60 sites" figure counted `use bevy_kana::` hits that are almost all math traits and must not be touched.

**Constraints from prior phases:**
- Phases 1–14b shipped the `hana_rubric` crate on `feature/rubric` in `/Users/natemccoy/rust/bevy_hana_rubric`; the path dependency points straight at that working tree, so no commit or branch state has to be selected. **Phase 4c moved the `keystroke` module (and the rest of `input/`) out of `bevy_kana` into `hana_rubric`**, so `Keystroke`, `KeystrokeSequence`, `SequenceMatcher`, and `MatchOutcome` are `hana_rubric` types, ungated, and `bevy_kana` no longer has an `input` feature at all.
- **No rev has to be computed, and none has to be recomputed.** The earlier constraint here — recompute the pinned rev after every phase that changes `hana_rubric`'s public surface — existed only because of the git-rev mechanism and is retired with it. A path dependency reads the working tree, so Phases 4a–14b's public surface is visible to hana automatically and cannot drift out of sync. The old "Phases 4a, 4c, and 11 are the only ones permitted to edit `bevy_kana`" rule is likewise retired: Phase 4c left `bevy_kana` with no input surface, and Phase 11's option B became a `hana_rubric` edit.
- **Phase 14b is the last phase to touch the public surface, and it added two names Phase 17 depends on:** `DerivedContext` and `KeymapPlugin::for_derived_context`. Keep the compile-time reachability check in this phase's gate — a `use hana_rubric::{DerivedContext, prelude::KeymapPlugin};` in hana that must compile. It is cheap and it fails loudly if the path ever points somewhere stale; without it, a wrong path surfaces as a mystery type error in Phase 17, one repo away from its cause. (Both names are also in `hana_rubric::prelude` as of 14b.)
- **`.claude/config/release.toml` needs no change for this phase.** Its single `[[publish_path_pins]]` entry (`bevy_kana = "0.3.0"`) exists because `bevy_lagrange` takes `bevy_kana` as a *normal* dependency. Phase 4c added `hana_rubric` as a **dev**-dependency of `bevy_lagrange` and `hana_valence` and a normal dependency of `fairy_dust` — cargo strips path-only dev-deps on publish, and `fairy_dust` is not published (workspace version `0.0.1`, no pin entry, itself only a path-only dev-dep of `bevy_lagrange`). A `hana_rubric` pin becomes necessary only if `hana_rubric` is published or `fairy_dust` becomes publishable.
- `bevy_kana` has **no `input` feature** by the time this phase runs — Phase 4c deleted it and moved its contents to `hana_rubric`. Any `features = ["input"]` left on a `bevy_kana` entry is now an error, not a requirement. (Before 4c the feature existed but was outside the default set, `default = ["math"]`, despite `lib.rs`'s doc comment claiming otherwise.)

**Resolved decision (2026-08-01): path dependency.** The choice was posed as pin-vs-publish; both were wrong, because a git rev makes cargo fetch from GitHub and so drags in a push, and publishing freezes an API that Phases 16–23 still change. A path dependency was the option the framing omitted: no push, no rev, no republish, and it matches how this workspace already declares its ten local crates. ~~Phase 24 converts it to a version dependency when both crates publish.~~ **Void as of 2026-08-03 — publishing left the plan; nothing converts it.** The original framing is kept below as the record.

**Superseded framing:**

**Decision: re-affirm "pin, not publish" before this phase dispatches (G2)**

Actual problem:
This phase's `G2` rationale — pin `bevy_kana` and `hana_rubric` by git rev rather than publishing them — was reasoned at a time when publishing `bevy_kana` had never been done and was treated as the risky path. Phase 1 has since published `bevy_kana 0.2.0` to crates.io successfully, so the precedent that argued against publishing no longer holds on its own terms.

What exists now:
- `bevy_kana 0.2.0` and `0.3.0` are both on crates.io; this workspace's member is `version = "0.4.0-dev"`.
- `hana_rubric` has never been published and is a new crate on `feature/rubric`.
- hana's dependency graph has not been re-read since Phase 1; the version numbers quoted in this phase's Spec are known stale.

What should change:
- Re-read hana's `Cargo.toml` and `Cargo.lock` and re-run `cargo tree -p hana -i bevy_kana -e normal` immediately before dispatching, then decide between:
  - **Pin by git rev** (current plan): one commit moves both deps, nothing is published, and the rev can be moved freely while the plan is still in flight.
  - **Publish**: `bevy_kana 0.4.0` (breaking — 4c removed `input`) plus a first `hana_rubric` release, and hana consumes both by version. Cleaner for hana, but it locks the API of a crate the remaining phases are still changing. Publishing `hana_rubric` would also require a `[[publish_path_pins]]` entry in `.claude/config/release.toml`, which today holds only `bevy_kana = "0.3.0"`.

Recommendation:
Keep the git-rev pin. Phases 4a and 11 may still edit `bevy_kana` and Phases 4a–13b all shape `hana_rubric`'s public surface, so publishing now means republishing after nearly every remaining phase. Publishing becomes the right move once the plan is exhausted, not here. The re-verification of hana's actual graph is still required regardless of which way this resolves.




**Acceptance gate:** `verify.sh check|test|lint hana` green, plus `verify.sh test hana bei_chord_overlap` and `verify.sh test hana cursor_integration_tests` — hana is bin-only, so `verify.sh test hana` pins `--lib`/`--bins` and those two existing targets run under no other line. `cargo tree -p hana -i hana_rubric -e normal` shows exactly one instance.

**Four more packages must be gated, because the `features = ["input"]` removal is a workspace-root edit.** `bevy_kana.workspace = true` appears in five member manifests, so a root-manifest feature change recompiles all five while `verify.sh … hana` covers one. Add: `verify.sh test hana_effects`, `verify.sh lint hana_effects`, and the same two lines for `hana_sdf`, `hana_tools`, and `hana_video`. Dropping these is how a feature removal ships green and breaks a sibling crate.

**Three falsifiable tests, all in `src/` under `#[cfg(test)]`** (a `tests/` target in a bin-only package compiles and never runs — same constraint as Phase 14):

1. **The dependency resolves and the trait is reachable** — the compile-time `KeymapContext` assertion relocated from Phase 14, now that `hana_rubric` is a dependency: assert `InteractionContext` satisfies the trait's bound list. This replaces the previously specified "constructs a `hana_rubric::Keystroke` and passes it to a `hana_rubric` API", which passed identically whether the graph held one `bevy_kana` instance or three and whether auto-registration was on or off — it proved only what `verify.sh check hana` already proved. **The import path is `crate::input::InteractionContext`** — hana is bin-only and `mod input;` is private (`crates/hana/src/main.rs:54`), so there is no external `hana::input::…` path and the assertion must live inside the binary.
2. **Auto-registration is actually on** — assert that a hana type carrying a reflected trait registration is present in the type registry with **no** explicit `register_type` call anywhere for it. **Use an existing `event!`-generated type carrying `#[reflect(Event)]` — e.g. `EscapeRequested` at `crates/hana/src/input/escape.rs:12`. Do NOT use `#[reflect(KeymapCommand)]`:** this phase's own Spec forbids any `command!` migration until Phase 16, so no hana type carries that attribute when this gate runs and the test cannot compile. The flag is already enabled through `bevy_app`/`bevy_ecs` (see the Spec), so this test's job is to prove the unification actually reaches the binary rather than to prove an edit landed. It fails if auto-registration is off, which is the mechanism the phase is named for.
3. **The macro source really moved** — assert that `crates/hana/src/main.rs` declares `#[macro_use] extern crate hana_rubric;` and no longer declares `#[macro_use] extern crate bevy_kana;`. **The old `rg 'bevy_kana::(event|action|bind_action_system|Keybindings|…)'` gate is a false pass and was removed:** it already returns essentially nothing *before* the phase — the sole pre-existing hit is `Keybindings` at `input/global_shortcuts.rs:3` — because the macros arrive through `#[macro_use]`, not through paths. A gate that is nearly green before the work starts proves nothing. `bevy_kana` remains a dependency of all five members afterwards (math traits), so `cargo tree -p hana -i bevy_kana -e normal` showing exactly one instance is still a valid check; the entry is never removed.

### Retrospective

**What worked:**
- The path dependency, macro import, `Keybindings` import, and lockfile update stayed isolated to seven files in `/Users/natemccoy/rust/hana_rubric`.
- In-source tests now prove `InteractionContext: KeymapContext`, `EscapeRequested` auto-registration, and the `hana_rubric` macro source; both reviews approved the diff.

**What deviated from the plan:**
- `cargo tree -p hana -i bevy_kana -e normal` is ambiguous because the catalyst graph already contains `bevy_kana` 0.1.0, 0.2.0-dev, and 0.3.0; the phase introduced none of them. Version-qualified queries confirmed each route, while `hana_rubric` resolves exactly once.

**Surprises:**
- The Work Order correctly called duplicate `bevy_kana` hygiene rather than correctness, but its final acceptance sentence still claimed the graph contained exactly one instance.

**Implications for remaining phases:**
- Phases 16–21 can import `hana_rubric` directly from hana and rely on the migrated macro surface before declaration-by-declaration `command!` conversion begins.
- Remaining gates must not use the unqualified inverse `bevy_kana` query as proof of a single package identity; any version-specific inspection must name the package version explicitly.

### Phase 15 Review

- Phase 16 names 33 declarations, relies on the crate-wide macro import, and had its held-prefix, command-event, literal-ID authorization, and exact call-site inventory resolved on 2026-08-01.
- Phase 17 now carries forward Phase 15's eighth contract test and remains blocked on its context-variant and selection-correlation decisions.
- Phase 18 now preserves the two held physical actions until Phase 19, names the correct manifest edits, and exempts the recovery action from any observer split until Phase 20.
- Phases 19–21 now name their real phase dependencies instead of cleared gate `G2`.
- Phase 20 now defers the choice of one authoritative recovery-chord execution path; the recommendation is the exclusive pre-routing handler only.
- Phase 21 now names both repositories and the concrete library/application files, removes explicit non-generic type registration, and defers the reflected active-context representation.
- Phase 22 now defers a recommended contiguous Phase 22/Phase 23 publication-versus-migration split, `hana_rubric 0.1.0`, and a dedicated nateroids worktree/inventory including `src/main.rs`.

### Phase 16 — hana: migrate 33 declarations to `command!` and relocate them · status: done

**Blocked by:** Phase 15, which cleared `G2` and made `hana_rubric` reachable. The phase's command-event and held-prefix decisions were resolved on 2026-08-01. (`G1` superseded — work on `feature/rubric` in `/Users/natemccoy/rust/hana_rubric`, reconcile before dispatch.)

#### Work Order

**Goal:** every command is declared with its id, authored title, description, and capability, next to the handler that implements it.

**Spec:**

**Corrected against `init/hana_catalyst` 2026-07-31 — the count is 33, not 32, and every line reference below moved.** `crates/hana/src/input/global_shortcuts.rs` is now 399 lines and holds **33** `action!` declarations at `:7-43`. All 33 switch to `command!` and **gain authored text — none carries a doc comment today.** The 33rd is `ModifySelection` at `:43`, spawned through `Keybindings::new::<ModifySelection>` with `ActionSettings { require_reset: true }` at `:51-57`; it and `FreezeTracking` are the held modifiers. The setup function is now named **`spawn_global_actions`** (`:50`), not `spawn_actions`. `input/mod.rs` carries **34** `pub use global_shortcuts::` lines — the 33 commands plus `GlobalShortcutsContext` at `:20` — and separately `use global_shortcuts::PermanentShortcutsContext;` at `:32`. `hana_rubric` adds three private modifier action types on top (`PrimaryShortcutsModifier`, `AltModifier`, `ControlModifier` in `crates/hana_rubric/src/command/keybindings.rs`). Re-count at dispatch: this file is under active development on that branch.

**Each invocation goes where its handler already is.** Leaving all 33 in `global_shortcuts.rs` would make that file a cross-domain data dictionary — camera, persistence, selection, transport, voice, tools, inspectors — satisfying several split criteria at once and separating every command type from its primary behavior.

Placement: `camera/editor_camera.rs` (`CameraHomeEvent` already lives there), `camera/flyover.rs`, `selection/operations.rs` (delete, duplicate, select-all, zoom), `selection/dimension_lock/`, `selection/rotate/`, `persistence.rs`, `transport.rs`, `voice/mod.rs`, `tool/light_block.rs`, `tool/metal_sphere.rs`, `input/escape.rs`, and the existing camera/light/selection/inspector setting modules for their switch commands.

**The command re-exports come out of `input/mod.rs`.** The generated types stay `pub` — `action!` and `event!` hardcode `pub struct $name;` and `command!` adds no visibility parameter (settled Phase 4a Decision C). `pub` inside a binary crate leaks nothing.

**Ids** follow `subsystem::verb_object` in snake_case, namespaced per the comment groupings already in `global_shortcuts.rs`: `camera`, `selection`, `dimension_lock`, `transport`, `scene`, `voice`, `spawn`, `inspect`. **These ids ship in user keymap files and outlive most other choices here** — there is no alias machinery in v1 (A1), so a rename later is a breaking change for users. The user authorized the delegate on 2026-08-01 to author the literal 33-id catalog under these rules; both reviews must inspect every id as persistent public configuration, not incidental implementation text.

**Capabilities:** `ModifySelection` and `FreezeTracking` are `Held` (Phase 19 rewires them). Commands belonging to `PermanentShortcutsContext` are `Unremappable` (Phase 20). Everything else is `OneShot`.

**Constraint from Phase 11c — a held key may not be the first stroke of any sequence.** `keymap/merged.rs:501-545` rejects a `Held` command whose single keystroke is the first stroke of any multi-stroke binding in the same **effective** binding set — the global scope and, separately, each context's set after global bindings are inherited into it. The rejection is `DiagnosticSeverity::Failure`. Phase 11c added this because held activation needs a `KeyCode` and the sequence-timeout path has none, so the hold was being silently dropped or attached to the wrong key; a hold-to-act key that engages only after the sequence timeout is not a hold.

**Check the two `Held` keystrokes against every sequence this phase authors before writing the default file.** Phase 12's `finish` rejects any shipped default that does not resolve, so a collision here is not a warning — it is an app that does not start.

**Held-prefix resolution — preserve a valid shipped hold when a user sequence conflicts.** Resolved by the user on 2026-08-01. When a default held `f` and a user-authored `f g` collide, reject only the invalid user sequence and keep the shipped `f` binding live. Emit one failure diagnostic at the user binding that names both commands, both source files, and says the default remains active. Intentional replacement remains possible and explicit: the user tombstones the default identity with `"f": null` and then binds `"f g"`. A collision entirely within one source still rejects every participant because there is no valid earlier source to preserve. Phase 19's acceptance gate owns both the default-preservation and explicit-tombstone cases.

**Scale — live inventory refreshed 2026-08-01.** 20 files import from `crate::input`; dispatch is **19 `bind_action_system!` call sites across 10 files plus 11 `bind_action_switch!` call sites across 8 files**. All are candidate edits once the re-exports leave `input/mod.rs`. The earlier 23/10 counts were stale.

**This phase does not change behavior.** The static `action!` → `Start<A>` → `bind_action_system!` → semantic event chain stays exactly as it is; `command!` wraps the same two macros and adds metadata. Bindings still come from `global_shortcuts.rs`'s `spawn_*` calls until Phase 17.

**Files:**
- `hana/crates/hana/src/input/global_shortcuts.rs` — remove the 33 declarations
- `crates/hana/src/input/mod.rs` — remove the 33 command re-exports. **Keep `GlobalShortcutsContext` (`:20`), `PermanentShortcutsContext` (`:32`), and `pub use interaction_context::InteractionContext;` (`:46`)** — the last is Phase 14's and Phase 17 imports it.
- The ~12 handler modules listed above — add `command!` invocations with authored text
- The ~20 files importing from `crate::input` — update import paths
- The **19** `bind_action_system!` call sites — update paths: `selection/operations.rs` (5), `selection/dimension_lock/mod.rs` (4), `selection/rotate/mod.rs` (2), `persistence.rs` (2), and one each in `voice/mod.rs`, `camera/editor_camera.rs`, `camera/flyover.rs`, `transport.rs`, `input/escape.rs`, and `tool/command_palette.rs`.
- The **11** `bind_action_switch!` call sites — update command-type paths: `camera/settings.rs` (4), and one each in `tool/light_block.rs`, `mouse_position.rs`, `selection/animation.rs`, `camera/grid_gizmo.rs`, `selection/dimension_lock/mod.rs`, `selection/entity/mod.rs`, and `light/mod.rs`.
- **`bind_action_switch!` is hana's OWN macro — do not repoint it.** It is defined at `crates/hana/src/switches.rs:11` and reached through `#[macro_use] mod switches;` (`crates/hana/src/main.rs:55-56`), so its 11 call sites need only their **command-type paths** updated, never an import swap. Add `crates/hana/src/switches.rs` and `crates/hana/src/main.rs` to this Files list.

**Constraints from prior phases:**
- Phase 4a's `command!` takes **both identifiers** — stable `macro_rules!` cannot derive `CameraHomeEvent` from `CameraHome`: `command! { action: X, event: XEvent, id: "…", title: "…", description: "…" }`.
- Phase 4a: **`ReflectKeymapCommand` must be imported unqualified at every call site** — bevy's reflect derive resolves `#[reflect(KeymapCommand)]` through an unqualified name in the invocation module. It is not the only one: `command!` wraps `action!` and `event!`, which expand to bare `#[derive(InputAction)]` and `#[derive(Event, Reflect)]` with **no internal imports**, so `InputAction`, `Event`, and `Reflect` must also resolve unqualified. Every one of the 33 call sites needs:
  ```rust
  use bevy::prelude::{Event, Reflect};
  use bevy_enhanced_input::prelude::InputAction;
  use hana_rubric::ReflectKeymapCommand;
  ```
- **Phase 15 imported `hana_rubric` with `#[macro_use]` at the crate root**, so `command!`, `action!`, and `event!` already resolve crate-wide. Do not add a per-module `use hana_rubric::command`; only the five derive-support names below need explicit imports at each declaration site.
- Phase 4a settled Decision C: generated command types stay `pub`. Do not attempt `pub(crate)`; the wrapped macros have no visibility parameter.
- Phase 4a: `id` is const-validated at expansion (exactly one `::`, snake_case segments); title and description are required and rejected empty by startup validation.
- Phase 15 made one `hana_rubric` instance reachable through the local path dependency and confirmed `reflect_auto_register`, so `#[reflect(KeymapCommand)]` types register automatically — **except generics (M4)**.

- **A `command!` call site needs five names imported unqualified**, not four: `InputAction`, `Event`, `Reflect`, `ReflectEvent`, and `ReflectKeymapCommand`. The expansion writes `#[reflect(Event, KeymapCommand)]`, so bevy's derive forms *both* `ReflectEvent` and `ReflectKeymapCommand` by concatenation in the caller's scope. **`hana_rubric::prelude::*` does not cover this** — 26 entries as of Phase 12, which also made the prelude no longer types-only: it now carries the free function `cancel_pending_sequences` alongside `ActiveCondition`, `Capability`, `CommandId`, `CommandInfo`, `CommandRegistry`, `ConditionName`, `Diagnostic`, `DiagnosticKind`, `DiagnosticSeverity`, `HoldPhase`, `Keybindings`, `KeymapCommand`, `KeymapContext`, `KeymapLoadFailures`, `KeymapPlugin`, `KeymapSystems`, `Keystroke`, `KeystrokeSequence`, `MatchOutcome`, `Modifiers`, `ReflectKeymapCommand`, `SequenceMatcher`, and the three keystroke error types — but **not** the `command!` macro and **not** `Event`/`Reflect`/`ReflectEvent`/`InputAction`. Write the five imports explicitly.
- **`command!`'s keys are positional and fixed**: `action`, `event`, `id`, `title`, `description`, and optionally `capability` — in that order, `capability` last. It is `macro_rules!` arm matching, not a keyword parser; a key out of order produces an opaque "no rules expected this token" error. Omitting `capability` yields `Capability::OneShot`. **`capability: Held` is a deliberate `compile_error!`** — a held command is declared with the leading `held,` marker and no `capability:` key, which is what gives its event the `phase: HoldPhase` field. `capability: Capability::Held` matches no arm at all (the generic arm takes a bare ident).
- **Every `KeymapCommand` impl must satisfy `Event + Reflect` and implement **both** `fn hold_phase(&self) -> Option<HoldPhase>` **and Phase 11b's `fn build() -> Self`**, and `#[reflect(KeymapCommand)]` additionally requires `for<'a> <Self as Event>::Trigger<'a>: Default` (`command/reflect_command.rs:53-57`) — a hard compile error, not a silent skip** (`crates/hana_rubric/src/command/reflect_command.rs:15`). `command!` generates the method — non-held forms return `None`, the `held,` form returns `Some(self.phase)`. A hand-written impl that omits it fails to compile; that is deliberate, so a held command cannot register as silently inert.
**Command-event resolution — add four real semantic events.** Resolved on 2026-08-01 as part of the authorized Phase 16–18 recommendations.

Actual problem:
This phase migrates 33 declarations to `command!`, but four action types have no command event. `ModifySelection` and `FreezeTracking` are consumed as `Action<A>` state (`hana/crates/hana/src/selection/click_select.rs:34`, `box_select/drag.rs:54`) and as `Start`/`Complete` observers (`selection/dimension_lock/position.rs:42,50`). `SpawnLightBlock` and `SpawnMetalSphere` are consumed through `UserMovablePlugin<T, A>` and its private `SpawnMovableRequested<T>` message (`movable/core/app_extension.rs`); neither has a semantic event either. Phase 4a implemented `KeymapCommand` on the **event** (`crates/hana_rubric/src/command/reflect_command.rs:15`), so `command!` cannot declare any of the four commands without one.

What exists now:
- `command!` has four public arms; every one that declares a command requires `event:` — there is no event-only or action-only form.
- `KeymapCommand: Event + Reflect` is a **supertrait** (`crates/hana_rubric/src/command/reflect_command.rs:15`), so the metadata carrier must itself be an event.
- This phase's Spec says it does not change behavior, and its Files list does not account for inventing new types.

Declare `ModifySelectionEvent` and `FreezeTrackingEvent` via `command! { held, … }`, plus one-shot `SpawnLightBlockEvent` and `SpawnMetalSphereEvent`. The held events are **not** dead types: Phase 4b's `register_held_observer` observes each one and writes its `CustomInput` on `HoldPhase::Begin` / `End`, so the event is the scripted, BRP, and test path into a hold that no keyboard is pressing. The two spawn events bridge into the existing typed `SpawnMovableRequested<T>` path while the existing `Start<A>` observers remain live through Phase 17; Phase 18 removes those physical one-shot actions and keeps the event route. **Caveat after Phase 11b:** the short-id BRP method reaches `HoldPhase::Begin` only — `invoke` carries no payload. Reaching `End` needs a hand-built event (tests) or stock `world.trigger_event`. Downstream of Phase 21's pending decision; the rationale for declaring these types is unaffected either way.

- **`command!` has a third public arm for held commands**, marked by a leading `held` token and carrying no `capability:` key: `command! { held, action: …, event: …, id: …, title: …, description: … }`. Its generated event is `pub struct $Event { pub phase: HoldPhase }`, not a unit struct. Use this form for every command migrated as `Held`.
- **`Held` commands are absent from the command palette** (Phase 4b, Decision E). They still need an authored title and description — those are the key-binding editor's label and the schema's hover text.

**Acceptance gate:** `verify.sh check|test|lint hana` green, plus `verify.sh test hana bei_chord_overlap` and `verify.sh test hana cursor_integration_tests` (bin-only package — those targets run under no other line).

**"Resolves through the registry at startup" must be asserted, not observed.** A startup smoke test proves nothing here: `finish_assembly` inserts `CommandRegistry::empty()` and lets the app start when validation fails (`crates/hana_rubric/src/keymap_plugin.rs:157-165`), and `RegistryValidationFailed` is `pub(crate)` (`:283`), so a hana-side test cannot see the failure. The gate is:

- `CommandRegistry::iter().count()` equals the number of declarations, read from the source rather than hardcoded — currently 33 `action!(` occurrences in `crates/hana/src/input/global_shortcuts.rs` on `feature/rubric`. Recount at dispatch rather than hardcoding the test.
- `KeymapLoadFailures::all_diagnostics()` (`crates/hana_rubric/src/diagnostic.rs:94-97`) is empty.
- Every command carries a non-empty title and description, and no duplicate id was rejected.

If either accessor turns out not to be reachable from hana, widening its visibility is in scope for this phase — an empty registry that boots silently is the failure mode this gate exists to catch.

`rg 'action!\(' crates/hana/src/input/global_shortcuts.rs` returns nothing. Every existing keyboard shortcut still works — behavior is unchanged this phase.

### Retrospective

**What worked:**
- All 33 commands now own stable ids, authored metadata, and capabilities beside their handlers; the two held commands and the permanent palette command retained their required capabilities.
- `ModifySelectionEvent`, `FreezeTrackingEvent`, `SpawnLightBlockEvent`, and `SpawnMetalSphereEvent` supplied the missing semantic-event side, and the two spawn events reach the existing typed movable-spawn path.
- The registry test derives the declaration count from the declaration sources and rejects empty metadata, duplicate ids, and retained startup diagnostics.

**What deviated from the plan:**
- The dual review corrected durable metadata before shipment: rotation now describes toggling controls, zoom uses focus-target terminology, and voice describes capture submission rather than stopping transcription.

**Surprises:**
- `resolve_zoom_target` can prefer a hovered entity outside the selection or expanded rotation controls, so selection-only ids would have promised narrower behavior than the handler provides.

**Implications for remaining phases:**
- Phase 18 must author the embedded keymap from the final declaration ids, including `camera::focus_target`, `camera::toggle_focus_visualization`, and `selection::toggle_rotation_controls`.
- Phase 19 can use the relocated `ModifySelection` and `FreezeTracking` command actions plus their held semantic events; Phase 21 can expose the final stable ids without aliases.

### Phase 16 Review

- Phase 17 now names the current six-behavior/eight-test inventory, updates the two lock-only expectations, and uses only public state plus diagnostics for its registration assertion.
- Phase 18 now places B and P in both lock contexts, names all 20 declaration sources and final catalog examples, and removes the dead generic movable action observers along with the other dead action-start halves.
- Phase 19 now owns the approved cross-source held-prefix algorithm, its tombstone cases, a semantic held-input lookup, and separate library/application verification and checkpoints.
- Phase 20 now treats `OpenCommandPalette`'s `Unremappable` capability as shipped verification rather than duplicate implementation work.
- Phase 21's catalog examples use the shipped ids, its registry invocation has a semantic outcome, and its existing active-context representation decision now recommends one explicit state instead of correlated fields.
