### Phase 17 — hana: `InteractionContext` becomes a declared derived context · status: done

**Blocked by:** Phases 14b and 15, both shipped. The lock-only and selection-correlation decisions were resolved on 2026-08-01. This phase is **not blocked by Phase 16**. `KeymapPlugin::finish_assembly` returns early when `defaults` is `None` (`crates/hana_rubric/src/keymap_plugin.rs:163-165`), so the context registers and drives `State<InteractionContext>` with no keymap file, no app name, and no disk worker in play. This phase deliberately wires the plugin *without* `.with_app_name`/`.with_defaults`; Phase 18 adds them. (`G1` superseded — work on `feature/rubric` in `/Users/natemccoy/rust/hana_rubric`, reconcile before dispatch.)

#### Work Order

**Goal:** hana's interaction context stops being a hand-written system and becomes a declared rule table, so its variants are authorable condition names in a keymap file.

**Spec:**

**Delete the hand-written derivation; do not extend it.** `crates/hana/src/input/interaction_context.rs` keeps the enum, its tests, and the new `lock_and_gizmo_share_a_selection` predicate. Its `derive_interaction_context` system goes away, as do the `.init_state::<InteractionContext>()` and `.add_systems(PreUpdate, interaction_context::derive_interaction_context)` registrations at `crates/hana/src/input/mod.rs:55,60`. `for_derived_context` owns the state registration and the evaluation together.

**Wire the plugin:**

```rust
use bevy::prelude::*;                        // any_with_component
use hana_rubric::prelude::*;                 // DerivedContext, KeymapPlugin
use hana_rubric::prelude::KeymapPlugin;

app.add_plugins(
    KeymapPlugin::new().for_derived_context(
        DerivedContext::new(InteractionContext::Resting)
            .when(
                InteractionContext::DimensionLockRotation,
                lock_and_gizmo_share_a_selection,
            )
            .when(
                InteractionContext::DimensionLock,
                any_with_component::<DimensionLock>(),
            ),
    ),
);
```

**The rotation rule must correlate the two facts on one selection.** `derive_interaction_context` requires an expanded `RotateAffordance`, and `RotateAffordance::selection` names the selection it controls. `lock_and_gizmo_share_a_selection` queries expanded affordances and returns true only when `locks.contains(gizmo.selection)`. A pair of independent `any_*` conditions is not equivalent: it can combine a lock on selection A with an expanded affordance for selection B. The lock-only rule follows the rotation rule because first match wins.

**Rebuild the eight existing tests on the plugin, using Phase 14b's fixture shape.** The recipe is `app_with_derived_context` (`crates/hana_rubric/src/derived_context.rs:434-442`): `App::new()`, plus `StatesPlugin`, plus `KeymapPlugin::new().for_derived_context(table)` — **no `with_app_name`, no `with_defaults`**. Under that shape `finish_assembly` returns before `KeymapPaths::new`, so no test touches the developer's real `~/.config/hana/`. Do **not** wire the full production plugin in a test; it reaches path resolution and the disk worker. The current inventory is six behavioral tests, one message-coverage test, and Phase 15's compile-time contract test. Preserve their intent, but update the two expectations changed by the new variant: lock-only resolves to `DimensionLock`, and removing `Expanded` while the lock remains returns to `DimensionLock`, not `Resting`. Add the cross-selection regression; the existing lock-plus-expanded case already proves the more-specific rule wins. Preserve `interaction_context_satisfies_hana_rubric_context_requirements` unless the production `for_derived_context` call itself makes the same bounds unavoidable; if it is retired as redundant, say so in the retrospective.

**Files:**
- `hana/crates/hana/src/input/interaction_context.rs` — delete `derive_interaction_context`; rebuild its tests on the plugin; the enum stays
- `hana/crates/hana/src/input/mod.rs` — add `KeymapPlugin` to `EnhancedInputAppPlugin`; **remove** `.init_state::<InteractionContext>()` (`:55`) and `.add_systems(PreUpdate, interaction_context::derive_interaction_context)` (`:60`)

**Constraints from prior phases:**
- **Phase 14 shipped `InteractionContext` with exactly two variants — `Resting` and `DimensionLockRotation`** (`crates/hana/src/input/interaction_context.rs:15-28`), deriving `States` + `EnumIter` + `AsRefStr` + `EnumMessage` with `#[strum(serialize_all = "snake_case")]`. This phase adds `DimensionLock`, making the accepted keymap names `resting`, `dimension_lock_rotation`, and `dimension_lock`. Import it as `crate::input::InteractionContext` — hana is bin-only with a private `mod input;` (`crates/hana/src/main.rs:54`), so no `hana::input::…` path exists.
- **Delete `.init_state::<InteractionContext>()` at `crates/hana/src/input/mod.rs:55`** — Phase 14b's `for_derived_context` calls `insert_state(fallback)` itself, inside its successful-registration branch. Leaving hana's own `init_state` in place is a duplicate registration. The enum's bound becomes `FreelyMutableState`, which `#[derive(States)]` already supplies; nothing changes on the enum.
- **`DerivedContext` and `KeymapPlugin` are both in `hana_rubric::prelude`** (27 entries; `DerivedContext` was added in Phase 14b's own commit). A single `use hana_rubric::prelude::*;` covers both the plugin entry point and the type its argument is named with.
- **Declaration order is priority; first match wins.** `DerivedContext::when` pushes onto an ordered table and the evaluator stops at the first rule returning `true` (`crates/hana_rubric/src/derived_context.rs:126`). A more specific context must be declared **before** the more general one that would also match, or the specific one is unreachable and startup emits an advisory naming it.
- **Rules must be predicates over current world data only.** No `Changed`/`Added`/`RemovedComponents`/`MessageReader`/`Local` in a `.when()` condition — the evaluator short-circuits, so a lower rule's change window is starved on any pass where a higher rule matches first.
- **Exactly one `for_*_context` call may exist.** `register_context` hard-rejects a second context source with a retained diagnostic (`crates/hana_rubric/src/condition.rs:331-341`), so `for_context::<C>()`, `for_state_context::<C>()`, and `for_derived_context(…)` are mutually exclusive — one per app. hana uses `for_derived_context` (R8, via the state path).
- **A duplicate registration is now silent, and the symptom is a completely dead keyboard.** Phase 14b set `is_unique() -> false` on all three context plugins, so a second registration no longer panics inside `add_plugins`; it returns a retained `Failure` diagnostic and does **not** set `RegistryValidationFailed`, so the app boots normally while `route_input` early-returns on every unresolved context. The gate below asserts against this directly.
- **The derived context reaches routing one or two stale routing passes after the world changes — accept this, do not "fix" it by restructuring library scheduling.** Phase 14b's evaluator writes `NextState` in `PreUpdate`; `StateTransition` runs *after* `PreUpdate` (`bevy_state-0.19.0/src/app.rs:335`); and both consumers — `sync_state_condition` and `route_input` — are chained inside `PreUpdate` (`crates/hana_rubric/src/keymap_plugin.rs:114-129`). A fact visible **before** the evaluator costs one stale pass; a gizmo that expands during `Update` of frame N changes after that frame's evaluator and costs two. A key pressed during either pass runs the **previous** context's command and cannot be undone. **Every test that mutates a source component must advance at least two frames before asserting on routing**, and no test may assert same-frame routing.
- **Any test this phase adds must live in `src/` under `#[cfg(test)]`, not in `crates/hana/tests/`.** hana is bin-only (`crates/hana/src/main.rs`, no `lib.rs`) and `verify.sh test hana` pins `--lib`/`--bins` from cargo metadata, so a `tests/` target compiles under `verify.sh lint hana` (which passes `--tests`) and **never executes**. A misplaced test looks healthy and proves nothing.

**Lock-only context resolution — add `InteractionContext::DimensionLock`.** Resolved on 2026-08-01 as part of the authorized Phase 16–18 recommendations.

Actual problem:
Phase 18 authors hana's shipped `keymap.default.jsonc`, and the Phase 7 constraint it inherits used `space` → `dimension_lock::pause_tracking` "under `dimension_lock`" as the worked example. There is no such condition. Phase 14 shipped exactly two variants — `Resting` and `DimensionLockRotation` (`crates/hana/src/input/interaction_context.rs:15-28`) — and the loader accepts only their snake_case names. `DimensionLockRotation` additionally requires the rotation gizmo to be **expanded**, so a binding meant for "a lock is engaged" has no name to attach to, and hana ships with either a startup-fatal default file or no lock-gated bindings at all. The variant must exist by the end of this phase for Phase 18 to have a name to bind against.

What exists now:
- Two variants, and (after this phase) one declared rule setting `DimensionLockRotation` when a `DimensionLock` and an expanded `RotateAffordance` both exist, else `Resting`.
- Phase 14's scope was cut to exactly this pair by user decision on 2026-07-31, on the grounds that `docs/hana/computed_state.md` names one concrete context and says not to build the framework speculatively. That same document's closing line — "Seed the pattern with the first feature that needs it" — is why adding a variant here is a decision rather than a violation.
- Phase 14's own scope bullet already anticipates this: "Later phases add variants as they migrate the shortcuts that need them."

Add a `DimensionLock` variant to the enum plus one more `.when()` entry — declared **after** the `DimensionLockRotation` rule, since first match wins and the rotation context is the more specific one. This makes `dimension_lock` a real condition name and the Phase 7 example correct as written:

  ```rust
  .when(InteractionContext::DimensionLockRotation, /* rotation rule, declared first */)
  .when(InteractionContext::DimensionLock, any_with_component::<DimensionLock>())
  ```

It is the first feature that needs the broader context — exactly the trigger `computed_state.md` names. Under Phase 14b the ordering requirement is structural rather than a branch to write by hand: declare the rotation rule first and the lock-only rule second. Add two tests covering lock-only selection and the priority of the more specific rotation context.

**Selection-correlation resolution — require the lock and expanded affordance to share a selection.** Resolved on 2026-08-01 as part of the authorized Phase 16–18 recommendations.

Actual problem:
The rule table asks two independent "does any entity match?" questions, but `DimensionLock` lives on a selection entity (`crates/hana/src/selection/dimension_lock/lock_state.rs:221`) and `RotateAffordance` carries a `selection: Entity` field naming the selection it belongs to (`crates/hana/src/selection/rotate/affordance.rs:52-55`). With two things selected — a lock on A and an expanded rotation gizmo on B — the context must not report `DimensionLockRotation` because no single selection is in that state. The broader `DimensionLock` context still applies to A. hana supports multi-select (`Super+A` selects all), so this is reachable, not theoretical.

What exists now:
- Two uncorrelated condition combinators joined by `.and_then(…)`.
- Phase 14's seven tests all use a single selection, so none of them fails on this.

Replace the uncorrelated combinator pair with a custom condition system. Phase 14b accepts any `fn(...) -> bool` system as a rule, so this is a declaration change, not a new mechanism:

  ```rust
  fn lock_and_gizmo_share_a_selection(
      locks:  Query<(), With<DimensionLock>>,
      gizmos: Query<&RotateAffordance, With<Expanded>>,
  ) -> bool {
      gizmos.iter().any(|gizmo| locks.contains(gizmo.selection))
  }
  ```

  Plus a test that spawns a lock on A and an expanded gizmo on B and asserts `DimensionLock`.
Add a test that spawns a lock on selection A and an expanded rotation affordance for selection B and asserts `DimensionLock`. The rule may report `DimensionLockRotation` only when one selection supplies both facts; the general lock rule remains active because a lock is still engaged.

**Acceptance gate:** `verify.sh check|test|lint hana` green. The eight existing tests are rebuilt on the plugin, the two lock-only expectations are updated for `DimensionLock`, and the new cross-selection case passes. `State<InteractionContext>` tracks the same single-selection world facts it did before this phase, adds the lock-only state, and rejects cross-selection false matches.

Two gate bullets that exist because Phase 14b's diagnostics are quieter than earlier phases':
- **Zero `DiagnosticKind::Context` entries of *any* severity** in `KeymapLoadFailures::all_diagnostics()` after startup. "No error-level diagnostic" is **not** sufficient: Phase 14b's three startup validations (unreachable variant, fallback-targeting rule stripped, duplicate target) all emit `DiagnosticSeverity::Advisory`, and `retain_context_diagnostics` logs advisories at `warn!`, not `error!`. A masked or unreachable rule in hana's table would pass an error-level check silently. Both `Diagnostic.severity` and `retained_diagnostics` are `pub` (`crates/hana_rubric/src/diagnostic.rs:72`, `:92`).
- **A real app reports `ActiveCondition::is_initialized() == true`, `State<InteractionContext> == Resting`, and zero context diagnostics after two updates.** `handle()` and `ConditionHandle` are crate-private to `hana_rubric`, so hana cannot assert them directly. This public combination still proves the context registered rather than silently leaving every key dead after a duplicate-registration failure.

### Retrospective

**What worked:**
- `EnhancedInputAppPlugin` now installs one ordered `DerivedContext<InteractionContext>` table and no longer registers a duplicate state or hand-written updater.
- Nine behavioral/contract tests prove startup initialization, zero context diagnostics, lock-only fallback, rotation priority, and selection correlation; the fresh dual review approved the corrected diff.

**What deviated from the plan:**
- The original cross-selection sentence still expected `Resting`, which contradicted the newly approved broader `DimensionLock` rule. The Work Order and test now correctly expect `DimensionLock`: an unrelated expanded gizmo cannot select rotation, but it also cannot hide a real lock.

**Surprises:**
- Adding the broader context changed the old correlation regression's correct fallback even though the correlation invariant itself stayed the same.

**Implications for remaining phases:**
- Phase 18 can safely bind lock commands under both `dimension_lock` and `dimension_lock_rotation`; cross-selection worlds retain the broader lock context.
- Phase 21's active-context surface must expose all three shipped names: `resting`, `dimension_lock`, and `dimension_lock_rotation`.

### Phase 17 Review

- Phase 18 now names the exact embedded-default expression, tests B and P through both lock contexts, preserves `DimensionLock` for cross-selection worlds, and advances two frames after source-component changes before routing assertions.
- Phase 19 now names the held-input lookup as a lookup outcome and keeps all context selection inside the compiled keymap rather than branching in held-action wiring.
- Phase 20's existing authoritative-path decision now includes removal of the empty permanent context, its registration/spawn, and the dead `Start<OpenCommandPalette>` observer if the exclusive handler is approved.
- Phase 21's active-context recommendation now uses explicit `AwaitingContext`, `GlobalRouting`, and `ResolvedCondition` states; its transition gate covers all three shipped context names after the required two-frame delay.
- Phase 21 also records the later decision needed to make `KeymapPaths` an inserted runtime resource and define missing-user-file behavior before the file-opening boundary.
- Phase 21's remote-invocation decision now returns one semantic outcome for invoked, unknown, and unsupported held commands without directing callers to the panic-prone stock reflected-event path.
- Phase 22's sequencing recommendation now uses contiguous Phase 22 and Phase 23 numbers and explicitly inventories nateroids `src/main.rs` before migration.

### Phase 18 — hana: `global_shortcuts.rs` becomes the default keymap · status: done

**Blocked by:** **Phase 16** (which chooses the 33 command ids this file must name) and **Phase 17** (which supplies the condition names to bind under). Phase 15 cleared `G2`. (`G1` superseded — work on `feature/rubric` in `/Users/natemccoy/rust/hana_rubric`, reconcile before dispatch.)

#### Work Order

**Goal:** the bindings live in a JSONC file instead of Rust, shipped as an embedded default.

**Spec:**

**`global_shortcuts.rs` stops being the keymap.** Its hardcoded `spawn_key` / `spawn_shift_key` / `spawn_platform_key` / `spawn_binding` calls (`spawn_actions`, `spawn_switches`, `spawn_commands`, `spawn_platform_commands`, lines 46–108) move into the embedded default `.jsonc` that ships via `include_str!`.

**Extend the plugin wiring Phase 17 established** — the same `add_plugins` call gains `.with_app_name("hana")` and `.with_defaults(include_str!("../../assets/keymap.default.jsonc"))`. Phase 17 left them off deliberately so the context could land without a keymap file; adding them here is what turns on path resolution, the companion files, and the disk worker.

**Every `spawn_platform_key` call site becomes `secondary-`, never `platform-`.** After Phase 11a the word "platform" means two different things in two APIs of the same crate, and getting this backwards silently moves every migrated shortcut to the Super key on Linux and Windows. `Keybindings::spawn_platform_key::<A>()` routes through `PlatformShortcutMode::current()` (`crates/hana_rubric/src/command/keybindings.rs:86-129`, `:188-190`) and binds physical Super on macOS but physical **Control** elsewhere — which is exactly what the new `secondary` token means. `Modifiers::with_platform` and the keystroke spelling `platform-` / `cmd-` / `super-` / `win-` mean physical Super on **every** platform. So a `spawn_platform_key` migrated to `cmd-s` keeps working on macOS and breaks on Linux, where it used to fire on Ctrl+S; migrated to `secondary-s` it is correct everywhere. **Translate every one of them to `secondary-`**, and check the resulting `.jsonc` for any remaining `cmd-` or `platform-` spelling that came from a `spawn_platform_*` call — each is a bug. Bindings that were `spawn_key` / `spawn_shift_key` carry no platform modifier and translate literally. **Do not delete `crates/hana_rubric/src/platform_shortcut_mode.rs`.** An earlier revision ordered its removal "once `keybindings.rs` goes". `keybindings.rs` does not go — this phase keeps `Keybindings::new::<ModifySelection>` and its `ActionSettings` — and `PlatformShortcutMode` has two live consumers there besides `spawn_platform_key`: the primary-modifier bindings at `command/keybindings.rs:86` and `spawn_key`'s modifier blocking at `:117`. The type is `pub(crate)` and absent from the public API, so nothing about it contradicts `Modifiers::with_platform` from outside the crate. Retiring it is separate work with its own call-site audit, not a side effect of this migration.

What remains in Rust: `GlobalShortcutsContext`, `PermanentShortcutsContext` (Phase 20), the two held actions' entity wiring (Phase 19), and Escape's physical action plus its two consumers pending the end-of-plan Escape review.

**IME suppression resets runtime-owned physical keymap state.** `KeymapSystems::Route` remains disabled for the entire open IME session. On both the closed-to-open and open-to-closed transitions, hana runs the public `hana_rubric::reset_physical_input(&mut World)` entry point. Its implementation lives in `crates/hana_rubric/src/keymap/runtime/dispatch.rs`: it cancels pending sequences, clears physical held sources while preserving semantic-event held sources, flushes resulting `CustomInput` transitions, and replaces the inhibited-key set with the keys currently down. The close transition therefore removes keys released during suppression while keeping keys still down inhibited until their release. Hana does not own a parallel held-key resource and does not route presses while the IME is open.

**Do not strand the two held actions or Escape between phases.** Keep a named legacy spawn path for `ModifySelection`, `FreezeTracking`, and `Escape`, including their existing physical bindings and `ActionSettings`, when the other `spawn_*` functions are removed. Phase 19 owns replacing the two held physical bindings with `Binding::Custom`. Escape stays on its existing physical action because one `Start<Escape>` currently fans out to both `EscapeRequested` and `CancelRotateEvent`; migrating only one event would break the other behavior. The end-of-plan Escape review owns deciding whether to consolidate that fan-out or model it through contexts.

**Translate each existing binding faithfully** — this is a behavior-preserving migration:
- `spawn_key::<A>()` → the bare key string (it blocks all modifiers today; the matcher's canonical `Keystroke` with an empty modifier set is the equivalent).
- `spawn_shift_key::<A>()` → `"shift-<key>"`.
- ~~`spawn_platform_key::<A>()` → `"cmd-<key>"`, which the parser folds into control off macOS inside `Modifiers::with_platform()`.~~ **Wrong — retired by Phase 11a and contradicted by this phase's own line above: translate to `"secondary-<key>"`.** `cmd-`/`platform-` now mean physical Super on every platform, so a `cmd-` translation ships every migrated shortcut on physical Super off macOS. There is no control collapse left to rely on. (`PlatformShortcutMode` lives at `crates/hana_rubric/src/platform_shortcut_mode.rs` as `pub(crate)` after Phase 4c — hana cannot see it.)
- `spawn_binding::<A, B>()` → whatever chord the bundle expresses.

**B and P are lock-context bindings, in both lock variants.** Bind `dimension_lock::toggle_coupling` (`B`) and `dimension_lock::freeze_tracking` (`P`) under both `dimension_lock` and `dimension_lock_rotation`. They are meaningful whenever a lock exists, and `dimension_lock_rotation` replaces rather than inherits the sibling `dimension_lock` block when the expanded rotation controls are active. Leaving them global would make the new lock-only context unnecessary and allow inert lock commands during `resting`; putting them in only one lock variant would break them in the other.

**Defaults are embedded and always load from the embedded copy, never from disk** — they must load with no config directory at all, the same constraint the recovery palette carries in `tool-graph.md`.

The default file carries **explanatory comments** (a use for choosing JSONC) including the documentation of the unremappable recovery chord required by D8.

**Escape resolution — preserve its complete Rust path for this migration.** D3 deliberately left this open, and the user resolved it on 2026-08-01: `escape.rs` continues to try `EscapeStep::{CloseOutputWindow, CloseInspectors, ClearSelection}` in order, stopping when the first step returns `EscapeHandled::Yes`; the separate rotation consumer continues to receive `CancelRotateEvent` from the same physical `Escape` action. Phase 18 does not put Escape in the JSONC default because a command declaration names one semantic event while the current physical action fans out to two. `init/hana_catalyst` also changed `escape.rs` (+18/−2) and made it call `hana_catalyst::Graph::disconnect`, pulling a `hana_catalyst` dependency into `input/` — preserve that landed behavior. A review task at the end of this plan reconsiders whether a later context model can express the full fan-out and ordering cleanly.

**Files:**
- `hana/crates/hana/src/input/global_shortcuts.rs` — delete the four `spawn_*` functions; keep the contexts; detect IME suppression transitions; expand routing, override, and held-input tests
- `hana/crates/hana/assets/keymap.default.jsonc` — create the embedded default keymap
- `hana/crates/hana/src/input/mod.rs` — add `.with_app_name("hana")` and `.with_defaults(include_str!("../../assets/keymap.default.jsonc"))` to the `KeymapPlugin` call Phase 17 added; keep `KeymapSystems::Route` disabled while IME suppression is open and run `reset_physical_input` on both transitions
- `hana/crates/hana/src/input/escape.rs` — keep both the ordered handler and its physical action route unchanged
- `hana/crates/hana/src/switches.rs` — add local event-only `bind_command_system!` and `bind_command_switch!` helpers used after one-shot action entities disappear
- `hana/crates/hana/src/movable/core/app_extension.rs`, `movable/core/mod.rs`, and `movable/mod.rs` — remove the now-dead `A: InputAction` generic and `request_spawn_movable_input<T, A>` observer from `UserMovablePlugin`; retain the typed `SpawnMovableRequested<T>` path reached by the Phase 16 semantic-event bridges
- `hana/crates/hana/src/tool/light_block.rs` and `tool/metal_sphere.rs` — instantiate the simplified user-movable plugin without a spawn-action type; keep the Phase 16 event observers as the only spawn-command route
- `/Users/natemccoy/rust/hana_rubric/Cargo.toml` — add workspace entries for `jsonschema` and `serde_json_lenient`
- `/Users/natemccoy/rust/hana_rubric/crates/hana/Cargo.toml` — add `jsonschema` and `serde_json_lenient` as dev-dependencies; `serde_json` already remains a normal hana dependency
- `crates/hana_rubric/src/keymap/runtime/dispatch.rs`, its module re-exports, `crates/hana_rubric/src/lib.rs`, and `prelude.rs` — add and test the public `reset_physical_input(&mut World)` API
- the 19 `bind_action_system!` call sites — convert 16 one-shot call sites to the local event-only `bind_command_system!`; retain the two `Escape` call sites and `OpenCommandPalette`'s action-start path
- the 11 `bind_action_switch!` call sites — convert all to the local event-only `bind_command_switch!`

**Constraints from prior phases:**
- Phase 16 relocated all **33** declarations and removed the re-exports from `input/mod.rs`; the ids to write into the default file are the ones it chose.
- **Read the final ids from all 20 declaration sources, not from stale examples elsewhere in this plan:** `camera/{editor_camera,flyover,grid_gizmo,settings}.rs`, `light/mod.rs`, `mouse_position.rs`, `persistence.rs`, `selection/{animation,click_select,operations}.rs`, `selection/dimension_lock/{mod,position}.rs`, `selection/entity/mod.rs`, `selection/rotate/mod.rs`, `tool/{command_palette,light_block,metal_sphere}.rs`, `transport.rs`, `voice/mod.rs`, and `input/escape.rs`. The Phase 18 default contains the 30 commands the current keystroke model can express. Exclude `selection::handle_escape` and `inspect::open_command_palette`, whose physical recovery paths remain in Rust, and temporarily exclude `selection::modify_selection`: its shipped binding is a bare Shift modifier, while the current runtime deliberately treats modifier edges as state only and never routes them as keystrokes. Phase 19 owns the representation decision before it replaces the legacy action. In particular the reviewed catalog uses `camera::reset_home`, `camera::focus_target`, `camera::toggle_focus_visualization`, and `selection::toggle_rotation_controls`.
- **Phase 17 supplies the condition names.** Only the snake_case names of `InteractionContext`'s variants are accepted — `resting`, `dimension_lock_rotation`, and `dimension_lock`. A name that is not a variant is a startup-fatal default.
- **Phase 17's derived state reaches routing after two application updates following a source-component mutation.** Tests that add/remove a lock or expanded rotation affordance must advance twice before asserting which binding routes.
- **A lock on selection A plus an expanded rotation affordance for selection B resolves to `dimension_lock`.** The unrelated affordance cannot select the rotation context, but it cannot hide the real lock either.
- Phase 12's `Plugin::finish` **rejects any shipped default that does not resolve** — a typo in the default file is a hard startup failure, not a warning. **Two qualifications from the shipped code.** First, the rejection is suppressed when `RegistryValidationFailed` is present — that is, when command declaration validation or context registration itself failed, leaving an empty registry against which nothing could resolve. In that state a typo in hana's shipped default is *not* fatal, and the app routes no input at all for the unrelated reason. Second, a failing shipped default makes `finish_assembly` return at `crates/hana_rubric/src/keymap_plugin.rs:185-187`, **before** `KeymapPaths::new`, schema generation, and `start_disk_worker` — so that run writes **no** `keymap.jsonc` stub, no `keymap.default.jsonc`, and no `keymap.schema.json`. Evaluate the companion-file gate bullets on a *healthy* launch; the failure case's observable is the absence of all three plus an error-level log.
- **Any test touching `KeymapPaths` must isolate `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` or it writes into the developer's real `~/.config/hana/`.** `KeymapPaths::new` consults that variable (`crates/hana_rubric/src/disk/paths.rs:83`). Copy the isolation pattern from `crates/hana_rubric/tests/keymap_companion_files.rs:26-32,146-165` — an `unsafe env::set_var` behind a process-wide mutex, since env mutation is global. This phase is where it starts mattering: Phase 17's plugin shape never reached path resolution, this one does.
- **Any test this phase adds must live in `src/` under `#[cfg(test)]`, not in `crates/hana/tests/`.** hana is bin-only, and `verify.sh test hana` pins `--lib`/`--bins`, so a `tests/` target compiles under lint and **never executes**.
- Phase 6: the file root is an **object** with `$schema` and `bindings`; blocks are ordered; later blocks override earlier ones at the same `(condition, sequence)` identity.
- Phase 7: a binding with a condition beats one without for the same keystroke. The mechanism: a global `space` plus a `space` under `dimension_lock_rotation` resolves with no ranking.
- Phase 9's `keymap.default.jsonc` companion is generated from these same embedded bytes and published `0444` as a read-only reference.

**Observer resolution — remove the dead action-start halves.** The user chose this direction on 2026-08-01. Add local `bind_command_system!(app, Event, command_fn)` and `bind_command_switch!(app, Event, switch)` helpers that register only the event consumers, then convert every command or switch whose `Action<A>` entity this phase removes. Do not leave `On<Start<A>>` observers that can never fire. **Exclude the two `Escape` consumers and `OpenCommandPalette`:** their physical action paths remain live for the reasons above and until Phase 20, respectively. Correct Phase 11's historical claim in the Phase 18 retrospective: the static action-start chain remains through Phase 17, then the JSONC keymap becomes the construction route for migrated one-shot commands.

The same dead-half rule applies to the two generic movable spawners. Once `SpawnLightBlock` and `SpawnMetalSphere` action entities disappear, `UserMovablePlugin<T, A>`'s `On<Start<A>>` observer can never fire. Remove that action generic and observer; the `SpawnLightBlockEvent` / `SpawnMetalSphereEvent` observers Phase 16 added remain and forward into the existing typed `SpawnMovableRequested<T>` message.

**Acceptance gate:** `verify.sh check|test|lint hana_rubric` and `verify.sh check|test|lint hana` green. Every shortcut that worked before this phase works after it — walk the full list from the pre-migration `spawn_*` calls. `ModifySelection`, `FreezeTracking`, and Escape still work through their preserved legacy physical bindings; remapping the two held physical actions is explicitly deferred to Phase 19, and Escape migration is deferred to the end-of-plan review. B and P route in both `dimension_lock` and `dimension_lock_rotation`; a cross-selection lock/expanded-affordance world routes the broader `dimension_lock` bindings, with two application updates after each source-component mutation before the routing assertion. The valid user override changes B from coupling to X-axis lock while an unaffected P press/release still drives the held command's semantic `CustomInput` true/false path; invalid entries remain rejected with the shipped B binding active. The pending-sequence IME test first proves `g h` dispatches X-axis lock, resets the count, then proves `g`, an IME open/close, and `h` do not dispatch while B remains suppressed during IME and resumes afterward. Held-input coverage starts P before IME, observes `CustomInput(false)` on open, releases P during suppression without a restart after close, accepts a later fresh P press, and proves a key held through close remains inhibited until release/reset. The `hana_rubric` unit test separately proves sequence cancellation, physical-source release and false flushing, semantic-event source preservation, and inhibited-key refresh. **Two expected differences, both from Phase 11's modifier model, are not regressions:** a shortcut whose keymap spelling is `cmd-`/`super-`/`win-` now means the *physical* Super key on Linux and Windows rather than Control, so any migrated binding that must stay Control off macOS is spelled `secondary-`; and `ctrl-` bindings now match physical Control on every platform, which they could not do off macOS before. Check each migrated spelling against those two rules during the walk rather than assuming a one-to-one carry-over. Startup succeeds with no config directory present **and all three companion files appear with no error-level diagnostic** — "startup succeeds" alone is true even when `allow_default_failures` suppressed every default rejection (`crates/hana_rubric/src/keymap_plugin.rs:177-184`). `~/.config/hana/keymap.jsonc`, `keymap.default.jsonc`, and `keymap.schema.json` appear after first launch; the stub is not overwritten on a second launch. A user file overriding one key changes exactly that key.

**hana's shipped `keymap.default.jsonc` validates against the `keymap.schema.json` generated by the same run** — `finish` resolves the shipped default against the registries, which does not catch a root or block member the schema forbids. This is the only remaining place the plan asserts what Phase 13b built, and it is in the consumer, where none of 13b's machinery is reachable. **The route:**

- `schema_bytes` is `pub(crate)` (`crates/hana_rubric/src/keymap/mod.rs:23`) and `lib.rs` exports no schema accessor, so hana cannot generate the schema in-process. It reads the published `keymap.schema.json` off disk after a full `finish()` plus a disk-worker run — the same shape as `crates/hana_rubric/tests/keymap_companion_files.rs`, which is the file to copy from.
- hana's manifest declares **neither** `jsonschema` nor `serde_json_lenient` (`/Users/natemccoy/rust/hana_rubric/Cargo.toml`). Both must be added as dev-dependencies: the validator, and the comment-tolerant reader — hana's shipped default carries explanatory comments by this phase's own Spec, and `serde_json` chokes on them.
- Copy Phase 13b's two conversion helpers (`crates/hana_rubric/src/keymap/schema.rs`, test module): the validator operates on `serde_json::Value`, so parse with `serde_json_lenient::from_str::<serde_json::Value>` and convert the schema with `serde_json::to_value`.

**This phase's shipped default is authored around comments, validated in Zed only.** Phase 13b's editor pass confirmed comments produce no diagnostics and the relative `$schema` resolves — but in Zed alone; VS Code was not tested and is not planned. If hana ever needs to support VS Code users editing their keymap, that is unverified ground and this comment-heavy default file is what would have to change.

#### Retrospective

**What worked:**
- The embedded JSONC default now owns all 30 commands the shipped keystroke model can represent, while the live Rust paths for `ModifySelection`, `FreezeTracking`, Escape, and the recovery palette remain available for their later phases.
- The invalid-user-entry policy is behavioral: a rejected override leaves the corresponding shipped binding active. The live Hana smoke proved both a healthy default `3` binding and the same fallback after an invalid override.
- Event-only command/switch consumers and typed `SpawnMovableRequested<T>` requests removed the dead action-start halves without disturbing the retained legacy actions.

**What deviated from the plan:**
- The IME boundary required a new public `reset_physical_input(&mut World)` API in `hana_rubric`, rather than being expressible only by disabling Hana's global shortcut context.
- Tests that need a window entity use feature-free entity ids; `hana_rubric` intentionally does not enable Bevy's window feature.

**Surprises:**
- The first corrected IME reset still ran before routing but was not explicitly after `InputSystems`; a key queued in the same frame as IME close could therefore evade inhibition. The final schedule orders the reset and activity synchronization after input production, and the test closes IME and queues the key before one update.
- Splitting the held-through-close test exposed that it had depended on state established by another test section. A local press/release baseline made the proof independent.

**Implications for remaining phases:**
- Phase 19 inherits one physical-state owner in `hana_rubric`; Hana's IME activity system must drop its remaining key inspection when the two held actions move to `Binding::Custom`.
- Phase 19 still needs the bare-Shift representation decision before `ModifySelection` can leave its legacy binding.
- Phase 20 retains the recovery palette's current physical action until its earlier exclusive handler becomes the sole route, and the end-of-plan Escape review still owns Escape's two-consumer fan-out.
- Phase 21 can rely on `keymap.jsonc`, `keymap.default.jsonc`, and `keymap.schema.json` being materialized from embedded defaults without overwriting an existing user file.

#### Phase 18 Review

- **Phase 19** — expanded the existing bare-Shift decision across the keystroke model, schema, key-edge routing, and held-source behavior; its acceptance gate now covers left/right modifier reference counting and chord non-duplication if approved. Renamed the held lookup success variant to `RegisteredHeldInput` so the guarantee is visible at the call site.
- **Phase 20** — the recovery handler now runs after `InputSystems` and before keymap routing, with same-frame and IME-open coverage. The embedded-default comment is recorded as already shipped by Phase 18 and needs verification only.
- **Phase 21** — added a pending three-phase split for library state, BRP routing/context reads, and repair UI. Path publication now recommends an always-present `KeymapPathAvailability::{Resolved, Unavailable}` resource rather than an absent `KeymapPaths` resource on failure.
- **Phase 22** — expanded the existing publication split decision to require a real Phase 23 Work Order and isolated releases from `feature/rubric`; corrected the repository homepage claim and made the `hana_rubric` release pin mandatory because `hana_diegetic` uses it normally.
- **Phase 22** — deferred whether the Escape ownership review moves before publication, so a possible routing/API change cannot follow the first public release unnoticed.
- **Deferred cleanup** — refreshed the `document.rs` extraction boundary against the current 1614-line file.

### Phase 19 — hana: held actions on `Binding::Custom`, and one owner for held state · status: done

**Blocked by:** **Phase 16** (declares the held commands) and **Phase 18** (preserves their legacy physical actions until this phase replaces them). Phase 15 cleared `G2`. (`G1` superseded — work on `feature/rubric` in `/Users/natemccoy/rust/hana_rubric`, reconcile before dispatch.)

#### Work Order

**Goal:** the two hold-to-act commands are remappable through the keymap without respawning their action entities, and only one system tracks held keys.

**Spec:**

**The two hold-to-act commands** (R3):
- `ModifySelection` — polled continuously in `selection/click_select.rs:34` and `selection/box_select/drag.rs:54`.
- `FreezeTracking` — `Start`/`Complete` observers in `selection/dimension_lock/position.rs:50`.

`FreezeTracking` already appears in the Phase 18 keymap. Add `ModifySelection` with the
decided bare-`shift` representation below. Both are freely remappable to **any single keystroke
including modifiers** (`f`, `shift-f`, `cmd-shift-f`). Binding either to a multi-key sequence is
rejected per-binding at load — Phase 7 already enforces this from the `Held` capability.

**Bare modifier-family bindings — decided 2026-08-02.** Add canonical modifier-family
primary triggers (`shift`, `ctrl`, `alt`, and `secondary`) and use `shift` for
`selection::modify_selection` in the embedded default. This is a library/runtime extension,
not a Hana-only spelling change: replace `Keystroke`'s assumption that every routed stroke has
a non-modifier `KeyCode` with a semantic primary trigger that is either an ordinary key or a
modifier family. Route left and right physical Shift as one logical held input with press
reference counting, and release it only when both physical keys are up. Modifier presses remain
state-only while a normal key is primary, so `shift-f` dispatches only `shift-f`, not both
`shift` and `shift-f`. This preserves Hana's shipped behavior while making the hold remappable
without exposing left/right physical-key duplication in the user file.

**Each keeps one statically spawned `Action<A>` bound to `Binding::Custom(id)`, where `id` is *read* from `CommandRegistry::custom_input()` — this phase creates no `CustomInput`.** The matcher writes that input's `ActionValue` before enhanced-input evaluates, so `Start`/`Complete` pairing, `require_reset`, and blocking remain enhanced-input's job. **Remapping a held key changes which physical stroke drives the custom input — not the action entity.**

**Make the approved held-prefix policy real in `hana_rubric`, not only in hana's gate.** `keymap/merged.rs` currently removes a held single-stroke binding and every longer sequence sharing its prefix. Change the merge to preserve source-layer provenance through conflict resolution: when a valid shipped-default hold conflicts only with a later user-authored sequence, reject the user sequence and retain the default hold. A conflict entirely within one source still rejects every participant. An explicit user tombstone of the default identity removes that earlier hold before prefix validation, allowing the longer user sequence to become live. The diagnostic is attached to the rejected user binding and names both commands and both source files.

**Catalyst introduced `ActionSettings { require_reset: true }` around `Keybindings::new::<ModifySelection>`.** Reconcile: `require_reset` changes whether removed bindings enter `PendingBindings`, and this phase stops removing bindings at all. Verify the setting still expresses what it was added for against the landed code, and record the finding.

**Held-key state gets exactly one owner: `hana_rubric`'s `keymap/runtime/` (`mod.rs`, `dispatch.rs`, `held.rs`, `key_edge.rs`)** (G3).

**The active `InteractionContext` is consumed only through the compiled keymap.** The held-action entity wiring reads the registry's custom input and never branches on `Resting`, `DimensionLock`, or `DimensionLockRotation`; context-specific behavior belongs in the keymap blocks Phase 18 authored.

Catalyst's `sync_global_shortcuts_activity` — a `PreUpdate` system ordered `.before(EnhancedInputSystems::Prepare)` — currently does two things: deactivates `GlobalShortcutsContext` while a `hana_diegetic::ImeInputBlocker` session is open, **and** holds it inactive until every held shortcut key is released.

**Keep the first, delete the second.** The second duplicates the runtime's held-source reference counts and already-down-key inhibition. Two systems in two schedule slots each keeping their own copy of "which shortcut keys are held", with nothing synchronizing them: the runtime can see a key as released and start matching while catalyst's system still believes it is held and keeps the context off.

After this phase: **context activity answers "should this context see input at all"; the runtime answers "given input, what matches, and what is still held."** Different questions, no shared state. `sync_global_shortcuts_activity` stops inspecting keys entirely — IME open means off, IME closed means on.

**Files:**
- `crates/hana/src/input/global_shortcuts.rs` — the two held actions' `Binding::Custom` wiring; strip the key-inspection half of `sync_global_shortcuts_activity` at `:79-105`. **Also simplify its run condition `global_shortcuts_activity_needs_sync` (`:107-112`)**, which today returns `input_blocker.is_changed() || contexts.iter().any(|activity| !**activity)`. The second disjunct exists only to keep re-polling while a held key holds the context off; once the key-inspection half is gone it is vestigial and the condition reduces to `input_blocker.is_changed()`. This phase's behavioral gate passes either way, so if it is not named here it will simply be left behind.
- `crates/hana/assets/keymap.default.jsonc` — add the shipped `shift` binding for `selection::modify_selection`
- `hana/crates/hana/src/selection/click_select.rs`, `selection/box_select/drag.rs`, `selection/dimension_lock/position.rs` — verify the polling and observer sites still work unchanged (they should; the action entity is stable)
- `crates/hana_rubric/src/keymap/merged.rs` — preserve the valid earlier-source held binding, reject only a conflicting later-source sequence, and add the explicit-tombstone and same-source cases
- `crates/hana_rubric/src/keystroke/mod.rs`, `keystroke/sequence.rs`, and `keymap/schema.rs` — represent and validate an ordinary-key or modifier-family primary trigger without confusing a bare modifier with a modifier on another key
- `crates/hana_rubric/src/keymap/runtime/key_edge.rs` and `runtime/dispatch.rs` — route modifier-family held input with left/right press reference counting and preserve normal chord matching
- `crates/hana_rubric/src/command/{registry,mod}.rs`, `crates/hana_rubric/src/{lib,prelude}.rs` — replace the ambiguous held-input `Option` lookup with its semantic public outcome and update callers/tests

**Constraints from prior phases:**
- Phase 11 owns held-source refcounts, already-down-key inhibition, and pending cancellation, and writes `CustomInputs` values **before `EnhancedInputSystems::Update`**. `sync_global_shortcuts_activity` runs `.before(EnhancedInputSystems::Prepare)` — earlier — so the ordering does not conflict.
- Phase 11 verified `CustomInput`, `CustomInputs::register_input()`, and `Binding::Custom` are public in `bevy_enhanced_input` 0.26.0 (`src/context/input_reader/custom.rs`, `src/binding.rs:112`).
- Phase 11's reason for this design, from source: removing an action's `ActionOf<C>` resets it and emits `Complete` from `Fired` (`context.rs:369`); removed bindings enter `PendingBindings` only with `require_reset` (`:553`). Respawning entities while a key is held completes the held action and can immediately reactivate its replacement from the still-held key.
- Phase 16 declared both commands with the `Held` capability; Phase 7 rejects sequence bindings for them at load.
- **The `CustomInput` already exists — do not allocate one.** Phase 4b allocates one per held command during registry construction. Replace `CommandRegistry::custom_input(&CommandId) -> Option<CustomInput>` with `HeldCommandLookupOutcome::{RegisteredHeldInput(CustomInput), KnownNonHeld, UnknownCommand}`: the current `Option` conflates a known one-shot command with an unknown id, and the result describes a registry lookup rather than pretending every result is an input. Read that result from `Res<CommandRegistry>` while building the key with `CommandId::try_from(ModifySelectionEvent::ID)` — which needs `hana_rubric::KeymapCommand` in scope for the associated const. The registry resource is inserted by Phase 12's `Plugin::finish`, which runs before `Startup`, so the spawn system can read it.
- **Do not register a `Begin`/`End` observer.** `CommandRegistry::initialize` already added one per held command; a second would double-write the same input.
- **This phase's "one owner for held state" means one *consumer*, not one writer.** Phase 11 counts two held sources — the physical-key matcher and Phase 4b's event observer — and drives the input from their sum. This phase wires `Binding::Custom` to that already-shared input; it does not arbitrate between the producers.
- **State the empty-registry behavior.** When command declaration validation fails, Phase 12's `finish_assembly` inserts `CommandRegistry::empty()` and the app starts anyway, so `CommandRegistry::custom_input()` returns `HeldCommandLookupOutcome::UnknownCommand` for both held commands and there is no id to bind. Skip the `Binding::Custom` spawn in that case and rely on the diagnostic `finish_assembly` already recorded — do not synthesize an id, and do not panic.
- **From Phase 16's held-prefix decision:** a user file adding `f g` against a default held `f` rejects only `f g`, keeps the default `f` live, and produces one diagnostic naming both bindings and both source files. A second case tombstones `f` explicitly and proves `f g` then becomes live.
**Every test this phase adds must live in `src/` under `#[cfg(test)]`, not in `crates/hana/tests/`.** hana is bin-only (`crates/hana/src/main.rs`, no `lib.rs`) and `verify.sh test hana` pins `--lib`/`--bins` from cargo metadata, so a `tests/` target compiles under `verify.sh lint hana` (which passes `--tests`) and **never executes** — it looks healthy and proves nothing. Same constraint as Phases 14, 15, and 17.

**Acceptance gate:** `verify.sh check|test|lint hana_rubric` and `verify.sh check|test|lint hana` green, with separate checkpoints in `bevy_hana_rubric` and `/Users/natemccoy/rust/hana_rubric`. Manual and automated: holding the bound key drives `ModifySelection`'s polled sites and `FreezeTracking`'s observers exactly as before; remapping either in the user keymap changes which key drives it, with no `Complete`/restart flicker; binding either to `g f` is rejected at load with the hold-to-act message; a default held `f` plus user `f g` retains `f` and rejects only `f g` with both commands and source files named; explicitly tombstoning default `f` makes user `f g` live; a same-source collision rejects every participant. The semantic held-input lookup distinguishes a registered held input, a known non-held command, and an unknown id. Either physical Shift starts the same logical hold, releasing one Shift while the other remains down does not end it, and `shift-f` dispatches only its chord. An IME session still suppresses global shortcuts; a shortcut key held across an IME open/close does not fire spuriously on close. `sync_global_shortcuts_activity` no longer reads key state — assert this behaviorally (the system produces the same activity value regardless of which keys are held) rather than by `rg`, which checks source text and not mechanism.

**Implementation summary:** Catalyst's `ActionSettings { require_reset: true }` is still required by the physical `Escape` action so a key held across IME context reactivation cannot restart the action. The setting is not required by `ModifySelection` or `FreezeTracking`: those two stable held actions now use only their `Binding::Custom` inputs and are not respawned or rebound when the keymap changes.

- Phase 4b owns the `CommandId` → `CustomInput` lookup; the hana-side `Action<A>` spawn reads it rather than allocating. Two producers write that input value — Phase 11's matcher for a physical key, and the command's own `Begin`/`End` semantic event for BRP, scripts, and tests (with the same Phase 11b caveat Phase 16 records: the short-id BRP route delivers `Begin` only) — so this phase's single owner of held state sees one signal either way and must not special-case the source.

### Retrospective

**What worked:**
- `Binding::Custom` on a statically spawned action entity delivered the stated goal: remapping a held key changes which stroke drives the input, with no `Complete`/restart flicker, because the entity is never respawned.
- Deleting the key-inspection half of `sync_global_shortcuts_activity` and its vestigial run-condition disjunct left one owner of held state with no synchronization problem to solve.

**What deviated from the plan:**
- The Work Order named `keystroke/sequence.rs` and `keymap/schema.rs` among the files to change for the modifier-family primary trigger; the landed change also required `keymap/compiled.rs`, `keymap/merged.rs`, `keymap/runtime/held.rs`, and `command/registry.rs`.
- `CommandRegistry::custom_input` was renamed `held_command_lookup` during review — the old name claimed every result was a custom input when two of the outcome's three variants are not. The hana-side wrapper that gave the same lookup a third name was deleted.
- Six review findings needed repair before the phase closed, two of them blockers: a runtime `assert!` on the input path (replaced by a `#[must_use] CustomInputTransition` that makes the bad state unrepresentable rather than asserted), and unroutable key codes — numpad, `NumLock`, media keys — being grouped with ordinary keys, which suspended an active bare-`shift` hold for as long as one was down.

**Surprises:**
- **`require_reset: true` reconciliation, the acceptance item that asked for a recorded finding.** Catalyst attached `ActionSettings { require_reset: true }` to both `ModifySelection` and `FreezeTracking`. `FreezeTracking` lost it silently at some point before this phase; nothing failed, because the setting only governs whether *removed* bindings enter `PendingBindings`, and this phase stops removing bindings entirely. The setting is still required by the physical `Escape` action, where a key held across IME context reactivation could otherwise restart it. Recorded rather than restored: neither held command needs it now.
- The three zero-allocation routing tests warm their lookup maps with the same keys before measuring, so they would pass with much smaller capacity constants than the four the phase introduced. The real bound comes from the insert sites, not from those tests — the closure review derived it independently and got 111, exactly the constant.
- Bevy's `World::resource_scope` carries a first-touch allocation cost. Replacing one `resource_scope` call site with `resource_mut` moved that cost onto a different call site and broke two zero-allocation tests by exactly one allocation each; restoring the original structure fixed it without touching a test.

**Implications for remaining phases:**
- Phase 20's recovery chord is the last consumer of the legacy `Start<A>` observer path. It is deliberately the one input path that does **not** route through the compiled keymap — that is the point of it, since it has to work when the keymap failed to load. The held-state single-owner rule constrains it only in that it keeps no held-key bookkeeping of its own: it reads `just_pressed` edges and calls `cancel_pending_sequences`.
- Phase 20 also needs a library change this plan did not anticipate. Phase 19 deleted the public panicking `OrdinaryKey::from_supported_key_code`, which Phase 20's chord constant was written against, so Phase 20 now adds a total const `OrdinaryKey::KEY_P` to `hana_rubric` and becomes a two-repository phase with two checkpoints.
- `CommandRegistry::custom_input` no longer exists under that name. Any remaining Work Order that names it must say `held_command_lookup`.

### Phase 19 Review

Architect review of Phases 20–22 against the working-tree code, 22 findings. Applied:

- **Phase 20's chord constant could not compile.** It was written against `OrdinaryKey::from_supported_key_code`, which this phase deleted as a public panicking constructor. Phase 20 now adds a total const `OrdinaryKey::KEY_P` to `hana_rubric` first — shape decided in the Work Order, because a fallible `const fn` would hit the workspace's `panic`/`unwrap_used`/`unreachable` denials at either its own body or hana's call site. Phase 20 becomes a two-repository phase: **Files** and **Acceptance gate** updated, two checkpoints.
- **Phase 20's dispatch path was unspecified.** Decided: the handler triggers `OpenCommandPaletteEvent` directly rather than going through `CommandRegistry::invoke`. This phase established that a declaration-validation failure inserts `CommandRegistry::empty()` and the app starts anyway — exactly the failure a recovery chord exists for, so a registry-mediated dispatch would be dead in its own worst case.
- **This retrospective originally told Phase 20 the opposite of its own spec** — that the chord must route through the compiled keymap. Corrected: the recovery chord is deliberately the one path that does not, and the single-owner rule constrains it only to keeping no held-key bookkeeping of its own.
- **Phase 20's library-side work is already done and tested.** `keymap/merged.rs:504-520` rejects any sequence whose first stroke equals a protected keystroke, with four shipped tests. The gate no longer re-enumerates alias, prefix, and platform-split cases under `verify.sh test hana`, which would re-prove library behavior from the wrong repository; it now asserts only the end-to-end hana wiring. The recovery-chord comment in `keymap.default.jsonc:4-5` is likewise already shipped.
- **Added Phase 20 constraint:** hana gates the whole `KeymapSystems::Route` set on IME state, and `.before(KeymapSystems::Route)` still holds when that run condition skips the set — which is what makes the "fires during an IME session" gate item true, and why there is no pending sequence to cancel in that case.
- **Phase 21's BRP-read gate was unreachable.** `ConditionName` is `#[reflect(opaque)]`, which BRP's serializer cannot render without serde type data. Decided: keep the newtype, add `#[reflect(opaque, Serialize, Deserialize)]` and the serde derives.
- **Phase 21 gained two missing Files entries** — the input-path reader of `ActiveCondition::handle()` in `keymap/runtime/dispatch.rs` (with a note that this phase's zero-allocation tests guard it), and the second writer in `keymap_plugin.rs`.
- **Phase 21 now owns documenting this phase's new keystroke spellings** — that a bare modifier (`shift`, `ctrl`, `alt`, `secondary`) is a legal binding value, and that `cmd`/`super`/`win`/`secondary` all round-trip as `platform`. No phase covered it; Phase 21 owns the error and repair surface, which is where a confused user lands.
- **Phase 22 named only one blocked consumer.** `fairy_dust` also normal-depends on `hana_rubric`, so it is blocked from release too. Its "nothing after Phase 21 changes either crate's public API" claim was false — Phases 20 and 21 both do — and its crate names predated the workspace-wide `hana_*` rename. Two of its items (the `hana_rubric` README, one manifest line) were already satisfied.
- **Line and symbol drift corrected across all three phases** — this phase rewrote `command/registry.rs` and `global_shortcuts.rs`, moving eleven cited anchors; the prelude grew from 28 exports to 33.

Deferred as `**Pending decision:**` blocks:

- **Phase 20** — the generated reference will print the chord as `platform-p`, contradicting the "Cmd+P / Ctrl+P" comment beside it in the same generated file.
- **Phase 21** — the existing three-way-split decision, strengthened: this phase needed six repairs including two blockers as a single cross-repository phase, and Phase 21 is strictly larger. Its "freeze the library contract first" rationale also weakened, since Phase 20 now touches the library too.
- **Phase 22** — `bevy_kana` is the last `bevy_`-prefixed member after the workspace rename; publishing `0.4.0` fixes that name for another cycle, and this is the cheapest moment to change it.

Checked and clean: the remaining phases introduce three new types — `CommandInvocationOutcome`, `KeymapPathAvailability`, and `ActiveConditionState` — each already a semantic replacement for a bare `Option` or a set of correlated fields, so the type-design pass found nothing to change. One bare `Option` in a domain-owned API remains, `KeymapCommand::hold_phase() -> Option<HoldPhase>`, but it is shipped API from Phases 4a/11b rather than something a remaining phase introduces; noted, not scoped in.
