### Phase 20 — hana: the unremappable recovery chord · status: done

**Blocked by:** **Phases 16 and 18**; Phase 18 removes the dead action-start halves but deliberately retains the recovery command's live `Start<A>` observer until this phase establishes one authoritative chord path. Phase 15 cleared `G2`. (`G1` superseded — work on `feature/rubric` in `/Users/natemccoy/rust/hana_rubric`, reconcile before dispatch.)

#### Work Order

**Goal:** the recovery palette chord works when nothing else does, and a user who tries to bind it is told why they cannot.

**Spec:**

**The requirement.** `/Users/natemccoy/rust/hana_rubric/docs/hana/tool-graph.md:416-420` requires the recovery chord to be unremappable, independent of `Ready`, and excluded from user key maps — the anchor is **not** `:175` (corrected 2026-07-31; the requirement reads "hardcoded and unremappable — `Cmd+P` on macOS, `Ctrl+P` on Windows and Linux", reinforced at `:82`, `:367`, `:3698`, `:5750`). **No `⌘⇧P` survives anywhere in that document**, so the "changed from `⌘⇧P`" note below is a confirmation, not an edit to make.

**Part of this phase is already built on `init/hana_catalyst`.** `command_palette_binding()` (`crates/hana/src/input/global_shortcuts.rs:87-94`) already selects `ModKeys::SUPER` vs `ModKeys::CONTROL` by `cfg!(target_os = "macos")`, and `spawn_permanent_commands` (`:73-83`) already spawns it into `PermanentShortcutsContext` with `require_reset: true`. (Anchors re-read after Phase 19 rewrote this file.) What remains is the `PreUpdate` exclusive handler, the `RECOVERY_CHORD: Keystroke` constant, and `.with_protected_keystroke`. **The chord is system-key + P — `⌘P` on macOS, `ctrl-P` elsewhere** (changed from `⌘⇧P` because hana does not print). Its job is to work when nothing else does — empty world, mangled graph, no `Ready` — because it opens the palette containing factory reset.

A keymap file makes this harder: the escape hatch must survive a user rebinding `cmd-p`, nulling it, **and** writing a file that fails to parse.

**Three parts (D8), as resolved by G3:**

1. **Registered in Rust unconditionally**, before any file is read and regardless of whether reading succeeds. Checked ahead of the keymap matcher; consumes the keystroke. **This is the survival property — it does not depend on the keymap system at all.** Catalyst already implements `OpenCommandPalette` on Cmd/Ctrl+P hand-rolled with `KeyCode::KeyP.with_mod_keys(…)`; the mechanism exists, so reconcile rather than rebuild.

   **Constraint from Phase 11c:** the chord handler must also **cancel any pending multi-stroke sequence** before opening the palette — otherwise a half-typed prefix stays armed and the next keystroke completes a binding the user never intended. Phase 11c ships the cancellation entry point (it clears `CompiledKeymap::global` and every entry in `matchers`); **Phase 12 makes it public**, since Phase 11c left it `pub(crate)` and hana is a separate crate. Phase 11c deliberately does not implement the chord itself — it has no access to the constant, which this phase owns.

   **The handler must run in `PreUpdate` after `InputSystems` and before `KeymapSystems::Route`, reading `ButtonInput` directly.** The first ordering makes a key produced in this frame visible; the second cancels pending sequences before routing. An enhanced-input observer will not do: `PermanentShortcutsContext` resolves in `EnhancedInputSystems::Update`, which `keymap_plugin.rs:143-145` orders *after* `route_input` (`route_input.in_set(KeymapSystems::Route).after(InputSystems).before(EnhancedInputSystems::Update)`). Routing an unmatched keystroke while a sequence is pending returns `Reprocess` and dispatches the deferred binding first (`keymap/runtime/dispatch.rs:200-228`) — so by the time an observer-based handler ran, the half-typed prefix would already have fired. Cancelling after routing is too late.

   **How the handler dispatches — decided, do not route through the registry.** Trigger `OpenCommandPaletteEvent` directly on the world (`world.trigger(…)`). Do **not** call `CommandRegistry::invoke(&CommandId, &mut World)` (`command/registry.rs:173`): Phase 19 established that a command-declaration validation failure inserts `CommandRegistry::empty()` and the app starts anyway, so a registry-mediated dispatch is dead in exactly the broken-startup case this chord exists to recover from. A direct trigger needs no registry lookup and no `world.resource_scope` (which `invoke` would require, since it takes `&self` and `&mut World`).

   **Wording correction:** part 1's "checked ahead of the keymap matcher; consumes the keystroke" describes a pre-emption that does not exist in the shipped schedule and never has. Nothing consumes a `ButtonInput` edge, and `PermanentShortcutsContext` resolves after routing. It is harmless today only because the loader refuses to bind the chord at all. Implement the `PreUpdate` handler above and the sentence becomes true; do not rely on it being true beforehand.
2. **Already satisfied — verify only.** The comment shipped in `keymap.default.jsonc:4-5` (Phase 18). The generated companion reference derives the canonical spelling from the shared constant rather than introducing another literal; the rendering itself is settled by the decision below (`cmd-p` on macOS, `ctrl-p` elsewhere, since the chord is `secondary-p`).
3. **The loader rejects any keymap entry claiming it** — Phase 7 implements the rejection; this phase supplies the constant and the `Unremappable` declarations. **The library half is already shipped and tested:** `keymap/merged.rs:504-520` rejects any sequence whose first stroke equals a protected keystroke, covered by `protected_keystroke_aliases_reject_bare_and_prefix_bindings` (`:1636`), `protected_keystroke_modifier_order_aliases_are_rejected` (`:1696`), `control_p_is_platform_specific_against_a_protected_platform_chord` (`:1725`), and `chained_protected_keystrokes_reject_each_user_binding` (`keymap_plugin.rs:713`). This phase adds no library rejection logic and its gate must not re-prove that behavior — see the acceptance gate.

**`PermanentShortcutsContext` is the mechanism.** Catalyst's second input context — documented as holding commands "intentionally absent from user key maps" — **is** D8's mechanism and a better one than the design had. Rather than the loader special-casing individual chords, commands that must stay reachable live in a context the keymap system never reads. D8's loader-rejection survives as the diagnostic emitted when a keymap file names a command belonging to that context.

- `PermanentShortcutsContext` **stays in hana**; it is a `bevy_enhanced_input` context (marker component plus `Actions::<PermanentShortcutsContext>` spawn). It does **not** move into `hana_rubric`.
- What crosses the boundary is only the *declaration* that a command is unremappable, carried by `command!`'s capability field. **hana owns the binding; `hana_rubric` owns the refusal.**
- Verify every command remaining in that context is `Unremappable`; Phase 16 already declared its sole command, `OpenCommandPalette`, with that capability. No capability edit remains unless the context gains another command before dispatch.

**Decided 2026-08-03 — the exclusive `PreUpdate` handler is the sole physical execution path for the recovery chord.**

The handler reads the `ButtonInput` edge directly and does not consume it, so leaving `PermanentShortcutsContext`'s physical Cmd/Ctrl+P action in place would let enhanced input observe the same edge and open the palette a second time. The handler cannot be dropped in favor of the existing action: it is the only path early enough to cancel a pending multi-stroke sequence before `KeymapSystems::Route`, and cancelling after routing is too late.

Therefore, in this phase:
- Remove the enhanced-input physical binding for `OpenCommandPalette`, keeping its `Unremappable` `command!` declaration for palette metadata and loader diagnostics.
- Remove the then-empty `PermanentShortcutsContext`, its `Actions::<PermanentShortcutsContext>` spawn, its plugin registration, and the dead `On<Start<OpenCommandPalette>>` observer half.
- The Work Order text below that describes `PermanentShortcutsContext` as the recovery chord's binding *mechanism* is superseded by this decision: it remains the mechanism for the *declaration* that a command is unremappable, not for the physical binding.

**Why not Rust-only and silent:** a user who binds `cmd-p` and sees nothing happen has no way to find out why. **Why not a protected entry in the keymap file:** then the chord is carried by the keymap system, so a catastrophic keymap failure takes the recovery path with it — the exact scenario it exists for.

**One canonical parsed keystroke constant (M1)** shared by the always-on handler, protected-entry validation, the generated default comment, and the tests. Not four string literals.

**Keep the protected list to exactly one entry.** Every protected keystroke is one the user can never use for anything else. The bar for a second: the app is unrecoverable without it — true of the recovery palette, nothing else currently.

**Decided 2026-08-03 — `Display` renders the Platform family under the running OS's own name for that key: `cmd-` on macOS, `super-` everywhere else. The canonical spelling `platform-` is retired from output.**

The generated companion header read `platform-p` while the prose beside it read "Cmd+P on macOS", disagreeing about the same key. Rather than annotate the mismatch or add a second rendering path, change the one arm that produces it.

Concretely, in `crates/hana_rubric/src/keystroke/mod.rs`:
- `impl Display for Keystroke` (`:311-315`) writes `"cmd-"` under `cfg!(target_os = "macos")` and `"super-"` otherwise, in place of the unconditional `"platform-"`.
- `Modifier::Platform`'s name (`:154`) follows the same rule wherever it feeds user-facing text.
- The round-trip invariant is unaffected: `cmd`, `super`, `win`, and `platform` all already parse to `ModifierFamily::Platform` (`:430-432`, `:447`), so `parsed(&keystroke.to_string()) == keystroke` continues to hold on both platforms.
- The tests that pin the old spelling (`:943-953`, asserting `parsed("cmd").to_string() == "platform"`, and the `secondary` round-trip at `:766`) are rewritten to assert the per-OS spelling under `cfg!`. `keystroke/mod.rs` is the only file in the crate containing a `"platform-"` literal — the blast radius is this module plus the diagnostics that embed a rendered keystroke, where the platform-native word is the wanted wording anyway.

**Why this is the whole change.** `reference_default_bytes` (`disk/companion_files.rs:21-47`) copies the embedded default JSONC **verbatim** and prepends a comment header; `to_string()` reaches the file on exactly one line, `// Protected recovery keystroke: <chord>`. Every binding a user might copy out of the reference keeps the spelling hana's author typed in `crates/hana/assets/keymap.default.jsonc`, so a `secondary-` binding stays `secondary-` on every machine and nothing in that file becomes machine-specific.

**Why not ship per-platform default keymaps (the Zed model).** Zed ships `default-macos.json` and `default-linux.json` with the literal modifier written in each because its parser has no platform-conditional token. `hana_rubric` has one — `secondary` resolves at parse time to Platform on macOS and Control elsewhere (`keystroke/mod.rs:425-429`, test at `:754`) — so a single file already expresses both. Splitting the defaults would not remove that token either: there is exactly one user keymap path (`USER_KEYMAP_FILE_NAME`, no per-platform variant), so a user running hana on more than one OS still needs a portable spelling in their own file. The split would buy nothing and duplicate every command declaration.

**Consequence for the chord constant.** The chord is `secondary-p`, and `Display` prints the *resolved* family, so the generated header reads `cmd-p` on macOS and `ctrl-p` on Linux and Windows — matching `keymap.default.jsonc:4-5` and `tool-graph.md:419-424` on both platforms with no wording change to either.

**Files:**

In `/Users/natemccoy/rust/bevy_hana_rubric`:
- `crates/hana_rubric/src/keystroke/mod.rs` — the total const `OrdinaryKey::KEY_P` constructor, **and** the per-OS Platform-family rendering decided above (`impl Display for Keystroke` at `:311-315`, `Modifier`'s name at `:154`, and the tests at `:766` and `:943-953` that pin the retired `platform-` spelling)
- `crates/hana_rubric/src/prelude.rs` — export it

In `/Users/natemccoy/rust/hana_rubric`:
- `crates/hana/src/input/global_shortcuts.rs` — `PermanentShortcutsContext`, the always-on handler, the canonical chord constant. **Phase 19 rewrote this file**: `command_palette_binding()` is now at `:87-94` and `spawn_permanent_commands` at `:73-83`.
- `crates/hana/src/input/mod.rs` — add `.with_protected_keystroke(RECOVERY_CHORD)` to the `KeymapPlugin` builder chain (`:22-28`), register the exclusive handler in `PreUpdate` alongside the existing `reset_physical_input`/`sync_global_shortcuts_activity` pair (`:37-47`), and remove the `PermanentShortcutsContext` import (`:12`) and `add_input_context::<PermanentShortcutsContext>()` (`:32`) when the exclusive handler becomes authoritative
- The handler module owning the palette command — mark it `Unremappable`
- `crates/hana/assets/keymap.default.jsonc` — **already done**: the recovery-chord comment Phase 18 shipped is present at `:4-5`. Verify only; no new wording is expected.
- `docs/hana/tool-graph.md` — the remaining M1 work: the "hardcoded and unremappable — `Cmd+P` on macOS, `Ctrl+P` on Windows and Linux" sentence is at `:419-424`; confirm the acceptance gate wording matches

**Constraints from prior phases:**
- Phase 7 rejects any sequence whose **first canonical stroke** is the protected chord (so `secondary-p x` can never complete), comparing **parsed, platform-normalized keystrokes, never raw strings** — left/right modifier spellings cannot slip through. It reports the stroke index and the reserved command. **The rule is unchanged by Phase 11's modifier model; which spellings collide is not.** The chord is `secondary-p`, so on macOS the colliding spellings are `cmd-p`/`super-p`/`win-p` (and `ctrl-p` is an ordinary binding), while off macOS `ctrl-p` collides and `cmd-p`/`super-p`/`win-p` are ordinary bindings meaning physical Super+P. See Phase 7's protected-chord section, whose own text predates the model change and describes the superseded collapse.
- The protected keystroke reaches `hana_rubric` through `KeymapPlugin`'s `.with_protected_keystroke(Keystroke)` builder call — see Phase 12's Decision A. hana owns the constant; the library owns the enforcement.
- Phase 7 also rejects any command declared `Unremappable` wherever it appears in a file.
- Phase 7: enforcement survives a parse failure because the consuming handler is installed before loading, and every previously committed generation already passed validation.
- **The recovery handler must be an exclusive system.** Phase 12 shipped `pub fn cancel_pending_sequences(world: &mut World)` (`crates/hana_rubric/src/lib.rs`, also in the prelude), so hana's handler cannot be an ordinary system taking `Res<ButtonInput<KeyCode>>` — it is `fn(&mut World)` reading `world.resource::<ButtonInput<KeyCode>>()` internally, ordered `.after(InputSystems).before(KeymapSystems::Route)`. Both system sets are publicly nameable from hana.
- **The handler dispatches by triggering the event directly — not through the registry.** `world.trigger(OpenCommandPaletteEvent…)`. The alternative, `CommandRegistry::invoke(&CommandId, &mut World)` (`command/registry.rs:173`), would need a `world.resource_scope` since it takes `&self` and `&mut World`, and — decisively — **Phase 19 established that a command-declaration validation failure inserts `CommandRegistry::empty()` and the app starts anyway.** That is precisely the failure a recovery chord exists for, so a registry-mediated dispatch would be dead in its own worst case. The direct trigger needs no registry.
- **hana gates the whole `KeymapSystems::Route` set on IME state, and Phase 19 added `reset_physical_input` beside it.** `input/mod.rs:30-34` now does `configure_sets(PreUpdate, KeymapSystems::Route.run_if(global_shortcuts::ime_session_is_closed))`, and `hana_rubric::reset_physical_input` runs `.after(InputSystems).run_if(ime_suppression_transitioned).before(KeymapSystems::Route)`. Ordering `.before(KeymapSystems::Route)` still holds when the set's run condition skips it, so **the handler fires during an IME session** — which is what the gate's "including while an IME session keeps ordinary keymap routing disabled" item actually tests. Note also that during IME suppression there is no pending sequence to cancel, because routing never ran.
- **`with_protected_keystroke` is inert unless `with_defaults` is also called.** The protected list reaches `MergedKeymap::from_sources` only through the `ReloadConfiguration` that `finish_assembly` builds, and `finish_assembly` returns before building it when `defaults` is `None`. A rejection test written against a plugin with no defaults will observe nothing being enforced and read as a false pass.
- Phase 1 moved `PlatformShortcutMode` out of `keybindings.rs` into its own module and Phase 4c relocated it to `crates/hana_rubric/src/platform_shortcut_mode.rs` as `pub(crate)`, so hana cannot name it. Platform normalization now happens inside `Modifiers::with_platform()`.
- **`Keystroke::from_ordinary_key(Modifiers, OrdinaryKey)` and the modifier builders (`Modifiers::none/with_control/with_platform`) are `const`** (`keystroke/mod.rs:45-93`, `:286`), **but there is no const constructor for `OrdinaryKey`.** The only `KeyCode` → `OrdinaryKey` path is `impl TryFrom<KeyCode> for OrdinaryKey` (`keystroke/mod.rs:192-201`), which is fallible and non-`const`; `OrdinaryKey`'s inherent impl is `COUNT` (`pub(crate)`) and `key_code()`. Phase 19 deleted the public panicking `from_supported_key_code` precisely because it was a panicking constructor. **So this phase adds a total const constructor to `hana_rubric` first, then builds the chord constant from it.**
- **The shape of that constructor is decided: a hand-written total associated constant, not a fallible const fn.** A `const fn` returning `Self` must be total, and the workspace denies `panic`, `unwrap_used`, and `unreachable`, so `match … { None => unreachable!() }`, `.unwrap()`, and a const `panic!` are all unavailable inside one. A `const fn from_supported_key_code(KeyCode) -> Option<Self>` would push the same problem to hana's call site, where a const context cannot unwrap without a lint exemption. Add instead:
  ```rust
  impl OrdinaryKey {
      /// The recovery chord's primary key. A total constant — no fallible
      /// conversion at a const site.
      pub const KEY_P: Self = Self(KeyCode::KeyP);
  }
  ```
  and export it through `prelude.rs`. Adding further per-key constants is out of scope; this phase adds only the one the chord needs.
- With that in place the chord is a plain constant with no runtime parse:
  ```rust
  // Phase 11 adopted Zed's two-bit modifier model: `with_platform()` is now the *physical*
  // Cmd/Win/Super key on every platform, so a bare `with_platform()` constant would make the
  // recovery chord Super+P on Linux — a window-manager chord, not Ctrl+P. The chord is the
  // `secondary` modifier: platform on macOS, control elsewhere.
  #[cfg(target_os = "macos")]
  const RECOVERY_CHORD: Keystroke = Keystroke::from_ordinary_key(
      Modifiers::none().with_platform(),
      OrdinaryKey::KEY_P,
  );
  #[cfg(not(target_os = "macos"))]
  const RECOVERY_CHORD: Keystroke = Keystroke::from_ordinary_key(
      Modifiers::none().with_control(),
      OrdinaryKey::KEY_P,
  );
  ```
- Phase 4a's `Unremappable` capability is set in the `command!` invocation.
- **A `command!` call site needs five names imported unqualified**, not four: `InputAction`, `Event`, `Reflect`, `ReflectEvent`, and `ReflectKeymapCommand`. The expansion writes `#[reflect(Event, KeymapCommand)]`, so bevy's derive forms *both* `ReflectEvent` and `ReflectKeymapCommand` by concatenation in the caller's scope. **`hana_rubric::prelude::*` does not cover this** — it has **42** exports after Phase 21 (which added `ActiveConditionState`, `CommandInfo`, `CommandInvocationOutcome`, `CommandLookup`, `ConditionHandle`, `DefaultKeymapSource`, `KeymapPathAvailability`, `KeymapPathFailure`, and `TimeoutOutcome` on top of Phase 19's `HeldCommandLookupOutcome`, `ModifierFamily`, `OrdinaryKey`, `PrimaryTrigger`, and `InvalidOrdinaryKeyCode`), including the free functions `cancel_pending_sequences` and `reset_physical_input`, but **not** the `command!` macro and **not** `Event`/`Reflect`/`ReflectEvent`/`InputAction`. Write the five imports explicitly.
- **`command!`'s keys are positional and fixed**: `action`, `event`, `id`, `title`, `description`, and optionally `capability` — in that order, `capability` last. It is `macro_rules!` arm matching, not a keyword parser; a key out of order produces an opaque "no rules expected this token" error. Omitting `capability` yields `Capability::OneShot`. **`capability: Held` is a deliberate `compile_error!`** — a held command is declared with the leading `held,` marker and no `capability:` key, which is what gives its event the `phase: HoldPhase` field. `capability: Capability::Held` matches no arm at all (the generic arm takes a bare ident).
- **Every `KeymapCommand` impl must satisfy `Event + Reflect` and implement **both** `fn hold_phase(&self) -> Option<HoldPhase>` **and Phase 11b's `fn build() -> Self`**, and `#[reflect(KeymapCommand)]` additionally requires `for<'a> <Self as Event>::Trigger<'a>: Default` (`command/reflect_command.rs:53-57`) — a hard compile error, not a silent skip** (`crates/hana_rubric/src/command/reflect_command.rs:15`). `command!` generates the method — non-held forms return `None`, the `held,` form returns `Some(self.phase)`. A hand-written impl that omits it fails to compile; that is deliberate, so a held command cannot register as silently inert.

**Every test this phase adds must live in `src/` under `#[cfg(test)]`, not in `crates/hana/tests/`.** hana is bin-only (`crates/hana/src/main.rs`, no `lib.rs`) and `verify.sh test hana` pins `--lib`/`--bins` from cargo metadata, so a `tests/` target compiles under `verify.sh lint hana` (which passes `--tests`) and **never executes** — it looks healthy and proves nothing. Same constraint as Phases 14, 15, and 17.

**Acceptance gate:** `verify.sh check|test|lint hana_rubric` and `verify.sh check|test|lint hana` green, with **separate checkpoints** in `bevy_hana_rubric` (the const `OrdinaryKey` constructor and its export) and `/Users/natemccoy/rust/hana_rubric` (the chord constant, handler, and plugin wiring). Tests: the exact chord opens the recovery palette from a same-frame key event produced by `InputSystems`, including while an IME session keeps ordinary keymap routing disabled; **the hana app passes `RECOVERY_CHORD` through `with_protected_keystroke` and a user binding of it is rejected end-to-end**. The library-side rejection is **already shipped and tested** — `keymap/merged.rs:504-520` rejects any sequence whose first stroke equals a protected keystroke, covered by `protected_keystroke_aliases_reject_bare_and_prefix_bindings` (`:1636`), `protected_keystroke_modifier_order_aliases_are_rejected` (`:1696`), `control_p_is_platform_specific_against_a_protected_platform_chord` (`:1725`), and `chained_protected_keystrokes_reject_each_user_binding` (`keymap_plugin.rs:713`) — so do **not** re-enumerate alias, prefix, and platform-split cases under `verify.sh test hana`; that re-proves library behavior from the wrong repository. **The rejection rule itself is unchanged** — reject anything whose first canonical stroke equals `RECOVERY_CHORD`. **reload while a sequence is pending does not break the chord**; the chord works with a whole-file parse failure in place, and with no config directory at all. The protected list has exactly one entry — assert this by binding a *second* arbitrary keystroke and confirming it is **not** rejected, so the test fails if the list ever grows, rather than by counting entries in source. `tool-graph.md`'s acceptance gate reads `⌘P` throughout. **Library-side, `Keystroke`'s rendering is asserted per-OS under `cfg!`** — a Platform-family keystroke renders `cmd-…` on macOS and `super-…` elsewhere, `secondary-p` renders `cmd-p` on macOS and `ctrl-p` elsewhere, and `parsed(&keystroke.to_string()) == keystroke` holds in both cases. No test may assert the literal string `platform-`.

### Retrospective

**What worked:**
- The `PreUpdate` exclusive handler landed exactly as specified — `.after(InputSystems).before(KeymapSystems::Route)`, reading `ButtonInput` directly, triggering `OpenCommandPaletteEvent` on the world with no registry lookup. It fires during an IME session, since `.before(KeymapSystems::Route)` still orders correctly when that set's run condition skips it.
- The total const `OrdinaryKey::KEY_P` constant sidestepped the panicking-constructor problem cleanly; `RECOVERY_CHORD` is a plain `const` with no runtime parse on either platform.
- Removing `PermanentShortcutsContext` and its enhanced-input binding left exactly one physical execution path, so no double-open is possible.

**What deviated from the plan:**
- **A production defect outside the Work Order was found and fixed.** The keymap file watcher compared `notify` event paths to the configured keymap path with `==`. macOS reports event paths fully resolved, so any configuration directory reached through a symlink — a dotfile-managed `~/.config`, and always `TMPDIR` — never matched, no dirty edge was raised, and **every keymap edit in production silently waited for the 5s poll audit instead of the 100ms debounce**. Fixed with `user_keymap_watch_targets(&KeymapPaths) -> [PathBuf; 2]` (configured + canonicalized) matched via `contains`, guarded by `a_native_watch_carries_an_edit_without_a_poll_audit` in `disk/worker/watch.rs`.
- `DiskWorker::run` was reordered to arm the watcher before the first read, widening scope beyond the Work Order. It closes a real startup window in which an edit is invisible until the poll audit.
- The `command!` declaration for `OpenCommandPalette` survives as an `InputAction` with no physical binding — a vestigial library API surface (`tool/command_palette.rs:26`), not a defect, but worth a backlog entry.

**Surprises:**
- **`SequenceMatcher::match_keystroke` does `self.pending.take()` and never restores it** (`keystroke/sequence_matcher.rs:157`). Routing itself drops an armed prefix, so a test that presses the chord through normal routing cannot tell whether the handler's `cancel_pending_sequences` did anything. The cancellation test has to suppress routing — via an open IME session — to discriminate at all.
- `reset_physical_input` also calls `cancel_pending_sequences`, a second false-pass path; the test module needed a `PhysicalInputReset::Absent` mode to exclude it.
- **A pending prefix genuinely cannot survive a reload** — a generation change replaces `CompiledKeymap` and discards pending sequences by construction (`keymap/reload.rs:531-555`). The reload test therefore arms, reloads, then *re-arms* before pressing the chord.
- The `#[cfg(test)]` first-read hold needed to make a reload observable spans three files (`watch.rs`, `runtime.rs`, `channels.rs`); all of it compiles out of the app.
- The command palette is a native OS text-input session, not app-rendered geometry, so **it never appears in a screenshot**. Runtime verification reads the primary window's `ime_enabled` field instead.

**Implications for remaining phases:**
- The 100ms debounce is now the real reload latency in production. Any later phase that waits on a keymap reload should use a ~2s timeout, not 5s+; a test that takes ~5s is signalling that the native watch regressed.
- `Keystroke`'s `Display` now renders the Platform family per-OS (`cmd-` on macOS, `super-` elsewhere), and `platform-` is retired from output. Any remaining phase asserting rendered keystroke text must assert under `cfg!`.
- `PermanentShortcutsContext` no longer exists. Phase 21's palette route commands cannot use it as a home for unremappable bindings; the `Unremappable` capability on the `command!` declaration is the only remaining mechanism.
- `hana_rubric::prelude` now also exports `OrdinaryKey::KEY_P`'s owning type and the free functions `cancel_pending_sequences` / `reset_physical_input`.

### Phase 20 Review

Architect review of the remaining phases against what Phase 20 shipped, 2026-08-03. Eleven findings; no remaining phase was made redundant. Applied:

- **Phase 21 — added a `Constraints from Phase 20` block.** Phase 20's retrospective implications were archive text a dispatched delegate never reads, which left Phases 21 and 22 no longer self-contained. The block now carries all five: `PermanentShortcutsContext` is gone (use `Unremappable` + `with_protected_keystroke`), the ~100ms reload and its `DISK_RELOAD_TIMEOUT = 2s` deadline with the reason 5s hides a regression, per-OS `Display` requiring `cfg!` in assertions, the changed prelude export set, and the disk-test fixtures that exist but are private to `global_shortcuts.rs`'s test module.
- **Phase 21 — corrected an inverted documentation deliverable.** The Work Order told the delegate to document that `cmd`/`super`/`win`/`secondary` "round-trip as `platform`". Phase 20 retired `platform-` from output; the entry now states the accepted-input spellings and the per-OS rendered spellings separately, including that `secondary` resolves to Cmd on macOS and Ctrl elsewhere. Left as written, this would have shipped false text into `keymap.default.jsonc`.
- **Phase 21 — acceptance gate now names the ~2s deadline** for its three disk-dependent items.
- **`## Gates` G3 resolution rewritten.** It described `PermanentShortcutsContext` as the live mechanism in plan-global context every delegate reads. Archived Phases 16 and 18 keep their original spellings as history.
- **Phase 22 — the nateroids step would have failed on its first `cargo check`.** `nateroids/Cargo.toml:44` requests `features = ["input"]`, which Phase 4c deleted, so the version-only bump the Work Order described leaves an unknown-feature error. Also recorded: nateroids already declares `bevy_enhanced_input` directly, and its `Keybindings` uses are confined to `src/input/global_shortcuts.rs`.
- **Phase 22 — recorded that nateroids inherits `hana_rubric`'s full dependency set** for four macros, since the crate has no `[features]` section. Framed as an accepted tradeoff to record in the migration notes; feature gating stays out of scope per the phase's own **Not in scope** line.
- **`## Deferred cleanup` — two entries added.** The vestigial `OpenCommandPalette` `InputAction`, marked explicitly as accepted debt so a Phase 21 delegate does not "fix" what Phase 21 forbids; and a named return type for `user_keymap_watch_targets`, whose `[PathBuf; 2]` encodes canonicalization failure as a duplicate array element.

Deferred to the user at Phase 21's pre-dispatch check, as `**Pending decision:**` blocks in its Work Order:

- **A `KeymapPlugin` built without defaults silently disables protected-keystroke enforcement** — `finish_assembly` returns early with no diagnostic, unlike its two sibling failure paths. Recommendation: add the diagnostic and replace the bare `Option<&'static str>` with a named type, both in Phase 21, since Phase 22 freezes the shape into `0.1.0`.
- **The Phase 20 watch fix covers a symlinked config directory but not a symlinked keymap file** — file-level symlinking is GNU stow's default, and that case still degrades silently to the 5s poll. Recommendation: extend the fix before publication.

---

### Phase 21 — `hana_rubric`: the semantic library API · status: done

**Blocked by:** **Phase 20** — its const `OrdinaryKey` constructor and its filesystem-watch fix both land in `hana_rubric` first.

#### Work Order

**Goal:** every state this crate publishes has a name. No caller has to read a function body to learn what an absent value meant. This is the last phase that changes `hana_rubric`'s public API.

**Decided (2026-08-03) — this is the first of a three-way split of the original Phase 21.** The original phase carried library state, remote command routing, and the keymap repair UI in one dispatch across two repositories. Phase 19 was a single cross-repository phase of smaller scope and needed six review repairs including two blockers. Split into **Phase 21** (this one, library API, `bevy_hana_rubric` only), **Phase 22** (hana's BRP method and active-context read), and **Phase 23** (hana's palette repair commands and error surface), in that order — the library contract is the widest-reaching of the three, so it is reviewed on its own diff. Each phase produces one checkpoint in one repository. The original phase's "freeze the library contract before either consumer is built on it" rationale is **not** the reason: Phase 20 already touched the library. The reason is review surface.

**Spec:**

**Every bare `Option` in this crate's public surface becomes a named type.** See the **Standing ruling** in `## Delegation Context`. A public-signature sweep on 2026-08-03 found exactly six; all six are in this phase. Two variants is the floor, not the target — check first whether the `None` arm is conflating more than one situation.

**Make the active keymap context readable over BRP.** Once Phase 18 gates bindings on context, "which context is active right now" is the first question a broken binding raises, and today nothing outside the process can answer it: `ActiveCondition` (`crates/hana_rubric/src/condition.rs`) derives no `Reflect`, and neither does `DerivedContextRules<C>` (`crates/hana_rubric/src/derived_context.rs:134`). Add `Reflect` + `#[reflect(Resource)]` to `ActiveCondition`; Phase 15 proved non-generic reflected types auto-register, so do **not** add an explicit `register_type::<ActiveCondition>()`. `ActiveCondition` holds a resolved handle, so also expose the name it resolves to rather than only the opaque handle; a remote reader that gets an integer it cannot map back to `resting` has learned nothing. Phase 22 consumes this.

**`ConditionName` is `#[reflect(opaque)]`, so exposing it is not enough on its own.** `condition.rs:32-34` is `#[derive(… Reflect)] #[reflect(opaque)] pub struct ConditionName(String)`. An opaque reflected type has no structural representation, so BRP's reflection serializer cannot render it without `ReflectSerialize`/`ReflectDeserialize` type data registered — Phase 22's "a BRP read returns each active context name" gate item is unreachable without this. **Decided: keep the newtype and add the serde type data** — `#[reflect(opaque, Serialize, Deserialize)]` plus `serde::{Serialize, Deserialize}` derives on `ConditionName`. The alternative, flattening the reflected resource to a plain `String`, discards the newtype's meaning at exactly the boundary where a reader most needs it.

**Decided (2026-08-03):** choose the reflected representation of the active context name

Actual problem:
`ActiveCondition` currently stores only a private, non-reflected `ConditionHandle`. Deriving `Reflect` on that shape either fails or exposes an opaque integer that BRP clients cannot map back to `resting` or `dimension_lock_rotation`.

What exists now:
- `condition.rs` owns `ActiveCondition` and the handle-to-condition registry.
- `keymap_plugin.rs` updates the active condition as routing state changes.

What should change:
- Replace `handle: Option<ConditionHandle>` plus `is_initialized: bool` with one semantic state representation that makes its three legal states explicit: awaiting context, global routing, or a named condition carrying both handle and authored name.
- Keep the runtime handle ignored by reflection and expose the authored `ConditionName`; make every transition replace the whole state atomically.

Recommendation:
Use `ActiveConditionState::{AwaitingContext, GlobalRouting, ResolvedCondition { handle, name }}` inside the reflected `ActiveCondition` resource. This removes invalid field combinations instead of adding a third correlated field, while preserving the fast handle and exposing the stable name to BRP.

**Resolved — approved as recommended.** Covered by the user's blanket ruling of 2026-08-03: *every bare `Option` in a domain-owned type or API becomes a self-documenting enum.* Implement `ActiveConditionState::{AwaitingContext, GlobalRouting, ResolvedCondition { handle, name }}` as written, replacing both `handle: Option<ConditionHandle>` and the correlated `is_initialized: bool`.

**Decided (2026-08-03):** make keymap paths a runtime resource and define open-before-stub behavior

Actual problem:
Phase 23's three palette commands are specified to read `KeymapPaths`, but `finish_assembly` currently keeps the resolved value local and passes it only to the disk worker. The resource is never inserted into the world. A first-run user may also invoke "open user keymap" before the worker has published the stub, and nothing yet defines whether that creates the file, reveals the directory, or reports an error.

What exists now:
- `KeymapPaths` is public, while `KeymapPaths::new()` returns `Option<Self>` (`disk/paths.rs:38`) and path resolution failures are represented only by absence.
- `finish_assembly` resolves the paths and starts the worker with a borrowed value, but palette systems cannot retrieve the same resolved paths from the world.
- Companion publication normally creates the user stub without overwriting it, but it is asynchronous and may not have completed when the command runs.

What should change:
- Replace the bare path-resolution `Option` at the owning boundary with an always-present semantic resource such as `KeymapPathAvailability::{Resolved(KeymapPaths), Unavailable(reason)}` before starting the worker. Recovery commands must be able to explain invalid app names or unavailable configuration roots instead of seeing no resource.
- Choose whether "open user keymap" atomically creates the normal stub when absent, or instead reveals the directory and reports that the file does not exist.
- Keep platform file opening behind one fallible boundary; path resolution, directory creation, and file publication remain platform-independent.

Recommendation:
Insert `KeymapPathAvailability` on both success and failure. Have "open user keymap" use the resolved case to atomically create the normal stub when absent, then invoke one fallible platform opener boundary; the unavailable case reports its reason. "Reveal config directory" creates the directory if needed and reveals it. This keeps the recovery commands useful and explainable without making them depend on worker timing.

**Resolved — approved as recommended.** Covered by the user's blanket ruling of 2026-08-03. This phase owns inserting `KeymapPathAvailability::{Resolved(KeymapPaths), Unavailable(reason)}` on both success and failure and replacing `KeymapPaths::new() -> Option<Self>` at the owning boundary. **The open-before-stub behavior it enables is implemented in Phase 23**, which owns the commands: "open user keymap" atomically creates the normal stub when absent then calls the single fallible platform opener; the `Unavailable` case reports its reason; "reveal config directory" creates the directory if needed and reveals it.

**Decided (2026-08-03):** raised by Phase 11b's review; resolved before dispatch.

**Decision: what happens when a remote client invokes a hold-style command by short id**

Actual problem:
Some commands are momentary — they stay active only while you hold the key, like a modifier that changes what dragging does. Phase 11b made `CommandRegistry::invoke` payload-free: it builds the command's event itself, and for a hold-style command it always builds the **begin** phase (`command/reflect_command.rs:207-210`, with `HoldPhase::Begin` at `:209`). `register_held_observers` then latches the input on (`command/registry.rs:179-183`; the generic `register_held_observer` is at `:186`). There is no argument left through which a caller could say "end". So a remote client that invokes `selection::modify` pins that hold **down indefinitely** — nothing releases it until a human presses and releases the real key.

What exists now:
- `invoke(&CommandId, &mut World) -> Option<()>` — no payload, no phase. **The real signature takes `&self` as well:** `pub fn invoke(&self, command_id: &CommandId, world: &mut World) -> Option<()>` (`crates/hana_rubric/src/command/registry.rs:173`). It borrows the registry *and* the world mutably at once, so a caller cannot hold a `Res<CommandRegistry>` across the call — go through `world.resource_scope::<CommandRegistry, _>(…)`, and copy out anything needed afterwards, because `resource_scope` removes the resource for the closure's duration.
- `Capability::is_palette_invocable()` already returns false for hold-style commands, and the palette already filters on it.
- Held events derive `Event, Reflect` only, so routing one through bevy's reflected trigger can panic (see the constraint below).

What should change — three implementable answers:
- **(a) Reject hold-style commands through the registry's semantic invocation result.** `CommandRegistry::invoke` returns `HeldCommandRequiresPhase` before building or triggering the event, and Phase 22's BRP method translates that result into a clear error. Do not point callers at stock `world.trigger_event`; the reflected-event path can panic for these held event types.
- **(b) Give the BRP method an optional phase argument.** This means reopening `KeymapCommand::build`'s signature to `build(value)` — the door 11b's "Signature scope" paragraph deliberately left ajar — and touching every impl, since `build()` currently takes no arguments.
- **(c) Accept the latch and document it.** Cheapest; leaves a remote client able to wedge an input on with no route back.

Recommendation:
**(a).** It is the only one that costs nothing elsewhere in the plan, and returning `CommandInvocationOutcome::{Invoked, UnknownCommand, HeldCommandRequiresPhase}` keeps capability filtering and invocation in one semantic operation. (b) reopens a trait signature across every command for a case no consumer has asked for; (c) ships a known wedge.

**Resolved — (a), approved as recommended.** Covered by the user's blanket ruling of 2026-08-03. `CommandRegistry::invoke` returns `CommandInvocationOutcome::{Invoked, UnknownCommand, HeldCommandRequiresPhase}` in place of `Option<()>`, rejecting a hold-style command before building or triggering its event. Do not reopen `KeymapCommand::build`'s signature — (b) is rejected — and do not direct callers to stock `world.trigger_event`, which can panic on these event types. Phase 22 translates the outcome into its BRP error response.

**Decided (2026-08-03):** raised by the Phase 20 architect review; resolved before dispatch.

**Decision: a `KeymapPlugin` built without defaults silently disables protected-keystroke enforcement**

Actual problem:
`KeymapPlugin` carries `defaults: Option<&'static str>`. When it is `None`, `finish_assembly` returns early (`crates/hana_rubric/src/keymap_plugin.rs:167-169`) and **records no diagnostic** — unlike the two failure paths immediately below it (`:206-225`), which both record one for a bad app name and for unresolvable `KeymapPaths`. An app assembled without defaults therefore comes up with the keymap system inert and nothing anywhere saying so. Phase 20's Work Order documented the consequence, but framed it as a test-writing hazard ("a rejection test written against a plugin with no defaults will read as a false pass") rather than as the defect it is.

What exists now:
- `defaults: Option<&'static str>` on the plugin — absence means "silently do nothing", indistinguishable from every other `None`.
- Two sibling failures in the same function record diagnostics; this one does not.

What should change:
- Record a diagnostic on the no-defaults path, so the state is visible to `all_diagnostics()` and to the error surface Phase 23 builds.
- Replace the bare `Option<&'static str>` with a type that states what absence means.

**Resolved — approved as recommended, do both.** Covered by the user's blanket ruling of 2026-08-03. Replace `defaults: Option<&'static str>` with `DefaultKeymapSource::{Embedded(&'static str), NotSupplied}` **and** record a diagnostic on the `NotSupplied` path. The diagnostic alone still leaves a published API whose `None` reads as "no defaults configured" rather than "keymap system inert". The variant name is the plan's proposal, not a user-chosen name — if a better one emerges while implementing, take it; what is decided is that the state is named and no longer silent.

**Files:**
- `crates/hana_rubric/src/condition.rs` — reflected `ActiveCondition` carrying `ActiveConditionState` and the authored context name; `ConditionName` gains `#[reflect(opaque, Serialize, Deserialize)]` plus the serde derives
- `crates/hana_rubric/src/keymap_plugin.rs` — `DefaultKeymapSource` in place of `defaults: Option<&'static str>`, the missing `NotSupplied` diagnostic, and both writers of the active condition kept synchronized (`enable_global()` at `:148` is one; `update_active_condition` at `condition.rs:222-228` is the other)
- `crates/hana_rubric/src/keymap/runtime/dispatch.rs` — input-path reader of `ActiveCondition::handle()`. The restructure must not add a branch or an allocation here; Phase 19's zero-allocation routing tests will catch it if it does.
- `crates/hana_rubric/src/disk/paths.rs` — `KeymapPathAvailability::{Resolved(KeymapPaths), Unavailable(reason)}` inserted as an always-present resource, replacing `KeymapPaths::new() -> Option<Self>` (`:38`) at the owning boundary
- `crates/hana_rubric/src/disk/worker/watch.rs` — the symlinked-keymap-file watch extension (decided 2026-08-03, resolution (a)); `user_keymap_watch_targets` (`:243`) gains the resolved keymap-file parent and a named return type in place of its positional `[PathBuf; 2]`
- `crates/hana_rubric/src/command/{registry,mod}.rs`, `crates/hana_rubric/src/{lib,prelude}.rs` — replace `invoke`'s ambiguous `Option<()>` with `CommandInvocationOutcome` and update exports/tests. **Also collapse the three `Option`-returning accessors** — `title` (`registry.rs:141`), `description` (`:147`), and `capability` (`:163`) each return `None` for the one same reason, "that id is not registered". Replace all three with a single lookup returning `CommandLookup::{Found(CommandInfo<'_>), UnknownCommand}`; `CommandInfo<'_>` already exists and already carries id, title, description, and capability together, so this states the miss once instead of three times.
- `crates/hana_rubric/src/keystroke/sequence_matcher.rs` — `resolve_timeout` (`:96`) returns a bare `Option<T>` on a per-frame path. Give it a named outcome. **First determine whether the `None` arm conflates two states** — "no sequence pending at all" and "a sequence is pending but has not yet expired" — because if it does, the type has three variants, not two, and the third is the one a reader currently cannot see.

This phase writes to `bevy_hana_rubric` only, and produces one checkpoint there. Phases 22 and 23 are the hana-side halves.

**Constraints from prior phases:**
- **Phase 4b's registry surface is:** `iter() -> impl Iterator<Item = CommandInfo<'_>>` (sorted by id; carries `id`, `title`, `description`, `capability`), `title`, `description`, `capability`, the semantic held-input lookup Phase 19 introduced, and `invoke(&CommandId, &mut World) -> Option<()>` (Phase 11b removed the payload and registry parameters). It retains **no** `TypeRegistration` and no type path — the type path is used to build validation diagnostics and then dropped.
- **`invoke` takes no payload — there is nothing to validate, and no payload-validation work in this phase.** ~~It hands the caller's `&dyn PartialReflect` to the cached `ReflectEvent` … validate the BRP-supplied payload before calling `invoke`, or add a checked variant.~~ **Deleted, not amended — Phase 11b removed the parameter.** `invoke(&CommandId, &mut World)` dispatches through a monomorphized `fn(&mut World)` that builds the event with `KeymapCommand::build()`, so `from_reflect_with_fallback` is never reached on this path and cannot panic. Do not commission a checked `invoke` variant.
- **The panic hazard is displaced, not gone — it lives on stock `world.trigger_event` now.** A held command's event derives `Event, Reflect` only (`event!`'s **field** arms, `command/event.rs:43`, `:57`; the `Default` derive is on the two **unit** arms at `:37` and `:51`). So a held event has neither `Default` nor `ReflectDefault`, and anything that routes a held event through bevy's reflected trigger — which `from_reflect_with_fallback` backs — can still abort the app. `clippy::panic` is denied workspace-wide.
- Phase 9's `KeymapPaths` is re-exported publicly from `hana_rubric`, but this phase must surface it through an always-present semantic availability resource rather than making callers infer failure from an absent resource.
- **Phase 20 fixed the config-*directory* symlink case** — the watch callback compared event paths to the configured path with `==`, but macOS reports fully resolved paths, so any config directory reached through a symlink never matched and every edit silently fell back to the 5s poll audit. A keymap reload now completes in roughly 100ms. `recreate_watcher` watches `paths.config_directory()` with `RecursiveMode::NonRecursive` (`disk/worker/watch.rs:209`); `user_keymap_watch_targets` (`:243`, private) canonicalizes that directory only.

**Decided (2026-08-03):** raised by the Phase 20 architect review; resolved by the user the same day.

**Decision: the Phase 20 watch fix covers a symlinked config directory but not a symlinked keymap file**

Actual problem:
Phase 20 fixed the case where the *config directory* is reached through a symlink. It did not fix the case where `keymap.jsonc` **itself** is a symlink. `recreate_watcher` watches `paths.config_directory()` with `RecursiveMode::NonRecursive` (`crates/hana_rubric/src/disk/worker/watch.rs:209`), and `user_keymap_watch_targets` canonicalizes that directory only. If the file is a symlink, edits land on a target outside the watched directory and produce **no filesystem event at all** — the reload falls back to the 5s poll audit, which is the exact behavior Phase 20 set out to fix. This is not an exotic setup: file-level symlinking is GNU stow's default, and dotfile managers are the common way a config directory gets populated.

What exists now:
- The directory-symlink case works and is guarded by `a_native_watch_carries_an_edit_without_a_poll_audit`.
- The file-symlink case silently degrades to a 5s reload. Nothing detects it, nothing reports it, no test covers it.
- No phase names the limitation.

What should change — two implementable answers:
- **(a) Extend the fix.** Canonicalize the keymap *file* path as well as the directory, and when the resolved file lives outside the watched directory, add a watch on its resolved parent. Costs one more watch target plus a test that symlinks the file rather than the directory.
- **(b) Record the limitation and ship.** State in the crate README and the keymap reference that a symlinked keymap file reloads on the 5s poll rather than immediately, and leave the code alone.

Recommendation:
**(a).** The degradation is invisible — the app still works, just five seconds late, so nobody reports it and it never gets found. The fix is a small extension of a mechanism Phase 20 already built and already tests. (b) is defensible only if the file-symlink case is judged out of scope, in which case the README text must be explicit rather than silent.

**Resolved — (a), extend the fix.** Zed solves this exact problem the same way, and its implementation is the reference for the shape. `watch_config_file` (`crates/settings/src/settings_file.rs:171`) canonicalizes the settings path before watching it and debounces at 100ms — the same latency target this crate adopted in Phase 20. The symlink handling itself lives in `RealFs::watch` (`crates/fs/src/fs.rs`, the `// Check if path is a symlink and follow the target parent` block): it watches the path as configured, calls `read_link`, and on success makes a relative target absolute, canonicalizes it, watches the resolved target, **and additionally watches that target's parent directory**.

Three facts from that implementation this phase must carry:

- **The parent watch is not redundant with the file watch.** Editors save by writing a temp file and renaming it over the target, which replaces the file. A watch registered on the old file then refers to something that no longer exists and goes silent after the first save; a watch on the containing directory catches the rename. Zed registers both because neither alone survives every editor. This crate's watcher is already directory-based (`RecursiveMode::NonRecursive` on `config_directory()`), so the equivalent move is: resolve the keymap file, and when its resolved parent differs from the already-watched directory, add a watch on that parent.
- **Guard the network/virtual-mount case.** Zed skips the parent watch when the parent would require a polling watcher, because `PollWatcher::watch()` recursively scans at registration and blocks on large virtual mounts. If a resolved parent falls back to polling here, do not add it; record the situation rather than hanging startup.
- **Cost on the common path is one failed syscall.** `read_link` errors on a non-symlink, so the whole branch is skipped for the ordinary case.

Follow the standing `Option` ruling when touching this code: `user_keymap_watch_targets` (`watch.rs:243`) currently returns a bare `[PathBuf; 2]` whose two elements mean "configured" and "canonicalized" positionally, and encodes "could not resolve" by cloning element 0 into element 1. Extending it to a third target is the moment to give it a named type whose variants state the resolved and unresolved cases.

**Acceptance gate:** `verify.sh check|test|lint hana_rubric` green, with one checkpoint in `bevy_hana_rubric`. Tests: `CommandInvocationOutcome` distinguishes invoked, unknown, and held-command-requires-phase results, and a held id is rejected **before** its event is built or triggered; `CommandLookup` returns `UnknownCommand` for an unregistered id and `Found` carries the full `CommandInfo` for a registered one; `KeymapPathAvailability` is present for both resolved and unavailable path states, and the unavailable case reports its reason without panicking; a `KeymapPlugin` built with `DefaultKeymapSource::NotSupplied` records a diagnostic that `all_diagnostics()` returns; `ActiveCondition` round-trips all three shipped context names — `resting`, `dimension_lock`, `dimension_lock_rotation` — through reflection with `ConditionName` rendering as its authored string rather than an opaque handle; Phase 19's zero-allocation routing tests still pass unchanged, which is what guards `dispatch.rs`. The symlink decision above resolved to (a), so its deliverable is part of this gate: a test that symlinks the keymap **file** (not the directory) and asserts the edit arrives without a poll audit, alongside the existing directory-symlink test `a_native_watch_carries_an_edit_without_a_poll_audit`. A rename-over-target save must also be carried, since that is what the resolved-parent watch exists to catch.

### Retrospective

**What worked:**
- All six bare `Option`s named in one dispatch; the blind review found no design defect in any of the new enums, only gaps in where they were applied.
- Reconstructing the pre-fix tree from `HEAD` plus the saved implementation diff let the closure review see the repair alone rather than re-auditing the phase.

**What deviated from the plan:**
- `ConditionRegistry::resolve -> Option<ConditionHandle>` survived beside `lookup` after implementation and was deleted in the fix round, converting `merged.rs:436`, `:635`, `:770` and its `dimension_lock_handle` helper. The pre-dispatch sweep counted the *public* surface; this one is `pub(crate)`.
- `KeymapPathAvailability` was inserted only on the success tail of `finish_assembly`; both early returns skipped it. Fix extracted `insert_keymap_path_availability` and calls it before the defaults check on every path.
- A new `DiagnosticKind::UnconfiguredKeymapPlugin` was added beyond the planned `NotSupplied` diagnostic: a plugin built with only a context source produced no `KeymapPluginConfiguration` at all, so `finish_assembly` returned before the `NotSupplied` path could report anything.
- `#[cfg(test)] pub(super) fn watch_test_worker(&KeymapPaths) -> (DiskWorker, CoalescingSlot)` added to `disk/worker/runtime.rs` — the only way to drive `recreate_watcher` twice from a test.
- `CommandInfo<'registry>` gained `Clone, Copy, Debug, Eq, PartialEq` because `CommandLookup::Found` carries it; several existing tests became `-> Result<(), String>` since `lookup` returns an enum and the style guide bars `unwrap`/`expect`.

**Surprises:**
- `ActiveCondition::resolve` cloned `ConditionName` on every context sync, allocating inside `KeymapSystems` just before `Route` each frame. Phase 19's zero-allocation tests call `route_input` directly and never run `sync_resource_condition`/`sync_state_condition`, so they could not catch it. The new test drives the sync system itself.
- `#[reflect(ignore)]` on the handle field forces `Default` on `ConditionHandle`, and the derived `Default` was a *valid* registry index — a reflected insert of `{"ResolvedCondition":{"name":"…"}}` reconstructed as condition 0 under an arbitrary name. Now an unissuable `usize::MAX` sentinel plus `is_registry_issued()`, and `route_input` returns rather than routing on it.
- `recreate_watcher` cleared `reported_watch_error` before reporting the symlink-parent failure, so that dedupe always compared against `None` and re-reported on every recreation.

**Implications for remaining phases:**
- **Phase 22:** `ActiveCondition` is readable over BRP as planned, but a remote *write* of `ActiveCondition` now routes nothing (the reconstructed handle is not registry-issued) instead of silently routing through the first-registered condition. Forcing a context from outside the process is not available; the read is unaffected.
- **Phase 22/23:** `CommandInvocationOutcome`, `HeldCommandLookupOutcome`, `CommandLookup`, and `CommandInfo<'registry>` all derive `Clone, Copy, Debug, Eq, PartialEq`.
- **Phase 23:** `KeymapPathAvailability` is genuinely always present now, including when the plugin was never configured, so the palette repair commands can read it unconditionally. The error surface should also render the new `DiagnosticKind::UnconfiguredKeymapPlugin`.
- **Phase 24:** two private helpers still take a bare `Option<&str>` app name — `insert_keymap_path_availability` and the pre-existing `record_unavailable_keymap_paths` (`keymap_plugin.rs`). Internal, not public surface; in scope for the closing pass if the ruling is applied crate-wide rather than to the published API.

### Phase 21 Review

Architect review of Phases 22, 23, and 24 against what Phase 21 shipped. Every remaining phase came through structurally clean — none redundant, none needing splitting, merging, or reordering, and 21 → 22 → 23 → 24 still holds with Phase 22 the correct first hana-side dispatch.

- **Phase 22 — migration bullet added to Files.** hana no longer compiles against this crate: `KeymapPaths::new` (`crates/hana/src/input/global_shortcuts.rs:998`, `:1079`, `:1233`) and `ActiveCondition::is_initialized()` (`interaction_context.rs:83`) were both deleted by Phase 21. Neither Work Order named the repair; it now belongs to Phase 22, which is the first phase that has to reach `verify.sh check hana`.
- **Phase 22 — `invoke` and its sibling accessors re-pointed** to `registry.rs:195` / `lookup :162` / `held_command_lookup :178` / `iter :145`. The `resource_scope` constraint was already correct.
- **Phase 22 — three constraints added:** the active-context read needs no new BRP method (`ActiveCondition` auto-registers; a stock resource read renders `{"ResolvedCondition":{"name":"resting"}}`), the read returns an `ActiveConditionState` variant rather than a bare name, and a remote *write* of `ActiveCondition` now routes nothing (`dispatch.rs:62-77`).
- **Phase 23 — both new startup diagnostics named.** The Work Order said Phase 21 added a `NotSupplied` diagnostic; what shipped is `DiagnosticKind::MissingDefaultKeymap` **and** `DiagnosticKind::UnconfiguredKeymapPlugin` (`diagnostic.rs:40-45`), both `Failure`.
- **Phase 23 — the sentinel-mapping rule gained a second arm.** When `KeymapPathAvailability::Unavailable`, `KeymapPaths::default_keymap()` does not exist to map to; the surface renders `KeymapPathFailure::reason()` (`disk/paths.rs:94-108`) instead.
- **Phase 23 — prelude count corrected 33 → 42** with the nine new names, and `ScopedXdgConfigHomeOverride` re-pointed to `global_shortcuts.rs:335`. The load-bearing half of that fact (no `command!`, `Event`, `Reflect`, `ReflectEvent`, `InputAction`) still holds.
- **Phase 23 — the platform file-opening boundary must name its outcome type**, a `Result` with a named failure or an enum stating opened / failed-with-reason. Not `Option<()>`, not `bool`; this value becomes user-facing text.
- **Phase 24 — Spec item 2 added: name the three bare `Option`s Phase 21's sweep did not reach.** `MatchOutcome::Reprocess { deferred }` (a public enum *field*; the sweep counted public signatures), and `app_name` on `KeymapPlugin` and `KeymapPluginConfiguration` — fixing the field rather than the two helpers, since the helpers inherit the `Option` from it and the state already has a name in shipped code (`KeymapPathFailure::AppNameNotConfigured`). Applied under the user's standing bare-`Option` ruling, not as a new decision.
- **Phase 24 — one constraint added:** its `verify.sh final` is the *first execution* of `crates/hana_rubric/tests/keymap_companion_files.rs`, which Phase 21 edited but no phase gate has ever run (`verify.sh test` pins `--lib`/`--bins`; `lint` only compiles `--tests`). A failure there should be read as inherited before it is read as caused by the new assertion.
- **`## Deferred cleanup` — one entry retired, two refs corrected.** The `user_keymap_watch_targets` named-return-type bullet is satisfied: Phase 21 replaced it with `UserKeymapWatch::{UnresolvedPath, WithinConfigDirectory, ThroughSymlink}` plus `SymlinkParentWatch::{NotNeeded, Established, Unwatched}`. `ResourceContextPlugin` is `condition.rs:326`, `StateContextPlugin` `:359`. The module-size rule is not newly tripped — `condition.rs` 463 non-test lines, `keymap_plugin.rs` 452, `registry.rs` 432, `watch.rs` 400.
- **Deferred to Phase 23 as a pending decision:** how the error surface learns where a diagnostic came from. `Diagnostic.source_path` is one `String` carrying four unrelated things, two of which are not paths, so the phase's absolute-path gate is unreachable for exactly the failures it calls most important.
- **Deferred to Phase 24 as a pending decision:** whether the closing phase re-verifies hana. It changes merge semantics in a crate hana consumes by path, then closes the plan without ever building hana again.

### Phase 22 — hana: BRP invocation by short command id and the active-context read · status: todo

**Blocked by:** **Phase 21** — it owns `CommandInvocationOutcome`, `CommandLookup`, and the reflected `ActiveCondition`, all of which this phase consumes. Also **Phases 16 and 18**. (`G1` superseded — work on `feature/rubric` in `/Users/natemccoy/rust/hana_rubric`, reconcile before dispatch.)

#### Work Order

**Goal:** external control can invoke a command by its short declared id, and can ask which keymap context is active right now.

**Spec:**

**A hana-specific BRP method resolving short declared ids.** Stock BRP `world.trigger_event` resolves via `TypeRegistry::get_with_type_path`, so it **cannot accept a declared id like `camera::reset_home`** — it wants the full Rust type path. A hana-specific method resolves the short id through the registry's side table first, then triggers.

This is why D1 chose the bevy type registry over a link-time inventory in the first place: one table serves the keymap loader, the command palette, schema generation, **and** BRP. External control is central to hana's mission, and a link-time inventory would be invisible to BRP.

**Background only — do not follow this path.** It is here to explain why stock BRP cannot accept a short id (it resolves by full type path). This phase's method calls `CommandRegistry::invoke(&CommandId, &mut World)` instead, per the constraint below. The stock path: `TypeRegistration::data::<ReflectEvent>()` then `ReflectEvent::trigger` (`bevy/crates/bevy_ecs/src/reflect/event.rs:67`), exactly as BRP's own `process_remote_trigger_event_request` does (`bevy/crates/bevy_remote/src/builtin_methods.rs:1480`).

**Hold-style commands are rejected, not latched.** Phase 21 resolved this: `invoke` returns `CommandInvocationOutcome::HeldCommandRequiresPhase` before building or triggering the event, and this method translates that into a clear error. **Do not** route callers to stock `world.trigger_event` as a workaround — a held event derives `Event, Reflect` only and has neither `Default` nor `ReflectDefault`, so bevy's `from_reflect_with_fallback` can abort the app on it. A remote client must not be able to pin a hold down with no route back to `End`.

**Expose the active keymap context over BRP.** Phase 21 makes `ActiveCondition` reflected and gives it `ActiveConditionState` carrying the authored `ConditionName`. This phase proves a remote reader can read it and that it tracks real world facts, not a startup constant.

**Files:**
- `crates/hana/src/video_plane/catalog_brp.rs` — **this is where hana's BRP extensions live; the precedent is exact.** `register_brp_method(app)` at `:97` writes into `RemoteMethods` at `:109` and registers an `AgentTool` through `AppAgentToolExt` (`:13-14`), with `#[cfg(test)]` at `:1512` and `mod tests` at `:1517` that assert `RemoteMethods::get(method)`. Follow that shape — it also gives this phase's gate a runnable form (assert the method is registered, then invoke it in-process) instead of implying a live BRP client.
- `crates/hana/src/input/interaction_context.rs` and `crates/hana/src/selection/dimension_lock/lock_state.rs` — construct the real three-context transition fixture used by the BRP-read gate
- **`crates/hana/src/input/global_shortcuts.rs` and `crates/hana/src/input/interaction_context.rs` — migrate the four call sites Phase 21's API change broke.** This phase is the first hana-side dispatch, so it owns the repair; nothing here compiles until it is done. `KeymapPaths::new` is gone — `global_shortcuts.rs:998`, `:1079`, `:1233` become `KeymapPathAvailability::for_app_name(name).into_resolved()`. `ActiveCondition::is_initialized()` is gone — `interaction_context.rs:83` becomes `ActiveCondition::state()` and matches on the `ActiveConditionState` variant.

This phase writes to `/Users/natemccoy/rust/hana_rubric` only, and produces one checkpoint there.

**Constraints from prior phases:**
- **`invoke` borrows the registry and the world mutably at once** — `pub fn invoke(&self, command_id: &CommandId, world: &mut World) -> CommandInvocationOutcome` (`crates/hana_rubric/src/command/registry.rs:195` as Phase 21 shipped it). The BRP method cannot hold a `Res<CommandRegistry>` across the call: go through `world.resource_scope::<CommandRegistry, _>(…)`, and copy out anything needed afterwards, because `resource_scope` removes the resource for the closure's duration. The sibling accessors moved too: `lookup` is `:162`, `held_command_lookup` `:178`, `iter` `:145`.
- **The active-context read needs no new BRP method.** `ActiveCondition` is a plain non-generic reflected `Resource`, so it auto-registers and a stock BRP resource read already renders it as `{"ResolvedCondition":{"name":"resting"}}`. Do not build a second custom method beside the invoke method.
- **The read returns an `ActiveConditionState` variant, not a bare name.** `AwaitingContext` and `GlobalRouting` are the other two legal answers and both must be handled.
- **A remote *write* of `ActiveCondition` now routes nothing.** Phase 21 made `ConditionHandle::default()` an unissuable `usize::MAX` sentinel and `route_input` returns on it (`crates/hana_rubric/src/keymap/runtime/dispatch.rs:62-77`), so a reconstructed resource fails closed instead of routing through whichever condition registered first. Forcing a context from outside the process is not available.
- Phase 16 chose the ids; this method accepts exactly those. Use `iter()` for any listing; the method resolves through the registry surface and does not build its own table.
- **All three shipped context names must be reachable:** `resting`, `dimension_lock`, and `dimension_lock_rotation`.
- Phase 12's `finish` and this method use the same exclusive-system pattern.

**Constraints from Phase 20 — read these before writing any test in this phase.**

- **`PermanentShortcutsContext` does not exist.** Phase 20 deleted the marker component, its `Actions::<…>` spawn, and its `add_input_context` registration. Do not reference it, do not spawn it, and do not add a command to it. A command that must survive a broken keymap is declared `Unremappable` and bound through `with_protected_keystroke` (`crates/hana/src/input/mod.rs`); the live handler precedent is `open_recovery_palette` in `crates/hana/src/input/global_shortcuts.rs`.
- **A keymap reload now completes in roughly 100ms, not 5s**, and the timeout that proves it is `DISK_RELOAD_TIMEOUT = Duration::from_secs(2)`. Any test here that waits on the disk worker uses that deadline. Do not raise it past 5s to make a test pass; that hides the exact regression the constant exists to catch.
- **`Display` for the Cmd/Super family renders per-OS**, so any assertion on rendered keystroke text must branch on `cfg!(target_os = "macos")` rather than hard-coding a spelling.
- **The `hana_rubric` prelude's export set changed.** It does not export the `command!` macro, `Event`, `Reflect`, `ReflectEvent`, or `InputAction`.

**Every test this phase adds must live in `src/` under `#[cfg(test)]`, not in `crates/hana/tests/`.** hana is bin-only (`crates/hana/src/main.rs`, no `lib.rs`) and `verify.sh test hana` pins `--lib`/`--bins` from cargo metadata, so a `tests/` target compiles under `verify.sh lint hana` (which passes `--tests`) and **never executes** — it looks healthy and proves nothing. `catalog_brp.rs` already follows this: its BRP tests are `#[cfg(test)]` in the same file (`:1512+`, `mod tests` at `:1517`).

**Acceptance gate:** `verify.sh check|test|lint hana` green, with one checkpoint in `/Users/natemccoy/rust/hana_rubric`. Tests: the method is registered in `RemoteMethods`; it triggers a command by short id; it rejects an unknown id with a useful error; it rejects a held id **without triggering it** and without directing the caller to stock `world.trigger_event`. **A BRP read returns each active context name — `resting`, `dimension_lock`, and `dimension_lock_rotation` — and changes as the real lock/affordance facts change.** Advance two application updates after every source-component mutation before reading the resource, or the test can observe the expected stale pass rather than the transitioned state.
