### Phase 23 — hana: the palette repair commands and the keymap error surface · status: todo

**Blocked by:** **Phase 21** — it owns `KeymapPathAvailability`, which these commands read. Also **Phases 16, 18, and 20**; Phase 20 owns the recovery path that makes these repair commands reachable. (`G1` superseded — work on `feature/rubric` in `/Users/natemccoy/rust/hana_rubric`, reconcile before dispatch.)

#### Work Order

**Goal:** a person whose keymap is broken can reach the file that broke it, and the error they see names the file by absolute path.

**Spec:**

**Three palette commands (R5):** open the user keymap, open the default reference, and reveal the config directory in the file manager.

- D6 writes bytes to `~/.config/hana/`, which Finder hides. Before the first-launch stub exists there is no user file at all, and **after a bad edit the person who most needs the file is the one whose keymap is broken.**
- **These commands must not depend on the user keymap** — a broken keymap must not hide its own repair route. They are palette-invocable and reachable through the recovery chord, but stay **outside** the minimal degraded-mode command set `tool-graph.md` specifies for the recovery palette.
- The keymap-error surface shows the **absolute path** with an open/reveal action; the path is also logged as a fallback.

**Open-before-stub behavior, decided in Phase 21:** "open user keymap" reads the `KeymapPathAvailability::Resolved` case and **atomically creates the normal stub when absent**, then invokes one fallible platform opener boundary; the `Unavailable` case reports its reason. "Reveal config directory" creates the directory if needed and reveals it. Neither depends on worker timing. Keep platform file opening behind that single fallible boundary; path resolution, directory creation, and file publication stay platform-independent. **Name what that boundary returns** — a `Result` with a named failure type, or an enum stating opened / failed-with-reason. Not `Option<()>` and not `bool`: this value is what the palette turns into user-facing text, so the reason has to survive the call.

**Also worth copying from Zed:** a query normalizer (`normalize_action_query`, `command_palette.rs:48`) so a user typing either the authored title or the raw `camera::reset_home` id matches the same row. Zed's palette label is a humanized rendering of the id; **hana's is the authored title** (R6) — but both should be searchable.

**Files:**
- `crates/hana/src/tool/command_palette.rs` — the three route commands declared with `command!`, palette query normalization, and the keymap-error surface with absolute-path open/reveal actions
- `crates/hana/assets/keymap.default.jsonc` and the generated companion reference — **document the keystroke spellings as Phase 20 left them.** Phase 19 shipped bare modifier-family primary triggers (`shift`, `ctrl`, `alt`, `secondary`) as legal binding values, and nothing in the plan yet tells a user that. **Corrected by Phase 20 — do not write the superseded rule.** Phase 20 retired `platform-` from rendered output entirely (`keystroke/mod.rs:148-166`, `:326-341`). The accepting and rendering spellings now differ, and the doc must say so:
  - **Accepted on input** for the Cmd/Super family: `cmd`, `command`, `super`, `win`, `platform` (plus `cmdleft`/`cmdright` as bare primaries).
  - **Rendered on output**, per running OS: `cmd` on macOS, `super` everywhere else. `platform-` is never emitted.
  - **`secondary` is resolved per-OS at parse time, not a family of its own** (`:440`, `:457`): macOS → the Platform family, so it renders `cmd`; every other OS → the Control family, so it renders `ctrl`.
  Text asserting that the spellings "round-trip as `platform`" is now false and must not ship — this is the one surface a confused user reads. This phase owns the error and repair surface, which is where a user lands when a binding they wrote did not do what they expected, so it owns this documentation too.

This phase writes to `/Users/natemccoy/rust/hana_rubric` only, and produces one checkpoint there.

**Constraints from prior phases:**
- Phase 3's durable diagnostic resource derives `Reflect` and `#[reflect(Resource)]`, so BRP can read the current failure set during debugging; the error surface reads the same resource. **It now holds two collections, and the surface must read both.** Phase 12 split `KeymapLoadFailures` into `diagnostics` (regenerated per load, partially cleared on success) and `retained_diagnostics` (build-time failures no later load can reproduce, never cleared). Context-registration failures — the ones that leave a user with an app that routes nothing — live *only* in the retained half, so a surface reading `diagnostics` alone shows an empty list on exactly the worst failure. Use the combined accessor `all_diagnostics()` (`crates/hana_rubric/src/diagnostic.rs:99-100`).
- **The error surface must split on `Diagnostic.severity` — that field became meaningful in Phase 14b.** This constraint was written when every diagnostic was a failure. `all_diagnostics()` now also carries `DiagnosticSeverity::Advisory` entries from 14b's derived-context validation (an unreachable variant, a stripped fallback-targeting rule, a duplicate target), which are authoring notes, not breakage. A surface that renders the stream flat presents "variant `dimension_lock` is unreachable" with the same weight as a parse error that broke the user's keymap. Both `Diagnostic.severity` and `retained_diagnostics` are `pub` (`diagnostic.rs:74`, `:94`). `DiagnosticSeverity` has exactly two variants, `Failure` and `Advisory`.
- **`source_path` is not always a file path.** Diagnostics produced before path resolution — every defaults-side failure — carry the sentinel `EMBEDDED_DEFAULTS_SOURCE` that `finish_assembly` stamps, not a filename. The absolute-path requirement needs an explicit rule for it: map the sentinel to `KeymapPaths::default_keymap()` for display and for the open/reveal action, since that is the file the published defaults land in. A surface that passes the sentinel straight to a file-open call gets a nonexistent path.
- **That mapping rule needs a second arm.** Both of Phase 21's new startup diagnostics carry the sentinel, and the `UnconfiguredKeymapPlugin` / `AppNameNotConfigured` cases coincide with `KeymapPathAvailability::Unavailable` — where `KeymapPaths::default_keymap()` does not exist to map to. In that case render `KeymapPathFailure::reason()` (`crates/hana_rubric/src/disk/paths.rs:94-108`) instead of a path, and offer no open/reveal action.
- **Phase 21 shipped two startup diagnostics, not one, and neither is named `NotSupplied`.** They are `DiagnosticKind::MissingDefaultKeymap` (a `KeymapPlugin` built with `DefaultKeymapSource::NotSupplied`) and `DiagnosticKind::UnconfiguredKeymapPlugin` (a plugin that produced no configuration at all — e.g. built with only a context source), both at `crates/hana_rubric/src/diagnostic.rs:40-45`. Both arrive through `all_diagnostics()` as `DiagnosticSeverity::Failure` via `startup_diagnostic` and must render as failures, not advisories — the app routes nothing in either state.
- Phase 16 chose the ids. Phase 20: these three commands are palette-invocable and reachable from the recovery chord, but are **not** in the recovery palette's minimal degraded-mode set.
- **A `command!` call site needs five names imported unqualified**, not four: `InputAction`, `Event`, `Reflect`, `ReflectEvent`, and `ReflectKeymapCommand`. The expansion writes `#[reflect(Event, KeymapCommand)]`, so bevy's derive forms *both* `ReflectEvent` and `ReflectKeymapCommand` by concatenation in the caller's scope. **`hana_rubric::prelude::*` does not cover this** — it has **42** exports after Phase 21 (which added `ActiveConditionState`, `CommandInfo`, `CommandInvocationOutcome`, `CommandLookup`, `ConditionHandle`, `DefaultKeymapSource`, `KeymapPathAvailability`, `KeymapPathFailure`, and `TimeoutOutcome` on top of Phase 19's `HeldCommandLookupOutcome`, `ModifierFamily`, `OrdinaryKey`, `PrimaryTrigger`, and `InvalidOrdinaryKeyCode`), including the free functions `cancel_pending_sequences` and `reset_physical_input`, but **not** the `command!` macro and **not** `Event`/`Reflect`/`ReflectEvent`/`InputAction`. Write the five imports explicitly.
- **`command!`'s keys are positional and fixed**: `action`, `event`, `id`, `title`, `description`, and optionally `capability` — in that order, `capability` last. It is `macro_rules!` arm matching, not a keyword parser; a key out of order produces an opaque "no rules expected this token" error. Omitting `capability` yields `Capability::OneShot`. **`capability: Held` is a deliberate `compile_error!`** — a held command is declared with the leading `held,` marker and no `capability:` key, which is what gives its event the `phase: HoldPhase` field. `capability: Capability::Held` matches no arm at all (the generic arm takes a bare ident).
- **Every `KeymapCommand` impl must satisfy `Event + Reflect` and implement both `fn hold_phase(&self) -> Option<HoldPhase>` and Phase 11b's `fn build() -> Self`**, and `#[reflect(KeymapCommand)]` additionally requires `for<'a> <Self as Event>::Trigger<'a>: Default` (`command/reflect_command.rs:53-57`) — a hard compile error, not a silent skip (`crates/hana_rubric/src/command/reflect_command.rs:15`). `command!` generates the method — non-held forms return `None`, the `held,` form returns `Some(self.phase)`. A hand-written impl that omits it fails to compile; that is deliberate, so a held command cannot register as silently inert.
- **`command!` has no event-only arm.** Each route command declared with it also generates an `InputAction` type that nothing spawns. That is accepted, not an oversight — do not hand-roll the event to avoid it. See `## Deferred cleanup`.
- **The palette filters on `Capability::is_palette_invocable()`** (Phase 4b, Decision E): false for `Held`, true for `OneShot` and `Unremappable`. These three route commands are `OneShot`, so they appear; held commands never do.
- Phase 13a: `keymap.jsonc`, `keymap.default.jsonc`, and `keymap.schema.json` are materialized from embedded defaults without overwriting an existing user file.

**Constraints from Phase 20 — read these before writing any test in this phase.**

- **`PermanentShortcutsContext` does not exist.** Phase 20 deleted the marker component, its `Actions::<…>` spawn, and its `add_input_context` registration. Do not reference it, do not spawn it, and do not add a command to it. A command that must survive a broken keymap is declared `Unremappable` and bound through `with_protected_keystroke` (`crates/hana/src/input/mod.rs`); the live handler precedent is `open_recovery_palette` in `crates/hana/src/input/global_shortcuts.rs`.
- **A keymap reload now completes in roughly 100ms, not 5s, and the timeout that proves it is `DISK_RELOAD_TIMEOUT = Duration::from_secs(2)`.** Phase 20 fixed a filesystem-watch defect (the callback compared event paths to the configured path with `==`, but macOS reports fully resolved paths, so any config directory reached through a symlink never matched and every edit silently fell back to the 5s poll audit). Three of this phase's gate items wait on the disk worker — the broken-keymap case, the no-config-directory case, and the companion-publication-failure case. **Use a ~2s deadline for each.** 2s is roughly four times the real reload and still below the 5s poll audit, so a test that starts needing more than ~2s is reporting that the native watch regressed rather than being flaky. Do not raise the deadline past 5s to make a test pass; that hides the exact regression the constant exists to catch.
- **`Display` for the Cmd/Super family renders per-OS**, so any assertion on rendered keystroke text must branch on `cfg!(target_os = "macos")` rather than hard-coding a spelling. See the `keymap.default.jsonc` entry in **Files** above for the accepted-versus-rendered split.
- **The `hana_rubric` prelude's export set changed.** It does not export the `command!` macro, `Event`, `Reflect`, `ReflectEvent`, or `InputAction`; see the five-explicit-imports constraint above.
- **The disk-dependent test fixtures this phase needs already exist, but are private to another file's test module.** `CONFIGURATION_ENVIRONMENT_LOCK` (`crates/hana/src/input/global_shortcuts.rs:274`), `ScopedXdgConfigHomeOverride` (`:335`), `companion_keymap_routing_app`, `wait_for_companion_files` (`:619`), and `wait_for_invalid_user_keymap_diagnostic` are all inside `mod tests` in `global_shortcuts.rs`, while this phase's **Files** put the palette and error-surface work in `crates/hana/src/tool/command_palette.rs`. **Do not silently duplicate ~150 lines of fixture.** Either promote them into a shared `#[cfg(test)]` helper module both files import, or place this phase's disk-dependent tests alongside the existing ones in `global_shortcuts.rs`; pick one and say which in the implementation report. There is no environment race to design around — `verify.sh test` runs `cargo nextest`, which isolates each test in its own process.

**Every test this phase adds must live in `src/` under `#[cfg(test)]`, not in `crates/hana/tests/`.** hana is bin-only (`crates/hana/src/main.rs`, no `lib.rs`) and `verify.sh test hana` pins `--lib`/`--bins` from cargo metadata, so a `tests/` target compiles under `verify.sh lint hana` (which passes `--tests`) and **never executes** — it looks healthy and proves nothing.

**Pending decision:** how the error surface learns where a diagnostic came from — raised by the Phase 21 architect review, 2026-08-03. **Resolve this before dispatching Phase 23.**

Actual problem:
This phase's gate says the error surface shows an absolute path. For the two failures it calls most important — "the ones that leave a user with an app that routes nothing" — there is no path to show, and the field it would read carries something else entirely. `Diagnostic.source_path` is a `String` that today holds four different kinds of value depending on which code path produced it, and only two of them are file paths.

What exists now:
- A real file path, for a keymap the worker actually read.
- The literal `"embedded defaults"` — `const EMBEDDED_DEFAULTS_SOURCE` (`crates/hana_rubric/src/keymap_plugin.rs:38`), which is **private** and exported from neither `lib.rs` nor the prelude, so hana can only match it by hardcoding the same string.
- The **application name**, not a path at all: `record_unavailable_keymap_paths` writes `app_name.unwrap_or(EMBEDDED_DEFAULTS_SOURCE)` (`keymap_plugin.rs:399`).
- An **empty string**, for every context-registration failure (`condition_diagnostic`, `crates/hana_rubric/src/condition.rs:311`) — the retained diagnostics this phase exists to surface.

What should change:
Replace `source_path: String` with an enum that states which of those four things it is — `DiagnosticSource::{KeymapFile(PathBuf), EmbeddedDefaults, ContextRegistration, PathsUnavailable(KeymapPathFailure)}` — so hana matches on a variant instead of guessing from a string's shape, and the "no path exists" cases have somewhere to say so. The cheaper alternative is to make `EMBEDDED_DEFAULTS_SOURCE` public and narrow this phase's gate to "shows an absolute path *when one exists*", leaving the empty-string and app-name cases to render as blank or as a name that looks like a path.

Recommendation:
Take the enum. The gate as written is unreachable otherwise, and the string-matching alternative puts a private constant's literal spelling into another repository, where a rename in `hana_rubric` breaks hana silently at runtime rather than at compile time. The cost is that this reopens `hana_rubric`'s public API after Phase 21 declared it closed — which Phase 24 already does for three bare `Option`s, so this joins that work rather than standing alone. Concretely: land `DiagnosticSource` as **Phase 24 Spec item 2**, alongside the `Option` naming, and dispatch Phase 23 after it. That reorders 23 and 24 — the alternative is a fifth item in this phase that edits `hana_rubric` while every other line of it edits hana.

**Acceptance gate:** `verify.sh check|test|lint hana` green, with one checkpoint in `/Users/natemccoy/rust/hana_rubric`. Tests: the three palette commands work with a **deliberately broken** user keymap in place; they work with **no** config directory; they work after a companion-publication failure. **Those three disk-dependent items each wait on the worker with a ~2s deadline** — see the Phase 20 constraint above for why 2s and not 5s. "Open user keymap" creates the stub when it is absent rather than failing, and the `KeymapPathAvailability::Unavailable` case reports its reason without panicking. The error surface shows an **absolute** path, maps the `EMBEDDED_DEFAULTS_SOURCE` sentinel to `KeymapPaths::default_keymap()` rather than passing it to a file-open call, falls back to `KeymapPathFailure::reason()` when `KeymapPathAvailability::Unavailable` makes that mapping impossible, renders both `MissingDefaultKeymap` and `UnconfiguredKeymapPlugin` as failures, and renders `DiagnosticSeverity::Failure` and `Advisory` entries distinguishably. Palette search matches both the authored title and the raw id.

### Phase 24 — `hana_rubric`: the closing correctness pass · status: todo

**Blocked by:** Phases 21, 22, and 23.

**Goal:** the last library-side correctness items land, and the whole workspace passes a full-target gate. Nothing publishes, nothing is renamed, and no consumer manifest changes.

Two facts that make that safe, verified 2026-08-03. hana already consumes `hana_rubric` by path (`/Users/natemccoy/rust/hana_rubric/Cargo.toml:46`) and already carries a bare `bevy_kana = "0.3.0"` at `:77` with no `features` key — Phase 15 completed both edits, so there is nothing left for a migration step to do. nateroids is untouched by this plan and stays that way.

**Spec:**

Four pieces, **in this order**. The Escape review is last — it is the final step of this phase and therefore the final step of the plan.

1. **The held-prefix merge rule** — see the pending decision below. Lands in `crates/hana_rubric/src/keymap/merged.rs`.
2. **Name the three bare `Option`s Phase 21's sweep did not reach.** The standing ruling — every bare `Option` becomes a self-documenting type — applies to these, and no other phase touches them.
   - **`MatchOutcome::Reprocess { deferred: Option<T>, keystroke }`** (`crates/hana_rubric/src/keystroke/sequence_matcher.rs:32-37`), exported from `lib.rs:71` and the prelude. Phase 21's sweep counted public *signatures*; this is a public enum *field*, so it survived. The `None` arm means "no completed prefix to fire before reprocessing this keystroke" — name that.
   - **`KeymapPlugin.app_name: Option<String>`** (`crates/hana_rubric/src/keymap_plugin.rs:46`) and **`KeymapPluginConfiguration.app_name`** (`:282`). Fix these at the source, not at the helpers: `insert_keymap_path_availability` and `record_unavailable_keymap_paths` both take `Option<&str>` only because they inherit it from these two fields, so naming the field once resolves all three. The absence already has a name in shipped code — `KeymapPathFailure::AppNameNotConfigured` — so the new type should agree with it rather than invent a second vocabulary for the same state.

   These are public-API changes, made after Phase 21 declared the surface final. That is deliberate: nothing outside this workspace consumes the crate, and Phase 21's declaration was about *scope discipline*, not a compatibility promise. If a rename breaks a call site in hana, this phase does **not** edit hana — see the pending decision on re-verifying hana below, which governs what happens then.
3. **Validate the shipped defaults against the real validator.** Carried forward, unanswered, since Phase 13b. `correct_document_passes_draft_seven_validation` (`crates/hana_rubric/src/keymap/schema.rs`) validates a five-line hand-written document, not the file this crate actually ships. The one test that reads a generated schema off disk — `crates/hana_rubric/tests/keymap_companion_files.rs` — checks descriptions structurally and never validates anything. Close it inside that integration test, where `jsonschema` is already a dev-dependency: validate `include_str!("../examples/keymap_demo.jsonc")` against the schema the test has just read back off disk. **This phase's gate is the only one in the plan that runs that file** — `verify.sh test hana_rubric` pins `--lib`/`--bins` from cargo metadata, so `tests/` targets never execute under it; only `verify.sh final` reaches them.
4. **The Escape ownership review — the last step of this phase, run after 1, 2, and 3 have landed.** See the decided block below. It is a review, not an implementation task: it reads the final context and routing model and records an answer. It changes no routing code.

**Decided (2026-08-03):** the Escape ownership review is the last step of this phase — raised by the Phase 20 architect review, resolved by the user the same day

Actual problem:
The deferred Escape task may conclude that the current physical action remains the permanent owner, or that the final context/keymap model can replace it. The second answer changes `hana_rubric`'s public routing surface, and the plan currently schedules the review under `## Deferred cleanup` — after every phase has closed — which puts a possible public-API change after the point where this plan claims the API is settled.

What exists now:
- Phase 18 preserves one physical Escape action that fans out to the ordered close/clear path and the separate rotation-cancel path.
- The Escape architecture review sits in `## Deferred cleanup`, after all phases.

What should change:
- Run the Escape architecture review here, in this phase, after the final routing/repair phase (23) and before the plan closes.
- If the review keeps Rust ownership, record that boundary in the plan and finish. If it chooses keymap migration, insert and complete an implementation phase before the plan closes.

Recommendation:
Move the review into this phase. It preserves the requested end-of-implementation timing while ensuring the plan does not declare the routing surface final ahead of a review that can change it.

**Resolved — run it in this phase, as its last step.** The Escape review is Spec item 3 and executes only after the held-prefix merge fix and the defaults validation have landed and the gate is green. Because it is the final step of the final phase, anything it commissions is by construction post-plan work: **it must not insert a new phase.** Record its conclusion in the plan — either the Rust action and its handlers are the permanent ownership boundary, or the context model can express the fan-out and a migration is written up as follow-on work outside this plan. The review reads code and decides; it does not edit routing.

The `## Deferred cleanup` entry for this review stays where it is as the description of what the review must weigh — the ordered `EscapeStep::{CloseOutputWindow, CloseInspectors, ClearSelection}` fallthrough and the separate `CancelRotateEvent` path, and the requirement that any migration change no behavior, duplicate no state, and never turn one Escape press into several commands. Its scheduling clause is superseded by this block.

**Decided (2026-08-03):** make the held-prefix precedence symmetric — raised by the Phase 20 architect review, approved by the user the same day

Actual problem:
Phase 19 made the held-prefix policy source-aware in `crates/hana_rubric/src/keymap/merged.rs`: when a held single-stroke binding conflicts with a longer sequence that shares its prefix, which one survives now depends on which layer each came from. The rule was specified for one direction only, and the mirror direction falls through to the older blanket rule that rejects every participant.

What exists now:
- **Shipped default binds `f` as a hold, user file adds `f g`** → the user's `f g` is rejected, the shipped `f` survives, one diagnostic names both commands and both source files. This is the approved behavior from Phase 16 and it is implemented.
- **User file binds `f` as a hold, shipped default already has `f g`** → both are rejected. The user's deliberate rebind produces nothing, and the shipped `f g` they were overriding also stops working.
- A conflict entirely within one source rejects every participant. That case is deliberate and not in question.

Neither governing principle produces the second outcome. "Later layers win" would keep the user's hold and drop the shipped `f g`. "A valid hold beats a conflicting sequence" would also keep the user's hold. The all-reject result is what happens when neither rule is consulted, and the user is left with a keymap where their own edit did nothing and something that used to work no longer does.

What should change:
Apply the same precedence in both directions — a valid hold wins over a conflicting longer sequence from the *other* source layer, whichever layer the hold is in. In the mirror case that means the user's `f` becomes live, the shipped `f g` is rejected, and the diagnostic is attached to the rejected shipped binding naming both commands and both source files, exactly as in the approved direction.

Recommendation:
Make it symmetric, in `keymap/merged.rs`. The alternative — leaving it — means a user rebinding a key that happens to be a shipped sequence prefix gets silence, which is the failure mode the whole diagnostic mechanism exists to prevent. Cost is one added case in the conflict resolver plus its two tests (user hold beats shipped sequence; explicit tombstone still overrides).

**Resolved — make it symmetric.** A valid hold beats a conflicting longer sequence from the *other* source layer, whichever layer the hold is in. The Phase 16 direction (shipped hold beats user sequence) is unchanged and its tests must still pass. In the mirror case the user's hold goes live, the shipped sequence is rejected, and the single diagnostic attaches to the rejected shipped binding naming both commands and both source files — the same shape as the approved direction, not a new diagnostic form. A conflict entirely within one source still rejects every participant; that behavior is deliberate and out of scope here. The all-reject mirror outcome was a fallthrough to the older blanket rule, not a chosen policy, which is why this is a defect fix rather than a policy change.

**Files:**
- `crates/hana_rubric/src/keymap/merged.rs` — the symmetric held-prefix precedence, per the pending decision above
- `crates/hana_rubric/src/keystroke/sequence_matcher.rs` — name the `deferred: Option<T>` field of `MatchOutcome::Reprocess` (`:32-37`)
- `crates/hana_rubric/src/keymap_plugin.rs` — name `KeymapPlugin.app_name` (`:46`) and `KeymapPluginConfiguration.app_name` (`:282`); the two `Option<&str>` helpers follow from those two fields
- `crates/hana_rubric/src/lib.rs` and `crates/hana_rubric/src/prelude.rs` — export whatever new names Spec item 2 introduces, alongside the existing `MatchOutcome` export (`lib.rs:71`)
- `crates/hana_rubric/tests/keymap_companion_files.rs` — validate the shipped `examples/keymap_demo.jsonc` against the schema read back off disk

**Constraints from prior phases:**
- **This phase writes to `bevy_hana_rubric` only, and produces one checkpoint there.** No other repository is touched. If a change appears to require editing hana or nateroids, that is a signal the change is out of scope, not that the scope was too narrow.
- **No manifest edits of any kind.** No `version` field, no dependency line, no `release.toml`. The plan's path dependencies are the intended end state for its duration.
- **`crates/hana_rubric/Cargo.toml` already carries a `[dev-dependencies]` section** with `jsonschema.workspace = true` and `serde_json.workspace = true` (added by Phase 13b, backed by root `Cargo.toml:82` and `:83`). The validation work in Spec item 3 needs no new dependency.
- **The workspace members are named `hana_*`** — `hana_clerestory`, `hana_lagrange`, `hana_valence`, `fairy_dust`, `hana_conduit`, `hana_diegetic`, `hana_lading`, `hana_liminal`, `hana_prosody`, `hana_rubric`, and `bevy_kana`. Earlier phases in this plan are archive records and keep their original spellings; only this phase's text is corrected. The `bevy_kana` name is the user's to change, after this plan.
- **`verify.sh final` is the workspace-wide gate and runs only here.** Everything deferred to "the full gate" throughout this plan — all targets, all examples, the `tests/` directory — resolves at this point and nowhere earlier.
- **This gate is the *first execution* of an integration test Phase 21 already edited.** `verify.sh test <pkg>` pins `--lib`/`--bins` (`verify.sh:62-71`) and `lint` only compiles `--tests`, so Phase 21's swap of `KeymapPaths::new` for `KeymapPathAvailability::for_app_name(…).into_resolved()` in `crates/hana_rubric/tests/keymap_companion_files.rs:90-97` has **compiled but never run**. This phase's `verify.sh final` runs it for the first time, in the same file where Spec item 3 adds new assertions. If it fails, check the inherited Phase 21 change before assuming the new assertion caused it.

**Pending decision:** whether this phase re-verifies hana before the plan closes — raised by the Phase 21 architect review, 2026-08-03. **Resolve this before dispatching Phase 24.**

Actual problem:
This phase changes how two keymap layers merge (`keymap/merged.rs`) and renames public types, in a crate hana consumes by path (`/Users/natemccoy/rust/hana_rubric/Cargo.toml:46` points at this working tree). Then it closes the plan on `verify.sh final` in `bevy_hana_rubric` alone. hana ships its own defaults and its own user keymap, so a merge-behavior change lands in hana the moment it rebuilds — but nothing in this plan ever builds hana again after that change.

What exists now:
- The gate is `verify.sh final` in `bevy_hana_rubric`.
- **Not in scope** forbids any change to `/Users/natemccoy/rust/hana_rubric`.
- The library and the application are separate checkouts on separate branches, joined only by that path dependency.

What should change:
Add `verify.sh check|test hana` to this phase's gate, run from the hana checkout after the two code items land. It is read-only — it compiles and tests, it writes nothing. The bar against editing hana stays exactly as it is; what changes is that a break becomes visible here instead of the next time someone runs the application.

Recommendation:
Add it, and treat a failure as a recorded finding rather than a license to edit hana. Concretely: if `check` or `test` fails, write the failure into the plan as follow-on hana work and close this phase anyway — the scope bar is the user's decision and this phase does not overrule it. The alternative, closing the plan without ever building the consumer of the crate it just changed, means the first person to notice is whoever runs hana next with no record of what caused it.

**Acceptance gate:** `verify.sh final` green in `bevy_hana_rubric`, with one checkpoint there. No bare `Option` remains in `MatchOutcome::Reprocess`, `KeymapPlugin`, or `KeymapPluginConfiguration`, and the two `Option<&str>` helper signatures are gone with them; `rg 'Option<' crates/hana_rubric/src/keystroke/sequence_matcher.rs crates/hana_rubric/src/keymap_plugin.rs` returns nothing in a public signature or public field. Tests: a user-file hold that conflicts with a shipped sequence sharing its prefix wins, the shipped sequence is rejected, and one diagnostic names both commands and both source files; an explicit tombstone still overrides regardless of direction; the Phase 16 direction (shipped hold beats user sequence) still passes unchanged. `crates/hana_rubric/tests/keymap_companion_files.rs` validates `examples/keymap_demo.jsonc` against the schema it read off disk and fails if the shipped defaults would be rejected by the validator this crate publishes. The Escape review runs last, after the three code items are green, and its outcome is recorded in the plan — either as the permanent Rust ownership boundary or as post-plan follow-on work. It must not add a phase.

**Not in scope:** publishing anything; renaming `bevy_kana`; editing `.claude/config/release.toml`; any change to `/Users/natemccoy/rust/nateroids` or `/Users/natemccoy/rust/hana_rubric`; any hana behavior change. Those are the user's own post-plan work.

## Deferred cleanup — after the plan is complete

Structural debt accepted knowingly during the run, to be addressed once every phase has landed rather than interleaved with feature work.

- **Move the two context plugins to the top of `crates/hana_rubric/src/condition.rs`.** `ResourceContextPlugin` (`:326`) and `StateContextPlugin` (`:359`) sit well below the imports, against `~/rust/nate_style/rust/module-structure.md:23` — "if the file defines `impl Plugin for`, put the plugin struct and its impl immediately after imports/constants." Phase 14b's fix pass applied exactly this rule to `DerivedContextPlugin` in `derived_context.rs`; `condition.rs` was left alone because the violation predates the phase and the move is noise inside a review diff. Pure code motion, no behavior change.
- **Split `crates/hana_rubric/src/keymap/document.rs`.** The file is now 1614 total lines with `mod tests` at `document.rs:1237`, so roughly 1236 non-test lines against this workspace's rule of 500 non-test lines plus independently testable sections. The legal cut is the source-position-tracking pass, contiguous at the end of the file: `SourceIndex` (`document.rs:787`) through `SourceIndexError` (`:1227`) — including `SourceIndex`, `SourceLocation`, `JsoncScanner`, the intervening indexed helper types, `StringToken`, and `SourceIndexError`. **`SourceLocation` must be imported back into `document.rs` from the new sibling**: the `pub(super)` source types that stay behind reference it. Move the pass to `crates/hana_rubric/src/keymap/source_index.rs` as a **sibling**, never `keymap/document/`, because `self_named_module_files = "deny"` forbids `document.rs` sitting beside a `document/` directory; `SourceLocation` and `StringToken` become `pub(super)`. This is *not* the `wire.rs` / `parse.rs` split Phase 6's Work Order forbade — that one separates the data shapes from the parsing logic, this one separates the two parsing passes.
- **Add an event-only arm to `command!`, retiring the vestigial `InputAction` types.** Every command declared with `command!` also generates an `InputAction` type; for a command with no physical binding, nothing ever spawns it. Phase 20 produced the first live instance: `OpenCommandPalette` (`crates/hana/src/tool/command_palette.rs:26`) is declared, nothing spawns it, and `tool/mod.rs` dropped the re-export. **This is accepted debt, not a bug to fix mid-plan** — Phase 21's Work Order explicitly rules that the missing arm is "accepted, not an oversight" and forbids hand-rolling the event to dodge it. Adding the arm later is an additive `macro_rules!` change, so publication does not freeze it. Recorded here so a delegate that reads Phase 20's retrospective does not "fix" what Phase 21 forbids.
- **Review whether Escape's complete physical-action fan-out should move into the keymap.** **Scheduled: this is Phase 24's last step (decided 2026-08-03), not post-plan work — the text here describes what the review must weigh.** Phase 18 deliberately preserves the physical `Escape` action because its `Start<Escape>` currently triggers both the ordered `EscapeStep::{CloseOutputWindow, CloseInspectors, ClearSelection}` fallthrough and the separate `CancelRotateEvent` path. After all context and routing phases have landed, review whether the final context model can express that fan-out and ordering without changing behavior, duplicating state, or turning one Escape press into multiple unintended commands. If it cannot, record the Rust action and handlers as the permanent ownership boundary rather than forcing a configuration-driven rewrite.
